City of Kingman Zoning Code at a Glance

User Guide Summary Overview

The City of Kingman Zoning Code governs how land can be used and developed within city limits.

Divides all land into zones and outlines what is:

o Permitted

o Conditionally permitted

o Not permitted

Sets development standards for each zone, including:

o Building height

o Parking requirements

o Setbacks (distances from property lines)

o Lighting and other design considerations

Zoning Code Structure

Titles 1 & 2 :

Outline the processes managed by the Development Services Department, such as:

o Development Review

o Conditional Use Permits

o Zoning Code Amendments 2. Titles 3 & 4:

Contains the city’s comprehensive land use matrix, development standards and use specific standards for different types of projects. 3. Title 5:

Definitions

Zoning Code Updates

The Zoning Code is not static; it may be updated periodically in response to:

o Community concerns

o Changing conditions

o Business trends

o Legislative updates

Collaborative Oversight

Although administered by the Development Services Department, the Zoning Code is implemented in collaboration with the:

Building Department

Engineering Department

Fire Department This coordination ensures safe and orderly development.

Additional Design & Development Standards

In addition to the Zoning Code, developers and builders must also comply with the following regulations:

City of Kingman Design & Administration Manual

City of Kingman Utility Regulations

City of Kingman Streets and Sidewalks Rules and Regulations

3

City of Kingman Standard Specifications and Details

City of Kingman Subdivision Ordinance

2018 International Building Code

2018 International Fire Code

How to Navigate This Code

The City maintains an online Zoning Districts Map that can be used to look-up the zoning of any property within the City as well as any overlays or special districts that may affect a property: www.CityofKingman.gov

The Land Use Matrix in Title 3 of the Zoning Code (Table 3-10.060.A) lists which uses are permitted, conditionally permitted, or not permitted for a zone.

Title 3, Division 3-20 through Division 3-70 of the zoning code describe development standards for each zone, overlay, and special district.

Some land uses within the Land Use Matrix have additional “use-specific standards” listed – these use-specific standards can be found in Title 3, Division 3-80.

Title 4 lists supplemental standards related to property development. These include, but are not limited to, driveway standards, landscaping standards, parking standards, signage standards, and lighting standards.

Frequently Referenced Sections

The following references are provided in response to frequently asked questions and are intended to help guide common project types.

Important:

Even when a specific section addresses a project type, all applicable standards in the Zoning Code must still be met . For example, an accessory building must comply with:

The use-specific standards found in Section 3-80.020 , and

The zone-specific development standards found in Title 3 .

Applicants are responsible for ensuring their projects meet all relevant zoning, design, and safety requirements outlined in the Zoning Code and related regulations.

Title 1: Title, Purpose, and Jurisdiction
Title 1: Title, Purpose, and Jurisdiction
1-10 Title

Sections:

1-10.010 Title

1-10.010 Title

A. This document shall be known and may be cited as “the City of Kingman Zoning

Code” or “the Zoning Code.” Within this Title it may also be known as “this Code” or “these regulations.”

B. The Zoning Map that is a part of this Zoning Code shall be known as “The City of

Kingman Official Zoning Map” or “Zoning Map”.

Remainder of page intentionally blank.

14

Title 1: Title, Purpose, and Jurisdiction

1-20 Legislative Intent and Purpose

Sections:

1-20.010 Legislative Intent 1-20.020 Purpose of the Zoning Code 1-20.030 Consistency with the General Plan and Other Adopted Regulations

1-20.010 Legislative Intent

In adopting this Code, it is the intent of the City to protect and promote the public health, safety, convenience, and general welfare of the citizens of the City by exercising all powers related to the regulation and use of land and structures within the City that are authorized by Arizona Revised Statutes (A.R.S.).

1-20.020 Purpose of the Zoning Code

A. The purpose of this Code is to gather and organize the zoning regulations for the

development of land within the jurisdiction of the City in a form that is comprehensive, straightforward, easily understood, and usable by those who wish to develop land, citizens, public officials, and City staff.

B. Another purpose of this Code is the implementation of the goals, policies and

strategies of the General Plan and other adopted plans of the City.

C. This Code intends to:

Promote the health, safety, convenience, and general welfare of the citizens of the City.

Facilitate the orderly growth of the City in conformance with the General Plan

and ensure the adequate provision of transportation, water, wastewater, schools, parks, and other public facilities.

Regulate the height and size of buildings and other structures and the density of

populations to provide for orderly growth and development of the City.

Provide for appropriate land conservation to protect natural resources, provide

adequate open spaces, and avoid harmful environmental impacts.

1-20.030 Consistency with the General Plan and Other Adopted Regulations

Any actions taken pursuant to this Code shall be consistent with the General Plan and all applicable adopted regulations.

15

Title 1: Title, Purpose, and Jurisdiction

1-30 Authority

Sections:

1-30.010 Authority 1-30.020 Responsibility for Administration 1-30.030 Applicability 1-30.040 Rules of Interpretation 1-30.050 Rules for Construction of Language 1-30.060 Computation of Time 1-30.070 Calculation of Fractions 1-30.080 State Law Requirements

1-30.010 Authority

This Code is enacted based on the authority vested in the City by the Arizona Constitution, Article XIII, the Kingman Municipal Code, and Arizona Revised Statutes, Title 9, Chapter 4.

1-30.020 Responsibility for Administration

A. Responsible Bodies and Individuals. This Code shall be administered by the Director of the Development Services Department

(Director).

1-30.030 Applicability

In addition to all applicable Federal, State, and County laws and regulations governing land use and development, this Code applies to all land within the City, as follows:

A. New Land Uses or Structures, Changes to Land Uses or Structures.

No building, structure, or land shall be used or occupied, constructed, replaced, enlarged, or altered, nor shall any applicable permit be issued after the Effective Date of this Code or any subsequent amendment to this Code unless in conformity with all the provisions of this Code and other applicable regulations.

B. Subdivisions.

Any subdivision of land proposed within the City after the Effective Date of this Code or any subsequent amendment to this Code shall be consistent with the requirements defined in Title 3 (Zones) for the appropriate zone, the City of Kingman Subdivision Ordinance, and all applicable requirements of this Code.

C. Property Owned by Federal or State Agencies.

The provisions of this Code shall not apply to property owned by the United States of America or any of its agencies, the State of Arizona, or any local agency not required to comply with this Code by Federal or State law. All exempt agencies are

16

Title 1: Title, Purpose, and Jurisdiction

encouraged to design any new projects in compliance with the standards set forth in this Code and to cooperate in meeting the goals and objectives of this Code and the General Plan.

D. Applications Filed Prior to the Effective Date.

Complete applications for new project entitlements and preliminary plats that are

filed prior to the Effective Date of this Code or any subsequent amendment to the Code may be approved under the provisions of the zoning code previously in effect. Applicants may also elect to develop in compliance with the provisions of the current version of the Code. Unless otherwise specified by this Code or a Review Authority, if a Building Permit application is not filed within one year of the date of approval of the application for new development, the approval shall expire and a new submittal in compliance with the Code in effect at that time would be required. No time extensions shall be permitted.

Applications for amendments to the Zoning Map filed prior to the Effective Date

of this Code or any subsequent amendment to this Code shall be governed by the provisions of the Code previously in effect unless the applicant elects to comply with the current version of the Code.

E. Planning Applications Filed After the Effective Date.

All applications for new project entitlements as well as amendments to the Zoning Map, filed on or after the Effective Date of this Code or any subsequent amendment to this Code, including modifications and amendments, shall conform to the provisions of this Code.

1-30.040 Rules of Interpretation

A. Where uncertainty exists regarding the interpretation of any provision of this Code

or its application to a specific site, the Director shall determine the interpretation of the provision.

B. In the event that a provision of this Code allows the exercise of discretion in the

application of a specific standard or requirement, but does not identify specific criteria for a decision, the following criteria shall be used in exercising discretion:

The proposed project complies with all applicable provisions of this Code;

The exercise of discretion will act to ensure the compatibility of the proposed

project with its site, surrounding properties, and the community; and

The decision is consistent with the General Plan.

C. The determination of the Director may be appealed in compliance with the

provisions of Division 2-80 (Procedures for Appeal).

D. Whenever any provisions within this Code impose overlapping or contradictory

regulations, or whenever any provisions of this Code and any other code, rule, or regulation impose overlapping or contradictory regulations, the provision which is more restrictive or imposes higher standards or requirements shall govern.

17

Title 1: Title, Purpose, and Jurisdiction

E. It is not intended that any provision of this Code nor any act by an administrative

official or Review Authority shall restrict or impair the right of any person to bring legal or equitable action for redress against nuisances, hazards, or injuries to persons or property.

F. The Department shall keep a record of interpretations made in compliance with

this Section. The record of interpretations shall be kept on file and shall be made available to the public. [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

1-30.050 Rules for Construction of Language

The following rules of construction shall apply:

A. The particular controls the general.

B. The words “shall,” “must,” “will,” “is to,” and “are to” are always mandatory.

C. “Should” is not mandatory but is strongly recommended; and “may” is permissive.

D. The words “includes” and “including” shall mean “including but not limited to. . .”

E. Unless the context clearly indicates the contrary, the following conjunctions shall

be interpreted as follows:

“And” indicates that all connected words or provisions shall apply.

“Or” indicates that the connected words or provisions may apply

singly or in any combination.

“Either . . . or” indicates that the connected words or provisions shall apply

singly but not in combination.

F. References to departments, divisions, sections, commissions, boards, or

other offices are to those of the City unless otherwise indicated.

G. References to a public official in the City are to that person who performs the

function referred to and includes a designee of such official.

1-30.060 Computation of Time

When a period of time is prescribed by this Code, it shall be working days only.

1-30.070 Calculation of Fractions

Any fraction greater than or equal to 0.50 shall be rounded up to nearest whole number. Any fractional unit less than 0.50 shall be rounded down to nearest whole number.

1-30.080 State Law Requirements

Where this Code references applicable provisions of State law (e.g., A.R.S. § 9-500.12) the reference shall be construed to be to the applicable State law provisions as they may be amended from time to time.

18

Title 1: Title, Purpose, and Jurisdiction

1-40 Severability

A. If any division, section, sentence, clause, phrase, word, portion, or provision of this Code

is held invalid or unconstitutional by any court of competent jurisdiction, such decision shall not affect, impair, or invalidate any other division, section, sentence, clause, phrase, word, portion, or provision of this Code which can be given effect without the invalid provision.

B. A determination by order of any court of competent jurisdiction that any section,

sentence, clause, phrase, word, portion, or provision of this Code does not apply to any particular property or structure, or to any particular properties or structures, shall not affect the application of such section, sentence, clause, phrase, word, portion, or provision to any other property or structure not specifically included in the court’s order.

Remainder of page intentionally blank.

19

1-50 Effective Date

Section:

1-50.010 Effective Date

1-50.010 Effective Date

This Zoning Code is hereby enacted as the zoning ordinance for the City and shall be

effective as of 12:01 A.M., June 6, 2021, unless this Zoning Code is referred to a vote of the people, in which case it will take effect, if at all, 10 days after the election approving its adoption. Subsequent amendments shall be effective 30 days following the date of adoption.

20

Title 2: Administration and Procedures
Title 2: Administration and Procedures
2-10 Purpose

Sections:

2-10.010 Purpose

2-10.010 Purpose

The purpose of this Title is to establish the organization, powers, and duties of the Review Authorities responsible for the administration of this Code, to provide for the orderly processing of development applications, and to establish provisions for the enforcement of this Code.

Remainder of page intentionally blank.

21

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

2-20 Administration

Sections:

2-20.010 Purpose

2-20.020 Board of Adjustment

2-20.030 City Council

2-20.040 Heritage Preservation Commission

2-20.050 Planning and Zoning Commission

2-20.060 Development Services Department

2-20.070 Historic Preservation Officer

2-20.010 Purpose

The purpose of this Division is to identify and establish the powers and duties of the Review Authorities. When carrying out their assigned duties and responsibilities, all Review Authorities shall interpret and apply the provisions of this Code as minimum requirements to achieve the purposes established in the General Plan and this Code.

2-20.020 Board of Adjustment

The Board of Adjustment shall have the powers and duties established in this Code subject to A.R.S. §9-462.06 and in City Code Chapter 2, Article IX (Advisory Boards, Committees, and Commissions). The purpose of the Board is to hear and decide requests for variances from zoning regulations and appeals of zoning administrator decisions.

The Board shall adopt rules in accordance with the provisions of this Code. Meetings of the Board shall be held at the call of the staff or the Chairperson, and at such other times as the Board may determine. The Chairperson, or in their absence, the acting Chairperson, may administer oaths and take evidence. The Board shall keep minutes of its proceedings showing the vote of each member upon each question and shall keep records of its examinations and other official actions, all of which shall be filed within five days of the meeting date in the Office of the City Clerk and shall be public record.

2-20.030 City Council

For the purposes of this Code, the Council shall have the power to hear; decide; review and approve, approve with conditions, continue, deny; designate; and/or establish the following:

A. The General Plan and amendments to the General Plan, specific plans, and such other plans as it may deem necessary and appropriate;

B. Appeals as authorized by Division 2-80 (Procedures for Appeals);

22

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

C. Amendments to the Zoning Map and amendments to the text of this Code in compliance with the provisions of Division 2-50 (Amendments to Zoning Code Text, Planned Development Zones, and the Zoning Map);

D. Development agreements in compliance with Section 2-40.040 (Development Agreements) and A.R.S. § 9-500.05;

E. Landmarks, Historic Properties, and Historic Districts in compliance with the provisions of Section 2-40.080 (Heritage Preservation Procedures);

F. Annexation of property and City rights-of-way and roadways in compliance with the provisions of Division 2-90 (Annexations);

G.  Schedule of fees and charges for the various applications and services provided,

as approved by resolution, in compliance with this Code;

H.  The Council may take such other action as it may deem desirable and necessary

to implement the provisions of these regulations and the General Plan; and

I. To act as the Board of Adjustment upon majority vote of the Council when a quorum of the Board cannot be achieved due to absence or disqualification.

2-20.040 Heritage Preservation Commission

The powers and duties of the Heritage Preservation Commission are established in City Code Chapter 2, Article IX, (Advisory Boards, Committees, and Commissions).

2-20.050 Planning and Zoning Commission

The powers and duties of the Planning and Zoning Commission (Planning Commission) are established in City Code Chapter 2, Article IX (Advisory Boards, Committees, and Commissions).

The Planning Commission shall have the power to review, decide, and make recommendations to the Council on matters as set forth in this Code. The Planning Commission may also make recommendations to the Council on policy matters regarding the interpretation, enforcement, or administration of the Code as well as General Plan amendments.

2-20.060 Development Services Department

The Development Services Department (Department) is responsible for carrying out the intent of the General Plan and Code in compliance with A.R.S. § 9-461.01 and City Code Chapter 2, Article VIII. [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

The Director of the Development Services Department (Director) shall oversee the Development Services Department and act as the Zoning Code Administrator to interpret, administer, and enforce the provisions of this Code, the Subdivision Ordinance, and the related policies established by the Council to the members of the public, City departments, and other branches of government in compliance with A.R.S. § 9-462.05. The Director has the authority to designate tasks and responsibilities to Department staff to assist with carrying out these

23

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

responsibilities.

2-20.070 Historic Preservation Officer

The Director or their designee shall act as the Historic Preservation Officer and has the following powers and duties:

A. Develop and direct all heritage preservation projects, activities, and investigations;

B. Conduct, as needed, an ongoing survey(s) to identify objects, structures, natural

features, sites, places, and areas within the City having historic, architectural, archaeological, cultural or aesthetic significance for the nation, region, State, or City;

C. Provide technical assistance and make professional recommendations on

preservation matters that are brought to the Heritage Preservation Commission, other commissions, or the Council;

D. Make recommendations to the Heritage Preservation Commission on the

designation of cultural resources as Landmarks or as Historic Zones;

E. Serve as liaison between the City and the State Historic Preservation Officer and

other government and non-governmental agencies in all matters pertaining to heritage preservation; and

F. Serve as liaison between the Heritage Preservation Commission and the public,

property owners, other City staff, the Council, and other government and nongovernmental agencies in all matters pertaining to heritage preservation.

24

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

2-30 Common Procedures

Sections:

2-30.010 Purpose

2-30.020 General Overview of Application Process

2-30.030 Fees

2-30.040 Due Diligence Review

2-30.050 Development Plan Review

2-30.060 RESERVED

2-30.070 Review Authorities and Application Types

2-30.080 Notice of Public Hearings

2-30.090 Findings Required

2-30.100 Final Decisions

2-30.110 Effect of Approvals and Denials

2-30.010 Purpose

The purpose of this Division is to establish common procedures and requirements for the preparation, filing, and processing of all development applications required by this Code, unless otherwise noted. This general process is designed to provide a consistent and efficient method for the City to implement its General Plan and other adopted goals, policies, and standards.

2-30.020 General Overview of Application Process

A. Application Submittals.

All applications for development approval must be submitted to the Department

on a City application form, accompanied by applicable fees, deposits, and other materials required by this Code and Department handouts. [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

Only the following persons or entities may submit an application:

a. The owner of the property;

b. An authorized agent of the owner;

c. A person acting in compliance with a purchase contract or exclusive option to purchase the property; or,

d. The Director on behalf of the Council.

25

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

B. Application Content.

The Director shall specify the form and content of all applications required by this

Code. The Department may require supporting materials as part of the application, including legal descriptions, statements, photographs, plans, drawings, renderings, material samples, and other items necessary to describe existing conditions and the proposed development.

The Director may waive the submission of specific materials or information if it is determined to be not needed for reaching a decision on the application.

The applicant shall be responsible for the accuracy and completeness of all

information submitted to the City.

Prior to and as a condition of final approval of a change to any land use

regulation or standard, the Director may require the owner to execute a Waiver of Claims for Diminution in Value in compliance with the A.R.S. § 121131 through § 12-1138.

C. Determination of Administrative Completeness and Substantive Review.

The Department shall review an application for completeness and accuracy

before it is accepted as being complete and accepted for processing. An application is considered complete when:

a. All necessary application forms, documentation, exhibits, materials, maps, plans, reports, and other information specified in the application form, and any additional information required by the Director have been provided and accepted as adequate.

b. All necessary fees have been paid and accepted.

c. In order to determine if the application is complete, the application may be submitted to other City departments, as appropriate.

Notification of Applicant.

a. The Department shall notify the applicant if the application is complete and has been accepted for processing. An application will only be reviewed, or a public hearing scheduled, after an application is determined to be complete.

b. If an application is determined to be incomplete, the Department shall provide written notice to the applicant along with an explanation of the application’s deficiencies and the items that are needed to complete the application.

No further processing of an incomplete application shall occur until the deficiencies are corrected. Permits authorizing the use, construction, reconstruction, or alteration of any structure may be withheld when inadequate information is submitted to determine if the proposed structure is in compliance with the requirements of this Code or other applicable City Codes.

c. An applicant may appeal the Department’s determination of completeness

26

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

to the Director (see Division 2-80 – Procedures for Appeals).

D. Expiration of Application.

If an incomplete application is not deemed complete within six months after

the first filing with the Department, the application shall expire and be deemed withdrawn.

If no new submittals have been made on a project in the substantive review

process within six months after the previous filing with the Department, the application shall expire.

It shall be the responsibility of the applicant to track the expiration date of

their project(s). If an extension is needed, the request for extension must be submitted in writing to the Department as described in subsection E., below.

After expiration of the application, a new application, including applicable

fees, plans, exhibits, and other materials must be submitted to commence processing of a new project application on the same property. The new application must comply with the Code in effect at the time that the new application is submitted.

E. Extension of Application.

The Director may grant one six-month extension upon written request of the applicant. The request for this extension must be received no less than 30 days before the expiration date.

If an extension is granted during the completeness review process, the applicant may request another extension during the substantive review process.

F. Additional Information.

After the application has been accepted as complete, the Department and/or any decision-making body may require the applicant to submit additional information in order to evaluate fully whether an application complies with the requirements of this Code, State, or federal law.

G. Referral of Application.

At the discretion of the Director, or where otherwise required by this Code, A.R.S., or federal law, an application filed in compliance with this Title may be referred to any public agency that may be affected by or have an interest in the proposed land use activity.

H. Concurrent Applications.

If more than one development approval is required, for example a Conditional

Use Permit and a site plan review, the Department may require, or an applicant may request, that the approvals be reviewed concurrently. The Director shall make the final determination as to the appropriateness of reviewing actions concurrently or separately. Depending on the level of approvals required, one decision may need to be made subject to receipt of approval from another Review Authority.

If a General Plan or Zoning Map amendment is required, a Variance,

Conditional Use Permit, or other development approval may not be granted

27

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

prior to the General Plan or Zoning Map amendment approval. However, such development applications may be heard at the same meeting with a related General Plan or Zoning Map amendment by the relevant Review Authority.

In the event that a Major General Plan amendment and a Zoning Map

amendment are required for the same property(ies), they shall be considered separately, with the Major General Plan amendment being approved before the Zoning Map amendment.

If a Minor General Plan amendment and a Zoning Map amendment are

required for the same property(ies), the Planning Commission may hear both requests at the same public hearing. Likewise, the Council may consider both requests together at its public hearing.

I. Availability of Materials.

Applications and supporting materials are public records pursuant to A.R.S. § 39121 through § 39-128. Public records may be reviewed and copied upon request by any person during normal business hours, unless protected from disclosure. Printed copies, emailed copies, and electronic records via flash drive will be made available at a reasonable cost as established by Council resolution.

The applicant shall clearly label each page of copyrighted or trademarked materials (e.g., ©, ®, TM) and such materials will be available for public inspection, but copyrighted materials will not be copied.

Staff reports to the Council, Planning Commission, or Board will be made available to the public only after they have been submitted to the Review Authority for which the report was prepared.

2-30.030 Fees

A. Planning Fee Schedule.

A schedule of planning fees shall be maintained by the Director and made available to the public. See City Code Chapter 2, Article VIII.

B. Required Fees.

All applications required by this Code shall be accompanied by the required fees set by resolution of the Council. Applications shall not be accepted without payment of the required fee.

2-30.040 Due Diligence Review

A. Due Diligence Review.

Due Diligence Review provides a general overview of a proposed

development’s compliance with this Code and other applicable policies and regulations. A Due Diligence Review is required for:

a. General Plan Amendments,

b. Zone Changes,

28

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

c. Subdivisions with 100 or more lots,

d. Annexations, or

e. As determined by the Director.

All other applicants shall apply for Development Plan Review (Section 2 30.050).

There is no fee required for a Due Diligence Review. A request for Due

Diligence Review must be submitted on forms prescribed by the City and accompanied by the information listed in the Due Diligence Application form.

The purpose of a Due Diligence Review is generally to:

a. Familiarize City staff and external agency staff, as may be

applicable, with the development proposal;

b. Determine application requirements and familiarize the applicant with

the review process and procedures;

c. Identify any potential problems as early in the process as possible; and

d. Identify land use and development policies which may affect the

development proposal.

Failure by the Department or external agencies to identify all

required studies or all applicable requirements shall not constitute a waiver of those studies or requirements.

The Department may consult with or request review by any City or

external agency with interest in the application during the Due Diligence Review.

B. Due Diligence Review Meeting.

After reviewing the application, a Due Diligence Review meeting shall be convened to inform the applicant or their representative of applicable City requirements, provide a preliminary list of issues that may be of concern, and suggest possible alternatives or modifications to the project, if applicable. Neither the Due Diligence Review nor the provision of information and/or pertinent policies shall be construed as either a recommendation for approval or denial. No formal action is taken at a Due Diligence Review meeting.

Following the meeting, the Department shall provide the applicant with a written summary of comments.

2-30.050 Development Plan Review

A. Purpose.

Development Plan Review is a required review to ensure that the applicant is

aware of the procedures and substantive requirements of the City and to identify any potential problems or concerns prior to submitting a project for review. Development Plan Review is required for all developments, except those that are one of the exempt project types or waived by the Director as

29

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

described below.

Development Plan Review is required for projects that also completed a Due

Diligence Review. It is the applicant’s responsibility to address comments received during the Due Diligence Review process to the greatest extent feasible before submitting a request for Development Plan Review.

Exemptions:

The following project types are exempt from Development Plan Review:

a. Construction of one new single-family residence or duplex,

b. Minor tenant improvements under 2,000 square feet not requiring a

change of occupancy,

c. Parcel plat,

d. Minor land division, or

e. As otherwise determined by Director.

B. Application for Development Plan Review.

Application Review.

a. Upon receipt of an application for Development Plan Review, the required

information listed in the Development Plan Review application, and required fee, the Director shall refer the Development Plan Review application to any applicable City departments or external agencies, which shall review the application for compliance with City standards and any applicable regulations.

b. Major developments (i.e., those over 20,000 square feet in gross floor

area or over 50 dwelling units) may be scheduled for a longer review period. If a longer review period is required, the Department shall notify the applicant in writing at the start of the review period.

C. Development Plan Review Meeting.

After reviewing the application, a Development Plan Review meeting shall be convened to inform the applicant or their representative of applicable City requirements, provide a preliminary list of issues that may be of concern, and suggest possible alternatives or modifications to the project, if applicable. Neither the Development Plan Review nor the provision of information and/or pertinent policies shall be construed as either a recommendation for approval or denial. No formal action is taken at a Development Plan Review meeting.

Following the meeting, the Department shall provide written comments and a submittal checklist for their specific project to the applicant.

D. Effect of Review.

Failure by the Department or external agencies to identify all required

studies or all applicable requirements shall not constitute a waiver of those studies or requirements.

Completion of Development Plan Review allows an applicant to submit an

30

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

application for review as described in the written comments provided to the applicant following the Development Plan Review meeting. If a project requires entitlements, the applicant would be required to complete that step before submitting a site plan and building permit for review, subject to Director determination.

The comments and checklist received during the Development Plan Review

meeting are valid for one year after the Development Plan Review meeting date. The applicant may request a one-year extension from the Director prior to the expiration date. After this date, an applicant would be required to submit a request for a new Development Plan Review meeting.

E. Waiver for Development Plan Review.

The Director may waive the Development Plan Review requirement if a

proposal meets one or more of the following requirements, at the discretion of the Director:

a. The proposal is relatively simple, with few development-related issues;

or,

b. The proposal involves expansion of industrial or commercial use less

than 2,500 square feet; or,

c. The proposal involves the improvement of a space 2,500 square feet

or less meeting the following:

i. Non-structural interior walls or partitions.

ii. No change to building footprint, height or utility services.

iii. No change of Occupancy Classification.

iv. No modifications to the site to include but not limited to traffic

circulation, parking requirements and access or drive aisles.

d. The proposal involves subsequent phases of an already approved

project; or,

e. The applicant has successfully completed many similar projects within

the City; or,

f. There are other features of the proposal that would not necessitate

Development Plan Review.

A Development Plan Review waiver must be submitted on a form

prescribed by the City, together with the information and materials requested in the waiver and the required fee.

2-30.060 RESERVED [ Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

A. RESERVED B. RESERVED C. RESERVED D. RESERVED E. RESERVED

All developments which require a permit are subject to inspection to ensure compliance with the provisions in this Code.

B. Appeals.

Any person, firm, or corporation aggrieved by a decision of the Review Authority in interpreting, applying, or enforcing this Code may file an appeal in compliance with the appeal provisions established in Division 2-80 (Procedures for Appeals).

2-30.080 Notice of Public Hearings

Notice shall be given in compliance with the requirements of this Section prior to consideration of any action shown in Table 2-30.080.A. All of the following applicable provisions shall be met:

A. Notice Requirements.

Public notification required under this Title shall be subject to the following requirements, unless as may be otherwise specified in the City Code or as set forth in A.R.S. Any changes to Arizona Law notice requirements shall have control over this provision in the manner of providing notice.

33

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

[Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

Table 2-30.080.A (Summary of Public Hearing Notice Requirements) summarizes

the notice requirements for various application types. No notices are required for application types not listed in the table.

All required notifications as specified in Table 2-30.080.A must include:

a. The date, time, and place of the hearing;

b. A general explanation of the matter to be considered;

c. A general description of the area affected; and

d. Indication of where additional information on the matter may be obtained.

e. If the matter to be considered applies to territory in a high noise or accident

potential zone as defined in A.R.S. § 28-8461, the notice must include a general statement that the matter applies to property located in the high noise or accident potential zone.

Notices as specified in Table 2-30.080.A must be provided as follows:

a. Published Notice.

A public hearing display advertisement (in accordance with A.R.S. § 9-462.04) shall be placed by the City, at least once in a local newspaper of general circulation within the City. The advertisement must be published at least 15 calendar days prior to the public hearing. The applicant shall be responsible for reimbursing the City for the costs associated with the required noticing.

b. Mailed Notice.

A notice of public hearing shall be sent by the City, via U.S. first class mail, at least 15 calendar days prior to the public hearing. The applicant shall be responsible for reimbursing the City for the cost of printing and postage for the

34

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

mailings in addition to providing the City with mailing labels containing the names and addresses of:

i. The applicant or representative and owners of the subject property;

ii. All real property owners, as shown on the latest Mohave County assessment records, within 300 feet of the outer boundary of the subject property. This notification area may be expanded if other policy adopted by the Council requires notification within a larger area or the Director determines the potential impact of the project extends beyond the required notification boundary;

iii. Residents, registered neighborhoods, and homeowners’ associations that may be impacted as a result of the application, and other neighborhood entities as determined by the Director;

iv. Interested parties which have requested that they be placed on a contact list for this application;

v. Interested parties that have been identified by the City;

vi. If the proposed project involves land that abuts other municipalities or unincorporated areas of Mohave County or a combination thereof, notice of the public hearing shall be sent to the planning agency of the adjacent governmental unit.

c. Posted Notice.

A notice of public hearing shall be posted on the lot, parcel, or tract of land that is the subject of the application by the applicant. [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024] Refer to the temporary sign standards in Division 4-90 for size requirements.

i. A minimum of one posting must be placed in a manner that is clearly visible from the public right-of-way adjoining the subject property;

ii. The Department may require additional signs to be posted on the lot, parcel, or tract of land.

iii. The posting(s) must be placed at least 15 calendar days prior to the public hearing. A signed affidavit of sign posting, including photographs of each sign posted and a map showing the location(s), must be provided by the applicant to the City.

iv. Notice(s) must be composed of weatherproof materials and printed so

that the following are visible from a distance of 100 feet:

(a.) The application type;

(b.) The application request;

(c.) Date, time, and location of the hearing.

v. The applicant shall maintain the sign in good condition throughout the required posting period.

35

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

vi. The sign posting must be removed by the applicant no later than 10

days after the public hearing and final action. A signed affidavit of removal shall be provided by the applicant to the City.

d. Optional Public Notice.

The Director may permit additional noticing of public hearings on the City’s website or social media platforms available on the internet to augment traditional forms of public noticing. Suitable projects that may be considered for optional public noticing include those that may result in major policy changes, affect large neighborhoods or districts, or that may have strong public interest.

The applicant also has the option of requesting to host a neighborhood meeting. If an applicant elects to host a neighborhood meeting, it must be convened at a publicly accessible time and place approved by the Director. Public notice of the meeting is also required via legal ad, mailing, and site posting a minimum of fifteen days prior to the meeting pursuant to the above requirements.

2-30.090 Findings Required

Findings must be based upon consideration of the application, plans, testimony, reports, and other materials that constitute the record and shall be in writing or included in the minutes of a meeting of the Board, Council, or Commission. For administrative decisions, the findings shall be established in the Notice of Decision that the Department shall issue following a decision. Subsequent Sections describe specific findings that must be made for different approval types.

2-30.100 Final Decisions

A. Time of Decision.

As applicable, a final decision shall be deemed to have been made at the time

action is taken by the Commission, Council or Board.

For decisions made by the Director, a final decision shall be deemed to have

been made on the date of issuance of a Notice of Decision by the Director.

B. Notice of Decision.

The Department shall mail the Notice of Decision to the applicant at the mailing address stated in the application and to any other person or entity requesting such notification in writing after a decision is made by the Director or any Commission, Council, or Board.

2-30.110 Effect of Approvals and Denials

A. The issuance of a permit or approval described in this Code authorizes the holder

to proceed in compliance with the terms of the permit or approval. The applicant must follow the procedures of this Title for any additional applicable permits or

36

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

approvals in order to complete the development and occupancy requirements for the subject property.

B. A new application concerning property for which a previous application has been

denied may only be considered when:

The new application does not involve the same request or allege the same

misinterpretation or hardships as the previous application;

The new application, in the opinion of the Director, involves a development

proposal which is materially different from prior proposals, or is responsive to negative findings established in the denial of the previous application;

A substantial change in the use of adjacent property has occurred

since the previous application was denied; or

A period of not less than one year has passed since the previous application

was denied and all appeals provided by the City have been exhausted.

37

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

2-40 Permits and Approvals

Sections:

2-40.010 Purpose

2-40.020 Building Permits and Certificates of Occupancy

2-40.030 Conditional Use Permits

2-40.040 Development Agreements

2-40.050 General Plan Amendments

2-40.060 Minor Improvement Permits

2-40.070 Minor Modifications to Development Standards

2-40.080 Heritage Preservation Procedures

2-40.090 Parking Lot Maintenance Permits

2-40.100 Preliminary Plats and Final Plats

2-40.110 Sign Permits – Permanent Sign Structures

2-40.120 Sign Permits – Temporary Wall Banner Signs

2-40.130 Site Plan Review and Approval

2-40.140 Temporary Use Permits

2-40.150 Zoning Verification

2-40.010 Purpose

This Division provides the specific requirements for the preparation, filing, and processing of applications required by this Code. For Common Procedures, refer to Division 2-30 (Common Procedures).

2-40.020 Building Permits and Certificates of Occupancy

A. Purpose.

This Section provides requirements and procedures for the issuance of Building Permits and Certificates of Occupancy to ensure that new development is in compliance with this Code.

A Building Permit must be approved before construction may commence. Building Permits are reviewed concurrent with site plans.

A Certificate of Occupancy is issued upon final inspection of the improvements authorized under a Building Permit.

B. Building Permit.

38

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

No development shall be constructed, or no new use established, until the

applicant or an authorized representative has obtained a Building Permit.

No Building Permit shall be issued unless the application for development

approval complies with the provisions of this Code, other applicable City codes (including, for example, provision for street and utility rights-of-way and easements), and specific prior approvals applicable to the proposed development.

C. Certificate of Occupancy.

No building or structure shall be occupied or used until the Building Official has

issued a Certificate of Occupancy and has determined that the building has been constructed and all on- and off-site improvements have been developed in compliance with the provisions of this Code and any other applicable City codes.

If the structure or use does not comply with the requirements of this Code or

with any other applicable City codes and ordinances, the Building Official shall not issue a Certificate of Occupancy, unless it is determined that a Temporary Certificate of Occupancy may be issued.

D. Temporary Certificate of Occupancy.

A Temporary Certificate of Occupancy may be issued by the Building Official

provided that:

a. The applicant demonstrates that the incomplete components of the building

and site (such as landscaping or private or public infrastructure) will not affect the public health, safety, and general welfare;

b. The applicant demonstrates that completion is impractical at the

time the Certificate of Occupancy is sought due to weather or other conditions; or

c. The applicant secures the completion of the construction with appropriate

assurances in a form acceptable to the Director and the City Attorney, and in an amount sufficient to complete the construction.

Requests for Temporary Certificates of Occupancy must contain:

a. A proposed agreement between the City and the applicant in which the applicant commits to complete the specified on-site improvements. The agreement shall have a maximum 12-month term and shall be nonrenewable.

b. The reason for the request;

c. The expected completion date;

d. Payment of applicable fees; and

e. Financial assurance acceptable to the City, the term of which shall exceed the term of the agreement by no less than 60 days.

2-40.030 Conditional Use Permits

39

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

A. Purpose.

The purpose of Conditional Use Permits is to review uses and activities that are generally permitted in an applicable zone, but that require more discretionary review and the possible imposition of conditions to mitigate the effects of the proposed use.

B. Applicability.

Title 3 (Zones) identifies land uses and activities that require a Conditional Use Permit.

C. Effective Date.

The Conditional Use Permit will have an Effective Date following the date of approval by the City Council unless appealed in compliance with Division 2-80 (Procedures for Appeals). [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

D. Findings.

The Planning Commission may recommend approval of the Conditional Use Permit only after making the following findings: [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

That the conditional use is consistent with the objectives of this Code and

the intent of the zone in which the site is located.

That granting the conditional use will not be detrimental to the public

health, safety, or welfare. The factors to be considered in making this finding shall include, but not be limited to:

a. Property damage or nuisance arising from noise, smoke, odor,

dust, vibration, or illumination;

b. Hazard to persons or property from possible explosion,

contamination, fire, or flood; and,

c. Impact on surrounding areas arising from unusual volume or character of traffic.

That the characteristics of the conditional use as proposed and as it may

be conditioned are reasonably compatible with the types of uses permitted in the surrounding area. The Conditional Use Permit shall be recommended for approval only when the Planning Commission finds that the applicant has considered and adequately addressed the following to ensure that the proposed use will be compatible with the surrounding area:

[Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

a. Bulk regulations, including proposed use, density, building height,

lot coverage, setbacks, etc. in relation to the site’s size and topography;

b. Access, traffic, and pedestrian, bicycle, and vehicular circulation;

c. Adequacy of site and open space provisions, including

resource protection standards, where applicable;

d. Noise, light, visual, and other pollutants;

e. Proposed style and siting of structure(s), and relationship to the

surrounding neighborhood;

40

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

f. Landscaping and screening provisions, including additional landscaping

in excess of otherwise applicable minimum requirements;

g. Impact on public

utilities;

h. Signage and outdoor

i. lighting;

j. Dedication and development of streets adjoining the property; and

k. Impacts on historical, prehistoric, or natural resources.

E. Conditions of Approval.

The Planning Commission or City Council may attach conditions of approval to a

Conditional Use Permit as are necessary to carry out the purposes of the General Plan, other adopted plans, or findings of Subsection D, above. Some of the conditions may include, but are not limited to: limitations on size, bulk, and location; requirements for additional landscaping or buffers; provision of adequate ingress and egress; mitigation of adverse environmental impacts; and other conditions such as the duration of the permit, hours of operation, and time limits on construction.

Conditions of approval will only be imposed if they are necessary to

ensure:

a. The intent and purpose of this Code are met;

b. Compatible and complementary development of the property; or,

c. The provision of appropriate off-site improvements will be fulfilled.

F. Effect of Approval.

Issuance of a Conditional Use Permit shall authorize only the use for which it

is issued, the approval for which shall run with the land.

All conditions of approval shall be binding upon the applicant, their

successors, and assigns; shall run with the land; shall limit and control the issuance and validity of Certificates of Occupancy; and shall restrict and limit the construction, location, use, and maintenance of all land and structures within the development.

If a Zoning Map amendment that does not allow the conditional use is

processed for the site after the use has been established, the use will be allowed to continue only as a legal, non-conforming use subject to the terms and conditions of the Conditional Use Permit and the requirements for nonconforming uses in Division 2-60.

G. Time Limits and Permit Implementation.

A Conditional Use Permit shall become null and void one year after the

Effective Date unless one of the following has occurred:

41

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

a. A Building Permit has been issued and construction begun and diligently

pursued;

b. The approved use has been established;

c. An extension has been granted by the Director at the request of the

applicant. Such extension shall be for a maximum of one year, and no extension may be granted which would extend the validity of the permit more than two years beyond the Effective Date of the permit; or, [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

d. The City Council has established a different expiration date for the

Conditional Use Permit, such as tying it to the expiration date of a Zoning Map amendment for the same property.

Development of the use shall not be carried out until the applicant has secured

all other permits and approvals required by these regulations, the City, or applicable regional, State, and federal agencies.

H. Compliance/Revocation.

The Director, upon inspection and review of any Conditional Use Permit,

shall notify the applicant in writing if it is determined that the use is not in compliance with the conditions of approval. If the applicant fails to correct the violation within 15 days of notification, the Director shall report the violation of the conditions of approval of the Conditional Use Permit to the City Council with a recommendation for possible revocation of the Conditional Use Permit. The Director shall notify the applicant in writing and set the matter of revocation for a public hearing following the procedures established in Section 2-30.080 (Notice of Public Hearings). [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

Findings for Revocation or Modification.

a. A Conditional Use Permit may be revoked or modified if, from the facts

presented at the public hearing, the City Council determines any one of the following findings: [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

i. The Conditional Use Permit granted is being exercised contrary to the conditions of approval of the Conditional Use Permit or in violation of any applicable law, license, ordinance, permit, or regulation;

ii. The use for which the Conditional Use Permit was granted is being or has been exercised in a manner that is detrimental to the public health or safety; or

iii. The Conditional Use Permit was obtained by fraud.

b. If the City Council finds, following the public hearing, any one of

findings in Subsection H.2, above, the Conditional Use Permit may be revoked and further operation of the use for which the Conditional Use Permit was approved shall constitute a violation of this Code. [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

42

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

2-40.040 Development Agreements

A. Purpose.

The purpose of this Division is to provide procedures for the processing of

development agreements in compliance with A.R.S. § 9-500.05.

B. Contents of Development Agreements.

Provisions Required.

A development agreement must include the following provisions:

a. The duration of the development agreement;

b. Provisions for the protection of environmental resources; if applicable;

c. The public benefit offered by the applicant as consideration for entering

into the development agreement; and

d. A waiver of claims for diminution in value for any changes in land use law

relating to the subject property and the related proposed development.

Provisions Prohibited.

A development agreement shall not include requirements for the City to exercise its legislative or quasi-judicial powers in a particular way.

Provisions Allowed.

A development agreement may include any of the provisions specified in A.R.S. § 9-500.05, as applicable to the development proposal that is the subject of the development agreement.

C. Consideration and Decision.

Staff Responsibilities.

a. The Director in consultation with the City Attorney and the City Manager shall direct the

negotiations with the applicant regarding terms of the development agreement.

b. Once negotiations are completed, the Director shall schedule the proposed

development agreement for approval by the Council in compliance with this Division.

Planning Commission Recommendation.

When considering a development proposal at a public hearing for which a development agreement is proposed, the Planning Commission may make a recommendation on the contents of a draft development agreement for that development.

The Planning Commission does not make formal recommendations on development agreements. If the development proposal tied to the development agreement does not require a public hearing at the Planning Commission, the Planning Commission would not review or provide comment on the development agreement.

Council Determination and Findings.

43

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

a. The Council shall consider approval of the development agreement at a

public meeting. When considering a development proposal for any other development proposal for the subject property, approval of the development agreement may be made conditional upon approval of the related application.

b. When approving the development agreement, the Council shall make the

following findings:

i. The development agreement provides benefit to the City;

ii. The development agreement is consistent with the purpose, intent, goals, policies, programs, and land use designations of the General Plan, any applicable specific plans, and this Code; and,

iii. The development agreement complies with the requirements of A.R.S. § 9- 500.05.

c. Approval of the development agreement shall be by resolution or

ordinance and shall not be enacted by emergency clause. The Effective Date of the agreement may be more but not be less than 30 days after the Council’s approval of the development agreement.

Execution and Recordation.

a. The persons authorized to sign the development agreement on behalf

of the applicant(s), owner(s), and all persons having an interest in the subject property shall execute the development agreement prior to approval by the Council.

b. If changes are made at the Council meeting where the development

agreement is considered, the persons authorized to sign the development agreement on behalf of the applicant(s), owner(s), and all persons having an interest in the subject property shall execute the revised development agreement within 10 days of the meeting and prior to the City signing the development agreement.

c. Within 10 days after all parties, including the City, have executed the

development agreement, the City Clerk shall record a copy, at the applicant’s expense, of the development agreement with the County Recorder. Recordation of the development agreement constitutes notice of the development agreement to all persons.

D. Amendment and Cancellation.

A development agreement may be amended or cancelled, in whole or in part, by mutual consent of the parties to the development agreement or by their successors in interest or assigns using the same procedure for entering into the agreement in compliance with Subsection C, above.

2-40.50 General Plan Amendments

A. Purpose.

44

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

The purpose of a General Plan Amendment is to enable amendments to the City’s General Plan to reflect changes in policy, plans, or conditions. Amendments may be initiated by the Council, Planning and Zoning Commission, Department, or a property owner or their representative.

B. Process

General Plan Amendments are classified as Major, Minor, or Administrative Technical Revisions. The Implementation section of the General Plan outlines differences between these categories and the approval criteria for proposed amendments.

Amendments to the General Plan are to be presented to the Council within twelve months from the date of application. Public outreach is required prior to the public hearings on a Minor or Major Amendment. Administrative Technical Amendments can be approved administratively with no public outreach required.

2-40.60 Minor Improvement Permits

A. Purpose.

This Section establishes the requirements for a Minor Improvement Permit to ensure that compliance with the applicable provisions of this Code is achieved. A Minor Improvement Permit must be approved prior to the placement, installation, construction, or development of the following structures that do not otherwise require a Building Permit as required by applicable City Building Codes:

Fences, Garden Walls and other retaining-type walls that would not otherwise

require a building permit;

New or additional driveway access, subject to approval by the City Engineer

and issuance of a right-of-way permit when applicable;

Attached or detached patio covers;

Accessory structures, such as sheds or storage containers exceeding 200 square feet.

Minor Improvement Permits for new or additional driveways may also be required to apply for a right-of-way permit issued by the City Engineer.

If plans and details for a fence or wall are included on a Building Permit application for other development on the same property, then a Minor Improvement Permit would not be required for those fences or walls.

B. Review and Final Decision.

The Department shall review the Minor Improvement Permit application and supporting documentation for compliance with the requirements of this Code to determine whether the Minor Improvement Permit can be issued. The applicant shall be notified in writing if additional information is required to review the application or if the application is denied.

2-40.070 Minor Modifications to Development Standards

A. Purpose.

45

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

The purpose of this Section is to ensure that Minor Modifications are only

approved for specific circumstances.

B. Applicability.

A Minor Modification may only be approved when there are special

circumstances applicable to the property, and the strict application of this Code would deny the owner of the property privileges enjoyed by other properties located nearby and in an identical zone.

Conditions shall be applied that would ensure that the Minor Modification does

not constitute an approval of special privilege(s) inconsistent with the limitations upon other properties in the vicinity and zone in which the subject property is located.

The authorization to approve a Minor Modification does not extend to making

any changes in the uses permitted in any zone.

The Director may approve a Minor Modification for only those circumstances

specified in Table 2-40.070.A (Types of Minor Modifications), and only after first making the findings specified in the following subsections. [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

Table 2-40.070.A (Types of Minor Modifications)

C. Findings and Final Decision.

The Director, in compliance with Subsection B, above, shall review the

application for a Minor Modification for compliance with the requirements of this Code. A public hearing shall not be required for the decision on a Minor Modification.

The Director may approve a Minor Modification subject to conditions so long as

the development will meet all the other standards or requirements established in this Code which apply to that development, and if the approval would be in compliance with the following findings:

a. There are special circumstances applicable to the property, including for example its size, shape, topography, location, or surroundings;

b. Approving a Minor Modification will not grant special privileges inconsistent with the limitations upon other properties in the vicinity and the zone in which the property is located;

c. The special circumstances applicable to the property are not selfimposed by any person presently having an interest in the property; and,

d. The requested Minor Modification will not allow the establishment of a use that is not otherwise permitted in the zone.

Each application shall be reviewed on an individual case-by-case basis and the

approval of a prior Minor Modification is not admissible evidence for the approval of a new Minor Modification.

47

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

The decision of the Director or may be appealed following the procedures

established in Section 2-80.

The Director may defer making a decision on a Minor Modification and instead

refer the application to the Planning Commission for review and final decision.

If a request for a Minor Modification is denied, the reason for the denial will be

provided in writing to the applicant.

D. Minor Modification to Run with the Land.

A Minor Modification approved in compliance with this Section shall continue to be valid upon a change of ownership of the business, parcel, service, structure or use that was the subject of the permit application in the same area, configuration and manner as it was originally approved in compliance with this Section.

A Minor Modification shall become null and void one year after the Effective Date unless one of the following has occurred:

A Building Permit has been issued and construction begun and diligently

pursued;

The approved use has been established.

2-40.080 Heritage Preservation Procedures

A. Purpose.

The purpose of this Section is to protect and enhance the cultural, historical, and archaeological heritage of the City by recognizing, preserving, enhancing, and perpetuating the use of those objects, structures, sites, and landscape features that represent distinctive elements of the City’s cultural, political, architectural, and archaeological history. The Council finds and intends that preservation of the City’s heritage is in the interest of the health, economic prosperity, education, cultural enrichment, and general welfare of the public. This Section implements the City’s General Plan and is implemented pursuant to the provisions of the National Historic Preservation Act of 1966, as amended, the Certified Local Government program (16 U.S.C. 470a 101(c)(1)), and A.R.S. § 9-462.01, providing the standards and procedures for heritage preservation. Information on the benefits to a property owner and the various incentive programs that are available to assist a property owner to preserve and protect cultural resources on their properties is available from the Historic Preservation Officer.

B. Applicability.

In addition to all other development standards provided in this Zoning Code,

compliance with the requirements of this Section, and review and approval pursuant to this Section are required for the following:

a. Designation of Historic Overlay Zones or Landmark Properties;

b. Development of a property within a Historic Overlay Zone or a Landmark

Property; and,

48

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

c. Cultural resource studies.

Exceptions. Compliance with the requirements of this Section is not

required for the following:

a. Work that the Building Official certifies as correcting an imminent hazard,

for which no temporary corrective measures will suffice in protecting the public safety;

b. Ordinary maintenance or repair of a property or structure, including

public infrastructure, that does not involve a change in any element of design and that does not have an impact that is greater than that of the original construction; and

c. Changes to the interior of structures that do not alter the exterior, the

site, or the setting of the cultural resource.

C. General Provisions.

Conflicting Provisions. When the provisions of this Section conflict with any

other laws, codes, or regulations, then the provisions of this Section shall govern, except for matters of life safety where the more restrictive of such laws, codes, or regulations shall apply.

Application Requirements. In addition to any specific provisions, for all

reviews, considerations, or approvals sought by this Section, an applicant shall submit a completed application on a form prescribed by the City in compliance with Section 2- 30.020, General Overview of Application Process. The application shall include the information and materials specified in the submittal checklist, together with the required fee established in City Code Chapter 2, Article VIII.

Consent Approval.

a. Applicability. The Historic Preservation Officer may review and

approve or conditionally approve the following:

i. Cultural resource studies that are letter reports; and

ii. Certificates of No Effect for minor work that has a limited impact in relation to the total cultural resource, including:

(a). Conforming signs excluding comprehensive sign programs;

(b). A remodel, addition, deck, or porch that does not expand the

floor area or any outdoor activity area by more than 10 percent or 200 square feet;

(c). An accessory structure that is not more than the lesser of 10

percent of the main building’s footprint or 400 square feet;

(d). Minor alterations such as storefront windows or doors, other

fenestration, awnings, shutters, gutters, porch rails, accessible features and facilities, paint colors, lighting, roofing, fencing, retaining walls, walkways, driveways, or landscaping;

(e). Demolition or removal of inappropriate features that are non

49

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

original, including additions, accessory structures and structures that are not cultural resources; and

(f). Modifications to support systems (mechanical, electrical,

satellite dishes and so forth), that are properly sited and screened.

iii. Any matter that the Heritage Preservation Commission refers to the Historic Preservation Officer for approval.

b. Process. Consent approval by the Historic Preservation Officer is an

administrative review and approval that occurs outside of a public meeting.

i. Heritage Preservation Commission Oversight. The Historic Preservation Officer shall regularly review consent matters with the Heritage Preservation Commission. Such reviews shall be informational for the Historic Preservation Officer and the Heritage Preservation Commission and are not ratification of approvals (already granted by the Historic Preservation Officer).

ii. Referral to Heritage Preservation Commission. The Historic Preservation Officer may refer any matter to the Heritage Preservation Commission for any reason and shall refer any matter to the Heritage Preservation Commission when a denial appears appropriate.

Concurrent Development Application Review. At the applicant’s option,

development proposals that require an approval pursuant to this Section may proceed concurrently with other development reviews and processes. However, no permit shall be granted, and no work shall commence, until an approval pursuant to this Section has been granted and mitigation measures have been incorporated into the final design and documentation of the development.

Expiration of Approvals.

a. Any approval pursuant to this Section shall automatically expire if the

plans are altered or construction proceeds in a manner such that the documentation submitted as the basis of the approval no longer accurately represents the work. See also Subsection F, below.

b. Any approval pursuant to this Section automatically expires one year

after the date of approval, unless the work associated with the approval is under way and due diligence toward completion of the work can be demonstrated.

Unknown or Undiscovered Conditions.

a. During the course of any work, all work that could impact the cultural

resource shall be stopped immediately and the Historic Preservation Officer shall be notified if:

i. A potential cultural resource is discovered which was previously unknown; or

50

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

ii. Any conditions are discovered that prohibit conformance with any approval or conditional approval issued pursuant to this Section; or

iii. Any conditions are discovered that warrant any deviation from plans that served as the basis of any approval or conditional approval issued pursuant to this Section.

b. The work shall remain stopped until the applicant has obtained new,

additional, or revised approvals pursuant to this Section.

Kingman Register of Historic Places.

a. The Kingman Register of Historic Places identifies properties or zones

designated by the Council as Landmark Properties or Historic Overlay Zones, which are depicted as such on the official Zoning Map of the City.

b. Supplemental to the Kingman Register of Historic Places, the Historic

Preservation Officer shall maintain lists, maps and other data of areas likely to contain cultural, historic, or archaeological resources and properties believed to be eligible for designation as Landmark Properties or Historic Overlay Zones but that are not yet designated.

c. Information concerning the nature and/or location of any

archaeological resource shall not be made available to the public, pursuant to Federal and State laws.

D. Designation of Historic Overlay Zones or Landmark Properties.

Purpose. Designation of a property as a Landmark Property or Historic

Overlay Zone formally recognizes its significance, and the need to preserve its historic features.

Applicability.

a. Historic Overlay Zone. A group of properties may be designated as a

Historic Overlay Zone if a majority of the properties are significant in accordance with the provisions of this Section or if they provide the necessary setting for a Landmark Property.

b. Landmark Property. An individual property, object, structure, site, sign,

or landscape feature may be designated as a Landmark Property within the Landmark Overlay zone if it is significant in accordance with the provisions of this Section.

Process for Designation of a Historic Overlay Zone. The designation of

property or properties as a Historic Overlay Zone is represented in Figure 240.080.1. (Process for the Designation of a Historic Overlay Zone or a Landmark Property), and shall follow the procedural steps described below:

a. An application for designation of property or properties as a Historic

Overlay Zone, or an amendment to a Historic Overlay Zone, shall be submitted to the Historic Preservation Officer, and shall be reviewed and a recommendation prepared in compliance with the review schedule on file with the Director. The designation of a Historic Overlay Zone requires submittal of the application requirements for a zone change as specified in

51

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

Division 2-50, and as modified by the submittal requirements established for an application for designation of a Historic Overlay Zone.

b. The Council, Heritage Preservation Commission, or an owner of affected

real property may initiate designation. If the proposal includes property other than that owned by the applicant, then a petition in favor of the request, and on a form prescribed by the City, must be signed by affected property owners representing at least 51 percent of the included parcels.

c. The Historic Preservation Officer’s recommendation shall be transmitted

to the Heritage Preservation Commission in the form of a staff report prior to a scheduled public meeting. The staff report shall include the following:

i. An evaluation of the consistency and conformance of the proposed amendment with the goals of the General Plan and any applicable specific plans; and

ii. A recommendation on whether the text amendment or Zoning Map amendment should be granted, granted with conditions to mitigate anticipated impacts caused by the proposed development, or denied.

d. A copy of the staff report shall be made available to the public and any

applicant prior to the Heritage Preservation Commission’s public meeting.

e. Prior to the Heritage Preservation Commission public meeting, the public

meeting shall be noticed in compliance with Section 2-30.080.

f. The Heritage Preservation Commission shall render its decision in the

form of a written recommendation to the Planning Commission and Council. The Heritage Preservation Commission may recommend approval, approval with conditions, or denial of the Landmark Property request.

g. Public hearings of the Planning Commission and Council shall be

noticed and conducted in accordance with Section 2-30.080. The Planning Commission and Council shall act on the Heritage Preservation Commission’s recommendation in accordance with the procedures established in Division 2-30 (Common Procedures).

h. In addition to the above procedures, new Historic Overlay Zones also

require a text amendment to the Zoning Code to create the new zone following the procedures outlined in Section 2-50.020 (Zoning Change (Zoning Map Amendment)).

i. Modification(s) to the boundaries of designated Historic Overlay Zones by

including or excluding properties shall be adopted in accordance with this process.

j. New Historic Overlay Zones require the adoption of development

standards and design guidelines that are specific to the district.

i. Adoption of development standards and design guidelines associated with a new Historic Overlay Zone shall be a fully

52

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

integrated part of the process for designation of the zone and adopted by an ordinance of the Council.

ii. Modification(s) to adopted development standards and guidelines shall be adopted in accordance with the process for designation of a new zone, except that the application requirements exclude the need for all other documentation.

k. Interim Protection for Nominations. Commencing with the Heritage

Preservation Commission making a recommendation for approval of a Historic Overlay Zone, building or demolition permits for any property within the proposed Historic Overlay Zone shall not be issued until any one of the following occurs:

i. The Historic Preservation Officer has reviewed the proposed work and determined that the proposed work is not subject to the provisions of this Section or will clearly not have a major impact on a significant resource.

ii. The Council has approved or denied the proposed Historic Overlay Zone. In the case of zone approval, all work in the new Historic Overlay Zone shall be subject to the provisions of this Section.

iii. Six months have transpired since the Heritage Preservation Commission’s recommendation for approval of the Historic Overlay Zone with no approval or denial.

Process for Designation of a Landmark Property. The designation of a

Landmark Property shall follow the procedural steps represented in Figure 2-40.080.1. (Processes for the Designation of a Historic Overlay Zone or a Landmark Property) and described below:

a. An application for designation of a Landmark Property, or an amendment

to a Landmark Property, shall be submitted to the Historic Preservation Officer, and shall be reviewed and a recommendation prepared in compliance with the review schedule on file with the Director. The designation of a Landmark Property requires submittal of a legal description and/or Assessor’s Parcel Number and the submittal requirements established for an application for designation of a Landmark Property.

b. The Council, Heritage Preservation Commission, or an owner of

affected real property may initiate designation. Property owner consent is required for designation of a Landmark Property.
