c. The Historic Preservation Officer’s recommendation shall be transmitted

to the Heritage Preservation Commission in the form of a staff report prior to a scheduled public meeting. The staff report shall include the following:

i. An evaluation of the consistency and conformance of the proposed amendment with the goals of the General Plan and any applicable specific plans; and

ii. A recommendation on whether the proposed Landmark Property

53

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

designation should be granted, granted with conditions to mitigate any anticipated impacts, or denied.

d. A copy of the staff report shall be made available to the public and any

applicant prior to the Heritage Preservation Commission’s public meeting.

e. The Heritage Preservation Commission shall render its decision in the

form of a written recommendation to the Planning Commission and Council. The Heritage Preservation Commission may recommend approval, approval with conditions, or denial of the Landmark Property request.

f. Public hearings of the Planning Commission and Council shall be

noticed and conducted in accordance with 2-30.080. The Planning Commission and Council shall act on the Heritage Preservation Commission’s recommendation in accordance with the procedures established in Division 2-50.

Figure 2-40.080.1. Process for the Designation of a Historic Overlay Zone or a Landmark

Property

Individual Signs of Historic or Cultural Significance.

a. Signs which may be unusual, significant, or meaningful to the City

streetscape and the City’s history may be worthy of special recognition and may be designated as a Landmark Property in accordance with the

54

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

provisions of this Section if they meet the following criteria:

i. The sign has been in continuous existence at its present location for not less than 50 years;

ii. The sign is of exemplary technology, craftsmanship or design for the period in which it was constructed; uses historic sign materials or means of illumination; and/or is unique in that it demonstrates extraordinary aesthetic quality, creativity, or innovation;

iii. The sign is structurally safe or is capable of being made so without substantially altering its historical character or significance;

iv. If the sign has been altered, it must be restorable to its historic function and appearance; and

v. The sign complies with movement, bracing, and illumination requirements contained in Division 4-90 for sign structures and installations.

b. Effect of Designation. When a sign is found to be significant,

designated as a Landmark Property restored to its historic function and appearance, the sign shall not be subject to the provisions of Division 4-90 (Sign Standards).

E. Development of a Property within a Historic Overlay Zone or a Landmark Property.

Purpose. This Section provides standards and procedures for the

preservation, reconstruction, rehabilitation, or restoration of properties within a Historic Overlay Zone and designated landmark properties.

General Applicability. Except as provided in Subsection B.2, above, all

proposed work on a within a Historic Overlay Zone or a Landmark Property, whether or not any other approval or permit is required, including demolition, shall be approved pursuant to this Section.

Process. Except as provided in Subsection C.2, above, prior to the granting of

any required approvals or permits and prior to the commencement of any work on a within a Historic Overlay Zone or a Landmark Property, the Heritage Preservation Commission or the Historic Preservation Officer shall review all work proposed and approve or conditionally approve the work in the form of a certificate of no effect, certificate of appropriateness, or certificate of economic hardship. The process for review and approval of work within a Historic Overlay Zone is represented in Figure 2-40.080.3, (Processes for Review of Development in a Historic Overlay Zone or a Landmark Property).

Guidelines. Design and development guidelines applicable to all Landmark

Properties or within properties within a Historic Overlay Zone are included in Appendix 1 (Design Guidelines for Historic Properties).

55

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

Figure 2-40.080.3 Processes for Review of Development in a Historic Overlay

Zone or a Landmark Property

Certification of No Effect.

a. Applicability. This approval is appropriate if the proposed work is

compatible with the historic or archaeological character of a cultural resource, such that there will be no major impact on the resource, thereby not diminishing, eliminating, or adversely affecting the significance or integrity of the resource.

b. Criteria for Approval. When approving a certification of no effect, the

Historic Preservation Officer or Heritage Preservation Commission shall find that:

i. The proposed work is consistent with the purpose and intent of this Section;

ii. The proposed work is compatible with its context:

(a). The appropriate context for a landmark or a historic property

is the property itself and, to a much lesser extent, the surrounding properties and neighborhood;

(b). The appropriate context of work in a Historic Overlay Zone is

the significant portions of the property itself, the surrounding properties, and the neighborhood;

iii. The cultural resources associated with the proposed work

56

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

have been sufficiently identified and evaluated;

iv. There are no major impacts to any on-site cultural resources; and

v. The proposed work is consistent with applicable development standards and design guidelines. See also Subsection 7 (Development Standards and Guidelines) of this Section.

Certification of Appropriateness.

a. Applicability. This approval is appropriate if the proposed work alters a

cultural resource but does so in such a way that is compatible with the historic or archaeological character of the resource and all major impacts are mitigated such that the work does not diminish, eliminate, or adversely affect the significance or integrity of the resource.

b. Criteria for Approval. When approving a certification of

appropriateness, the Heritage Preservation Commission shall find that:

i. The proposed work is consistent with the purpose and intent of this Section;

ii. The proposed work is compatible with its context:

(a). The appropriate context for a landmark or a historic property

is the property itself and, to a much lesser extent, the surrounding properties and neighborhood;

(b). The appropriate context of work in a Historic Overlay Zone is

the significant portions of the property itself, the surrounding properties, and the neighborhood;

iii. The cultural resources associated with the proposed work have been sufficiently sought, identified, and evaluated;

iv. Major impacts on cultural resources are sufficiently mitigated; and

v. The proposed work is consistent with applicable development standards and design guidelines.

Certification of Economic Hardship.

a. Applicability. This approval is appropriate if the proposed work, including

demolition and appropriate mitigation measures, will deprive the property owner of reasonable use of or a reasonable economic return on the property; or will result in a substantial reduction in the economic value of the property; or will result in a substantial economic burden on the property owner because the property owner cannot reasonably maintain the property in its current form.

b. Criteria for Approval. When approving a certification of economic

hardship, the Heritage Preservation Commission shall find that:

i. The cultural resources associated with the proposed work have been sufficiently identified and evaluated;

ii. An economic hardship exists (a lack of reasonable use or return, a

57

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

substantial reduction in the value, or a substantial burden);

iii. Preservation is economically infeasible;

iv. The economic hardship is not a self-created hardship;

v. Alternative development has been fully explored; and

vi. Alternative financing has been fully explored.

c. Temporary Delay of Demolition.

i. Demolition permits shall not be issued for any building or structure located in a Historic Overlay Zone or for a Landmark Property until redevelopment plans for the building or structure have been approved by the Review Authority, including the issuance of a Building Permit.

ii. If a Certificate of Economic Hardship is denied by the Heritage Preservation Commission, no demolition shall be permitted for a period of one year from the date of the public meeting when the request was denied. During the temporary delay period, the applicant shall consult in good faith with the Heritage Preservation Commission, State, and local preservation groups, and interested parties in a diligent effort to seek an alternative that will result in the preservation or sale of the property. The property owner shall advertise the property for sale at a fair market value based on appraisals. Following the temporary delay period, if no other plan demonstrates a reasonable alternative and no purchaser has been found, the proposed demolition will be allowed, subject to the issuance of the appropriate permit by the Building Official.

Development Standards and Guidelines. The following standards and

guidelines apply to all approvals granted pursuant to this Section:

a. Industry Standards and Guidelines.

i. Archeology and Historic Preservation: Secretary of the Interior’s Standards and Guidelines as currently amended and annotated by the National Park Service.

ii. The Secretary of the Interior’s Standards for the Treatment of Historic Properties with Guidelines for Preserving, Rehabilitating, Restoring and Reconstructing Historic Buildings.

iii. Preservation Briefs and other similar best practice documents published by the National Park Service.

b. Zone-Specific Development Standards and Guidelines. These

standards and guidelines are available from the Director.

i. Kingman Design Guidelines for Historic Properties; and

ii. Others as may be adopted in association with any designation

58

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

of a new Historic Overlay Zone.

F. Cultural Resources.

Cultural resources are an important consideration in an application for development of a property affecting a Historic Overlay Zone or a Landmark Property. Professionally prepared cultural resource studies are therefore a requirement of an application for development. The type and format of studies required are determined based on the particular circumstances of the property on which development is proposed. Cultural resource studies assess the significance and integrity of potential resources, major impacts that would result from the proposed work and mitigation measures that could eliminate or offset any major impacts. This Section provides detailed requirements for cultural resource studies and explains how such assessments are performed.

Culture Resource Studies.

a. Purpose. To identify significant cultural resources and potential impacts

of proposed development so that mitigation measures can be established for major impacts prior to development of the property.

b. Applicability.

i. Cultural resource studies are required for all public and private developments involving a property in a Historic Overlay Zone; Landmark Properties; or properties adjacent to a Historic Overlay Zone or Landmark Property.

ii. When warranted by the specific conditions of the site or proposed work, the Historic Preservation Officer may determine that a cultural resource study is not required based on the following conditions:

(a). The structure is not significant or lacks integrity; or

(b). The proposed work is excepted from this Section pursuant to

2-40.080; or [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

(c). The proposed work does not have major impacts, diminish the

significance or integrity of the resource, is reversible, or is temporary; or

(d). Other circumstances under which it is reasonable to

conclude that a cultural resource study is not warranted.

iii. The requirement to prepare a cultural resource study does not in and of itself mean that the resources are significant.

c. Specific Application Requirements.

i. Types of Studies. Upon consultation with the Historic Preservation Officer and based on the resources that are known or likely to be present, the applicant shall provide an archeological resource study and/or a historic resource study.

ii. Preparation. Cultural resource studies shall be prepared by professionals qualified in accordance with the Secretary of the

59

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

Interior’s Standards and Guidelines for Archeology and Historic Preservation (36 CFR 61 Appendix A) as currently amended and annotated by the National Park Service.

iii. Report Format. The Historic Preservation Officer will work with the professional conducting the study to determine which one of the following report formats is appropriate:

(a). Letter Reports. A letter report is appropriate when:

(i). Site conditions, historic records, or previous research

or studies indicate that cultural resources are not likely to be present; or

(ii). The integrity of a cultural resource is already severely

compromised; or

(iii). The proposed work will not compromise the significance or

integrity of the cultural resource; or

(iv). When no mitigation measures are warranted.

The report need only demonstrate that one of these conditions exist.

(b). Phase 1 Cultural Resource Studies. When a letter report is

not appropriate, a Phase 1 Cultural Resource Study shall be prepared. A Phase 1 Cultural Resource Study shall:

(i). Identify the presence of cultural resources;

(ii). Evaluate the potential for additional cultural resources being discovered;

(iii). Assess the significance of identified and potential cultural

resources;

(iv). Assess the integrity of identified resources;

(v). Assess identified and potential impacts proposed;

(vi). Provide measures to mitigate major impacts on cultural

resources; and

(vii). Advise whether Phase 2 or Phase 3 Cultural Resource

Studies will be required.

(c). Phase 2 Cultural Resource Studies. A Phase 2 Cultural

Resource Study is required when major impacts are proposed for a significant resource that has integrity and when no other mitigation measures are proposed that would maintain the significance and integrity of the resource. A Phase 2 Cultural Resource Study includes all of the contents of a Phase 1 Cultural Resource Study plus complete text descriptions, asbuilt plans, and archival grade photography that fully document all physical aspects of the resource(s), including its setting. For

60

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

archeological resource studies, the required field research shall also include sampling subsurface exploration to the satisfaction of the State Historic Preservation Office and coordinated with an appropriate repository.

(d). Phase 3 Cultural Resource Studies. A Phase 3 Cultural

Resource Study is only used for archeological resources and requires complete data recovery, which must be systematically excavated, inventoried, recorded, and mapped. The planned recovery must be designed to the satisfaction of the State Historic Preservation Office and coordinated with an appropriate repository.

(e). National Historic Preservation Act Section 106

Documentation. Documentation prepared pursuant to Section 106 of the National Historic Preservation Act of 1966 and approved by the Arizona State Historic Preservation Officer may serve as one of the above report formats. This alternate format is appropriate when the level of review and content of the Section 106 documentation meets the requirements of this Section.

iv. Content. A cultural resource study shall be submitted as a bound document and in an electronic format in a form as determined by the Historic Preservation Officer, and shall contain text, plans, photographs, and other appropriate documentation.

d. Process.

i. Heritage Preservation Commission Review. The Heritage Preservation Commission shall review and accept cultural resource studies and may approve or conditionally approve proposed mitigation measures. Alternatively, the Heritage Preservation Commission may require additional research, documentation, or mitigation measures prior to acceptance. Letter reports may be accepted by a consent approval process described in Subsection C3, above.

ii. When a cultural resource study has been accepted, it shall be offered for curation to the appropriate repository as directed by the Historic Preservation Officer or the State Historic Preservation Office, and in accordance with the standards set forth in 36 CFR 79.9 and 79.10.

iii. The processes for consideration of cultural resources are provided in Figure 2-40.080.2, Processes for Consideration of Cultural Resources.

61

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

Remainder of page intentionally blank.

Figure 2-40.080.2 Processes for Consideration of Cultural Resources

e. Required Recommendations by the Report Preparer.

i. A Phase 1 Cultural Resource Study shall include a recommendation for the preparation of a Phase 2 Cultural Resource Study when:

(a). The assessment of whether a cultural resource’s presence or

significance is indeterminate; or

(b). Major impacts are proposed for a significant resource that has

integrity and when no other mitigation measures are proposed that maintain the significance and integrity of the resource.

ii. A Phase 2 Cultural Resource Study shall include a recommendation for the preparation of a Phase 3 Cultural Resource Study when:

(a). Significant archeological resources are present in the

62

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

development area; and

(b). Actual or potential impacts are major impacts; and

(c). When no other mitigation measures are proposed that

maintain the significance and integrity of the resource.

Determination of Significance of Cultural Resources. The criteria for

determining the significance of a cultural resource is based on the potential of the cultural resource to contribute to our understanding of the past.

a. A cultural resource is significant if:

i. It is listed or eligible as a National Historic Landmark, or for the National Register of Historic Places, or the Arizona Register of Historic Places; or

ii. It is associated with events or persons in the architectural, engineering, archeological, scientific, technological, economic, agricultural, educational, social, political, military, or cultural annals of the City, the State of Arizona, or the United States of America; or

iii. It represents the work of, or for, an important individual; or

iv. It embodies distinctive characteristics of type, period, region, artistic values, or methods of construction, including being the oldest of its type or the best example of its type; or

v. It has yielded, or may be likely to yield, information needed for scientific research, such as important archaeological resources.

b. A resource is generally not significant if:

i. It is less than 50 years old at the time of application; or

ii. The features, materials, patterns, and relationships that contributed to its significance are no longer present or no longer have integrity.

c. Requirement to Meet the Criteria, Regardless of Age. Properties

that are 50 years old are not automatically significant. In order to be significant, all resources, regardless of age, must be demonstrated to meet the criteria for determining the significance of a cultural resource.

Determination of Integrity. Integrity is the ability of a property to convey its

significance and is based on significance, i.e., why, where, and when a property is important. Integrity is the authenticity of a property’s physical identity clearly indicated by the retention of characteristics that existed during the property’s period of significance. Ultimately, the question of integrity is answered by whether or not the property retains the identity for which it is significant.

a. Historic properties either retain integrity (convey their significance) or they do not.

b. The historic physical features that represent the significance of a

property must remain and must be visible enough to convey their

63

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

significance. However, it is not necessary for a property to retain all its historic physical features or characteristics. The property must retain sufficient physical features, historic character, and appearance that enable it to convey its historic identity and the reasons for its significance.

c. To retain historic integrity a property will always possess several, and

usually most, of the following seven aspects of integrity:

i. Location. The place where the historic property was constructed or the place where the historic event occurred.

ii. Design. The combination of elements that create the form, plan, space, structure, and style of a property. Design includes such elements as o rganization of space, proportion, scale, technology, ornamentation, and materials.

iii. Setting. The physical environment of a historic property. Whereas location refers to the specific place where a property was built or an event occurred, setting refers to the character of the place in which the property played its historical role.

iv. Materials. The physical elements that were combined or deposited during a particular period of time and in a particular pattern or configuration to form a historic property. A property must retain the key exterior materials dating from the period of its historic significance.

v. Workmanship. The physical evidence of the crafts of a particular culture or people during any given period in history or prehistory.

vi. Feeling. A property’s expression of the aesthetic or historic sense of a particular period of time. It results from the presence of physical features that, taken together, convey the property’s historic character.

vii. Association. The direct link between an important historic event or person and a historic property.

d. Integrity is not the same as condition. Integrity relates to the presence or

absence of historic materials and character-defining features. Condition relates to the relative state of physical deterioration of the property. Integrity is generally more relevant to the significance of a property than condition. However, if a property is in such poor condition that original materials and features may no longer be salvageable, then the property’s integrity may be adversely impacted and compromised.

e. To be considered authentic, a property must incorporate a substantial

amount of the original features and materials. While new material can exactly copy significant features, if too much historic material is replaced with new material, the integrity of the property is lost, and integrity can never be recreated. The precise replication of features with new materials may produce a building that looks like a historic building, but without substantial retention of actual historic materials, the integrity of the

64

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

property is lost.

Determination of Major Impacts to Cultural Resources. Impacts to

resources are major when they directly or indirectly alter or destroy any of the characteristics that make the resource significant, including when they may diminish the integrity of the resource including its location, design, setting, materials, workmanship, feeling or association.

a. Major impacts include:

i. Physical destruction or damage to all or part of the resource;

ii. Alteration to all or part of the resource that is not consistent with applicable standards and guidelines;

iii. Relocation or isolation of the cultural resource from its setting;

iv. Excessive replacement of original materials;

v. Alteration of the character of the cultural resource’s setting;

vi. Introduction of visual, audible, or atmospheric elements that are out of character with the cultural resource or its setting; or

vii. Neglect of a cultural resource resulting in its deterioration or

destruction.

b. An impact is generally not major if:

i. It does not alter the resource; or

ii. It is reversible; or

iii. It is temporary.

Mitigation Measures.

a. Purpose. To the greatest extent feasible, mitigation measures minimize

or offset major impacts on resources with a general threshold of reducing the impacts to a level that is less than a major impact.

b. Applicability. All proposed work that will or may have a major impact

on a significant cultural resource, as determined by an appropriate cultural resource study, shall incorporate mitigation measures.

c. Professional Design Required . The preparer of a cultural resource study

shall design the appropriate mitigation measures. These may include alternative projects, alternative designs, additional work, or other means. The appropriate type and scope of measures varies depending on the cultural resource and impacts, and shall be recommended based on the professional expertise of the preparer and the following:

i. For Potential Resources or Potential Impacts . Construction monitoring by the report preparer is an acceptable mitigation measure. If monitoring indicates that the work will produce a major impact to a significant cultural resource, construction shall cease in the area of the resource and the report preparer, subject to

65

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

approval pursuant to this Section, shall develop and apply appropriate mitigation measures.

ii. For Identified Major Impacts. The following mitigation measure designs are presented in order of general preference:

(a). Avoidance of significant cultural resources or impacts by

not taking a certain action or parts of an action;

(b). Preservation of cultural resources in place;

(c). Minimizing major impacts by limiting the degree or

magnitude of the action and its implementation;

(d). Allow other parties to acquire cultural resources, cultural

resource sites, or conservation easements;

(e). Data recovery.

iii. Human Remains. Federal and State laws provide standards and regulations for the handling, care and removal of human remains.

Standards and Guidelines. The following standards and guidelines apply to

the preparation, review, and acceptance of cultural resource studies pursuant to this Section:

a. Archeology and Historic Preservation: Secretary of the Interior’s

Standards and Guidelines as currently amended and annotated by the National Park Service.

b. The Secretary of the Interior’s Standards for the Treatment of Historic

Properties with Guidelines for Preserving, Rehabilitating, Restoring and Reconstructing Historic Buildings .

c. Preservation Briefs and other similar best practice documents

published by the National Park Service.

G. Violations and Enforcement.

All work authorized as a result of an approval granted pursuant to this

Section shall conform to any requirements included with it. Deviations from the plans that served as the basis of the approval of a certificate of appropriateness, or from any conditions of approval, constitute a violation of the provisions of this Section. Violations shall be governed by the provisions of Division 2-100 (Enforcement).

It shall be the duty of the Historic Preservation Officer and/or the City Building

Inspector to inspect periodically and assure compliance of any work performed pursuant to the provisions of this Section. Enforcement shall be governed by the provisions of Division 2- 100 (Enforcement).

H. Appeals.

Any person, firm, or corporation aggrieved by a decision of the Historic Preservation Officer or the Heritage Preservation Commission in interpreting, applying, or enforcing this Section may file an appeal in accordance with the

66

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

appeal provisions established in Section 2-80.020.

2-40.90 Parking Lot Maintenance Permits

A. Purpose.

A Parking Lot Maintenance Permit is required to ensure the following to the maximum extent feasible when parking lots are repaved, resealed, or restriped:

Compliance with the applicable provisions of Division 4-80, specifically

regarding parking for disabled persons;

Safety of pedestrians and vehicles; and,

The number of required parking spaces is maintained.

B. Review and Final Decision.

The Department shall review the Parking Lot Maintenance Permit application

and supporting documentation for compliance with the requirements of this Code.

To the maximum extent feasible, existing nonconforming parking areas constructed prior to the Effective Date of this Code should be restriped consistent with the applicable provisions of Division 4-80 (Parking and Loading). However, if the Director determines that such restriping would be infeasible, the parking area may be restriped as it was before, except that the required number and dimensions of handicap accessible parking spaces shall be provided in compliance with the standards provided in Division 4-80 to the maximum extent feasible.

If the Department determines that the parking area will be overlaid with new

asphalt and that there may be implications to drainage, existing utilities, or that revisions to the restriping plan are needed and that a follow-up inspection(s) is required, then the required fee established in City Code Chapter 2, Article VIII shall be applied.

If the Parking Lot Maintenance Permit application is denied, the reason shall

be stated in writing.

2-40.100 Preliminary Plats and Final Plats

A. Overview.

For information on the subdivision process and requirements for subdivision design, refer to the Subdivision Ordinance, Municipal Code Section 2-146, and the fees listed in City Code Chapter 2, Article VIII.

The Director may waive the need to prepare a Preliminary Plat in situations that involve minimal changes to an existing lot or parcel such as a minor land division, lot line adjustment, parcel assemblage, or plat corrections. If the Preliminary Plat requirement is waived, a Parcel Plat or Final Plat will be required. This will be determined during the Due Diligence Review or Development Plan Review.

67

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

B. Review and Final Decision.

Preliminary and Final Plats require approval by both the Director and City Engineer.

2-40.110 Sign Permits – Permanent Sign Structures

A. Purpose.

This Section establishes the permitting requirements for permanent sign structures as described in Division 4-90 to ensure compliance with the applicable provisions of this Code.

B. Sign Permit Requirement.

Where a Sign Permit is required by Division 4-90, it shall be unlawful for any

person to erect, place, display, alter, repair, or relocate a permanent sign structure without first obtaining approval of a Sign Permit from the Department.

A sign permit is not required for a sign on property used exclusively for a

single-family residence or duplex if the sign is otherwise compliant with the requirements in Division 4-90 (Sign Standards).

A sign permit is not required for the following:

a. Changes to the face or copy of changeable signs;

b. Changes to the face or copy of an existing single-tenant or multi-tenant

freestanding or building mounted non-illuminated sign from one business to another with no structural or lighting modifications to the sign;

c. The normal repair and maintenance of conforming or legal non-conforming

signs, except as identified in Section 4-90.050 (General Requirements for All Signs).

C. Review and Approval.

Review and Determination.

The Department shall review the Sign Permit application and supporting documentation for compliance with the standards of Division 4-90 and determine whether the Sign Permit may be issued or if additional information is required from the applicant to complete the permit application. If the Sign Permit application is denied, the reason shall be stated in writing.

Expiration.

A Sign Permit shall expire and become null and void if the work authorized in compliance with the permit is not commenced within 180 days from the date of issuance of such permit, or if work is suspended or abandoned for a period 90 days or more at any time after the work has commenced.

2-40.120 Sign Permits – Temporary Wall Banner Signs

A. Purpose.

This Section establishes the permitting requirements for temporary wall banner

68

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

signs to ensure compliance with the applicable provisions of this Code.

B. Temporary Sign Permit Requirement.

It is unlawful for any person to erect, place, display, alter, or relocate a temporary wall banner sign without first obtaining approval of a Temporary Wall Banner Sign Permit from the Department.

As outlined in Division 4-90, a sign permit is not required for any other type of temporary sign.

C. Duration of Temporary Wall Banner Sign Permit.

The Temporary Wall Banner Sign Permit will be valid for display of a temporary wall banner sign for 90 days per year. The 90 days may be consecutive or may be non-consecutive based on the need of the applicant, not to exceed four display periods in a calendar year.

D. Review and Approval.

Review.

The Department shall review the Temporary Wall Banner Sign Permit application and

supporting documentation for compliance with the standards of Division 4-90.

Determination.

The Department shall review the Temporary Wall Banner Sign Permit application and

supporting documentation for compliance with the standards of Division 4-90 and determine whether the Temporary Wall Banner Sign Permit may be issued or if additional information is required from the applicant to complete the permit application.

If the Temporary Wall Banner Sign Permit application is denied, the reason shall be stated in writing.

Authorization.

Issuance of a Temporary Wall Banner Sign Permit authorizes the holder to install a

temporary sign(s) in compliance with the terms of the permit.

2-40.130 Site Plan Review

A. Applicability.

Site plan review shall be required for all new permitted uses, conditional uses, changes of occupancy, or intensification of uses in any Zone, except for the following:

Detached single-family dwellings, duplexes, and related accessory structures,

buildings less than 200 square feet in area, attached or detached patio covers and other approved uses in approved subdivisions or recorded lots or parcels; [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

Interior tenant alterations or improvements which do not affect

parking or lighting requirements or exterior building appearance;

Nonstructural remodeling of a building façade treatment; and

Sign permits for properties not otherwise subject to site plan review.

69

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

B. Application for Site Plan Review.

Application Requirements.

a. Unless waived by the Director, completion of a Development Plan Review

meeting shall be required for all developments requiring site plan review before an application for Site Plan Review may be submitted as outlined in Division 2-30.

b. An application for Site Plan Review shall be submitted on a form provided

by the Department, in compliance with Division 2-30 together with the information and materials requested in the application checklist and the required fee established in City Code Chapter 2, Article VIII.

c. For projects that also require a Building Permit, Site Plan and Building

Permit reviews and approvals must be completed concurrently. A Building Permit will not be approved until the Site Plan has been approved, and vice versa.

d. As required, grading permits and engineering-related plans, such as street

improvement plans, traffic impact analysis, or drainage improvements, shall be reviewed concurrently with site plan review as determined by the City Engineer.

Standards of Review.

When considering an application for Site Plan Review, the Director shall consider the extent to which it:

a. Complies with all requirements of this Code;

b. Complies with the terms and provisions of any prior Zoning Map

Amendment, Conditional Use Permit, or other approvals affecting the site;

c. Adequately and safely provides for vehicular or pedestrian safety, both on

and off site, by reason of properly arranged vehicular or pedestrian ingress and egress and internal circulation, or that excessive traffic congestion will not be created; and,

d. Makes adequate provision to protect adjoining properties and structures

from excessive and unreasonable nuisances, including for example, noise, vibration, gases, or odors, which might interfere with the use and enjoyment of surrounding properties.

Review and Approval.

a. Receipt of Application.

i. Upon receipt of an application for Site Plan Review, the

Department shall review the building permit application in accordance with Section 2-40.020, concurrently with the site plan.

ii. The review and administrative approval of charter schools shall be

conducted on an expedited basis in compliance with A.R.S. § 15189.01.

70

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

b. Minor Modifications to Approved Site Plans.

The Director may approve Minor Modifications to approved site plans provided that the modifications will not cause a change in the character of the development.

c. Decision by the Director.

Upon receipt of the reports from other departments or agencies, as applicable, the Director shall approve the site plan application as submitted o r deny the application.

If a Building Permit is also required, the site plan and building permit must be approved at the same time.

d. Conditions of Approval.

Standard conditions for the protection of the public health, safety, and welfare, shall be applied to site plan approvals. No project-specific conditions shall be approved.

Expiration.

a. The site plan approval shall expire one year following the date upon

which the final approval became effective unless a request for an extension of time is made by the applicant to the Department at least 30 days prior to the date of the expiration of the original approval in accordance with established application submittal scheduling requirements.

A site plan approval subject to expiration may be extended one time only for an additional six months by the Director provided that no revisions to the development are necessary to comply with amendments to the Code that may have taken effect since the time of the original approval.

b. Upon the expiration of the original site plan approval, if no extension has

been granted, or a granted time extension has expired, then the original site plan approval shall expire and a new site plan would be required to be submitted for approval.

2-40.140 Temporary Use Permits

A. Purpose.

The purpose of this Section is to allow for short-term activities that are compatible with adjacent and surrounding uses when conducted in compliance with this Code. Temporary uses are intended to be transitional in nature, generally allowing for emergency situations, construction activity, or the establishment of small, low overhead businesses.

B. Time Limits.

Unless otherwise provided for in Subsection C, below, a Temporary Use

Permit shall be valid for up to 180 days.

The same temporary use may only be established at a maximum of

71

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

three different locations, each for a maximum of 180 days (i.e., a total of 18 months maximum).

C. Allowed Temporary Uses.

A Temporary Use Permit shall be required for the temporary activities shown in the Land Use Matrix in Title 3 and described below.

Concerts, Carnivals, Craft Fairs, Farmers Markets, Flea Markets, Indoor

Special Events, Vehicle Sales, and other Special Events.

Regulations and standards for special events such as concerts, carnivals,

farmers markets, flea markets, indoor special events, or vehicle sales are established in Kingman Municipal Code Chapter 7, Section 7-76 (Special Event Permit). Additional use-specific standards for farmers markets are listed in Division 3-80.

a. Temporary Use Event Requirements:

i. Property Owner Authorization.

ii. Limited to 12 events per year per location.

iii. Located at a fixed location on public or private property (i.e.

fairgrounds, city parks, or schools) within City limits subject to property owner authorization. Events held on City-owned property shall also be subject to a Special Use Permit.

b. Temporary Use Event Signage permitted only in conjunction with a

Temporary Use Permit and subject to the following standards:

i. Temporary signage shall be limited to banners only, not to exceed 24 square feet in area.

ii. Number of banners posted shall not exceed eight (City-wide) and no

more than two per intersection.

iii. Shall be affixed to a fence or staked with all four corners secured.

iv. Shall not be located within the vehicular triangular line of sight.

v. Shall not exceed an overall height of three feet when ground mounted.

vi. Must only be located on private property with property owner consent.

vii. May not be placed within the City right-of-way.

viii. Shall not be erected greater than 14 days prior to the event.

ix. Shall be removed within 48 hours upon conclusion of the event.

x. Display of banners associated with recurring or consecutive events

shall not be displayed greater than 12 times in a calendar year.

xi. The front of the banner shall include the Temporary Use Permit

Number in the lower right-hand corner. The letters and numbers shall be black in color having a height of four inches.

Construction Yards – Off-site.

Off-site contractors' construction yards for an approved construction project

72

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

require a Temporary Use Permit. Written authorization from the private property owner(s) or property management company(s) representing the property owner(s) shall be provided prior to the establishment of the off-site construction yard. The construction yard shall be removed immediately upon completion of the construction project or the expiration of the Building Permit authorizing the construction project, whichever occurs first.

Food and Merchandise/Street Vendors.

Regulations and standards for food and merchandise/service vendors are

established in Kingman Municipal Code Chapter 8, Section 8-81 (Mobile Food Vendors).

Temporary Real Estate Sales Offices.

A temporary real estate sales office may be established within the area of an

approved development, solely for the first sale of lots or homes. An application for a Temporary Use Permit for a temporary real estate office may be approved for a maximum of two years from the date of approval, with the option of one two-year extension subject to the approval of an additional Temporary Use Permit.

Temporary Structures.

A temporary classroom, office, or similar structure, including a manufactured or

mobile unit, may be approved for a maximum of one year from the date of approval, as an accessory use or as the first phase of a development. An extension of one year may be granted by the Director. A temporary structure proposed for a longer time period shall comply with all provisions of this Code applicable to a permanent structure on the same site.

Temporary Donation Bins.

Donation bins shall be subject to the following requirements:

a. Donation bins are allowed with a Temporary Use Permit which must be

renewed annually. Each renewal requires a new Temporary Use Permit application and fee. Temporary Use Permits must be renewed within 60 days prior to the first day of every calendar year.

b. Donation bins are permitted in all commercial, industrial, and planned

development zones.

c. Placement Standards.

i. Donation bins must be located on a paved surface.

ii. Donation bins must not be located within the front or corner side setbacks, required landscaped areas, or within required parking spaces.

iii. Donation bins must not obstruct pedestrian or vehicular circulation, or be located within the public right-of-way, drive aisles, fire lanes, loading zones, or any other location that may cause hazardous conditions, or constitute a threat to the public health, safety, and welfare.

73

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

d. The number of donation bins per lot or parcel shall be determined as follows:

i. Maximum one donation bin on lots or parcels with an area less than one acre;

ii. Maximum two donation bins on lots or parcels with an area of one acre and less than three acres; and;

iii. Maximum four donation bins on a lot or parcel with an area of three acres or more.

e. No more than two donation bins may be clustered together and

located within 1,000 feet of any other donation bin.

f. Donation Bin Standards.

i. Each donation bin must have a firmly closing lid.

ii. Each donation bin must be clearly marked to identify the specific items and materials to be collected for donation.

iii. The name and telephone number of the entity obtaining the Temporary Use Permit shall be affixed to the donation bin on an area no larger than one foot by one foot.

iv. Donation bins shall have a capacity no greater than seven cubic yards.

g. The property owner will control the temporary use permit. As such, the

owner or authorized agent may rescind their authorization by submitting a cancellation request to the City in writing. At the request of the property owner or authorized agent, the permit will be revoked.

h. Donation bins must be maintained in good repair, and any rust,

scratches, dents, graffiti, etc. must be removed within 24 hours of discovery or notification.

i. All donated items must be collected and stored in the donation bin and its

contents cleared at least once a week. Any items or materials left outside of the donation bin must be removed within 24 hours of discovery or notification, whichever occurs first.

j. If a donation bin is damaged or vandalized, it must be removed within five

business days of discovery or notification. If there is a public health, safety, or welfare concern identified by the Director, the donation bin must be removed.

k. The City and property owner may remove and dispose of any donation bin (including its contents) which is unauthorized, unpermitted,

or is otherwise in violation of this Subsection.

D. Exempt Temporary Activities.

The following temporary activities are allowed without a Temporary Use Permit:

Construction yards – On-site.

On-site contractors' construction yards for an approved construction project are authorized so long as the construction yard is removed within 30 days of

74

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

completion of the construction project, or the expiration of the Building Permit authorizing the construction project, whichever occurs first.

Emergency Facilities.

Emergency public health and safety facilities and activities, including disaster preparedness shelters established in an existing facility (such as churches or other places of worship) that provide temporary shelter from extremely cold weather or other unusual emergency situations are authorized for the duration of the emergency.

Real Estate – Open House Events.

Open house/real estate events are events where a property is made available for public viewing, without requiring a prior appointment.

Seasonal Sales.

Seasonal sales (e.g., Christmas trees, pumpkins) are permitted for up to 45 days in commercial zones.

Special Event on Public Property.

Activities conducted on City-owned property may be authorized by the City and permitted with a Special Event Permit issued by the City Clerk’s Office.

Temporary Work Trailers.

A work trailer may be used as a construction office or a temporary work site for employees provided that:

a. The use is authorized by a Building Permit for the trailer and the Building

Permit for the permanent structure; and,

b. The use is appropriate because:

i. The trailer or mobile home will be in place during the construction of a subdivision, or the construction or remodeling of a permanent commercial or manufacturing structure, for a maximum of one year, or upon expiration of the Building Permit for the permanent structure, whichever occurs first;

ii. The applicant has demonstrated that the temporary work site is a short-term necessity for a maximum of one year, while a permanent work site is being obtained; and,

iii. The trailer will be removed prior to final building inspection or the issuance of a Certificate of Occupancy for the permanent structure.

Yard Sales.

Any parcel or lot is authorized to have no more than five sales per year, and no individual sale may exceed three consecutive days. Yard sale time periods must be spaces spaced a minimum of four days apart.

2-40.150 Zoning Verification

75

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

A. Purpose.

Zoning Verification Letters may be requested from the Department by a property owner or a representative for a property owner who is seeking verification of the zoning status for a property. Other associated information may also be requested and provided, if available, such as any development approvals granted by the City, existing nonconformities, or violations.

B. Process for Review.

Upon receipt of a complete zoning verification request, the Department shall:

Verify the zoning and surrounding zoning in which the property is

located;

Determine the current use of the property;

Identify if there are any documented violations or nonconformities for the

property;

Research and review the development history of the subject property;

Conduct any other research or background checks to reasonably provide the

information as requested by the property owner; and

Prepare a final Zoning Verification Letter.

Assess a fee in accordance with City Code Chapter 2, Article VIII. [Ord. 1971,

11/19/2024]

76

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

2-50 Amendments to the Zoning Code Text, Planned Development Zones, and the

Zoning Map

Sections:

2-50.010 Purpose

2-50.020 Zone Change (Zoning Map Amendment)

2-50.030 Planned Development Zones

2-50.040 Zoning Code Text Amendment

2-50.010 Purpose

This Division establishes the procedures for amendments to the Zoning Map (frequently

referred to as a “zone change” or “rezoning”) and to the text of this Code.

2-50.020 Zoning Change (Zoning Map Amendment)

A. Purpose.

The purpose of a zone change is to enable amendments to the City’s Official

Zoning Map for any lot or parcel in the City.

A zone change is not intended to relieve particular hardships nor to confer special

privileges or rights on any person. A zone change should not be used when a Conditional Use Permit, Variance, or Minor Modification to the Development Standards could be used to achieve a similar result.

Changes to the development standards of Zones shall be processed as Code text

amendments pursuant to Section 2-50.040 (Zoning Code Text Amendment).

B. Applicability.

The procedure for a zone change shall apply to all proposals to revise a zone classification or boundary line shown on the Official Zoning Map. The provisions of the Zoning Map may periodically be amended or repealed pursuant to A.R.S. § 9462.01.

A Planned Development Zone (PDZ) or overlay zone may be applied in place of or in accordance with any zone in the City and must be processed as a Zoning Map Amendment.

Requests to amend the Zoning Map may be initiated by the Director, Planning Commission, Council, the owner of the subject property, or the owner’s agent.

C. Application Submittal and Review Procedure.

Due Diligence and Development Plan Review Meeting.

A Due Diligence Review Meeting followed by a Development Plan Review Meeting shall be required, unless waived by the Director, before an

77

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

application can be submitted.

Staff Review and Action. [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

a. An application for a text amendment to this Code or an amendment to the

Zoning Map shall be submitted to the Department and shall be reviewed and a recommendation prepared in compliance with the Review Schedule on file with the Department.

b. No PDZ shall be adopted unless a Preliminary Development Plan (PDP)

has been submitted for review. The PDP outlines the requirements of the PDZ, including the development standards and uses allowed within the PDZ.

c. An applicant may submit additional information beyond the minimum

required in b y the Department.

d. The Department’s recommendation shall be transmitted to the Planning

Commission in the form of a staff report prior to the public hearing. A copy of the staff report shall be made available to the public and any applicant prior to the public hearing.

Findings Required. [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

An amendment to the Zoning Map may be approved only if all of the following

findings are made;

a. The proposed amendment is consistent with and conforms to the goals

of the General Plan and any applicable specific plans;

b. The proposed amendment will not be detrimental to the public interest,

health, safety, convenience, or welfare of the City and will add to the public good; and

c. The affected site is physically suitable in terms of design, location, shape,

size, operating characteristics and the provision of public and emergency vehicle (e.g., fire and medical) access, public services, and utilities (e.g., fire protection, police protection, potable water, schools, solid waste collection and disposal, storm drainage, wastewater collection, treatment, and disposal), to ensure that the requested zone designation and the proposed or anticipated uses and/or development will not endanger, jeopardize, or otherwise constitute a hazard to the property or improvements in the vicinity in which the property is located.

Scheduling and Notice of Public Hearings. [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

The zone change application shall be scheduled and noticed for public hearings before the Planning Commission and Council in accordance with Section 2-30.080 (Notice of Public Hearings).

Review and Decision. [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

a. Planning Commission Review and Recommendation.

i. The Planning Commission shall conduct a public hearing on the

application. The Planning Commission may request additional

78

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

information that is relevant to assist in the review of a Zoning Map amendment subject to the findings established in this Section.

ii. The Planning Commission shall render its decision in the form of a

written recommendation to the Council through the Director.

iii. The Planning Commission may recommend approval, approval with

modifications and/or conditions, or denial of the proposed amendment. If the Planning Commission fails to make a recommendation to the Council within 30 days after closing the public hearing, the Planning Commission shall be deemed to have recommended denial and the application shall be scheduled by the Director for public hearing and action by the Council.

b. Council Review and Decision.

i. Upon receipt of a recommendation from the Planning Commission,

the Council shall conduct a public hearing and take action on any application to amend the Zoning Map. The Council may refer the application back to the Planning Commission for further study.

ii. The Council may request additional information that is relevant to

assist in the review of a Zoning Map amendment subject to the criteria established in this Section.

iii. The Council shall review the zone change application and approve,

approve with conditions, or deny the zone change based on the findings required by this Section.

c. Conditions of Approval.

The Council may impose such reasonable and appropriate conditions and safeguards as are necessary to a Zoning Map amendment request to:

i. Carry out the purposes of the General Plan or other applicable specific plans;

ii. Ensure all required findings are satisfied and compatibility with

adjacent land uses has been assured;

iii. Reduce or minimize any potentially injurious effects on adjacent properties;

iv. Protect the character and scale of the neighborhood; or

v. Protect the health, safety, or general welfare of the community.

d. Legal Protest.

If the owners of twenty percent or more of the property by area and number of lots, tracts and condominium units within 300 feet of the affected property, EXCLUDING GOVERNMENT OWNED PROPERTY, file a protest in writing against a proposed amendment, the change shall not become effective except by the favorable vote of three-fourths of all members of the Council. If any members of the Council are unable to vote on the legal protest because of a conflict of interest, then the required number of votes for passage of the question shall be three- fourths of the remaining membership of the Council, provided that the required number of votes shall not be less than a majority of the full membership of the legally established Council. A

79

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

protest filed pursuant to this Subsection shall be signed by the property owners opposing the proposed amendment and filed in the office of the City Clerk not later than 12:00 noon one business day before the date on which the Council will vote on the proposed amendment or on an earlier time and date established by the Council in writing and as indicated on the public notice items. [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

Post-Decision Actions and Limitations. [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

a. Processing of Conditional Zone Changes.

i. When a zone change is subject to condition(s), the official Zoning Map shall include a notation that the zoning is conditional, and the number of the zone change application file where the conditions are

established.

ii. Any condition imposed by the Council shall be established in the

ordinance changing the zone classification.

iii. Any proposed revisions or changes to an approved conditional zone change application shall be submitted in the same manner

and subject to the same approval process as the original

application.

b. Expiration of Conditional Zone Changes and Revocation.

If the Council approves an amendment to the Zoning Map with a condition that is required to be completed within a specific time period and the condition is not satisfied within that time period, the following actions may be taken:

i. The Council or Planning Commission may initiate an amendment to remove the condition or extend the time period and direct the Director to prepare an ordinance to do the same pursuant to the procedures set forth in Subsection 2-50.020.C; or

ii. The Council or Planning Commission may revoke the Zoning Map amendment and revert the zoning to its prior zoning classification for failure to comply with the conditions of the rezoning ordinance, pursuant to A.R.S. § 9-462.01 and direct the Director to notify the property owner by certified mail and prepare an ordinance to revert the zoning pursuant to the procedures set forth in Subsection 250.020.C; or

iii. The property owner may apply to amend or remove the condition pursuant to the procedures set forth in Subsection 2-50.020.C.

2-50.030 Planned Development Zones

A. Purpose

The Planned Development Zone (PDZ) provides for a more flexible regulatory procedure by which the purposes of the General Plan and Zoning Code may be

80

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

accomplished. A PDZ allows the applicant and City to respond to unique circumstances afforded by a given property or pursue unique development opportunities that would benefit the community. Other intended purposes of the PDZ include:

a. Enabling creative approaches for land use utilization and a mix of uses;

b. Preserving property with unique physical or historic features;

c. Promoting public and private open space as an integral part of land

development design; d. Facilitating development by permitting greater flexibility than allowed by

the strict application of the Code in exchange for more creative and imaginative designs with a higher level of amenities and public benefits than may otherwise be possible under the base zoning districts;

e. Providing compatibility between residential and nonresidential and/or

mixed-use areas thereby minimizing potential noise and visual impacts to residential areas from adjacent more intensive uses; and

f. Ensuring compliance with the General Plan.

B. Preliminary Development Plan Requirement.

A Preliminary Development Plan (PDP) shall be prepared in support of a proposed PDZ when the property owner or their authorized agent is seeking to establish development standards, land uses, or relief from the City’s conventional zoning or development requirements due to the project site having unique topography, utility, land use, land ownership, or other conditions.

A PDP shall not create lots less than 2,000 square feet in area and all lots shall continue to meet the minimum lot width standards to allow for access and utility placement per the city’s adopted standards

If approved, the PDP shall then guide development within the PDZ. For any standards or approved uses not specifically modified by the PDP, the applicant must comply with the requirements listed in this Code. The Department maintains a list of approved PDZs and their PDPs.

A PDP shall create distinct planning areas within the PDP to demonstrate compliance with the Planned Development Zone purpose. These planning areas shall be defined with a conceptual plan that demonstrates the location of amenities that benefit the public, mixed density or housing types, varied setbacks and street scape articulation.

The PDP shall be flexible in its application to allow for the varied housing types to be used interchangeably within the respective planning areas. At the developer’s request and subject to approval by the Director, an updated PDP shall be prepared should the developer wish to modify the PDP.

Should a modification result in intensification or reflect a change in the overall character of the PDP. The PDP will be subject to further review by the Planning and Zoning Commission and City Council and re-processed as a Zoning Code Change.

At the discretion of the Director the requirement of the preliminary plat may be

81

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

waived, provided the PDP is prepared in accordance with 2-50.030 (2) (a).

Allowed Uses within a PDP

a. The uses and density included in the PDP must be in conformity with the

General Plan.

b. The following use limitations apply:

i. In a PDZ, multiple residential building types (e.g. single-family homes

(attached or detached), duplexes, and multi-family residential building types) may be developed, provided the overall development provides for a mixture of housing types and is consistent with the General Plan density regulations and the building types on the edge of the PDZ are consistent with the form and scale of existing surrounding neighborhoods, if any. ii. Additional uses such as commercial uses, industrial uses, places of

worship, preschools and schools may be allowed within a PDZ provided the proposed use would not adversely affect any existing residential uses in the adjoining area or within the PDZ area.

Contents Required in a PDP

a. Development standards must be established for each PDZ as part of the

Preliminary Development Plan approved by the Council. Development standards should be consistent with the development standards for the types of zones proposed to the greatest extent feasible. Some flexibility should be permitted on an as-needed basis to facilitate creative developments and shall include but are not limited to:

Lot size sizes

Lot width and depth

Minimum frontages (shall meet the city’s engineering standards for access

and utilities)

Building height

Private and Recreational open space

Public amenity to include conceptual programming or recreational

features.

Architectural standards to ensure high quality development consistent with

the mixed housing types as proposed by the PDP and a varied street scape.

a. Varied architectural styles

b. Architectural finishes

c. Varied roof lines

b. The PDP submittal to the Department shall include a project narrative that

provides details on:

i. The PDP’s relationship to this Code, the General Plan, and other similar

82

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

guidelines, including a description of any standards that are proposed to be revised for the PDZ area;

ii. General street patterns with attention to future circulation throughout the

neighborhood;

iii. General location and size of potential school sites, parks, or other public

facilities;

iv. Location of proposed land uses including proposed densities for

residential areas;

v. General phasing schedule, as applicable; and,

vi. Methods proposed for sewage disposal, water supply, storm drainage,

and other similar utilities.

c. Additional reports including, but not limited to, preliminary stormwater reports,

geotechnical summaries, environmental assessments, traffic studies, or similar may be required by the Director.

d. The hillside development regulations (See Kingman Municipal Code, Section

5-5 (Hillside Development Standards) may be modified by a PDP approved by the Council.

e. Where a conflict in development standards occur, the regulations specified in

the PDP will apply.

C. Reversion

If the Director determines that the applicant has not commenced construction within two years of the date the final PDZ is approved by the Council or the applicant fails to comply with the Council-approved phasing schedule, the PDZ may be revoked following the procedures established in Section 2-50.020.

2-50.040 Zoning Code Text Amendment

A. Purpose.

This Section describes the review and approval procedures for amending the text of this Code to respond to changed conditions, changes in public policy, or to advance the general welfare of the City.

B. Applicability.

Pursuant to A.R.S. § 9-462.01, the provisions of the Code may periodically be amended or repealed.

C. Application Submittal and Review Procedure.

Initiation of a Text Amendment.

An application for a Code text amendment may be initiated by a citizen, the Council, Planning Commission, or the Director. The Department shall prepare the text amendments for consideration by the Planning Commission

83

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

and Council at public hearings.

If a citizen is requesting a text amendment, they must submit an application to the City and pay the applicable fee. The Department will prepare the text amendment, but the applicant is recommended to provide additional information or studies in support of the text amendment being requested.

Staff Review and Action.

The Director’s recommendation on the text amendment shall be transmitted to

the Planning Commission in the form of a staff report prior to the public hearing. A copy of the staff report shall be made available to the public prior to the public hearing.

Scheduling and Notice of Public Hearings.

The application for a Code text amendment shall be scheduled for public

hearings before the Planning Commission and Council and noticed in accordance with Section 2-30.080 (Notice of Public Hearings).

Findings for Code Text Amendments.

An amendment to the text of this Code may only be approved if all the following

findings are made and it is determined that the proposed text amendment:

a. Is consistent with the General Plan, other adopted plans, and other City

policies;

b. Will not be detrimental to the public interest, health, safety, convenience,

or welfare of the City;

c. Is necessary to address a demonstrated community need;

d. Is necessary to respond to substantial changes in conditions and/or policy; and

e. Is internally consistent with other applicable provisions of this Code.

Review and Decision.

a. Planning Commission Review and Recommendation.

i. The Planning Commission shall conduct a public hearing on the text amendment.

ii. The Planning Commission shall render its decision in the form of a written recommendation to the Council. The recommendation shall include the reasons for the recommendation (Refer to Section 2-50.040.C.4).

iii. The Planning Commission may request additional information that is relevant to assist in the review of a Code text amendment.

iv. The Planning Commission may recommend approval, approval with modifications and/or conditions, or denial of the proposed text amendment. If the Planning Commission fails to make a recommendation to the Council within 30 days after closing the public hearing, the Planning Commission shall be deemed to have recommended denial and the application shall be scheduled for

84

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

public hearing and action by the Council.

b. Council Review and Decision.

i. Upon receipt of a recommendation from the Planning Commission, the Council shall conduct a public hearing and take action on any application to amend the text of this Code. The Council may refer the application back to the Planning Commission for further study.

ii. The Council may request additional information that is relevant to assist in the review of a Code text amendment.

85

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

2-60 Nonconforming Provisions

Sections:

2-60.010 Purpose

2-60.020 Regulations Applicable to All Nonconformities

2-60.030 Nonconforming Uses

2-60.040 Nonconforming Structures

2-60.050 Nonconforming Lots of Record

2-60.060 Nonconforming Outdoor Lighting

2-60.070 Nonconforming Signs

2-60.010 Purpose

This Division provides regulations to allow the continuance of nonconforming uses and buildings or structures that were lawful before the adoption or amendment of this Code, but which would be prohibited or restricted based on the current regulations. Further, this Section provides for the eventual elimination of such nonconforming uses and buildings or structures.

2-60.020 Regulations Applicable to All Nonconformities

A. Authority to Continue.

Any nonconformities that lawfully existed on the Effective Date or any nonconformity that is created as a result of the adoption of this Code or any subsequent amendment to the text of this Code, may be continued as a legal, nonconformity only in accordance with the provisions of this Division.

B. Proof of Nonconforming Status.

The property owner shall provide sufficient evidence to the satisfaction of the Director that the subject property is a legal nonconformity as specified in this Division.

C. Change of Ownership or Tenancy.

Changes of ownership, tenancy, or management of property with an existing nonconformity may occur, but the use must not be abandoned or discontinued for a period greater than six consecutive months. If a nonconforming use is abandoned or discontinued for a period greater than six consecutive months, any future use must be in compliance with this Code.

D. Maintenance and Minor Repair.

86

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

Repairs and routine maintenance work required to keep nonconforming buildings or structures in sound condition are allowed. This includes structural alterations, which would require a Building Permit. The Building Official shall determine the extent of the alteration necessary to maintain a building or structure in safe condition.

Minor repairs to site features such as lighting, landscaping, and signage for nonconforming properties or uses are permitted to keep the site in a safe condition. No improvements that would exacerbate the nonconformities shall be permitted.

E. Buildings and Structures Under Construction.

Buildings and structures for which valid Building Permits have been issued prior to the Effective Date may be completed and used in accordance with the plans and specifications upon which such Building Permits were granted, provided construction is commenced within 30 days after the issuance of the permits and work is diligently pursued and not discontinued until completed, except for reasons beyond the builder’s control, as determined by the Building Official.

F. Establishment of New Use

If a nonconforming use, structure, or building is abandoned for a period of six consecutive months, then the nonconforming use may not be reestablished and the site must be brought into compliance with the standards of this Code to the maximum extent feasible as determined by the Director. This includes site features such as landscaping, lighting, signage, and parking.

If a nonconforming use is brought into conformance, then any subsequent uses must also be compliant.

2-60.030 Nonconforming Uses

Nonconforming uses of land, buildings, or structures are subject to the following additional limitations:

A. Expansion, Enlargement, and Modification.

Any nonconforming use shall not be enlarged, extended, moved, or substituted unless the use is brought into compliance with this Code.

B. Change of Use.

Except as provided in this Division, a nonconforming use shall not be expanded

or extended beyond the floor area or lot area that it occupied on the Effective Date of this Code or the Effective Date of any amendment to this Code rendering such use nonconforming.

If a nonconforming use is converted to a conforming use, no nonconforming

use may be resumed.

A nonconforming use may be changed to a permitted use or a conditionally

permitted use for the zone in which the property is located. A nonconforming use shall not be established or replaced by another nonconforming use.

C. Loss of Nonconforming Status.

If a nonconforming use is discontinued for a continuous period of six months, the

87

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

rights to legal nonconforming status shall terminate. A new use may be established for the site, but the new use shall comply with all applicable current City codes and requirements.

The initial decision as to whether a prior existing, non-conforming use has been abandoned shall be made by the Director. The Director’s decision may be appealed by the affected property owner within three months of the ruling following the procedures established in Division 2-80.

2-60.040 Nonconforming Structures

Nonconforming structures are subject to the following additional limitations:

A. Expansion, Enlargement and Modification.

No nonconforming building or structure may be enlarged or altered in a manner that

increases its nonconformity. The expansion or alteration must comply with all applicable City codes and regulations.

If a nonconforming use is operating within the nonconforming building or structure,

the area occupied by the nonconforming use may not be expanded.

The structural alteration of legal nonconforming residential units and residential

accessory structures may be allowed subject to issuance of a Conditional Use Permit in the C-1, C-2, C-3, I-1, and I-2 Zones and the DD-1 and DD-2 Downtown District Zones.

B. Destruction and Replacement.

Residential Buildings or Structures.

a. A nonconforming single- or multi-family dwelling unit that is involuntarily

damaged or destroyed may be reconstructed or replaced with a new structure within the same footprint and with the same height, volume, gross floor area, and number of dwelling units. A Building Permit for the necessary repairs or reconstruction shall be submitted within six months from the date of the damage and the reconstruction shall be diligently pursued to completion.

Non-Residential Buildings or Structures.

If a nonconforming building or structure used for non-residential purposes is involuntarily damaged or destroyed the nonconforming building or structure may only be reconstructed in certain situations, as described below:

a. 75 Percent or Less.

If the cost of repairing or replacing the damaged portion of the structure is 75 percent or less of the appraised value of the structure immediately before the damage, the structure may be restored to its nonconforming condition, provided that a Building Permit is secured, reconstruction is started within six months from the date of the damage, and the reconstruction is diligently pursued to completion.

b. Exceeds 75 Percent.

88

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

If the cost of repairing or replacing the damaged portion of the structure exceeds 75 percent of the appraised value of the structure immediately before the damage, or the structure is voluntarily razed or is required by law to be razed, the structure shall not be restored except in full compliance with the applicable regulations for the zone in which it is located, and the nonconforming building or structure shall not be reconstructed.

c. Appraised and Estimated Values.

i. All appraised values referred to in this Section shall be determined by a State licensed appraiser and confirmed by the Building Official.

ii. Estimates of the cost of repairing or replacing the damaged portion of the structure for purposes of this Section shall be made by the owner for review and approval by the Building Official. Estimates shall be based on the minimum cost of construction in compliance with the Building Code.

iii. The Building Official’s decision may be appealed by the affected property owner within three months of the ruling following the procedures established in Division 2-80.

2-60.050 Nonconforming Lots of Record

A. Development of a Legal Non-Conforming Lot. A legal, non-conforming lot, existing at the

Effective Date of this Code or the amendment to the Code that made the lot non-conforming, may be developed with a use permitted in the zone for which the lot is zoned, subject to complying with the current standards of that zone except for the standards affected by lot size, width, and depth as applicable.

B. Construction. No construction shall occur on a parcel or lot that does not comply with the

applicable area, depth, or width requirements of this Code unless it meets at least one of the following criteria, as documented by the applicant to the satisfaction of the Director.

1. Approved Subdivision.

The parcel or lot was created by a recorded subdivision as a legal building site (See City of Kingman Subdivision Ordinance).

2. Individual Parcel or Lot Legally Created by Deed.

The parcel or lot is under one ownership and was legally created by a recorded deed as a legal building site before the Effective Date or the amendment that made the parcel or lot nonconforming; or

3. Partial Government Acquisition.

The parcel or lot was created in compliance with the provisions of this Code but was made nonconforming when a portion was acquired by a governmental entity. This provision does not apply if the government paid severance damages for loss of building rights.

C. Combination and Separation of Legal Non-Conforming Lots. This section is intended to

allow the combination and re-separation or lot split of previously legally combined lots, even if

89

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

the resulting lots do not conform to current minimum lot size, width, or other dimensional requirements, provided that such lots were lawfully created and separately conveyed prior to their combination.

Combination resulting in a lot area less than 6,000 square feet

Legal non-conforming lots regardless of lot area but less than 6,000 square feet may be combined under a parcel plat without having to meet the minimum lot width, depth of area requirement of 6,000 square feet provided the combination does not increase the degree of non-conformity with respect lot area or to any structure located on the lots.

Separation of Legal Non-Conforming Lots
