A lot may be re-separated into its original configuration if all of the following conditions are met:

a. The lots were lawfully created and recorded prior to January 1, 1945; b. The lots were legally combined through record of survey and deed, plat, or another

recorded instrument; c. The lot has not been constructed upon or otherwise improved; d. The proposed re-separation restores the original lot lines as they existed prior to the

combination; however, in no case shall the lot area be less than 5,000 square feet e. Each resulting lot has access to a private or public right-of-way; f. The re-separation does not increase the degree of non-conformity with respect to

any structure located on the lots.

Nonconformity

a. The re-separated lots may be smaller or otherwise non-conforming to current zoning

standards; however, such lots shall be deemed legal non-conforming lots upon approval of the re-separation and in no case shall the lot area be less than 5,000 square feet. b. Any new development or redevelopment on the re-separated lots shall comply with

applicable zoning regulations to the extent possible, except for those standards made non-conforming by the re-separation.

Application and Approval Property owner shall submit a completed application and a parcel plat prepared in accordance with the City of Kingman Subdivision Ordinance and:

a. A survey or plat showing the original lot configuration; b. Evidence of prior legal status of the lots; c. Proposed re-separation plan; d. Documentation of utilities, access, and conformance with the standards in this

section. e. The Director may approve the application upon finding that the standards in this

section are met.

Limitations

a. This provision shall not be construed to permit creation of new lots that were not

previously lawfully established.

90

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

b. No lot shall be re-separated if the combination was required as a condition of a prior

land use approval, unless that approval is formally modified.

2-60.060 Nonconforming Outdoor Lighting

A. General.

If at the Effective Date of this Code or the amendment to the Code that made

the lot non-conforming, any existing outdoor lighting which is being used in a manner or for a purpose which is otherwise lawful but does not comply with the provisions of Division 4-70 (Outdoor Lighting Standards), shall be deemed legal but nonconforming, and shall be allowed to continue provided such fixtures are extinguished between the hours of 11:00 PM and/or no later than 30 minutes after the business closes, whichever is later, and remain off until the business reopens by an automatic shut-off device.

No modification or replacement shall be made to a nonconforming outdoor

light fixture unless the fixture thereafter conforms to the provisions of Division 4-70 (Outdoor Lighting Standards).

Whenever a nonconforming use, structure or lot is abandoned for a period

of six consecutive months and then changed to a new use any existing outdoor lighting shall be reviewed and brought into compliance as necessary for the entire building, structure or premises.

2-60.070 Nonconforming Signs

A. Continuance.

Any sign legally existing on the Effective Date of this Code or the amendment to the Code that made the lot non-conforming, but which does not comply with the provisions of this Code shall be deemed a nonconforming sign and may be continued or maintained as a nonconforming sign only in accordance with the terms of this Section.

B. Expansion, Enlargement, and Modification.

No nonconforming sign shall be moved, altered, re-erected, relocated, or

replaced unless it is brought into compliance with the requirements of this Code.

Nonconforming signs are required to be maintained in good condition

consistent with applicable Arizona law. Nothing in this Code shall prevent the repair or restoration of a nonconforming sign or sign structure to a safe condition or normal maintenance operations performed on a sign or sign structure.

C. Loss of Nonconforming Status

A legal nonconforming sign that has been damaged to the extent of more than 50

percent of the cost of reconstruction of the sign structure or is temporarily or permanently removed by any means, including an “act of God”, shall be removed

91

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

or rebuilt, repaired or replaced only in compliance with the provisions of Division 4-90.

The property owner is responsible for providing sufficient information to the Director to make this determination. The Director’s determination may be appealed by the affected property owner within three months of the ruling following the procedures established in Division 2-80.

Removal of a nonconforming sign, or replacement of a nonconforming sign with a

conforming sign, is required when the use of the sign and/or the property on which the sign is located has been abandoned, ceased operations, become vacant, or been unoccupied for a period of six consecutive months. Rental payments or lease payments and taxes shall not be considered as a continued use.

In the event this should occur, such conditions will be considered as evidence of abandonment, requiring removal of the sign by the owner of the property, their agent, or a person having the beneficial use of the property, building, or structure upon which such sign or sign structure is erected within 30 days after written notification from the Director. If, within the 30-day period, the sign(s) is (are) not removed, enforcement action consistent with A.R.S. § 9.462.02 shall be pursued.

92

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

2-70 Variances

Sections:

2-70.010 Purpose

2-70.020 Filing of Application

2-70.030 Review Process

2-70.040 Findings for Granting Variances

2-70.050 Conditions

2-70.060 Effects of Approval or Denial

2-70.010 Purpose

The purpose of a Variance is to provide a means for relief from the literal application of the

terms of these regulations where circumstances or conditions not created by or in control of a property owner would create practical difficulties or hardships that undermine the intent of these regulations or public safety and welfare.

2-70.020 Filing of Application

A. A petition for a Variance may be initiated only by the owner of the affected property,

an agent authorized in writing to act on the owner's behalf, or a person having a written contractual interest in the affected property.

B. An application for a Variance shall be submitted to the Department or in writing on a

form prescribed by the City in compliance with Section 2-30.020 (General Overview of Application Process). The application shall include the information and materials specified in the checklist for Variances, together with the required fee established in City Code Chapter 2, Article VIII.

2-70.030 Review Process

An application for a Variance shall be reviewed by the Department in compliance with the Review Schedule on file with the Department. The recommendation of the Director shall be submitted to the Board prior to the public meeting and shall establish whether the Variance should be granted or denied and the grounds for such recommendation.

Notice for the public hearing must be provided as outlined in Section 2-30.080.

At the meeting, the Board shall review the application in light of the standards established in Section 2-70.040 (Findings for Granting Variances), the recommendation of the

93

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

Director, the testimony at the public meeting, and the record. The Board shall then grant the Variance, grant the Variance subject to specified conditions, or deny the Variance.

2-70.040 Findings for Granting Variances

A Variance shall only be granted if the applicant demonstrates all the following findings:

A. That, because of special circumstances applicable to the property, including its

size, shape, topography, location, or surroundings, the strict application of these regulations will deprive such property of privileges enjoyed by other property of the same classification in the same zone;

B. That granting of a Variance will be subject to conditions, as needed, to ensure

that the adjustment authorized is the minimum variation needed and that it will not constitute a grant of special privileges inconsistent with the limitations upon other properties in the vicinity and zone in which such property is located;

C. That the special circumstances applicable to the property are not self-imposed

by any person having an interest in the property; and,

D. That the Variance will not allow the establishment of a use which:

Is not otherwise permitted in the zone; or,

Would result in the extension of a non-conforming use or structure.

2-70.050 Conditions

A Variance may be granted subject to conditions that are necessary to carry out the purpose of this Code and ensure that the Variance authorized meets the required findings in Section 2-70.040 (Findings for Granting Variances). Conditions serving to prevent or minimize adverse effects upon other property may include limitations on size and location, hours of operation, requirements for landscaping or buffers, lighting, and ingress and egress.

2-70.060 Effects of Approval or Denial

A. After the Board approves a Variance, the applicant shall follow the requirements of

this Code for any additional applicable permits in order to proceed with the development of the subject property. All subsequent orders, decisions, determinations, and interpretations made by the City in compliance with those procedures shall be consistent with the Variance granted to the applicant.

B. Any Variance granted will be void if the use is not commenced or if a Building Permit

has not been obtained within one year of the permit being granted, unless the Board stipulated a different time limit when the Variance was approved.

C. A request for an extension of an approved Variance will be processed as a new

request in compliance with this Division.

D. No request for Variance which has been denied by the Board may be

resubmitted as substantially the same request, for a period of one year

94

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

from the date of denial.

E. If a building or structure is erected on a parcel where a Variance has been approved,

and the parcel is the subject of an approved zone change, such structure may become a legal non- conforming structure if following adoption of the approved zone change the structure does not comply with the standards of the new zone.

Remainder of page intentionally blank.

95

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

2-80 Procedures for Appeals

Sections:

2-80.010 Purpose

2-80.020 Appeals of Approvals

2-80.030 Appeals to the Board of Adjustment

2-80.010 Purpose

A. This Division establishes the procedures for appeals of administrative and

interpretative decisions.

B. Any action taken by a Review Authority can be appealed by special action to the

Superior Court in compliance with the Arizona Revised Statutes and the Arizona Rules of Procedure for Special Actions.

2-80.020 Appeals of Approvals

A. Purpose.

This Section establishes procedures for review of determinations rendered by staff, the Director, Planning Commission, or the Council.

B. Filing of Appeals.

An appeal shall be filed within 10 days following the actual date the decision was rendered. The recipient of the appeal may waive or extend this deadline only upon determining that the person filing the appeal received no notice of the order, requirement, decision, or determination being appealed.

Every appeal shall be filed on forms provided by the City and shall be complete in all respects before being accepted for filing. The appeal shall specifically state the pertinent facts and the basis for the appeal. A nonrefundable filing fee must accompany all applications for appeals.

Once an appeal is filed by an aggrieved person, any action on the associated development is suspended until the appeal is processed and a final decision is rendered by the applicable Review Authority unless otherwise specified in this Code or unless the Director certifies to the Board that a stay would cause imminent peril to life or property.

Upon receipt of an appeal, the officer receiving the appeal shall transmit all papers, records, and other information regarding the subject matter of the appeal to the body hearing the appeal.

96

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

C. Appeal of a Decision by Staff.

A decision of the staff regarding the completeness of a development application

or other determination may be appealed by writing to the Director.

Appeals of staff decisions shall be filed with the Director.

No public hearing is required for appeals of staff decisions that are resolved by

the Director.

D. Appeal of a Decision by the Director.

A decision of the Director may be appealed to the Planning Commission in

compliance with the appeal procedures established in this Section by any person aggrieved by such decision.

Appeals of a decision of the Director shall be filed with the Department. The

Department would present the appeal to the Planning Commission for determination.

The Director shall prepare a recommendation in the form of a staff report for

submittal to the Planning Commission prior to the scheduled public hearing. The staff report must establish whether the appeal should be granted or denied, and the grounds for the recommendation.

E. Appeal of a Decision by the Planning Commission.

A decision of the Planning Commission may be appealed to the Council by any

person aggrieved by such decision.

A recommendation of the Planning Commission to the Council may not be appealed.

Appeals of decisions of the Planning Commission shall be filed with the Director.

F. Appeal of a Decision by the Council

A decision of the Council may be appealed to the Board.

Appeals of a Council determination shall be filed with the City Clerk.

2-80.030 Appeals to Board of Adjustment

A. Appeals to the Board.

Appeals to the Board may be taken by persons aggrieved or by any officer,

department, board, or bureau of the City affected by a decision of the City by filing with the officer from whom the appeal is taken. The officer from whom the appeal is taken shall forthwith transmit all papers constituting the record upon which the action appealed from was taken.

The Board shall fix a period within 45 days for hearing the appeal and shall

give notice of hearing by both publication in a newspaper of general circulation in accordance with A.R.S. § 9-462.04 and by posting the notice in conspicuous places close to the property affected.

97

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

No appeal shall be taken to the Board until and unless the Director has first

made a determination on an application.

B. Board Process.

The Board shall:

Hear and decide appeals in which it is alleged there is an error in an order,

requirement, or decision made in the enforcement of the Zoning Code.

Hear and decide Variances to the terms of the Code of the City only if, because

of special circumstances applicable to the property (including its size, shape, topography, location, or surroundings), the strict application of the Code will deprive such property of privileges enjoyed by other property of the same classification in the same zone.

Reverse or affirm, wholly or partly, or modify the order or decision appealed

from and make such order or decision as ought to be made, and to that end shall have the powers of the officer from whom the appeal is taken.

The Board may not:

a. Make any changes in the uses permitted in any zone or make any changes in the terms of the Zoning Code provided the restriction in this paragraph shall not affect the authority to grant Variances.

b. Grant a Variance if the special circumstances applicable to the property are self- imposed by the property owner.

C. Public Hearing.

Public notification of the appeal shall be provided in compliance with Section 2 30.080 (Notice of Public Hearings).

The Board shall, within 30 days of receipt of an appeal, hold a public meeting

concerning the matter.

At the time of the hearing, all interested parties shall be heard and any

discussion shall be had. The minutes of the hearing shall be taken and copies made available to the public in the Office of the City Clerk.

The concurring vote of a majority of the Board is necessary to reverse an order

or decision of an administrative official, or to decide in favor of the applicant, on any matter brought to the Board's consideration on appeal.

Upon a concurrence of a majority of the members of the Board, the Board shall

rule whether a building, structure, or use is included or excluded from said district regulation, or whether a variance therefrom shall be allowed, as applicable. [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

The rulings of the Board shall be written and verified by the Chairperson and be

attached to the copies of the minutes of the hearing. This document shall state the reasons and findings of fact the Board used in reaching its decision. Copies of both the minutes and the ruling shall be mailed to all interested parties. The rulings and the minutes shall be available to the public in the Office of the Clerk.

D. Appeals to the Superior Court.

98

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

A person aggrieved by a decision of the Board may appeal the decision directly to the Mohave County Superior Court by filing a complaint for special action within 30 days after the Board has rendered its decision. Filing the complaint shall not automatically stay proceedings on the decision sought to be reviewed, but the Court may grant a stay on the application. The Court shall affirm or reverse, in whole or in part, or modify the decision reviewed.

E. Reapplication.

No application for an appeal that is the same or substantially the same as a request which has been acted upon by the Board shall be filed within one year of the date of the Board’s action.

Remainder of page intentionally blank.

99

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

2-90 Annexations

Sections:

2-90.010 Purpose

2-90.020 Initiation of Annexation

2-90.030 Procedures

2-90.010 Purpose

The purpose of this Division is to provide procedures that comply with and implement A.R.S. § 9- 471 et. seq., the General Plan, and other relevant City policies and regulations for annexing real property to the City, so that the City may respond to changing development conditions and requests by property owners outside the City limits for City services. This Division shall apply to all applications to annex property into the City.

2-90.020 Initiation of Annexations

A. Council or City Manager.

The Council or the City Manager may direct the Department to review specific property to determine whether it may be legally annexed and to contact property owners to determine whether they will sign an annexation petition.

B. Owner Initiation.

One or more property owners may submit an application to annex their property into the City.

2-90.030 Procedures

A. Due Diligence and Development Plan Review

A Due Diligence Review is required for all annexation applications.

A subsequent Development Plan Review meeting is required, unless waived by the Director.

B. Annexation Application

Applications for annexations shall be submitted to the Department in compliance with the application procedures established in Section 2-30.020 (General Overview of Application Process) and shall include the information and materials specified in the Department handout for annexations together with the required fee in compliance with City Code Chapter 2, Article VIII.

In conformance with A.R.S. § 9-471 et. seq., the applicant should include as much information as possible on how the development will be provided with appropriate levels of infrastructure and services within 10 years after the annexation. If a General Plan amendment or zone change is anticipated following annexation, the applicant

100

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

should present preliminary information regarding these requests.

C. Procedures for Review and Approval

All applications for annexations of real property shall be reviewed, processed, and approved in conformance with A.R.S. § 9-471 et. seq.

The Director and Planning Commission shall make a recommendation on the requested annexation to the Council. The Council shall have the authority to make a determination on the request. Public notification is required prior to all public hearings as outlined in this Code.

In conformance with State law, areas annexed into the City shall initially be assigned land use zone classifications which permit densities and uses no greater than those permitted by Mohave County immediately before annexation. Subsequent changes in the zone of the annexed areas shall be made according to the procedures established in Division 2-50 (Amendments to the Zoning Code Text, Planned Development Zones, and the Zoning Map).

Remainder of page intentionally blank.

101

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

2-100 Enforcement

Sections:

2-100.010 Purpose

2-100.020 Violations

2-100.030 Enforcement Authority

2-100.040 Penalties

2-100.050 Other Powers and Remedies

2-100.060 Enforcement to Stop Work Order

2-100.010 Purpose

The purpose of this Division is to establish provisions for enforcing this Code. Any violation of this Code shall be subject to the enforcement remedies and penalties provided in this Division or by State law.

2-100.020 Violations

A. It shall be unlawful for a responsible party to allow or permit, facilitate, suffer, or aid or abet

any violation of any provision of this Code, or to fail to perform any act or duty required by this Code. A responsible party includes an owner, occupant, lessor, lessee, manager, licensee, agent, or other person having lawful possession or control over a building, structure, or parcel of land.

B. Any building or any use of property in violation of this Code shall be declared unlawful and

a public nuisance. The Director shall commence action for the abatement or removal of the matter as provided by law.

C. It shall be the duty of every citizen to cooperate with the enforcement of the regulations of

this Code.

D. Each day any violation of any provision of this Code, or the failure to perform any act or

duty required by this Code, continues shall constitute a separate offense.

E. It shall be unlawful to erect, construct, reconstruct, alter or use any land, building or

other structure without first obtaining a Building Permit from the Building Official when required by this Code.

2-100.030 Enforcement Authority

The Director, or their designee, has authority to enter any building, structure, or premises or any part thereof, at any and all reasonable times in compliance with legal requirements governing administrative inspections of private property, for the purpose of performing his/her

102

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

official duty.

The Director is authorized to commence an enforcement action in compliance with this Division by issuing a citation for civil sanctions in the Kingman Municipal Court in compliance with Kingman Municipal Code Section 1-8 (Penalty or Sanction). The Director may also seek the issuance of a complaint by the Chief Prosecutor of the City for criminal prosecution as appropriate.

The police department, fire department, public works department, or any other City department shall assist in the enforcement of this Code at the request of the Director, their designee, the City Manager, or the Council.

2-100.040 Penalties

Any person found responsible for violating this Code shall be subject to the penalties established in Kingman Municipal Code Section 7-166 (Penalties).

2-100.050 Other Powers and Remedies

The Director has other enforcement rights, powers, and remedies as are and may from time to time be provided for or permitted by Arizona law for the enforcement of zoning regulations, or for the litigation by action or appeal of controversies in regard to zoning regulation or enforcement.

Administrative remedies may include withholding or revoking plan approval, permits, certificates, or other authorizations as outlined in Section 7-169 of the Municipal Code. Written notice of a violation shall be served to the owner, authorized agent, or contractor. The written notice shall state the nature of the violation, the specific provision violated, and the date and time by which the violation shall be corrected. If corrective action is not taken within the allotted time, the property owner may be subject to the penalties established in Kingman Municipal Code Section 7-166 (Penalties).

Any permit, certificate, or license issued in conflict with the provisions of this Code shall be void.

2-100.060 Enforcement by Stop Work Order

The Director may issue an order to stop work on any development, excavation, building, or structure on any land on which there is an uncorrected violation of a provision of this Code or of a permit or other form of authorization issued pursuant to this Code pursuant to the applicable provisions of the Building Code. The written stop work order shall state the nature of the violation, the specific provision(s) violated, and the date and time by which the violation shall be corrected.

If a stop work order is issued, no construction shall proceed except to correct the violation or to comply with the order.

103

Title 2: Administration and Procedures

2-110 Planning and Engineering Design Standards

Sections:

2-110.010 Purpose

2-110.020 Initial Adoption and Incorporation into this Code

2-110.030 Amendments and Deviations

2-110.010 Purpose

The purpose of the Planning and Engineering Design Standards (PEDS) is to further outline the City’s design standards and development expectations for development projects within the City.

2-110.020 Initial Adoption and Incorporation into this Code

The PEDS shall initially be adopted by Ordinance by the City Council. The standards included in the PEDS, and any subsequent amendments, shall then be incorporated into this Code.

2-110.030 Amendments and Deviations

The Director shall be responsible for administration of the PEDS. The Director may approve amendments or deviations to the standards of the PEDS if the amendment or deviation being requested would meet or exceed the standard in question.

If the Director cannot administratively approve an amendment or deviation to the PEDS, the Director shall refer the matter to the Planning and Zoning Commission for review and approval or denial. In this situation, the requested amendment or deviation shall be processed consistent with the zone text amendment process described in Section 2-50.040.

104

Title 3: Zones
Title 3: Zones
3-10 Establishment and Designation of Zones

Sections:

3-10.010 Purpose

3-10.020 Applicability

3-10.030 Establishment and Designation of Zones 3-10.040 Zoning Map

3-10.050 Regulations within Zones

3-10.060 Establishment of Land Uses

3-10.010 Purpose

This Title establishes the zones applied to property within the City and adopts the City’s Zoning Map in accordance with the authority granted in ARS § 9-462.01. The Title also indicates land uses that are allowed by right or require approval of a Conditional Use Permit (CUP).

3-10.020 Applicability

The requirements of this Title apply to all proposed development within the conventional zones, overlay and special district zones, and planned development zones in the City and are considered in combination with the standards in Division 3-80 (Specific to Uses) and Title 4 (Supplemental Standards to Zones). If there is a conflict between any standards, the provision which is more restrictive or imposes higher standards shall govern. Where uncertainty still exists, the Director shall make a determination.

3-10.030 Establishment and Designation of Zones

The incorporated areas of the City are divided into conventional zones, planned development zones, and overlay zones. The zones implement the General Plan by providing a mix of uses that are compatible with the character of the City’s many neighborhoods, districts, and corridors.

A. Zones.

The City of Kingman is divided into three categories of zones:

Conventional Zones.

105

The Conventional Zones include single-family subdivisions, other suburban residential areas, commercial and retail areas, and industrial areas.

Overlay and Special District Zones.

The Overlay and Special District Zones contain standards that apply in addition to standards in the underlying base zone. Examples include the Historic Overlay District and Kingman Design Review Overlay.

Planned Development Zones.

The Planned Development Zones apply to specifically defined areas of the City where alternative development standards may be applied to facilitate development of specific land use types.

B. The zones within the City are listed in Table 3-10.030.A (Zones):

3-10.040 Zoning Map

A. Adoption

The Zoning Map, which divides the City into zones, together with all explanatory notes

provided on the Zoning Map, is adopted by reference, and shall be a part of this Code.

B. Zone Boundaries and Zone Classification Changes

Where designated on the Zoning Map, the boundaries defining a zone align with

street, alley, or lot lines or follow the boundary of the City limits. Zone boundaries may be specifically indicated by dimensions in adopting ordinances, in which case their location shall be determined by the scale of the Zoning Map.

If the location of the zone boundaries is unclear or the zone boundaries do not

align with street, alley, or lot lines, the Director shall determine the location of the zone boundaries.

107

Title 3: Zones

C. Maintenance of the Zoning Map

The Zoning Map shall be kept on file in the office of the Director and shall be maintained in electronic format by the Information Technology Division, as authorized by the Director.

If, in conformance with the provisions of this Code, changes are made to zone boundaries, zone classifications, or other matters set forth on the Zoning Map, such changes shall be entered on the Zoning Map within 30 days following the effective date of the ordinance adopting the change.

D. Copies of the Zoning Map

Paper copies of the Zoning Map may be certified as true and correct by the Director.

The Zoning Map is available online on the City’s website. Electronic copies of the Zoning Map shall not be certified as true and correct.

3-10.050 Regulations within Zones

Within each zone, there are specific requirements that guide development, including but not limited to, location, height, size, density, and setbacks. Refer to the standards contained herein and in Title 4. Compliance with these requirements shall be verified through the Development Review Process. Any proposed development not in compliance with these regulations shall constitute a violation and be subject to the enforcement actions outlined in Division 2-100.

Some rows of the Land Use Matrix shown in the following Section on Table 3-10.060.A contain use-specific standards that these uses must comply with in addition to the requirements of Title 4. Use-specific standards are listed in Division 3-80. Where allowed, temporary uses must also comply with Section 2-40.140 (Temporary Use Permits).

3-10.060 Establishment of Land Uses

A. Land Use Matrix.

Table 3-10.060.A: The City of Kingman Land Use Matrix shows allowed land uses within the zoning districts described above. The following symbols are used in the table:

P = Use is permitted by right;
CUP = Use is permitted with approval of a Conditional Use Permit;
Blank space = Use is not permitted.

Additional use-specific standards are also listed in the Land Use Matrix for certain uses that may be permitted within a zone but that may require additional measures to ensure compatibility with adjacent developments.

108

Title 3: Zones

B. Uses Not Specifically Listed.

Uses not specifically listed in the Land Use Matrix are prohibited. If it is unclear whether a proposed use not specifically listed in the Land Use Matrix would be permitted, conditionally permitted, or prohibited, the Director shall determine if the use is substantially similar to a permitted or conditionally permitted use. The Director’s determination shall not amend, repeal, or waive any other standard or requirement established in these regulations. [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

3-20 Residential Zones

Sections:

3-20.010 Intent

3-20.020 Development Standards

3-20.030 Miscellaneous Standards - Residential Zones

3-20.010 Intent

A. Single-Family Zones.

The Single-Family Residential Zones (R-1-6 through R-1-40) apply to areas of the

City intended to provide for and encourage single-family residential development in a suburban setting. As such, these zones allow limited community facilities, parks, educational, and/or accessory uses.

The R-1-5A zone is a legacy zone created solely to consolidate the various

development standards of the former R-2-PDD zones into a single zone with universal development standards. The R-1-5A zone is not permitted to be expanded into other areas of the City and these standards may not be used for any development other than within an existing Planned Development District (PDD). For full details on the R-1-5A zone, refer to the respective PDD documents for the specific lot, parcel, or area in which a proposed development is located. PDD documents are available from the Department.

The Rural Residential (RR) Zone applies to areas of the City appropriate for both

low density residential development and limited agricultural uses in a rural setting. This zone allows predominantly large lot single-family development.

B. Multi-Family Zones.

The Multi-Family Residential Zones (R-2 through R-4) are established to provide low, medium, and high-density multi-family residential development with shared amenities on site. The multi-family residential zones provide opportunities for affordable housing, promote design flexibility, efficient use of open space, and separation of pedestrian and vehicular traffic.

C. Residential Manufactured Home Zones.

The Manufactured Housing Zones (R-MH-6, R-MH-20 and R-MH-40) are applied to areas of the City appropriate for the orderly planned development of manufactured housing parks and subdivisions to accommodate manufactured houses as a primary use. Similar to the Single-Family Residential Zones, these zones allow limited community facilities, parks, educational uses, and accessory uses.

D. Special Districts and Overlays.

Certain areas of the City with unique characteristics have been identified where

120

Title 3: Zones

additional requirements apply in addition to the standards provided in the base zones. Developers shall determine whether any of their projects are located within any of the following special districts or overlay zones. If a project is located within one of these areas, the project must comply with the applicable standards for the zone as well as the applicable standards for the overlay:

Historic Overlay (refer to Section 3-70.020 for specific standards and location)

2 KDRO (refer to Section 3-70.030 for specific standards and location).

3-20.020 Development Standards

The development standards for the residential zones are listed in the following tables: Table 3-20.020.A (Development Standards (R-1 and RR Zones)) and Table 3-20.020.B (Development Standards (Multi-Family and Manufactured Home Zones)).

3-20.030 Miscellaneous Standards - Residential Zones

A. Architectural Design Standards

All properties within the City must comply with the general site planning and architectural design standards described in Division 4-20 of this Code and the building height requirements listed in Division 4-30 of this Code.

B. Encroachments

Division 4-40 of this Code describes specific situations where limited encroachments into the minimum required setbacks may be allowed. This includes details on allowed minor encroachments for patio covers within single-family residential zones.

C. Fences, Walls, and Screening

All properties within the City must comply with the fence, wall, and screening standards described in Division 4-50 of this Code. This includes compliance with the fence and wall height requirements listed in Table 4-50.050.A.

D. Landscaping

All new residential development within the City must comply with the landscaping standards described in Division 4-60 of this Code. When a muti-family property is significantly remodeled, the development must also comply with the standards in Division 4-60.

Section 4-60.020 describes limited exceptions to the landscaping requirements.

E. Outdoor Lighting

All new and major additions to developments, buildings, or structures within the City must comply with the lighting standards described in Division 4-70 of this Code.

124

Title 3: Zones

F. Parking

All new buildings and changes of uses must comply with the parking and loading standards described in Division 4-80 of this Code. Section 4-80.060 also lists requirements for parking and storage of RVs, trailers, and boats on residential properties.

If a home occupation is proposed to be operated out of the residence, the parking requirements in the use-specific standards for home occupations in this Title must also be followed.

G. Signs

All properties within the City must comply with the sign standards described in Division 4-90 of this Code. There are also limited exceptions listed in Division 4-90.

H. Additional Requirements - All Residential Zones

Land subdivided or developed within each residential zone must be served by

public water and sewer facilities. See the City of Kingman’s Utility Regulations for specific details.

All lots must have frontage on and have vehicular access from a public or

private street. In the R-2, R-3, and R-4 Zones, or for manufactured home parks, additional secondary permanent vehicle access must be shown on the site plan approved by the Director.

Any devices used to transport a manufactured home or similar dwelling to the

site for installation, including the hitch, wheels, and axles, must be removed from the site after installation.

Any subdivisions or legal parcels of record recorded prior to the effective date of

this Code or any subsequent amendment to this Code may not comply with current lot dimensions and may be legal nonconforming lots. Refer to the standards in Section 2-60.050 for standards governing nonconforming lots.

I. Additional Requirements – R-2 and R-3 Zones.

The Director may modify the minimum floor area per unit requirement as

described in Table 2-40.070.A at the request of the applicant subject to the following criteria:

a. Only one unit type may be modified;

b. The development must meet all other criteria for the zone;

c. The developer must demonstrate that the proposed modification will

further the public interest, by for example, increasing affordable housing opportunities; and

d. Other units provided in the development mix are at least 25 percent

greater in area than the minimum required floor areas.

125

Title 3: Zones

Common open space, provided to meet the minimum usable open space per

unit requirement, must be accessible to all dwelling units by sidewalks or local paths.

a. Individual balconies or patios may be used for up to twenty-five percent of

the required outdoor space, but to qualify the balconies or patios must be at least 40 square feet with no dimensions less than 5 feet.

b. Indoor recreation areas may be used for up to twenty-five percent of the

required usable open space, provided the space is designed for communal use by all residents and contains space for gathering, or exercise and fitness equipment.

c. Minimum usable outdoor living space may be located within setback

areas. The site plan must demonstrate that there are no structural encroachments into the setback area except those that may be allowed by Division 4-40 and that the space is usable.

126

Title 3: Zones

3-30 Commercial Zones

Sections:

3-30.010 Intent

3-30.020 Development Standards

3-30.030 Miscellaneous Standards - Commercial Zones

3-30.010 Intent

A. Neighborhood Convenience Zone (C-1).

The Neighborhood Convenience (C-1) Zone is intended to provide for limited neighborhood commercial areas that provide convenience goods or services meeting the needs of adjacent residential neighborhoods. The standards of this Zone support compatibility between commercial and residential uses.

B. Community Business Zone (C-2).

The Community Business (C-2) Zone is intended to provide for the development of business and service uses to serve community needs in a pedestrian-friendly, neighborhood environment. The standards of this Zone are designed to ensure that goods and services are compatible with adjacent non-commercial development.

C. Community Business - Hualapai Mountain Road Zone (C-2-HMR).

The Community Business Hualapai Mountain Road (C-2-HMR) Zone is intended to

provide for the development of limited business and service uses to serve community needs within the Hualapai Mountain Road Area Plan. Within this Zone specialized design standards require developments to be designed within a broad range of southwestern architectural styles that are compatible with surrounding buildings, as well as the colors and textures of the surrounding natural environment.

D. Service Business Zone (C-3).

The Service Business (C-3) Zone is intended to provide for the development of business and service uses to meet the needs of the community and region. This Zone accommodates general retail, commercial, and service uses with good vehicular circulation and sufficient parking.

E. Kingman Crossing Planned Development Zone (KC-PDZ-1806)

The Kingman Crossing Planned Development Zone (KC-PDZ) is intended to provide for the development of business and service uses designed to meet the needs of the Kingman Crossing area located on both sides of Interstate-40. This zone allows a wide variety of goods and services for businesses that would benefit from good exposure in a distinct commercial setting. Provisions of this district are designed to ensure that such commerce will be compatible with adjacent, noncommercial development and minimize undesirable effects of heavy traffic or other operating characteristics.

127

Title 3: Zones

F. Hualapai Mountain Medical Center Planned Development Zone (HMMC-PDZ-1663)

The Hualapai Mountain Medical Center Planned Development Zone (HMMC-PDZ) is intended to provide for the development of the Hualapai Mountain Medical Center Subdivision with uses and services to the needs of the community, region and public at large.

G. Special Districts and Overlays.

Certain areas of the City with unique characteristics have been identified where

additional requirements apply in addition to the standards provided in the base zones. Developers shall determine whether any of their projects are located within any of the following special districts or overlay zones. If a project is located within one of these areas, the project must comply with the applicable standards for the zone as well as the applicable standards for the overlay:

Historic Overlay (refer to Section 3-70.020 for specific standards and location)

2 KDRO (refer to Section 3-70.030 for specific standards and location).

3-30.020 Development Standards

The development standards for the commercial zones are listed in the following tables.

3-30.030 Miscellaneous Standards – Commercial Zones

A. Architectural Design Standards

130

All properties within the City must comply with the general site planning and architectural design standards described in Division 4-20 of this Code and the building height requirements listed in Division 4-30 of this Code.

B. Encroachments

Division 4-40 of this Code describes specific situations where limited encroachments into the minimum required setbacks may be allowed.

C. Fences, Walls, and Screening

All properties within the City must comply with the fence, wall, and screening standards described in Division 4-50 of this Code. This includes compliance with the fence and wall height requirements listed in Table 4-50.050.A. Screening is also required for mechanical equipment and refuse or recycling containers.

D. Landscaping

All new commercial development within the City must comply with the landscaping standards described in Division 4-60 of this Code. When an existing building or structure is significantly remodeled, the development must also comply with the standards in Division 4-60.

Section 4-60.020 describes limited exceptions to the landscaping requirements.

E. Outdoor Lighting

All new and major additions to developments, buildings, or structures within the City must comply with the lighting standards described in Division 4-70 of this Code. Section 4-70.040 includes lighting requirements for special uses such as gas stations and parking garages.

F. Parking

All new buildings and changes of uses must comply with the parking and loading standards described in Division 4-80 of this Code, including the minimum parking spaces required per use in Table 4-80.030.A. Section 480.030 includes bicycle parking requirements for commercial uses.

G. Signs

All properties within the City must comply with the sign standards described in Division 4-90 of this Code.

H. Additional Requirements – All Commercial Zones.

All business and service uses, including the display of merchandise, must

be conducted wholly within an enclosed building, except where outdoor display or storage may be allowed as shown in the Land Use Matrix. Any outdoor display or storage must comply with the use-specific standards for outdoor display and storage. [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

With the exception of dedicated self-storage facilities, accessory storage

of merchandise must be within an enclosed building, unless an outdoor

Title 3: Zones

131

storage or use area is shown on an approved site plan in compliance with the outdoor storage or use use-specific standards.

All commercially zoned lots must have frontage on a public street.

All developments in commercial zones must have a refuse and/or

recycling storage area constructed and provided in accordance with City standards, including the screening standards in Division 4-50.

Parcels within a commerce or shopping center must comply with the

perimeter setbacks but may have reduced setbacks internal to the master site if the overall development complies with parking, screening, and landscaping requirements with approval from the Director.

3-40.010 Intent

3-40.020 Development Standards

3-40.030 Miscellaneous Standards - Industrial Zones

3-40.010 Intent

A. Light Industry Zone (I-1).

The Light Industry (I-1) Zone applies to areas of the City appropriate for the development of clean and quiet, low-impact industrial uses that do not have external effects which could be offensive or hazardous to residential or commercial uses.

B. Heavy Industry Zone (I-2).

The Heavy Industry (I-2) Zone applies to areas of the City appropriate for certain industrial structures and uses having external effects which may be offensive or hazardous to residential, commercial, or light industrial uses. This Zone is located in areas of the City that reduce the potential for land use conflicts.

3-40.030 Miscellaneous Standards – Industrial Zones

A. Architectural Design Standards

All properties within the City must comply with the general site planning and architectural design standards described in Division 4-20 of this Code and the building height requirements listed in Division 4-30 of this Code.

B. Encroachments

Division 4-40 of this Code describes specific situations where limited encroachments into the minimum required setbacks may be allowed.

C. Fences, Walls, and Screening

All properties within the City must comply with the fence, wall, and screening standards

135

Title 3: Zones

described in Division 4-50 of this Code. This includes compliance with the fence and wall height requirements listed in Table 4-50.050.A. Screening is also required for mechanical equipment and refuse or recycling containers.

D. Landscaping

All new industrial development within the City must comply with the landscaping standards described in Division 4-60 of this Code. When an existing building or structure is significantly remodeled, the development must also comply with the standards in Division 4-60.

E. Outdoor Lighting

All new and major additions to developments, buildings, or structures within the City must comply with the lighting standards described in Division 4-70 of this Code.

F. Parking

All new buildings and changes of uses must comply with the parking and loading standards described in Division 4-80 of this Code, including the minimum parking spaces required per use in Table 4-80.030.A and bicycle parking requirements.

G. Signs

All properties within the City must comply with the sign standards described in Division 4-90 of this Code.

H. Additional Requirements – Industrial Zones.

No use shall be established in any industrial district which causes or emits any dust,

gas, smoke, fumes, odors, noises, vibrations, electromagnetic disturbance, radiation, or other similar effects which is or may be detrimental to the public health, safety, or general welfare. All uses must be continuously maintained so that they are neither obnoxious nor offensive by reason of the above emissions.

Parcels within an industrial center must comply with the perimeter setbacks but may

have reduced setbacks internal to the master site if the overall development complies with parking, screening, and landscaping requirements with approval from the Director.

3-50.030 Miscellaneous Standards – ROS Zone

3-50.010 Intent

The Recreation and Open Space (ROS) Zone applies to areas of the City intended to provide for permanent open space and recreational uses on land with natural environments and unique geologic features. The ROS Zone is also intended to reduce the potential for flood, fire, subsidence, or erosion impacts that may otherwise result from development of properties highly susceptible to these hazards.

3-60.020 Downtown District Zones Overview

3-60.030 Downtown District Zone Standards

3-60.040 Miscellaneous Standards – Downtown District Zones

3-60.010 Intent

The purpose of the Downtown District (DD) Zones is to reinforce existing historic downtown areas and facilitate development of walkable mixed-use urban environments.

3-60.020 Downtown District Zones Overview

Each Downtown District Zone is established based on the desired physical form and character of the area. The zones focus on historic and walkable environments ranging from lower intensity development in primarily residential neighborhoods to higher intensity development in downtown environments. Table 3-60.020.A (Downtown District Zones Overview) provides a summary of the Downtown District Zones.

3-60.040 Miscellaneous Standards – Downtown District Zones

A. Architectural Design Standards

All properties within the City must comply with the general site planning and architectural design standards described in Division 4-20 of this Code and the building height requirements listed in Division 4-30 of this Code.

B. Encroachments

Division 4-40 of this Code describes specific situations where limited encroachments into the minimum required setbacks may be allowed.

C. Fences, Walls, and Screening

All properties within the City must comply with the fence, wall, and screening standards described in Division 4-50 of this Code. This includes compliance with the fence and wall height requirements listed in Table 4-50.050.A.

D. Landscaping

All new development within the City must comply with the landscaping standards

141

Title 3: Zones

described in Division 4-60 of this Code. Section 4-60.020 describes limited exceptions to the landscaping requirements.

E. Outdoor Lighting

All new and major additions to developments, buildings, or structures within the City must comply with the lighting standards described in Division 4-70 of this Code.

F. Parking

All new buildings and changes of uses must comply with the parking and loading standards described in Division 4-80 of this Code, including the minimum parking spaces required per use in Table 4-80.030.A.

G. Signs

All properties within the City must comply with the sign standards described in Division 490 of this Code.

H. Additional Standards

For any change of use without an increase in floor area, even if the change of use increases the demand for parking (e.g., a change from a retail use to a bar or restaurant use), additional parking otherwise required for the change of use is not required within the Downtown District Zones. Applicants and developers are encouraged to provide appropriate parking to meet the demands of the new use to the greatest extent feasible.

142

Title 3: Zones

3-70 Overlay and Special District Zones

Sections:

3-70.010 Intent

3-70.020 Historic Overlay Zone

3-70.030 Kingman Design Review Overlay Zone (KDRO)

3-70.040 Interstate 40 Corridor Overlay Zone

3-70.050 Miscellaneous Standards – Overlay and Special District Zones

3-70.010 Intent

The Overlay Zones are applied to certain areas of the City with unique characteristics where additional requirements apply in addition to the standards provided in the base zones.

3-70.020 Historic Overlay Zone

A. Purpose and Intent.

The Kingman Historic Overlay Zone is intended to support the City’s desire for the recognition, preservation, enhancement, and perpetuation of objects, structures, natural features, sites, places, and areas within the City that have historic, architectural, archaeological, cultural, or aesthetic significance in the interest of the health, economic prosperity, education, cultural enrichment, and general welfare of the public.

B. Applicability.

The boundaries of the Kingman Historic Overlay Zone are mapped on the Historic District Overlay Map shown in Figure 3-70.020.A. These boundaries are further described as applying to the south one-half of Blocks 1, 2, and 3 of Kingman Townsite, from North First Street to North Fourth Street, from the alley to East Andy Devine Avenue, and the area between East Andy Devine Avenue and the BN&SF Railroad tracks, bordered by the Powerhouse and the Santa Fe Train Depot.

C. Heritage Preservation Procedures and Standards.

Section 2-40.080 (Heritage Preservation Procedures) provides details on the procedures and standards specific to the designation of Historic Zones and Landmark Properties. Design and development guidelines applicable to all Landmark Properties or within properties within a Historic Overlay Zone are included in Appendix 1 (Design Guidelines for Historic Properties).

143

Figure 3-70.020.A

Title 3: Zones

144

Title 3: Zones

3-70.030 Kingman Design Review Overlay (KDRO) Zone

A. Purpose and Intent.

The Kingman Design Review Overlay Zone is intended to aid in the implementation of the General Plan, to encourage sound design principles, assure the compatibility of uses, encourage attractive buildings, and to promote and enhance the health, safety, and general welfare of the residents in the community.

B. Applicability.

The KDRO establishes site development standards, building design standards, and building materials standards in the designated areas established in the Kingman Design Review Overlay Maps, Figures 3-70.030.A and 3-70.030.B, on the following pages.

C. KDRO Standards.

The site development requirements of the underlying zone apply to development

on the property with the exception that all developments must have a minimum setback of 20 feet from all property lines.

See Section 4-20.040 for details on additional specific development and design

standards.

3-70.040 Interstate 40 Corridor Overlay Zone

A. Purpose and Intent.

The Interstate 40 (I-40) Corridor Overlay contains specific. signage standards for the below described area adjacent to Interstate 40.

B. Applicability.

The I-40 Corridor consists of a strip of land 1,000 feet in width on either side of the I-40 right-of-way lines, extending a maximum of 3,000 feet in each direction from the centerline of the interstate right of way as shown on the following Figures 3-70.040.A, 370.040.B, and 3-70.040.C.

3-70.050 Miscellaneous Standards – Overlay and Special District Zones

A. Architectural Design Standards

All properties within the City must comply with the general site planning and architectural design standards described in Division 4-20 of this Code and the building height requirements listed in Division 4-30 of this Code.

B. Encroachments

Division 4-40 of this Code describes specific situations where limited encroachments into the minimum required setbacks may be allowed.

C. Fences, Walls, and Screening

All properties within the City must comply with the fence, wall, and screening standards described in Division 4-50 of this Code. This includes compliance with the fence and wall height requirements listed in Table 4-50.050.A.

D. Landscaping

All new development within the City must comply with the landscaping standards described in Division 4-60 of this Code. Section 4-60.020 describes limited exceptions to the landscaping requirements.

E. Outdoor Lighting

All new and major additions to developments, buildings, or structures within the City must comply with the lighting standards described in Division 4-70 of this Code.

F. Parking

All new buildings and changes of uses must comply with the parking and loading standards described in Division 4-80 of this Code, including the minimum parking spaces required per use in Table 4-80.030.A.

G. Signs

All properties within the City must comply with the sign standards described in Division 490 of this Code.

3-80.010 Purpose and Applicability

This Division provides additional site planning, development, and operating standards for certain land uses and activities that require special standards to ensure their compatibility with existing or planned uses in the vicinity.

3-80.020 Accessory Buildings, Structures, and Uses

A. Accessory Buildings and Structures General Requirements.

Accessory buildings and structures are permitted in all zones in compliance with this

Section, provided they are located on the same lot as the principal use, under the same ownership as the principal use, and incidental and subordinate to the principal use or building.

There must be a principal structure on the parcel or a Building Permit for a principal

structure issued prior to, or simultaneously with, the issuance of a Building Permit for an accessory building or structure.

Sheds and structures less than or equal to 200 square feet in floor area do not require

a Building Permit but are required to comply with all other provisions of this Code, including Section 2-40.060 for Minor Improvement Permits.

155

Title 3: Zones

Where an open carport or patio cover is attached to one side of a dwelling and is

visible from the street, no additional open carport or patio cover is allowed to be attached to the opposite side of the dwelling, other than a covered entry up to 60 square feet in area.

Accessory structures in multi-family residential, manufactured home, and non residential zones must be shown on an approved site plan and must comply with the setbacks of the zone.

B. Accessory Uses General Requirements.

An accessory structure must be used in compliance with the permitted uses of the

zone within which it is located.

Accessory buildings or structures must be incidental to the primary use and must not

alter the character of the primary use. Within the industrial zones, accessory offices, showrooms, or other similar uses may constitute no greater than twenty-five percent of the gross floor area of the primary building or structure on the site.

No manufactured home, mobile home, trailer, semi-trailer, bus, recreation vehicle, or

boxcar may be used as an accessory building or structure.

No accessory building may be used as a dwelling unit, unless designed specifically as

an Accessory Dwelling Unit (ADU).

C. Standards for Accessory Buildings or Structures.

The following table identifies the standards for accessory structures or buildings.

3-80.030 Accessory Dwelling Units (ADUs)

A. Applicability.

Accessory Dwelling Units (ADUs), including tiny homes and factory-built buildings used as an ADU, are subject to the following requirements.

B. Design and Development Standards.

An ADU must meet the following design and development standards:

A lot or parcel with a single-family dwelling may have one attached and one detached ADU.

One additional ADU is permitted on a lot or parcel that is one acre or more in size, if at least one

of the ADUs on the lot or parcel is a restricted-affordable dwelling unit.

The ADU must not have a separate address than the primary residence.

All utility services for accessory structures must come from the primary structure’s

utility connections.

Full kitchen facilities are allowed inside.

The maximum livable area of the ADU must not be greater than 75 percent of the

livable area in the primary residence or 1,000 square feet, whichever is less.

Setbacks a. The ADU must conform to the development standards for single-family dwellings within the

same zone with regard to height, setbacks, lot size or coverage, and building frontage. b. The ADU may not be located in the required front yard setback. c. The ADU side and rear setbacks must be a minimum of five feet.

If a home occupation is proposed to be operated out of an ADU, the home occupation

must comply with the use-specific standards for home occupations in this Code.

C. Findings for Approval of ADUs.

The exterior design of the ADU is compatible with the primary residence and does not

157

Title 3: Zones

dominate it or surrounding properties. This has been achieved through use of compatible and complimentary architectural building forms, construction materials, colors, landscaping, and other methods that conform to acceptable construction practices.

Major access stairs, decks, entry doors, and windows on one and one-half and two story structures face the primary residence to the maximum extent feasible, or the rear alley, if applicable. Windows that face neighboring side or rear setbacks shall be installed so the bottom of the window is a minimum of six feet above the floor.

Buildings, structures, and other features of the site plan, such as walkways and

driveways, shall be oriented and located to maintain natural and historic resources to the maximum extent feasible and to minimize alteration of natural site conditions.

An ADU may not be built on top of a current or planned public utility easement unless

the property owner receives written consent from any utility that is currently using the public utility easement or may use the public utility easement in the future. Any applicable written consent must be submitted to the Department with the application for the Building Permit for the ADU.

3-80.040 Accessory Wind Energy Systems

A. Purpose.

The purpose of this Section is to accommodate Accessory Wind Energy Systems (AWES)

in appropriate locations within the City while minimizing any adverse visual, safety, and environmental impacts. In addition, a permitting process for AWES is established to ensure compliance with the provisions of the requirements and standards of this Section.

B. Approvals.

No AWES may be erected, constructed, installed, or modified without first obtaining a

Building Permit and must comply with all applicable sections of the Building Code.

Small DC AWES (i.e., 200 to 1,000 watts) are exempt from Building Permit review,

except that an electrical permit must be obtained prior to installation.

Permanent or temporary signs or logos are prohibited on any component of an

AWES, except as follows:

a. Manufacturer’s or installer’s identification on the turbine or tower; and,

b. Appropriate warning signs and placards.

The following additional information must be provided on the site plan or on

additional plans and drawings as necessary to assist in the review of the Building Permit application:

a. A site plan that shows the location of existing and proposed structures,

maintenance drop zone for working on the wind turbine without crossing property lines, any easements on the property, setbacks, and the location of the proposed AWES;

b. A cross-section of the property showing the height of the AWES structure

relative to the nearest existing and proposed buildings;

c. Manufacturer’s specifications on size, color, and recommendations for installation, if

possible neutral, non-reflective, desert colors shall be used, and the structure shall not be illuminated unless required by a State or Federal Agency;

158

Title 3: Zones

d. Structural calculations and plans for the tower and its foundation stamped

by any engineer licensed within the State of Arizona;

e. A line drawing of the electrical components in sufficient detail to allow for a

determination that the matter of installation conforms to the adopted electrical code;

f. For all AWES that connect to the electric grid, a copy of an inter-connected

agreement with the applicable public utility must be submitted; and

g. Documentation necessary to show compliance with applicable FAA regulations,

particularly for any installations near an airport or heliport.

C. Location and Siting Requirements.

Electrical collection system conduit must be placed underground within the interior of

each parcel, except that the collection system may be placed overhead near substations or points of collection to the electric grid.

All ground mounted electrical and control equipment must be labeled and secured to

prevent unauthorized access.

AWES may be located within all zones subject to the location and siting standards

provided in the following table.

159

as measured from finished grade to the top of the small DC AWES. No moving portions of the blade of

systems.

D. Obsolescence and Removal.

If the AWES remains inoperative or non-functional for a continuous period of six

consecutive months, the system shall be deemed to be abandoned and shall constitute a public nuisance. Owners shall remove abandoned systems at their expense after a demolition permit has been obtained.

Removal includes demolition and removal of the tower and all related above grade

structures, except that any landscaping, grading, or below-grade foundations may remain.

The owner of a property with AWES assumes all risk associated with diminished

performance of the system caused by any present or future adjacent development that may interfere with the system’s ability to produce power at its rated capacity.

3-80.050 Airports or Aircraft Landing Fields

Private airports or airstrips for the landing and take-off of aircraft and hang gliders must

comply with the following standards:

A. The site shall be a minimum of 20 acres.

B. Landing areas and strips shall be setback a minimum of 300 feet from any property

line.

C. A minimum of one off-street parking space per hanger or tie-down space shall be

provided.

D. Restaurants or cafes are allowed accessory uses. If proposed as an accessory

use, this use must be taken into consideration when calculating the amount of required parking.

E. Safety fencing may be required around the landing strip areas.

F. The private airport or aircraft landing field must comply with all federal, state, and

local regulations and shall not interfere with any governmental or public airport operations.

3-80.060 Amusement or Theme Park

A. The lot shall be a minimum of 10 acres.

B. A minimum 400-foot frontage on a public street is required.

C. Vehicular access shall be from arterial roads, except secondary fire-medical access

may be taken where appropriate.

3-80.070 Animal Boarding Facility, Shelter, Hospital or Clinic

A. A report from a qualified acoustical and/or environmental engineer must be prepared,

noting that there are no negative impacts on neighboring residential uses or identifying mitigation measures to reduce impacts.

B. The building shall incorporate soundproof barriers and be designed to properly

eliminate odors, waste, and other contaminants from adjacent properties.

C. No outdoor boarding shall be permitted for small animals. All activities shall occur

within the building except for outdoor exercise areas.

Title 3: Zones

160

Title 3: Zones

D. Outdoor exercise areas must be 100 feet from an adjacent residentially zoned property.

E. Short-term boarding may be permitted as an accessory use for animal hospitals or

clinics to house animals requiring overnight care or observation of a veterinarian or their staff.

3-80.080 Animal Keeping and Agricultural Operations

A. Standards for the keeping of animals, included hoofed animals, fowl, and domestic

animals, are included in Chapter 3 (Animals) of the Kingman Municipal Code.

B. Agricultural structures for maintenance of farming equipment shall be located a

minimum of 200 feet from any lot line.

C. Open outdoor storage is limited to plants, bulk organic dirt, or packaged fertilizer.

D. Killing and dressing of animals raised on the premises and intended for domestic use

is permitted within the RR and R-MH-40 zones, provided that any building used for this purpose is setback a minimum of 50 feet from any property line.

3-80.090 Animal Slaughter and Meat Processing Facility

No noise or odors at levels that would constitute a nuisance may be allowed at the property

boundaries.

3-80.100 Automobile Fueling Service Station and Convenience Store

A. Pump stand canopies shall be architecturally similar to the main structure and the

character of the area.

B. Pump islands or plug-in stands shall be located a minimum of 50 feet from street right of-way or non-residential property lines and a minimum of 75 feet from a residential property line. An automobile service station must store all vehicle parts within a completely enclosed building.

C. Driveways shall be developed in conjunction with adjacent uses, maximizing shared

driveway access wherever possible. Interior curbs shall be constructed to separate driving surfaces from sidewalks and landscaping.

3-80.110 Automobile, Truck, and Trailer Rental Facility

A. Vehicles and trailers for rent must be stored in a designated area that does not impact

the required parking. This area must be shown on an approved site plan.

B. For rental facilities that are not within commercial centers, the areas used for parking

and storage shall be paved and screened.

C. On-site service and repairs of automobiles is prohibited except for hand washing,

vacuuming, window cleaning, and checking fluids. Opening to service bays, if any, must be designed to minimize the visual intrusion into adjoining properties or right-ofway.

D. Buildings or structures must be set back from all residential zones by a minimum of 50

161

Title 3: Zones

feet.

3-80.120 Automobile/Vehicle Repair Garage – Major/Minor

A. All storage of vehicles awaiting needed parts or repair must be within the building or

within a yard enclosed by a solid fence or wall that meets the requirements of Division 450, except for driveway openings.

B. Vehicle repair and service work may be performed outside of an enclosed building, but

any vehicle that does not have the repair completed by the end of the business day must be placed within the enclosed yard or building. Vehicles shall not be stored on the property more than 30 days.

C. All vehicle parts must be stored within a completely enclosed building.

D. Service bay openings shall be designed to minimize noise and visual intrusion onto

adjacent properties or right-of-way.

E. Minor repair garages shall be limited to services such as installation of minor parts only

(such as batteries, windshield wipers, hoses, fuses, lights, radios, and other similar minor elements) and shall not include engine, transmission, and differential service or similar installation.

F. Tire repair shops are required to comply with the requirements described in this Section.

All tires stored on site must be located within an enclosed building.

3-80.130 Automobile/Vehicle Sales and Leasing

A. Buildings or structures must be set back from all residential zones by a minimum of 150

feet.

B. Loading areas must be identified and reserved on the site for the unloading of vehicles

and boats brought to the site by carriers.

C. Outdoor storage and display areas, if any, must be identified on the site plan and comply

with the use-specific standards for outdoor storage and display areas.

D. If on-site servicing and repair are permitted as an accessory use, they must comply with

the use-specific standards for those uses.

E. Decorative roll-up doors may be used for concierge delivery or drop-off of vehicles for

servicing. These doors shall be shut when the business is not in operation.

F. Automobiles and/or vehicles not on display but stored on the site shall be stored to the

rear of the primary building on the site and screened from view, unless otherwise determined by the Director.

3-80.140 Automobile/Vehicle Washing Facilities (Car Wash)

A. Car washes may be permitted as an accessory use to vehicle fueling stations or service

facilities.

B. Sufficient stacking area for cars waiting to enter the wash area must be provided on-site. If

this stacking area is adjacent to the street, it shall be screened by landscaped berm,

162

Title 3: Zones

screen wall, or combination thereof no less than three feet tall.

C. The wash tunnel exit shall be oriented away from any adjacent residential uses.

3-80.150 Bars and Taverns

A report from a qualified acoustical and/or environmental engineer may be required by the Director if there are neighboring residential uses. This report shall document if there are no negative impacts or if there are mitigation measures that could be incorporated to reduce impacts.

3-80.160 Bed and Breakfasts

A. A bed and breakfast must be operated by the property owner living on the site and the

rooms must be part of a single-family dwelling.

B. A bed and breakfast is limited to a maximum of four guest bedrooms. No alterations to

the exterior of the residence that alters the residential character are allowed, except those that may be required by health and safety codes.

C. Food may only be served to registered overnight guests. Guest room cooking facilities are

prohibited.

D. Maximum guest stay is limited to thirty consecutive days.

3-80.170 Brick, Clay, Concrete, Stone, or Similar Product Manufacturing

A. A minimum setback of 1,000 feet is required between any on-site roadways or activity

areas and any residential zoning districts in the vicinity of the site.

B. The City may impose limits on the time of operations if deemed necessary to limit

impacts to properties in the vicinity.

C. Provisions shall be made for the on-site reduction and containment of dust and other

particulates. This could include paving of on-site roads or other methods of dust abatement.

D. The site shall have access from a collector or arterial street.

E. Any outdoor storage is subject to the use-specific standards for that use described in

this Code.

F. Buildings accessory to the primary use are permitted but are subject to the use specific standards in this Code.

G. Quarrying operations shall comply with the above requirements and the following:

The site plan must show the area to be excavated and proposed locations of

equipment, storage of harvested materials, and any buildings. All equipment and buildings must be sited to minimize impacts to adjacent uses.

The access drive from the site shall be paved a minimum of 50 feet from the right of-way of the public road. Provisions shall be made to remove dirt, mud, or debris from vehicle wheels exiting the site.

3-80.180 Bus, Rail Passenger Terminal

A. A brief description of planned operations, including security measures, and a parking

163

Title 3: Zones

assessment is required with the site plan submittal to document the parking need for the site.

B. The description of planned operations must describe all security measures for the site.

C. Restroom facilities must be provided for visitors to the site during hours of operation.

Additional accessory buildings are permitted, subject to the development requirements of the zone and any applicable use-specific standards.

D. If storage of busses is proposed at the site, this must be clearly indicated on the site

plan and screened in compliance with the outdoor storage use-specific standards.

3-80.190 Cemeteries

A. Cemeteries for human burial shall be a minimum of 10 acres and located on a collector or

arterial street.

B. Accessory uses incidental to operation of a cemetery are allowed but must be setback from

the property line by a minimum of 50 feet. A crematorium is not an allowed accessory use. A mausoleum is an allowed accessory use.

C. A 50-foot landscaped buffer shall be provided along the property lines.

D. No graves shall be within the required setbacks.

3-80.200 Commercial Campgrounds

A . Permitted Uses.

A single-family residence or manufactured home for the manager’s office and

residence.

Recreation and social centers, and outdoor recreational facilities.

Laundry facilities, maintenance building, and/or facilities.

RV sites up to a maximum of 15 percent of the campground area.

Service buildings must be permanent buildings and constructed in accordance with

City requirements. Facilities in service buildings such as lavatories, toilets, showers, etc., must be in installed in accordance with County and State Health Department requirements.

B. Size Requirements.

The minimum site area for a commercial campground is two acres.

Tent sites must provide a minimum of 500 square feet per site with a 10-foot

separation between tent sites.

Recreation areas or common open space must be provided at a ratio of 100 square

feet per tent or RV space.

A minimum 10-foot minimum setback is required between the tent or RV spaces and

the campground’s perimeter.

RV sites must comply with the site size requirements in the use-specific standards for

an RV park.

C . Parking and Circulation.

On-street parking is prohibited on any private drives, streets, or roads internal to the

164

Title 3: Zones

commercial campground. Each space must be provided with a paved or graveled space for parking.

A parking area must be provided at the office to allow parking during registration, if

applicable.

Individual tent or RV spaces must be accessible from an internal private drive with

each lot accessible from an approved private street or road. Internal roads within a commercial campground must have a minimum width of 20 feet and must be improved to minimum City standards (except width). No space is permitted to have direct access to a public street or way.

3-80.210 Community Gardens

A. General.

Community gardens must consist of land used for cultivation of fruits, vegetables, plants,

flowers, or herbs by multiple users. The land must be served by a water supply sufficient to support the cultivation practices used on the site.

B. Standards.

The site must be designed and maintained so that water and fertilizer will not drain

onto adjacent property.

There must be no retail sales on site, except for produce grown on the site with approval of a

CUP and in compliance with all state and local regulations.

Only the following buildings or structures are allowed:

a. One shed for the storage of tools limited to a maximum of 200 square feet in
