floor area and placed in compliance with the setback standards of the zone;

b. One greenhouse, made of glass, plastic, or fiberglass, limited to a maximum of

200 square feet in floor area and placed in compliance with the setback standards of the zone; and

c. Other small hardscape areas and amenities (such as benches, bike racks, raised/

accessible planting beds, compost or waste bins, picnic tables, seasonal farm stands, fences, garden art, rain barrels, and children’s play areas).

b. The combined area of all structures must not exceed 15 percent of the community

garden site area.

A fence designed in compliance with Division 4-50 and designed to be complimentary

to any structures on the site shall be installed around the perimeter of the garden.

Onsite storm water systems and irrigation must comply with the City’s Stormwater

Regulations.

C. Maintenance Required.

Maintenance must be performed on an ongoing basis as needed and in compliance with City

property maintenance codes.

Required maintenance includes irrigation, weeding, mowing, pruning, replacement of

dead or diseased plants, cleaning, raking, snow removal, pest control, removal of wildfire fuels, and otherwise caring for and repairing all the landscape materials.

Maintenance of plant materials, specifically including trimming and pruning, must

165

Title 3: Zones

conform to applicable horticulture and arboriculture standards.

Composting equipment and waste receptacles shall be screened from neighboring

uses so as to not create a nuisance as defined in the Municipal Code. Only containers designed for composting may be used for composting, no open piles are permitted.

D. Abandoned or Unproductive Community Gardens.

If a community garden left in an unproductive state for longer than a period of 12 months, the individual(s) responsible for the community garden must ensure that it is returned to its original form or otherwise developed in compliance with this Code. Reestablishment of an abandoned or unproductive community garden will require approval of a new site plan.

3-80.220 Construction and Work Trailers

A. A work trailer may be used as a construction office or a temporary work site for employees

provided that:

The use is authorized by the Building Permit for the permanent structure; and,

The use is appropriate because:

a. The trailer will be in place during the construction of a subdivision, or the

construction or remodeling of a permanent commercial or manufacturing structure for a maximum of one year, unless otherwise specified by the Building Permit or the Building Official;

b. The applicant has demonstrated that the temporary work site is a short-term

necessity for a maximum of one year; or,

c. That the trailer will be removed prior to the issuance of a Certificate of Occupancy

for the permanent structure(s).

The trailer and parking areas shall be shown on a site plan submitted with the Building

Permit application, ensuring that their location will not interfere with construction ingress and egress to the site.

3-80.230 Convents or Monasteries

Convents or monasteries may include facilities for public assembly, dormitories, or live-work spaces. If

any of these uses are proposed as a part of the covenant or monastery, those components must comply with the applicable use-specific standards.

3-80.240 Cottage Court Housing

A. Purpose.

This Section establishes standards for a grouping of three or more detached single-family residential buildings arranged to share a courtyard with pedestrian access to the buildings from the courtyard and fronting street(s). The courtyard is intended to be an outdoor room that can be seen by the public. Parking is located at the rear of the lot.

B. Site Design Standards.

Each building must include a pitched roof that spans the entire width and length of

the building.

166

Title 3: Zones

Building entries must be raised 1.5 feet from the adjacent sidewalk grade to provide

privacy for occupants. Units along side streets may have a second entry from the courtyard.

One or more separated or interconnected courtyards of at least 25 feet by 30 feet and

not exceeding 100 feet in length must be provided.

The site must comply with the applicable supplemental standards outlined in Title 4.

Figure 3-80.240.1 Typical Cottage Court Site Diagram

3-80.250 Data Center

A. A report from a qualified acoustical and/or environmental engineer may be required by

the Director if there are neighboring residential or similarly sensitive uses. This report shall document if there are no negative impacts or if there are mitigation measures that could be incorporated to reduce impacts.

B. A water use report may be required if there are concerns regarding water demand to

support operation of the data center. The report shall document if there are no negative impacts or if there are mitigation measures that could be incorporated to reduce impacts.

3-80.260 Day Care, Home and Center

A. Day Care, Home.

No more than one individual not residing on the premises may be employed in the

167

Title 3: Zones

home day care use.

The home day care must be conducted in a manner that does not create adverse

impacts on the residential character of the neighborhood.

During the hours of operation of the day care use, the amount of floor area devoted to

this accessory use is not limited.

The home day care shall also be subject to the applicable requirements for home occupations

listed in this Division.

B. Day Care, Center.

Day care center facilities must comply with all applicable State and fire codes,

including Arizona Revised Statutes. At a minimum, this includes 75 square feet of fenced outdoor play area per child.

A six-foot high solid fence or wall must be constructed on all property lines or around

the outdoor activity areas of day care centers, except in the front setback or within a traffic safety visibility area, in compliance with Division 4-50.

All day care center facilities must have public access from a collector or arterial street.

The required drop-off spaces must be connected to the primary building via a sidewalk or if a

sidewalk is not feasible, enhanced paving must be provided for pedestrian walkways through parking areas.

3-80.270 Dormitory

A. A dormitory is permitted as an accessory use to a school or convent/monastery with

approval of a CUP.

B. Permanent residents within dormitories are limited to students of the school or those

dedicated to the religious order.

C. Additional guest rooms may be provided at the covenant/monastery dormitory for temporary

events, such as religious retreats; however, this use shall clearly be incidental and subordinate to the dormitory use.

3-80.280 Drive-through Facility

A. Design Objectives.

A drive-through facility may only be permitted if the Department first determines that the design and operation will avoid congestion, excessive pavement, litter, and noise.

B. Limitation on Location.

The drive-through must only be located along the building’s façade away from

adjacent residential uses.

To the maximum extent feasible, drive-through lanes must not be located

between a property line and the front of the building or along a street frontage.

The drive-through must be located a minimum of 25 feet from any right-of-way,

75 feet from any residential uses, or 300 feet from the entrance or exit of any other drive-through.

The entrance and exit of any drive-through lane must be a minimum of 50 feet

from an intersection of public rights-of-way (measured at the closest

168

Title 3: Zones

intersecting curbs).

C. On-site Circulation Standards.

The drive-through facility must be integrated with the internal circulation and traffic control of the site as follows:

1. Drive-through Lane Design.

a. The drive-through lane must be designed with a minimum 12-foot interior radius

at curves and a minimum 11-foot width.

b. To the maximum extent feasible, the drive-through lane must be screened by a 3 foot-tall wall designed to match the building materials of the primary building(s) on the site as illustrated in Figure 3-80.280.1 (An Appropriately Screened Drivethrough Lane). If a 3-foot-tall wall is not feasible, dense landscaping achieving the same effect as a wall may be allowed by the Director.

Figure 3-80.280.1 An Appropriately Screened Drive-through Lane

2. Drive-through Stacking Area.

A clearly identified area must be provided for vehicles waiting for drive-up or drivethrough service that is physically separated from other on-site traffic circulation.

a. The stacking area must accommodate a minimum of six cars from the preview or

order board to the service window. No vehicles waiting for drive-up or drive-through service are allowed to stack in the public right-of-way. Additional stacking space may be required at the direction of the City Engineer and upon review of a queuing analysis. [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

b. The stacking area must be separated from other traffic by concrete curbing or

paint striping on at least one side of the lane.

c. Stacking areas are prohibited adjacent and parallel to the street or public right of- way unless appropriately screened as described above and shown in Figure 3-80.280.1.

169

Title 3: Zones

3. Walkways.

An on-site pedestrian walkway must not intersect a drive-through aisle to the maximum extent feasible. In limited circumstances where a walkway must cross a drive-through aisle, the crossing shall incorporate enhanced paving and pedestrian crossing signs.

3-80.290 Equestrian and Livestock Facilities

A. No activities that would generate more vehicular traffic than is normal to an area

with single-family residences shall be permitted unless the site has direct access to an arterial or collector street.

B. Occasional shows or activities that would generate vehicular traffic may be

permitted with approval of a CUP or a Temporary Use Permit for a special event.

C. Commercial stables shall have a minimum site area of 10 acres; however, this

minimum may be waived if the site is adjacent to equestrian trails.

D. All livestock containment areas and temporary manure storage areas shall be set

back a minimum of 100 feet from any property line.

E. A plan for physical containment and location of manure storage must be provided

with the site plan application to minimize impacts to adjacent properties.

F. A minimum of one off-street parking space shall be provided for each three stalls in

an equestrian recreation facility. Additional parking must be provided for shows or special events.

3-80.300 Fairgrounds

A. The temporary use of recreation vehicles by exhibitors during events is allowed.

B. A site-built or manufactured permanent caretaker’s residence is allowed within the

fairground property. This residence must comply with the setback and design standards in this Code.

C. All parking areas must be paved or have another method of dust control.

3-80.310 Famers Markets

A. If applicable, county health department approval or copies of business license(s) of all vendors

shall be required before issuance of a Special Event Permit or Temporary Use Permit.

B. Farmers markets are subject to the following requirements:

The sales area must be a minimum of 50 feet from any residential development.

A farmers market must specialize in the sale of farm fresh produce and flowers.

No greater than 60 percent of all vendors at the market may offer goods for sale such as hand crafted items and food-related items. All other vendors shall be certified farmers or farms.

The operator of the farmers market shall be the Temporary Use Permit holder. A list of

vendors, including references to valid business licenses shall be included with the permit

170

application and regularly updated if new vendors are added.

3-80.320 Flammable Liquid, Gas, and Bulk Fuel – Storage and Sale

Hazardous materials must be stored consistent with all state and federal requirements. The Department may impose additional requirements if deemed necessary for the protection of the safety and welfare of the community.

3-80.330 Galleries, Studios

A. Galleries or studios operating in residential zones are subject to the use-specific

requirements for home occupation and/or live-work, as applicable.

B. Outdoor storage or display areas are subject to the use-specific standards for those uses.

3-80.340 Golf Courses

A. The golf course site shall be a minimum of 30 acres and nine holes.

B. Parking shall be provided at a rate of three space per hole of the golf course.

C. For golf courses in residential developments, up to 10 percent of the required parking

spaces at the golf course may be golf cart parking spaces.

D. Accessory uses incidental to operation of a golf course, including clubhouse areas,

swimming pools, or restaurants, are allowed but must be setback from the property line by a minimum of 50 feet.

E. A golf practice range may be included provided that it is clearly incidental to the primary

golf course use. This means the range may not be lighted or operated outside the golf course hours.

F. A single double-pylon sign up to 60 feet tall, not to exceed 100 square feet in sign area,

is permitted per golf course in accordance with the requirements of this Code unless otherwise determined by the Director. An Electronic Messaging System (EMS) may be incorporated, subject to the limitations in this Code.

3-80.350 Heliport, Helipad

A. A heliport may be permitted as an accessory use for a hospital, medical facility, news

station, emergency service, or similar use with a Conditional Use Permit.

B. Have a lot size no less than five (5) acres

C. Helicopter landing pads shall be a minimum of 1,000 feet from all residentially zoned

property or a minimum of 500 feet from all other zoned properties.

Title 3: Zones

171

Title 3: Zones

D. Federal Aviation Administration approval of the air space and air traffic of the proposed

operation and a planning report analyzing environmental impacts including, but not limited to, noise levels and mitigation, traffic generation, land use compatibility, number of daily operations, hours of operation, flight corridor, and the identification of any adverse impacts to the operation of other airports shall be approved and implemented prior to the issuance of any permit for a helicopter landing facility.

3-80.360 Home Occupations

A. The use of the dwelling as a home occupation must be clearly subordinate to its use for

residential purposes.

B. The home occupation shall only be conducted by the resident(s) of the dwelling with no

more than one employee not residing at the residence working in the dwelling.

C. Alteration in the residential character of the premises is prohibited.

D. The home occupation must be conducted inside the dwelling or inside an accessory

structure, unless the Department determines that the activities that are proposed to occur outside are substantially similar to activities that would normally occur in a residence and at a similar intensity as would be expected to normally occur in a residence (for example, private swim lessons).

E. The home occupation must not exceed 50 percent of the dwelling, and all allowed

accessory structures, or 450 square feet, whichever is less. This does not apply to agricultural activities.

F. Mechanical equipment used in connection with the home occupation must be limited to

that normally found in a dwelling.

G. Exterior storage of business equipment, materials, merchandise, inventory, or heavy

equipment is prohibited.

H. The home occupation must not utilize flammable liquids or hazardous materials in

quantities not customary to a residential use, in compliance with the adopted City Fire Code and Building Code.

I.  The home occupation must not be conducted in such a manner or advertised in such a

way as to generate more pedestrian or vehicular traffic than typical for the zone within which it is located based on the standards in the current edition of the Trip Generation Manual published by the Institute of Transportation Engineers.

J. Vehicles of patrons of the home occupation must be parked in authorized parking

locations, either on the lot where the use is conducted or in public parking adjacent to the lot.

K. No on-site advertising is permitted, and windows may not be used as display areas or to

offer merchandise for sale. L. The home occupation must not create any radio, television, computer, or power

line interference, or noise audible beyond the boundaries of the site.

M. The parking, storage, or the continuous operation of registered commercial vehicles

capable of carrying a payload of more than one and a half tons is not allowed in association with a home occupation. Examples of common commercial vehicles include

172

Title 3: Zones

box vans, box cargo vans, stick bed trucks, heavy construction or industrial vehicles, or big rigs.

N. Delivery trucks may not continually operate out of the residential premises as a function

of the home occupation. Additionally, no home occupations that require regular deliveries of merchandise or equipment by large semi-trucks shall be permitted.

O. A home occupation requires the issuance of a Business License prior to commencement

of the home occupation.

Any change in use of home occupation, or if the applicant/permit holder of a home occupation no longer resides in the home where the home occupation permit was originally issued, will result in the automatic termination of the home occupation. A new owner of a home occupation or a change in use of a previously approved home occupation will require the issuance of a new Business License form the City prior to commencement of the home occupation.

P. In cases where there are questions as to the suitability of a home occupation, the Director

shall make a determination.

Q. Complaints by citizens or local residents related to the home occupation may be filed with

the Department. If the Department finds that there is a violation of these requirements that cannot be mitigated, the home occupation use may be terminated by the Department.

R. The following are examples of uses that are typically appropriate as home occupations,

provided they comply with the provisions of this Code: home offices, art studio, music lessons, swim lessons, or tutoring with no more than four students at a time, in-home day care, catering, or dressmaking or sewing/tailoring.

S. The following uses are not permitted as home occupations: Motor vehicle repair,

painting, servicing, or cleaning, building contractor/construction activities other than office-related uses, animal clinics or veterinary offices, medical, dental, physical therapy, or psychotherapy offices, or restaurants.

3-80.370 Homeless Shelter

Homeless shelters must meet the following development and performance standards:

A. A neighborhood meeting is required before approval of a homeless shelter. Notice

must be mailed to all occupants and property owners within a 750 feet radius of the external boundaries of the property, unless otherwise directed by the Director.

B. Homeless shelters must be located a minimum of 1,000 feet from any residential

property line.

C. The homeless shelter may provide one or more of the following specific common

facilities for the exclusive use of the residents and staff:

Central cooking and dining room(s);

Recreation room;

Counseling center;

Day care facilities for children; or

173

Title 3: Zones

Other support services.

D. Food distribution is only allowed within commercial or industrial zones within the City and

is only allowed with approval of a Conditional Use Permit or Temporary Use Permit, as appropriate.

E. Outdoor facilities must be designed to provide security for residents’ visitors, employees,

and the surrounding area.

F. The agency or organization operating the shelter shall comply with the following requirements:

Staff and services must be provided to assist residents in obtaining permanent

shelter and income.

The provider shall provide a written management plan with the Conditional Use

Permit submission.

The facility shall maintain good standing with City and/or State licenses for the

owner(s), operator(s), and/or staff on the proposed facility.

3-80.380 Hospitals

A. The site shall be a minimum of 5 acres with a minimum frontage of 300 feet, unless the

minimum frontage of the zone is greater.

B. The site plan for a hospital must show circulation routes, and the location of buildings,

incinerator and storage areas.

C. If a helipad is proposed, the helipad must comply with the use-specific standards for

helipads contained in this Code.

3-80.390 Institutional Residential

A. General Licensing Requirements.

A nursing home shall be licensed by the state for the entire duration that the home

is in operation as a nursing home.

The owner or operator of a group foster care home shall register with the state

before commencing operations. This includes completing all required inspections and renewing their registration annually as required by the state.

In the event that the state revokes a person’s license to operate a nursing home or

group foster care home, then the person operating the home shall immediately inform the City.

Local, state, or federal licenses and/or certifications and proof of City inspection

shall be posted in a conspicuous place in the property.

B. General Site Requirements.

The Department has the authority to approve the location of any nursing home or

group foster care facility.

No institutional residential use may be located within 1,200 feet of another

institutional residential use of the same type, measured from the closest property boundaries of each site. Dormitories and convents or monasteries are exempt from this requirement.

174

Title 3: Zones

A minimum 300 square feet of living space shall be provided for each resident.

An automatic fire sprinkler system shall be installed.

For institutional residential uses located within a residence, no exterior changes

that would alter a building’s residential character shall be made to the exterior of the building.

No signage or similar forms of identification may be installed at an in-home nursing

home, other than that permitted for a residential dwelling.

3-80.400 Live/Work

This Section provides standards for the development of live/work units and for the reuse of existing residential, commercial, and industrial structures to accommodate live/work opportunities. A live/work unit must be designed to be consistent with the design and mass of buildings in the vicinity. For live/work units in residential zones, this includes complying with the density requirements of the zone.

The standards of this Section do not apply to mixed-use developments as defined by this Code. There are separate use-specific standards in this Code for mixed-use developments.

A. Limitations on Use.

The nonresidential component of a live/work development must be compatible with the residential component of the live/work development and developments in the surrounding area. A live/work unit may not be established or used in conjunction with any of the following activities:

Sexually oriented businesses;

Vehicle maintenance or repair (e.g. body or mechanical work, including boats and

recreational vehicles), vehicle detailing and painting, upholstery, and similar uses);

Storage of flammable liquids or hazardous materials beyond that normally associated

with a residential use;

Welding, machining, or any open flame work;

Medical Marijuana dispensary; and

Any other activity or use as determined by the Director not to be compatible with

residential activities and/or to have the possibility of affecting the health or safety of live/work unit residents because of the potential for the use to create dust, glare, heat, noise, noxious gasses, odor, smoke, traffic, vibration, or other impacts, or would be hazardous because of materials, processes, products, or wastes.

B. Occupancy Requirement.

The residential space within a live/work unit must be occupied by at least one

individual employed in the business conducted within the live/work unit. No more than one employee not residing at the residence is permitted to be employed at the live/work unit.

The living and work spaces may not be separately leased.

C. Design Standards.

1. Floor Area Requirements.

175

Title 3: Zones

The floor area of the living space must be at least 50 percent of the total floor area for a live/work unit in a residential zone. In a nonresidential zone, the floor area of the working space must be at least 50 percent of the total floor area.

2. Separation and Access.

Access to each live/work unit must be provided access from a public street or common access areas, corridors, or halls. The access to each unit must be clearly separate from other live/work units or other uses within the structure.

3. Facilities for Commercial or Industrial Activities.

A live/work unit intended to be used for commercial or light industrial uses must be designed to accommodate these uses as evidenced by the provision of flooring, interior storage, ventilation, and other physical improvements of the type commonly found in exclusively commercial or light industrial facilities used for the same work activity.

4. Location.

Nonresidential space may be incorporated into the ground floor or upper floors of a live/work unit in a residential zone. In a nonresidential zone, nonresidential space must be incorporated into the ground floor of a live/work unit.

3-80.410 Lodging (Hotels, Motels, Resorts, Timeshares)

Conference facilities may only be permitted as an accessory use. Parking must be provided for the conference space, if any, in addition to the parking required for the guest rooms.

3-80.420 Manufactured Homes

A. Purpose.

This Section is intended to establish appropriate standards for the development of residential manufactured homes in planned, integrated manufactured home parks and subdivisions.

B. Placement of a Manufactured Home.

Manufactured home parks and subdivisions must comply with the development

standards of this Code.

A manufactured home placed on a lot or parcel outside of a manufactured home

subdivision or park must be situated in a manner consistent with the development standards for that zone and be designed to be consistent with the architectural character of the surrounding area.

This includes treatment of the stem wall to have the appearance of a conventional site-built home, with no skirting material permitted. Building materials must be the same or higher quality than surrounding site-built homes. Garages or carports must be consistent with the design of the main structure.

All manufactured homes shall be required to meet the current Housing and Urban

Development (HUD) Code standards, be certified under the National Manufactured Housing Construction and Safety Standards of 1974 and comply with state office of manufactured housing regulations.

A manufactured home shall not have been constructed more than 10 years prior to

176

Title 3: Zones

the date of application for building permit application.

C. Manufactured Home Subdivisions.

Recreational facilities, such as a playground or community building incidental to a

Manufactured Home, shall be permitted in manufactured home subdivisions as accessory uses.

No more than one manufactured home is permitted per lot in a manufactured home

subdivision.

D. Manufactured Home Park

The minimum area of a manufactured home park is three acres.

There may be no more than 10 manufactured home sites per acre of the manufactured

home park.

Manufactured homes and their accessory buildings (including storage, carports, and/or

awnings) must cover no more than 50 percent of the total manufactured home site. No accessory building may encroach on any required front, rear, or side setback for the manufactured home park.

Manufactured home parks must comply with the development standards in Title 4 for

parking, screening, landscaping, lighting.

Maps showing the location of all parking spaces, buildable areas, accessory buildings,

common buildings, and for all common open areas, must be made a part of the site plan approval.

The maximum length and width of a manufactured home must be shown on the map

for each manufactured home space.

RVs may be permitted by Conditional Use Permit within a manufactured home park

located in the R-MH-6 zone, provided all requirements set forth in the RV park usespecific standards are met for RV spaces.

RV sites may compromise no more than 40 percent of the total number of available

spaces when located within a manufactured home park. The locations of all RV spaces must be clearly identified on an approved site plan and RVs may not be permitted in spaces designated for manufactured homes.

E. Temporary Placement of Manufactured Homes or Recreational Vehicles.

Manufactured Homes and RVs may be used as temporary offices under the

following circumstances and with approval of a Temporary Use Permit:

a. As a construction office while a new building is under construction on the

same lot.

b. As a real estate sales office.

c. To operate a business by the owner or lessee during the time of construction of

a new building on the same commercial or industrial site.

The temporary manufactured home or RV must be removed from the site prior to the

issuance of a Certificate of Occupancy for the new building on the same lot.

Temporary placement of a manufactured home or RV may also be subject to the use specific standards for construction trailers. If used as real estate sales office, the model home complex use-specific standards are also applicable.

177

Title 3: Zones

3-80.430 Marijuana Uses

A. All marijuana uses permitted by the State of Arizona are allowed in the City subject to the

requirements included in this Section and in compliance with applicable state law.

B. The following standards apply to all marijuana uses.

The use must be located in a permanent building and is not allowed to be located

in a trailer, cargo container or motor vehicle;

The use must not be located within 500 feet of any other marijuana use. This distance

must be measured from the exterior walls of the building or portion thereof in which the business is conducted or proposed to be conducted;

The use must not be located within 500 feet of a residentially zoned property. This

distance must be measured in a straight line from the exterior walls of the building or portion thereof in which the business is conducted or proposed to be conducted to the zoning boundary line of the residentially zoned property;

The use must not be located within 2,500 feet of a preschool, kindergarten,

elementary, secondary, or high school, place of worship, public park, mental health facility, substance abuse rehabilitation center or public community center. This distance must be measured in a straight line from the exterior walls of the building or portion of the building in which the business is conducted or proposed to be conducted to the property line of the protected use;

Operating hours for all marijuana uses are limited to not earlier than 8:00 a.m. and not

later than 9:00 p.m. of the same day;

Drive-through services, off-site delivery, on-site consumption, and outdoor seating areas are

prohibited;

All marijuana uses shall display a current City business license applicable to the

specific marijuana use;

Marijuana remnants or by-products must be disposed of in accordance with State and

local regulations and must not to be placed within the marijuana uses’ exterior refuse containers;

No dust, fumes, vapors, or odors may be emitted into the environment;

All marijuana products must be stored in a secure area when the business is not open;

and,

A marijuana facility must have alarm and video surveillance systems as well as a

documented security plan showing compliance with this Code and state standards.

C. A marijuana dispensary and the cultivation of marijuana may only be established

together, subject to the following conditions and limitations:

The applicant shall provide a copy of the Application for a Dispensary

Registration Certificate to the Arizona Department of Health Services;

The applicant shall provide a copy of the operating procedures adopted in compliance with

A.R.S § 36-2804;

The dispensary and/or cultivator shall have a secure storage area that can be

enclosed and locked as required by law; and

Medical marijuana infusion facilities will only be permitted within a medical marijuana

178

Title 3: Zones

dispensary or at a medical marijuana offsite cultivation location.

3-80.440 Micro-Brewery or Micro-Distillery

Micro-breweries and micro-distilleries must meet the following development and performance standards:

A. Micro-breweries and micro-distilleries must comply with all applicable local and State regulations.

B. A taproom is permitted within the micro-brewery or micro-distillery where customers, for a fee, may

sample and consume the product without food service. The area of the taproom must be no more than 50 percent of the gross floor area of all structures on the premises used for a micro-brewery or micro-distillery.

3-80.450 Mixed-Use

A. Mixed-use developments foster pedestrian-oriented residential and commercial development by

providing more housing options, reducing traffic congestion, providing a stronger economy in commercial areas, and encouraging pedestrian trips. Mixed-use developments also have the potential to provide opportunities for affordable housing and facilitate infill development of vacant or underutilized parcels within already developed areas where infrastructure is already in place. In order to accomplish these goals, higher intensities of land use are permitted for mixed-use structures than for the individual uses permitted in a zone.

A mixed-use development combines residential and nonresidential uses, or different types of

nonresidential uses, on the same site with the residential units either located above the nonresidential uses (vertical mixed-use) or allowed at ground level behind street-fronting nonresidential uses (horizontal mixed-use) as shown in Figure 3-80.450.1.

Mixed-use developments must incorporate a minimum of two uses.

If any one of the uses of the mixed-use development requires the approval of a Conditional Use

Permit, then the development in its entirety will be subject to the Conditional Use Permit.

Uses must be compatible with development in the vicinity of the project site and incorporate

design features to minimize potential impacts on adjacent properties.

179

Title 3: Zones

Figure 3-80.450.1 Typical Mixed-Use Development Combinations

B. Design Considerations.

A mixed-use development must be designed to achieve the following objectives:

The design must provide for internal compatibility between the residential and

nonresidential uses on the site. An acoustical study prepared by a qualified acoustical or environmental engineer may be required by the Director to demonstrate that there would be no negative impacts on the residential component by the commercial component, particularly if residential and nonresidential uses are proposed to be located in a single building.

The design must take into consideration existing and potential future uses on

adjacent properties and must include specific design features to minimize potential impacts.

The design must ensure that the residential units are of a residential character, and

that appropriate privacy between residential units and other uses on the site is provided.

Site planning and building design must provide for convenient pedestrian access

from streets, courtyards, plazas, and walkways.

Site planning and building design must be compatible with and enhance the adjacent

and surrounding neighborhood in terms of building design, color, exterior materials, landscaping, lighting, roof styles, scale, and signage.

C. Site Layout and Development Design Standards.

Building coverage, building height, parking, landscaping, etc., standards of the

underlying zone will apply to provide a control on the bulk and mass of the development and the number of residential units permitted.

Pedestrian-Oriented Commercial Space is required to comply with the following:

a. Ground floor commercial space must have a customer entrance opening directly

onto the sidewalk.

b. Floor-to-ceiling height of the ground floor commercial space of minimum 14 feet. 3. Residential units must not occupy the ground floor street frontage space adjacent to a primary street in a vertically-integrated mixed-use building. The primary street is the street with the highest classification compared to other streets that front a lot or parcel, as indicated by the Department.

The use of shared parking provisions may be incorporated into mixed-use developments

in compliance with Division 4-80. 5. Commercial loading areas must be located away from residential units and screened

from view from the residential portion of the development to the maximum extent feasible, in compliance with Division 4-50.

Areas for the collection and storage of refuse and recyclable materials must be located

on the site in locations that are convenient for both the residential and nonresidential uses.

A mixed-use development must be designed to provide residential uses with common or

private usable open space, which may be in the form of roof gardens, individual balconies, or other means as approved by the Department.

180

Title 3: Zones

3-80.460 Model Home Complex

A. A site plan shall be required for all model home complexes and a Certificate of

Occupancy must be issued before the site can commence operation.

B. The model home complex shall be used only to market homes being built in the master

development in which it is located.

C. Model homes within a single-family development shall be converted into a residential

use once the last lot in the development has sold. This includes removing any temporary parking, sales offices, flag poles, lighting, trap fencing, signage, or similar improvements.

Model homes within a multi-family development or manufactured home park may be maintained indefinitely, provided the external residential character of the model is maintained.

D. Outdoor storage of construction materials at the model home complex is not permitted.

E. If a manufactured home or other temporary building is proposed to be used as a sales

office, they must comply with the use-specific standards for those uses in this Code. A Temporary Use Permit shall be required as described in Section 2-40.140.

3-80.470 Motocross and BMX Racetracks

A. A 100-foot setback must be provided from all nonresidential zones and a 500-foot setback

must be provided from all residential zones, measured from the racetrack to the boundary line. An earthen berm and landscaping may be located within the required setback.

B. Outdoor lighting is prohibited after 11:00 P.M.

C. A Traffic Impact Study and a Dust Abatement Plan detailing how dust and particulate

matter will be controlled for the racetrack and parking lot is required with the application for a Conditional Use Permit.

3-80.480 Outdoor Storage and Use Areas

A. Outdoor Dining/Seating.

Outdoor dining, patios, or seating areas must be associated with a permitted use.

For the purposes of state liquor license spacing regulations, these outdoor spaces shall be considered part of the premises.

Outdoor dining/seating shall not obstruct sidewalk accessibility.

Any decorative barrier element separating the outdoor dining/seating area from

adjacent pedestrian traffic shall be handicapped accessible and not exceed 40 inches in height. Such barriers shall be compatible to the architectural design of the primary building.

Decorative lighting is allowed but must comply with the lighting standards in

Division 4-70.

181

Title 3: Zones

B. Outdoor Display/Sales.

Outdoor display/sales areas shall be shown on the site plan. Display platforms

shall be a minimum of 30 feet from other display platforms and not exceed four feet in height.

The maximum display/sales area shall not exceed 10 percent of the total net lot

area and shall be a minimum of 10 feet from any property line.

Farm, construction, and other similar equipment shall be displayed in the down and

transportable position.

The outdoor display/sales area must only be used for merchandise sold on the

property within the business building.

Outdoor displays may not be located within any required landscaping or parking

areas and may not obstruct access to or within the site.

For merchandise that is of an appropriate size to be placed within a building (i.e.,

not large farm or construction equipment), the merchandise must be displayed only during business hours and brought inside at the close of each business day. Exceptions may be made for permanent outdoor display areas if shown on an approved site plan.

Within the DD and C-2 zones, the display area shall be limited to 25 percent of the

store frontage and located within 25 feet of the customer entrance.

Within the DD zone, businesses may display merchandise outside of an enclosed

building, including on the public sidewalk, subject to the following standards;

a. A Business Entertainment Zone (BEZ) Permit must be approved by the

Engineering Department;

b. All display merchandise must be placed only adjacent to the building frontage;

c. A minimum sidewalk width for free movement of pedestrians of five feet must

be maintained; and

d. Merchandise must only be displayed during business hours.

C. Outdoor Storage/Use.

Outdoor storage is not permitted within the required setbacks.

Outdoor storage must be screened from residential uses and any public right-of way.

3-80.490 Places of Assembly

A. Places of assembly must be scaled consistent with the form and character of the

surrounding neighborhood.

B. Places of assembly within residential zones must comply with the development standards

of the residential zone. This includes height, setback, and lighting requirements.

C. Places of assembly must be located a minimum of 50 feet from any adjacent residential use.

D. Places of assembly with 250 seats or more must have primary access from a main

thoroughfare.

E. Places of worship must also comply with the above standards.

182

Title 3: Zones

3-80.500 Recreational Vehicle (RV) Park

A. Purpose.

This Section establishes the regulations and standards for the efficient and orderly layout of recreational vehicle (RV) parks and to ensure their compatibility with surrounding uses and activities.

B. Permitted Uses.

The following uses are allowed within a RV park.

One recreational vehicle is permitted on each RV space. RVs include stationary,

permanently set and movable units.

One single-family residence or manufactured home for the manager’s office and

residence.

Recreation and social centers, and outdoor recreational facilities.

Laundry facilities, maintenance building, and/or facilities.

Boat and RV storage for visitors or residents of the site while occupying the site.

Boat and RV storage is subject to the accessory outdoor storage use-specific standards.

Campgrounds and freestanding tent sites up to a maximum of 15 percent of the

campground area.

A manufactured home park may be developed in conjunction with a RV park in a

C-2 or C-3 zone provided the manufactured home park meets all requirements set forth in the use-specific standards for manufactured home parks. The manufactured home park may not occupy more than 40 percent of the site area.

C. Accessory Structures.

The following structures are permitted when accessory to either an RV or manufactured home site as described below:

Recreational Vehicles: Covered carports, patio awnings and detached storage

areas.

Manufactured Homes: Covered carports, patio awnings, armadas, storage

buildings and room additions.

Recreational Facilities, laundry buildings, service retail store, manager’s office,

storage buildings, sanitary facilities, and fences must be constructed in compliance with the provisions of this Section and all other applicable City regulations.

D. Park Development Standards.

The minimum area for a RV park is two acres.

The maximum number of RVs is 15 RV spaces per acre of the park or the

proportional equivalent for areas less than one acre.

3. Park Spaces .

a. The minimum area for a RV space is 1,950 square feet with a minimum width

of 30 feet and a minimum depth of 65 feet.

183

Title 3: Zones

b. Manufactured homes will not be permitted on RV spaces which do not meet

the manufactured home space size requirements established in the usespecific standards for manufactured homes.

c. Campground areas within any RV park must provide a minimum of 500 square

feet for each tent site with a 10-foot separation between tent sites.

4. Setbacks and Separations .

a. Parking pads for RV spaces must be arranged to provide a minimum 10 - foot

separation between vehicles.

b. A minimum 10-foot minimum setback is required between the RV spaces and

the RV park’s perimeter.

5. Fences.

a. All RV parks are required to install opaque screening such as fences, walls, or

vegetative screening, a minimum of six feet in height along the park’s exterior property lines, in compliance with the requirements of Division 4-50.

6. Parking.

a. On-street parking is prohibited on any private drives, streets, or roads internal

to the RV park. Each RV parking space or automobile parking space must be a paved or graveled space.

b. A parking area must be provided at the office to allow parking during

registration.

7. Streets and Access.

a. All RV parks must be accessible from a public or private street.

b. Individual RV spaces must be accessible from an internal private drive with

each lot accessible from an approved private street or road. Internal roads within a RV park must have a minimum width of 20 feet and must be improved to minimum City standards (except width). No RV space is permitted to have direct access to a public street or way.

c. Entrances to RV parks must be designed to minimize congestion and traffic

hazards through designated driveways that allow the free movement of traffic on adjacent streets.

d. Dead end streets must provide access to no more than 12 spaces and must

be provided at the closed end with a turnaround having an outside roadway diameter of at least 60 feet.

e. A series of connected roads is required for all roads providing access to more

than 12 spaces.

f. Internal roads or private drives must be paved or graveled to minimize dust.

8. Sidewalks.

The Director may require the installation of sidewalks internal to RV parks on a caseby-case basis.

9. Service Buildings and Facilities.

a. All service buildings must be permanent buildings and constructed in

accordance with City requirements. Facilities in service buildings such as

184

Title 3: Zones

lavatories, toilets, showers, etc., must be in installed in accordance with County and State Health Department requirements.

b. All service buildings must be easily accessible to the RV spaces they serve

and be maintained in a clean and sanitary condition.

c. Where individual sewer connections are not provided at each space, sanitary

stations must be provided for the clean and efficient disposal of waste from all holding tanks and for refilling water storage tanks. Sanitary stations shall be approved by the Mohave County Health Department.

10. Recreational Areas .

a. Each RV space must be provided with water, electricity, and a picnic table,

open fires at RV spaces will be prohibited unless in an approved fire ring or barbecue in which any open fires must be contained.

b. One or more communal recreational areas must be centrally located, free of

traffic hazards, and easily accessible to all park residents. The area of these communal recreational areas, which must not include private roads, streets, or driveways, is based upon a minimum of 200 square feet per recreational vehicle space. The minimum size of a communal recreational area is 2,500 square feet.

c. Recreation areas or common open space must be provided at a ratio of 100

square feet per RV, manufactured home, or tent space. Picnic tables, fire rings or barbeques, and other recreational facilities may be located in communal open spaces in addition to on individual spaces.

3-80.510 Recycling Facilities

A. Recycling Collection Facilities.

Indoor recycling collection facilities must be located a minimum of 200 feet from

any residential zoning district. Outdoor recycling collection facilities must be located a minimum of 1,320 feet from any residential zoning district.

No outdoor storage shall be permitted at an indoor recycling collection facility.

At an outdoor recycling collection facility, storage is not permitted above the height

of the screen wall.

Processing operations are limited to cleaning, compacting, sorting, wire stripping,

shearing, and baling.

All areas of the facility open to vehicular traffic must be paved. B. Recycling Reverse Vending Machines.

Recycling reverse vending machines must be located a minimum of 200 feet from

any residential zoning district and 100 feet from any right-of-way access point.

Recycling reverse vending machines must not occupy more than 350 square feet

of space and must not be placed within any required setback, parking, or landscaping areas.

The name and telephone number of the recycling reverse vending machine owner

and a notice stating that no materials may be left outside the recycling reverse vending machine must be posted on the reverse vending machine in an easily identifiable location.

185

Title 3: Zones

3-80.520 Research, Development, and Testing Laboratories

A. A report from a qualified acoustical and/or environmental engineer may be required by the

Director if there are neighboring residential uses. This report shall document if there are no negative impacts or if there are mitigation measures that could be incorporated to reduce impacts.

B. If the laboratory uses flammable liquids or hazardous materials in quantities not

customary to a use for the zone in which the laboratory located, additional approval from the City Fire Department may be required.

C. Hazardous materials must be stored consistent with all state and federal requirements.

The Department may impose additional requirements if deemed necessary for the protection of the safety and welfare of the community.

3-80.530 Schools

A. K through Grade 12 Standards (Private).

School sites shall not have access from an arterial.

All building setbacks shall be a minimum of fifty feet.

Open space, including play areas, shall be a minimum of 25 percent.

A pick-up/drop-off area shall be designed to accommodate a minimum of five cars at a

time, and a separate school bus pick-up/drop-off area shall be designed to accommodate a minimum of three busses at a time.

Signage and special paving or other treatments shall be used for pedestrian pathways

that cross vehicular use areas, including the pick-up/drop-off areas.

Dumpsters shall be screened and located a minimum of 75 feet from any residential

property lines.

If parking is provided along a street frontage, a minimum 3-foot-high landscaped berm

or fence/wall shall be provided where the parking occurs.

Security fencing of the campus and outdoor play areas shall be view fencing and allow

for emergency vehicle access.

Off-site improvements may be required to accommodate peak traffic volumes.

B. Microschools

1.. Micro-schools are limited to 20 students per day.

Shall meet the prescribed parking requirements per Table 4-80.040 A

Shall adhere to the requirements of the International Building and Fire Codes prior to

operation.

C. College, Universities, and Trade School Standards.

School sites shall provide adequate on-site parking facilities to adequately serve

student needs without impacting surrounding neighborhoods or uses.

Off-site improvements may be required to accommodate peak traffic volumes.

Special paving or other methods shall be used for pedestrian pathways that cross

186

Title 3: Zones

vehicular use areas

Dumpsters shall be screened and located a minimum of 75 feet from any residential

property lines.

3-80.540 Self-Storage Facility

Self-storage facilities must meet the following development and performance standards:

A. No business shall be operated out of a storage unit or outdoor storage area. All storage

must be within an enclosed building, except for accessory outdoor storage shown on an approved site plan and designed in accordance with the use-specific standards for outdoor storage and all applicable requirements of this Code. The front office area may include accessory retail sales related to the primary business.

B. The maximum area of an individual storage unit is limited to no more than 1,000 square feet.

C. The storage of refuse, compost, explosives, flammable materials, and other

noxious or dangerous materials, as determined by the Fire Marshal, is prohibited.

D. The minimum vehicle drive aisle width must be no less than 21 feet.

E. Minimum fire lane width must comply with the Fire Code.

F. Outdoor boat, trailer, or RV storage is limited to 10% of the overall site and must be

located in the interior of the site and screened from view of surrounding properties and right-of-way as described in Division 4-50.

G. Indoor boat, trailer, or RV storage may occupy up to 100 percent of a building. Outdoor

storage at such a site would still be limited to 10 percent of the overall site and must be screened from view of surrounding properties and right-of-way as described in Division 4-50.

H. All buildings that are visible from a public street shall feature architectural enhancements

such as variation of material types, colors, or rooflines consistent with the character of surrounding uses as required by Division 4-20. Architectural plans must be submitted to the Department for review.

3-80.550 Sexually Oriented Businesses

A. Location Requirements for Sexually Oriented Businesses.

Sexually oriented businesses are subject to the following location

requirements:

a. No sexually oriented business shall be operated or maintained within 1,000

feet of another sexually oriented business; and

b. No sexually oriented business shall be operated or maintained within 1,320 feet

of a boundary of a residential zone; the property line of a lot devoted to a residential use in any zone; a child care facility; a private, public, or charter school; a preschool, nursery, kindergarten, or similar use; a public playground; a public or private recreational facility; a religious institution; a library; a public park; a public or private community building; a teen dance center; an amusement park; or a game center.

The distance limitations must be measured as the shortest line between the property

187

Title 3: Zones

lines of the relevant properties involved.

Any sexually oriented business that fails to comply with this Section but which was

lawfully operating before the Effective Date of this Code or the revision to this Code that created the nonconformity, will not be deemed to be in violation of this Section and is subject to the nonconforming use requirements described in Division 2-60.

A sexually oriented business lawfully operating in conformity with this Section does

not violate this Section if another location-restricted use listed in this Section subsequently locates within 1,000 feet of the existing sexually oriented business.

A sexually oriented business lawfully operating is not rendered in violation of these

provisions by the subsequent rezoning of land to a residential zone or if one of the location-restricting uses described in this Section subsequently locates within the 1,320-foot-boundary.

B. Miscellaneous Provisions for Sexually Oriented Businesses.

These provisions shall not be construed as permitting any use or act that is

otherwise prohibited or made punishable by law.

The maintenance of two or more sexually oriented businesses in a single building that

are not at least 51 percent owned by the same entity shall be treated as two separate sexually oriented businesses for purposes of applying the location provisions described above. 3. All sexually oriented businesses must meet the location criteria prescribed in this Section.

The Board of Adjustment shall not have the jurisdiction to grant variances from the location standards. 4. Signage and on-site displays may not depict specific anatomical areas or specific sexual

activities in a manner that would be visible to the public from adjacent streets, sidewalks, or walkways. 5. An applicant must provide a map showing the property proposed for the sexually oriented

business and the adjacent properties, land uses, and buildings within a radius of 1,320 feet of the exterior boundary of the property in question shall be submitted with the Conditional Use Permit application.

3-80.560 Solar Energy Systems

A. General.

If a solar energy array tracks the sun’s movement across the sky, the height shall be

measured at the array’s uppermost vertical position.

If the solar energy system remains inoperative or non-functional for a continuous

period of six consecutive months, the system shall be deemed to be abandoned and shall constitute a public nuisance. Owners shall remove abandoned systems at their expense after a demolition permit has been obtained.

The owner of a property with a solar energy system assumes all risk associated with

diminished performance of the system caused by any present or future adjacent development that may interfere with the system’s ability to produce power at its rated capacity.

188

Title 3: Zones

All roof mounted solar energy systems solar panels must have a non-reflective

coating to minimize glare and must be arranged on the site to minimize glare impacts to adjacent properties.

B. Roof Mounted Solar Energy Systems.

Roof mounted solar energy systems are allowed by right in all zones subject to

compliance with the standards in this Section . [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

Within the Kingman Historic Overlay District roof mounted solar energy systems

require approval of a Conditional Use Permit and a Certificate of Appropriateness.

Roof mounted solar energy systems are excluded from the determination of building

height.

Location.

a. No portion of any roof-mounted solar energy system may extend below the roof

line or above the highest point of the roof. This includes all wires, pipes, and other associated appurtenances related to the solar energy system.

b. All solar panels shall be aligned within the existing roof edges. [Ord. 1971,

11/19/2024]

C. Ground Mounted Solar Energy Systems.

Ground mounted solar energy systems are allowed by right in all zones subject to compliance with the standards in this Section:

Location.

Ground mounted solar energy systems must only be placed in the rear or side yard.

Setbacks.

All ground mounted solar energy systems must comply with the setbacks for an accessory building or structure in the underlying zone.

Height.

The maximum height measured to the top of the solar energy system or supporting structures must not exceed eight feet.

Screening.

Appropriate landscaping and/or fencing shall be installed for safety purposes and to screen ground mounted solar systems from public view, in compliance with Division 4-50 and Division 4-60. The minimum fence height shall be four feet but must be of adequate height to shield the system from public view.

Ground mounted solar energy systems must be appropriately labeled and secured from unauthorized access.

These provisions may be modified by the Director if strict compliance would result in reduced energy production as established by evidence provided by the applicant. [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

Maintenance.

The ground area under solar energy system installations must be maintained free of weeds and litter.

3-80.570 Sport Courts

189

Title 3: Zones

A. In residential zones, sport courts and associated facilities shall not be permitted in the front yard

and must be within the height limitations for the underlying zoning district.

B. Sport courts without lighting shall be set back a minimum of 5 feet from the edge of the playing

surface to all lot lines.

C. Protective fencing or netting may be allowed along a residential property line that abuts a ballfield,

sport courts, or golf course.

3-80.580 Swimming Pools and Spas

The following setbacks apply to pools and spas:

A. In single-family and manufactured home residential zones, the water’s edge of an

above-ground or in-ground swimming pool or spa may not be located in the front yard and must not be closer than four feet to any property line.

B. In all other zones, swimming pools and spas must be setback from a property line in

accordance with the setback standards for the zone but must not be closer than four feet to a rear property line.

C. It shall be the responsibility of the property owner and occupant of the property to install

and maintain the fences, latches, and gates in proper working condition.

3-80.590 Tattoo Parlor and Body Piercing

A. Tattoo and/or body piercing establishments shall maintain the following minimum separations,

measured in a straight line in any direction:

1,000 feet from the lot line of another tattoo and/or body piercing establishment;

500 feet from the lot line of a residential district or residential use; and,

500 feet from the lot line of a K-12 school.

B. An existing tattoo and/or body piercing establishment lawfully operating as a conforming use shall

not be rendered a nonconforming use by the subsequent location of any of the uses listed above. If the establishment is abandoned or business license is not renewed, then it would become nonconforming.

C. Permanent facial cosmetics services (such as permanent makeup services and similar) shall be

exempted from this Section.

3-80.600 Tiny Homes

A. A Tiny Home shall not have a floor area larger than 860 square feet, excluding lofts.

The minimum floor area of a Tiny Home shall not be smaller than 300 square feet.

[Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

B. Tiny Homes may be used as ADUs or in Cottage Court housing developments subject to

the use-specific standards for these uses described in this Division.

190

Title 3: Zones

3-80.610 Truck Service Station and Convenience Store (Truck Stop, Truck Wash)

A. A truck stop or travel plaza must be located no more than 500 feet from an interstate

or state highway interchange right-of-way and minimum lot size shall be 20 acres.

B. A sound attenuation wall, a minimum of six feet high, shall be installed along all

property lines that abut a residential zoning district or use.

C. Any fuel dispenser pumps shall be a minimum of 100 feet from any residential zoning

district and at least 40 feet from any property line or public right-of-way line.

D. Amenities such as shower and restroom facilities, laundry facilities, truck wash

facilities, and restaurants shall be allowed as accessory uses.

3-80.620 Wireless Communication Facilities

A. Purpose and Intent.

The purpose of this Section is to provide standards for the placement and design of

wireless communications facilities to preserve the unique visual character of the City, promote the aesthetic appearance of the community, and ensure public health, safety, and welfare.

The intent of this Section is to:

a. Encourage the joint use of new and existing wireless communication facilities

rather than construction of additional single-use towers;

b. Encourage users of wireless communications facilities to locate towers and

associated facilities in areas where the impact on the community is minimal;

c. Encourage the configuration of wireless communications facilities in a way

that minimizes adverse visual impact through careful design, use of stealth facilities, appropriate siting, screening, and other innovative techniques;

d. Enhance the ability of the providers of wireless communications services to

provide services to the community quickly, effectively, and efficiently; and

e. Regulate wireless communications facilities in a manner that is compliant with

federal, state, and local laws.

B. Applicability.

This Section applies to all wireless communications facilities, including small cell facilities located outside of the public right-of-way. Facilities located in the public right-ofway, including 5G facilities, must be consistent with the standards and requirements established in the City of Kingman Uniform Standards for Public Works Construction.

C. Application Requirements.

All new wireless communications facilities may be permitted by right, subject to site

plan review, if the facility complies with the development requirements of the zone in which it is proposed to be located. A Conditional Use Permit shall be required if the facility does not comply with the development standards of the zone in which it is proposed to be located.

In addition to the standard information required for site plan or Conditional Use Permit

review, and application for a new wireless communication facility shall include the additional following information:

191

Title 3: Zones

a. A copy of a title report or other legal instrument(s) demonstrating legal

access to the proposed site for the purpose of facility construction and maintenance.

b. Exact transmitting frequencies, transmitter output power, effective radiated

power, and duty cycle information.

c. Information demonstrating constraints related to coverage area, existing poles

and capacity, or other considerations when co-location is not possible.

d. Plans must show a minimum of two additional sites on each tower to allow for

future co-location of additional antenna arrays.

An eligible facility request for a modification of an existing wireless communication

tower or base station, involving collocation of new transmission equipment, removal of transmission equipment, or replacement of transmission equipment on a stealth communication facility, co-location facility that does not include a substantial change to the physical dimensions of the tower or base station may be approved by the Director.

Applicants for an eligible facility request are only required to provide documentation

that is reasonably related to determining whether the request is consistent with federal requirements. Substantial changes are defined in the following Section.

Proposed expansions or modifications of a previously approved wireless

communications facility, including additional users, antenna configuration, or change in radio frequencies, require Conditional Use Permit approval unless one of the following determinations is made by the Director. If one of the following determinations is made, then the application requires a site plan review and approval:

a. The proposed expansion or modification is an eligible facility request that does

not include a substantial change;

b. The proposed expansion or modification would not alter the visual appearance of

the facility (e.g., new stealth wireless facilities installed entirely within an existing structure so that no exterior modifications to the existing structure or facility are readily visible);

c. The proposed expansion or modification is to co-locate a new facility on an

existing, approved wireless communication tower, with approved co-location sites and the proposal complies with this Section and the previously approved colocation site; or

d. The proposed expansion or modification is to install a replacement facility which

is consistent with the approval for the facility being replaced and meets the requirements of this Section.

Permit applications for wireless communications facilities must be reviewed and acted

upon in accordance with legally mandated review time periods.

D. Substantial Change.

As described in federal law, a substantial change to a wireless communication facility includes any one of the following:

An increase in the height of a wireless communication facility tower of more than 20

feet or 10 percent whichever is greater;

An increase in the height of a wireless communication facility base station of more

than 10 feet or 10 percent, whichever is greater;

A protrusion from the edge of the wireless communication tower that is greater than

192

Title 3: Zones

20 feet, or more than the width of the tower structure at the level of the antennae, whichever is greater;

A protrusion from the edge of a wireless communication facility base station of more

than six feet;

The installation of more than the standard number of new equipment cabinets for

the technology involved, to a maximum of four cabinets;

The excavation or deployment of equipment outside the current site of the

wireless communication tower or base station;

The change would defeat the existing concealment elements of the wireless

communication tower or base station; or

The change would not comply with conditions associated with the prior approval of

the wireless communication tower or base station unless the non-compliance is due to an increase in height, increase in width, addition of cabinets, or the new excavation that does not exceed the “substantial change” thresholds listed above.

E. Site Selection.

All requests for new installations must demonstrate that there is no available space on existing towers or other suitable support structure within the established service area. Sites for wireless communications facilities, including 5G facilities, must be selected according to the following order of preference:

Stealth communication facilities, such as wireless communication facilities

installed within existing structures (e.g., church steeple, roof top stairwell or equipment enclosures, etc.).

Co-location facilities (i.e., locating equipment from more than one provider on

a single facility).

In locations where existing topography, vegetation, or other structures provide the

greatest amount of screening.

On lots which will not require significant visual mitigation as determined by the

Department.

F. General Design Requirements

1. Setback.

a. The minimum required setback for all parts of the wireless communication

facility, i.e. the wireless communication tower and all ground-mounted equipment, must comply with the minimum setback requirement established for the zone in which the wireless communication facility is located.

b. The setback requirement is not applicable when the proposed facility is

constructed on or in an existing structure and does not extend more than 20 feet above the existing structure.

2. Maximum Height.

The maximum permitted height for Wireless Communication Facilities by zone is established in the following table:

193
