3. Lot Area.

The lot or parcel on which the wireless communication facility is located must comply with applicable lot or parcel area requirements for the zone in which the wireless communication facility is located.

4. Monopole Construction.

Preference will be given to monopole wireless communication towers, especially when located in residential zones.

5. Screening.

a. To the greatest extent feasible, wireless communication facilities must be

sited and constructed in a manner that preserves the preexisting character of surrounding buildings and the existing vegetation on the site.

b. All wireless communication facilities must be painted a neutral nonreflective

color or be painted and/or textured to match adjoining structures or vegetation so as to reduce visual obtrusiveness, unless otherwise required by the Federal Aviation Administration, Federal Communications Commission (FCC), or other competent state or federal regulatory agency.

c. To the maximum extent feasible, wireless communication facilities must be

placed directly above, below or incorporated with vertical design elements of a building or structure.

d. An equipment shelter or cabinet that supports wireless communication facilities

must be concealed from public view in compliance with Division 4-50, or to the greatest extent feasible, designed to be compatible with the architecture of surrounding structures.

194

Title 3: Zones

e. The Planning Commission may require landscape or other materials to screen

the base of a wireless communication tower and ground-mounted equipment if determined necessary based on the facility’s location and surrounding context area.

6. Lighting.

No lighting of wireless communication facilities is allowed unless required by the FAA, FCC, or another federal or state agency of competent jurisdiction. All lighting must comply with the lighting standards established in Division 4-70, unless in conflict with requirements of the FAA, FCC, or other federal or state agency of competent jurisdiction.

7. Signs.

No advertising or signs are permitted on any wireless communication facility.

8. Parking and Access.

a. A wireless communication facility must include sufficient room for maintenance

vehicles and other equipment used for maintenance to maneuver on the property.

b. Vehicles nor equipment not used for maintenance must not be stored or parked

on the property.

c. A gravel access drive and parking area for service vehicles is required.

9. Electromagnetic Interference.

Emissions standards for electromagnetic fields must comply with FCC standards.

10. Collocation.

All new wireless communication facilities must be constructed to be capable of accommodating at least one additional wireless communication installation for another service provider.

11. Removal.

When a wireless communication facility is abandoned or becomes obsolescent, the applicant or any subsequent owner of the facility shall remove the facility within six months of obsolescence or abandonment and return the site to its preexisting condition. Obsolescence is defined as being replaced by new technology. Abandonment would occur when the provider is no longer operating a viable telecommunication network using this facility for a period of six consecutive months.

G. Sale, Transfer or Assignment of a Wireless Communication Facility.

No sale, transfer, or assignment of a wireless communication facility by an

owner/operator of such facility shall take place, whether by forced or voluntary sale, lease or assignment, name change, or merger, without prior written notice to the City. The written notice of such sale, transfer, name change, merger or assignment must include full identifying particulars of the proposed transaction and must include the name, address and telephone number of the transferee, purchaser, or assignees.

This Section does not apply to a sale, transfer, or assignment to any existing affiliate

of the FCC Licensee, owner/operator of such facilities, or to any mortgage, hypothecation, or financing of the assets of the FCC Licensee or owner/operator of

195

such facilities.

H. Federal, State, and Local Laws.

All wireless communications facilities, including those not requiring a planning permit, must comply with all applicable requirements of federal, State, and local laws.

I. Exclusions.

The following are exempt from this Section:

Any tower and antenna under 60 feet in total height above the ground that is

owned and operated by an amateur radio operator licensed by the Federal Communications Commission; and

Any receive-only consumer device designed for over-the-air reception of

television broadcast signals, multi-channel multi-point distribution service, or direct broadcast satellite service.

Title 3: Zones

196

Title 4: Supplemental Standards
Title 4: Supplemental Standards
4-10 Purpose

Sections:

4-10.010 Purpose

4-10.010 Purpose

This Title provides standards that are supplemental to the regulations of each zone, and are specific to particular aspects of development, such as landscaping, parking, outdoor lighting, signage, and others. These standards are intended to promote development that compliments the unique character of Kingman while preserving and enhancing the City's natural resources and environments.

Remainder of page intentionally blank.

197

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

4-20 Architectural Design Standards

Sections:

4-20.010 Purpose

4-20.020 Applicability

4-20.030 General Site Planning and Architectural Design Standards

4-20.040 Kingman Design Review Overlay (KDRO)

4-20.010 Purpose

The purpose of this Division is to provide general site planning and architectural design standards to protect and enhance the character of Kingman’s neighborhoods as well as its surrounding natural environment.

4-20.020 Applicability

A. The general site planning and architectural design standards in this Division apply to all properties

within the City, including those within the Kingman Design Review Overlay (KDRO) and Planned Development Zones (PDZ). Where conflict exists between the KDRO or a PDZ and the general standards, the standards in the KDRO or PDZ shall take precedence.

B. The KDRO standards in Section 4-20.040 apply to properties within the KDRO areas along Bank

Street and within the Hualapai Mountain Road Area shown on the Kingman Design Review Overlay Map (Figures 3-70.030.A and 3-70.030.B).

C. This Division establishes minimum standards. The City encourages developers and landowners to

exceed these minimums whenever possible.

4-20.030 General Site Planning and Architectural Design Standards

The standards established below will further enhance Kingman’s unique local architecture and natural environment.

A. Architectural Standards.

All buildings and structures must be finished consistent with the below requirements. The Director may approve the use of other materials provided they satisfy the intent of this Division and are appropriate based on the context of the project:

All building facades must consist predominately of the following materials:

a. Modular masonry materials such as brick, block, and stone;

b. Precast concrete or aggregate panels with a decorative finish;

c. Stucco or stucco-like materials;

d. Metal, subject to the below standards; and

e. Wood or vinyl siding.

The following materials may be used as a secondary or accent material consisting of no more than

25 percent of the area of the walls on the exterior of a building:

a. Metal, subject to the below standards;

198

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

b. Glass, except on the first story or for a minimum four-story building where glass is permitted

to make up to 75 percent of the building exterior;

c. Plastic; and

d. Highly reflective materials.

Buildings constructed of metal may be used in all zones, subject to the following development

standards. All metal buildings must include design features that break up long, plain walls to enhance the aesthetic appeal and maintain consistency with other development in the area.

a. All Zones.

i. For primary residential, commercial, or industrial structures, a minimum of 25 percent of the metal building’s façade must be a material other than metal or be designed to give the appearance of another material for all elevations.

ii. For primary residential, commercial, or industrial structures, the first four feet above

grade of a metal building or structure shall be completed with a decorative finish such as stucco, brick, wood-like paneling, or decorative metal paneling featuring similar architectural finishes.

iii. A metal building used as an accessory structure must be designed and painted in a

manner that is complimentary to the primary building or structure on the site. This includes, but is not necessarily limited to, architectural features, rooflines, scale, and paint colors.

iv. Quonset hut, bunker, or half-round metal buildings are prohibited.

b. Industrial and Recreational Open Space Zones.

i. Metal buildings and/or structures may not be constructed with exterior walls made of unpainted or untreated metal surfaces, including corrugated sheet metal, except that untreated copper or Corten steel is permitted.

ii. If a site has existing metal buildings that were installed prior to adoption of this Code and

have been in continuous use, then any additional metal buildings may match the existing metal buildings on the site.

c. Residential Zones.

i. A metal building used as an accessory structure located on an interior lot and to the rear of the primary structure shall be painted to match the primary structure.

ii. A metal building used as an accessory structure located on a corner lot or an interior lot

and situated forward of the rear building elevation of the primary structure or visible from the street side yard shall be designed to be architecturally compatible with the primary structure (i.e., shall match the exterior elevations of the primary structure, roof pitch, color, and scale).

iii. Windows and doors shall be residential in appearance, proportionally sized, and

symmetrically arranged. Large roll-up doors shall be located on the side or rear of the building. Forward or street-side yard facing garage doors shall be architecturally compatible with the primary dwelling.

Large, blank, featureless building façades or roofs are not allowed on any buildings, regardless of

construction material used. Instead, building elevations must incorporate architectural details to provide interest and vibrancy. Vertical and horizontal relief is required.

a. Relief may include:

i. Changes in wall depth or height,

199

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

ii. Use of different materials or textures, or

iii. Projections, recesses, windows, columns, trim, or other similar detailing.

All exterior elevations shall feature architecture detailing to provide a finished appearance on all

sides of a building or structure.

Simply and proportionally designed stairway elements, if present, are required to compliment the

architectural massing and form of the building. Unenclosed, open tread metal or prefabricated stairs are prohibited.

Breezeways must be designed to incorporate similar roof lines, building materials, and colors as

the primary structure. Breezeways must not span a distance greater than 15 feet.

B. Site Layout Standards.

A system of well-designed and safe pedestrian walkways must be incorporated to link the various

components of a development and adjacent public or private pedestrian walkways. In addition, walkways shall be provided to connect dwellings with adjacent land uses including parks, shopping centers, or schools.

These walkways shall minimize auto/pedestrian conflicts and maximize convenient pedestrian access.

Parking in multi-family residential and nonresidential developments must be provided to the rear or

side of buildings to the greatest extent possible. All parking areas must be appropriately screened as described in Division 4-50 and laid out according to the standards described in Division 4-80.

Buildings must be positioned to enhance significant natural features that exist on a site. The

features can be enhanced by:

a. Locating entry plaza or open spaces to frame a view, such as an existing rock outcropping,

mountain, or stand of mature trees; and

b. Siting buildings to minimize the destruction of natural features allowing sites to take

advantage of natural onsite resources.

C. Circulation Standards.

The primary vehicle access into a multi-family residential project must be through a dedicated

entry drive rather than through a parking lot drive.

Upon the determination of the City Engineer, a Traffic Impact Analysis may be required for new

developments to determine access needs, traffic control needs, drainage management plan, and/or a cost sharing plan. The City of Kingman Streets and Sidewalks Development Rules and Regulations establishes the standards for specific analysis thresholds.

Access points along primary gateway roadways shall be placed and designed in accordance with

ADOT and/or City requirements as applicable. The Department and City of Kingman Public Works Department maintain lists of such roadways.

Developments and streets within the City must be designed to encourage walking and biking as

alternative modes of transportation where applicable.

D. Utility Standards.

All on-site electric utility and all other communication and utility lines for buildings must be placed

underground as regulated by Section 9-5 of the Kingman Municipal Code.

Development on lots and slopes that have good solar access are encouraged to consider solar

power or solar water heating as alternative energy solutions.

200

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

E. Residential Subdivision Design Standards.

This Subsection allows flexibility in the application of front and rear setbacks for new residential

subdivisions to provide opportunities for variety in the placement of residential buildings to create more diverse and interesting streetscapes. This can be accomplished through application of the following standards:

Frontage types such as stoops, porches, etc. (Refer to Division 4-100 (Specific to Private

Frontages)) can encroach into front setback areas to the full extent of the depth of the frontage type.

The front setback for residences in single-family residential zones (except the RR Zone) may be

reduced by a maximum of five feet on alternate houses on a block provided by the rear yard is increased by an equal amount. This more flexible setback standard only applies to the primary residence and all garages and carports must maintain a minimum 20-foot setback from the front property line.

4-20.040 Kingman Design Review Overlay (KDRO)

A. Purpose.

The City encourages buildings within the KDRO to be designed within a broad range of Southwestern architectural styles and requires high quality and creative developments that are architecturally compatible with surrounding buildings, colors, and textures of the surrounding environment.

Properties within the KDRO must comply with the standards of this Code and the additional standards for the KDRO. In the event of a conflict between the KDRO and the Code, the KDRO supersedes for properties within the overlay zone. Copies of the KDRO are maintained by the Department and available upon request.

B. Circulation.

Projects must be designed to minimize traffic conflicts and congestion.

Connectivity between developments must be maximized in order to enhance walkability and

bikeability.

Street layout must respond to topography. Curvilinear streets are generally discouraged, but may

be allowed on a case-by-case basis to minimize potential impacts to topography.

For access onto major and minor arterials and collector streets, curb cuts must be in compliance

with the City of Kingman Streets and Sidewalks Development Rules and Regulations.

Access to roads and collector streets must be limited to street intersections within the KDRO.

Where a corner lot abuts Hualapai Mountain Road, Mission Boulevard, or Bank Street and a collector or local side street, driveway access shall be permitted only on the non-arterial side street . as follows:

a. One driveway is permitted when the frontage is less than 300 feet.

b. Two driveways are permitted when the frontage is 300 to 600 feet.

c. Three driveways are permitted when the frontage is greater than 600 feet.

Driveways.

a. The use of shared driveways between adjacent parcels will be required when appropriate

and is encouraged whenever possible.

b. Adjacent driveways must be no closer to each other than 60 feet unless there is no

alternative access available.

201

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

c. A main driveway into a site must be designed to have adequate space for stacking of

vehicles.

If over 40 parking spaces are required by the use, a minimum of 50 percent of the required parking

spaces must be located to the side or rear of the proposed building to the greatest extent possible.

C. Architectural Standards.

The design of a new development project must be compatible with the character of the

surrounding built and natural environment in terms of building orientation, architectural character, landscaping, and natural site design.

Disturbance of the natural environment must be minimized. Projects must be designed to retain

natural site topography to the greatest extent possible and fit the natural existing conditions rather than significantly altering a site to accommodate a project. Excessive cuts and fills are to be avoided.

Buildings must be compatible with the colors and textures of the surrounding environment.

Appropriate primary building or structure colors include, but are not limited to, warm earth tone colors, rustic reds, green earth tones, tans, and browns as determined by the Director.

Long blank walls are prohibited. Maximum uninterrupted wall length must not exceed 20 feet

without an architectural feature breaking up the expanse.

Architectural features may include columns with contrasting but complimentary colors, pilasters,

tile contrasts, varied facades, window design, or parapets.

Parapets must be varied in relief on elevations and not designed as one continuous line and

height.

Parapet caps must include contrasting complimentary color(s) from the prime color of the building.

Entryways shall not be flush with the building wall, but must be recessed at least one foot or shall

be covered with human-scale overhangs or trellises.

D. Building Materials.

Exposed metal strips are prohibited unless integral to the architectural design. The exterior

building façade, including the trim and roof fascia, must consist of wood, stucco, horizontal siding, brick, masonry, veneer, adobe, or other facsimile of a building material commonly used in construction.

All four building elevations must be designed and finished to the same degree.

Highly reflective materials that create glare are prohibited.

If the roof material is visible to adjacent properties or rights-of-way, the materials used must

consist of asphalt, composition wood shingles, concrete, fiberglass, corrugated metal limited to copper or custom metal which mimics another material, ceramic tiles, or slate or built-up gravel materials.

E. Signs.

The standards listed below are in addition to the standards within Division 4-90 (Signs).

Signs must compliment and reflect the architectural theme of the principal buildings.

Billboards, roof, and pole signs are prohibited.

Only one monument-style freestanding sign is allowed per property.

Monument signs are allowed up to six feet in height, with a total sign area not to exceed 35 square

feet.

202

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

F. Landscaping.

The standards listed below are in addition to the standards within Division 4-60.

Existing natural features, including rock outcrops and water courses, must be incorporated into the

landscape design of a project whenever possible.

Pedestrian circulation areas and courtyards must be designed to provide as much shade as

possible.

Sidewalks, street trees, and streetscape landscaping to be irrigated and maintained by the

adjacent property owner, are required for each development. Landscaping in rights-of-way shall comply with the landscaping and plant location requirements found in the Streets and Sidewalks Development Rules and Regulations.

G. Screening.

The standards listed below are in addition to the standards within Division 4-50.

Service features, such as electric or telephone boxes, irrigation boxes, any exposed

communication equipment, or similar equipment shall be shown on the site plan and screened or designed with compatible colors and materials. Residential zones are exempted from this Section.

Screening materials may include dense vegetation and/or opaque walls or fences that are

architecturally compatible with principal buildings.

Developments in all commercial and multi-family residential zones must be screened from

adjoining single-family residential zones, even if a street or alley is located between the different zones. Split face block, stucco, compatible wrought iron fences, or architecturally compatible wood or similar materials may be used with landscaping to provide an effective screen

All utility stations and substations must be screened with landscaping and/or a wall or opaque

fence.

All utilities must be placed underground, subject to the standards in Section 9-5 (Underground

Utilities) of the City of Kingman Municipal Code.

Remainder of page intentionally blank.

203

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

4-30 Building Height

Sections:

4-30.010 Purpose

4-30.020 Applicability

4-30.030 How Building Height is Measured

4-30.010 Purpose

This Division establishes the standards for determining how building height is measured. These standards must be used in combination with the building height standards established in Title 3 (Zones).

4-30.020 Applicability

The standards found within this Division apply to all development within the City.

4-30.030 How Building Height is Measured

A. Overall Building Height.

Overall building height is measured vertically from the natural grade of the building’s exterior to the

height of the highest roofline or parapet required to screen any rooftop equipment.

The following building elements are excluded from the height measurement:

a. Chimneys;

b. Spires; and,

c. Church Steeples.

The following building elements may exceed the maximum height requirements of a zone by no

more than 10 feet:

a. Stair and elevator towers/penthouses;

b. Tanks; and,

c. Solar collectors.

B. Crawl Spaces.

Unfinished crawl spaces shall be included in the height determination.

Unfinished crawl spaces taller than three feet shall be screened with landscaping from view of public

streets and adjacent properties.

Unfinished crawl spaces shall not exceed five feet in height measured from the exterior finished

grade to the finished floor of the floor above.

C. Basements/Basement Garages.

Exterior walls of basements or basement garages visible from a street or adjacent property shall not exceed nine feet in height measured from the exterior finished grade to the finished floor of the floor above and must be screened with landscaping from view of the street or adjacent properties.

D. Flagpoles.

204

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

The height of flagpoles shall be limited to the allowable building height of the zone in which the flagpole is located as described in Title 3 (Zones).

4-40 Encroachments

Sections:

4-40.010 Purpose and Applicability

4-40.020 Items Allowed and Not Allowed within Setback Areas

4-40.030 Encroachments into Required Setbacks

4-40.010 Purpose and Applicability

This Division provides standards for the allowed encroachment or projection of certain structures into required setback areas.

4-40.020 Items Allowed and Not Allowed within Setback Areas

A. The following are items that may be permitted within setback areas:

Driveways;

Landscaping, including trails or paths;

Outdoor dining, where allowed; and,

Utility meters and similar equipment.

B. Structures, parking spaces, or drive aisles are not permitted within the required building or landscape

setback areas.

4-40.030 Encroachments into Required Setbacks

The following sections describe limited circumstances where encroachment into the setbacks may be allowed. Additional encroachments may be permitted on a case-by-case basis pursuant to the Minor Modifications to Development Standards process described in Title 2 of this Code. Table 4-40.030.A (Allowed Encroachments into Setbacks) establishes elements that may encroach into required setbacks.

A. Access Ramps for Disabled Persons.

All access ramps for disabled persons may encroach into a required setback, provided they are not more than 36 inches in height and remain five feet from the front property line, two feet from the side property line, and 10 feet from the rear property line.

B. Rain Barrels and Cisterns.

Rain barrels and cisterns used for rainwater harvesting, or any above or belowground storage containers with no greater capacity than 100 gallons, are allowed to encroach into side and rear setbacks to the property line, and up to five feet into a front setback.

C. Patio Covers.

In all single-family residential zones, except the RR Zone, patio covers constructed with noncombustible materials may extend into the front and rear setbacks as follows:

Extend into a front yard setback for no more than three feet as measured from the support posts or

columns; and [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

205

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

Extend into a rear yard or interior side yard setback no more than five feet as measured from the

support posts or columns. [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

The patio cover overhang shall not be closer than two and a half feet to any property line, except

when a property abuts a parcel dedicated to the public for a park, golf course, open space, utilities, or drainageway in which case the patio cover overhang may extend no closer than four feet from the rear property line.

These standards also apply to awnings, canopies, decks, porches, stoops, carports, and

balconies provided they do not encroach into front yard setbacks, unless otherwise permitted by this Code.

Remainder of page intentionally blank.

206

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

207

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

4-50 Fences, Walls, and Screening

Sections:

4-50.010 Purpose

4-50.020 Applicability

4-50.030 Permit Required

4-50.040 Siting and Height Standards

4-50.050 General Fence and Screening Standards

4-50.010 Purpose

This Division provides standards for the allowed types and dimensional requirements of fences, walls, and screening. The standards regulate walls, fences, retaining walls, and vertical combinations of those items in order to:

Enhance the visual appearance of the built environment in the City;

Establish an attractive streetscape;

Ensure visual compatibility with public spaces; and

Promote street and neighborhood character.

4-50.020 Applicability

The requirements of this Division apply to all fences, walls, and screening unless otherwise stated.

4-50.030 Permit Required

A Minor Improvement Permit (see Section 2-40.060) is required for the installation of walls and fences described in this Division, unless the wall or fence is shown on a Building Permit application for other development on the same property.

The permit application shall include a wall plan showing all fence or wall locations, materials, heights, and colors.

4-50.040 General Fence and Wall Standards

A. Allowed Fence, Wall, and Screening Materials.

Chain-link fencing, with or without:

a. Wood slats;

b. Vinyl slates; or,

c. Double picket weaving.

Masonry wall, with or without grout;

Stucco Walls;

Redwood/cedar board or other wood fences;

Vinyl PVC;

Vinyl-coated polyester fences;

208

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

Stacked railroad ties;

Wrought iron;

Wooden picket fences;

Split rail fences;

Bamboo screens or living plants; or,

Additional products may be approved if they are similar to those on the above list at the discretion

of the Director.

B. Miscellaneous Fence and Wall Standards.

Fences surrounding tennis courts, handball courts, and other similar athletic courts may exceed

six feet in height provided the portion over six feet is chain link or a similar open material.

In all zones, decorative walls that are three feet in height or less and designed as an integral part

of a landscaping scheme are allowed within all required yards.

Barbed wire fences not exceeding five feet in height may be placed on property lines adjacent to a

required side and/or rear yard on residential zoned lots 40,000 square feet or greater in area and on all property lines within the Rural Residential (R-R) and Recreational Open Space (ROS) Zones.

If an industrial lot abuts a residential district shown on an approved land use plan, a minimum

eight-foot-tall decorative masonry wall shall be constructed on the boundary between the two uses. A four-foot landscape buffer must be provided on the side of the fence facing the residential district.

If there is a dedicated alley or public roadway separating the industrial property from the residential property, the alley or public roadway serves as the buffer and the masonry wall is not required unless specified by ordinance or the Director.

A minimum of one gate shall be provided per residential lot to provide emergency access to the

side and rear yards.

4-50.050 Fence and Wall Placement and Height Standards

A. The placement and height standards for fences, walls, and screens are included in Table 4-50.050.A

(Placement and Height Standards for Fences and Walls).

B. If a solid fence or wall is to be located on or immediately adjacent to a property line and there is an

elevation difference between adjoining properties, the height of the fence or wall is measured from property with the higher finished grade.

C. All fences and walls must comply with the site visibility triangle standards in Division 4-60 when

applying the standards in Table 4-50.050.A.

D. No fences or walls may be located within, across, or over a utility easement without express

authorization from the easement holder.

4-50.060 Screening Standards

A. Utility Cabinets.

210

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

Utility cabinets in non-residential zones, on private property and not within a Public Utility Easement

(PUE) or Right of Way (ROW), excluding residential electrical service panels, must be screened by solid fences and walls that match the building materials of the primary building on the site, or evergreen landscape materials as shown in Figure 4-50.060.A (Utility Box Screening). Utility cabinets must be sited to the rear or side of a building, or in a location where visibility from public rights-of-way is minimized to the maximum extent feasible. [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

Figure 4-50.060.A Utility Box Screening

B. Equipment Screening.

In all zones, all mechanical and electrical service equipment must be screened from public view to

the height of the tallest equipment with materials architecturally compatible with the finishes and character of principal structures. Figure 4-50.060.B (Rooftop Equipment Screening) illustrates the required rooftop screening, and Figure 4-50.060.A shows an example of required screening for ground mounted equipment.

If mechanical equipment on a residential property is installed in the side yard or otherwise not

visible to the public, then no additional screening shall be required. The applicant must provide sufficient documentation to the Department to demonstrate how their equipment is designed to meet this standard.

Figure 4-50.060.B Rooftop Equipment Screening

211

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

C. Screening for Refuse and Recycling Containers.

Refuse and recycling containers must be screened by a solid fence or wall a minimum of six feet

in height on at least three sides and designed to match the building materials of the primary building on the site.

Where feasible, enclosures for refuse and recycling containers shall be sited to the rear or side of

a building , or in a location where visibility from public rights-of-way is minimized.

212

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

4-60 Landscaping

Sections:

4-60.010 Purpose

4-60.020 Applicability

4-60.030 Landscape Plan Review

4-60.040 Landscaping Standards

4-60.050 Water Use and Irrigation Standards

4-60.060 Maintenance

4-60.010 Purpose

A. Purpose.

The purpose of this Division is to foster the creation of sustainable landscapes appropriate to the

unique natural characteristics of Kingman and to establish regulations for the installation and maintenance of landscaping according to recognized xeriscape best practices in accordance with the General Plan.

Located on the eastern edge of the Mojave Desert, Kingman has a semi-arid climate with hot summer temperatures, cold winters, low precipitation, and a relatively high altitude. Kingman’s local climate creates a unique environment for landscaping that must be approached thoughtfully and holistically.

The intent of this Division is to establish landscaping standards to:

Establish and preserve sustainable landscaping that protects and promotes the unique,

natural character of Kingman;

Ensure an appropriate balance between the value of responsible water use and the value

of well-designed landscape areas;

Improve community aesthetics by providing buffering between different land uses and

appropriately designed landscapes;

Protect native trees and plants, low-maintenance naturalized plants, and other natural

resources; and

Improve the quality of the environment by enhancing air quality and reducing the spread of

invasive plant species.

B. Xeriscape Landscaping.

Xeriscape landscaping incorporates water-conserving designs that consider soil and drainage

factors, microclimates, grouping of plants with similar water requirements, efficient irrigation systems, native vegetation, paving permeability, and low-water-using and drought tolerant vegetation.

The xeriscape principles in Table 4-60.010.A (Xeriscape Principles), should be applied to all

landscaping areas.

213

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

Table 4-60.010.A: Xeriscape Principles
Col2
Good Landscape Planning and Design
Careful consideration of a site’s size and shape, soil type,
topography, and building configuration is essential in developing a
good landscape plan. The use of the space, the amount of
available sun, location of views, regional and microclimate
conditions, and a preliminary assessment of landscape watering
zones should also be considered in early planning of a xeriscape

landscape.
Use of Drought Tolerant and Low

Water-use Plants
Primarily drought tolerant and low water use plants shall be used.
Some drought tolerant and low water use plants provide shade
and texture, while others are appropriate for borders, accent
areas, seasonal color, and year-round greenery. Native
wildflowers and grasses are typically suitable for revegetating

disturbed areas.
Appropriate Lawn Areas
Lawns shall only be located in areas where they provide
functional benefits. Lawn areas planted close to a building may
provide a cooling effect. Lawns should not be planted in odd,
shaped areas that cannot be watered efficiently. Lawn areas can
often be planted with modern, low-water-use, drought-tolerant
grasses (Refer to Appendix 3, Recommended Landscape Plant

Material List).
Efficient Irrigation


Install irrigation systems so that they provide an adequate amount
of water at the proper time to the root zone of plants. Each plant
and vegetative type has its own water needs, and the planting
arrangement and irrigation system should be designed to reflect
those needs. Moisture sensors shall be installed on irrigation
controls to measure real time soil moisture. Each water use zone
should be on a different valve. Additionally, stormwater runoff

should be routed into landscaped areas to offset irrigation needs.
Soil Improvement
Organic matter should be added to existing soils to increase
water-holding capacity and provide beneficial nutrients to plants.
In low water-use zones, loosening the soil may be all that is

needed.
Use of Mulches
Organic mulches consisting of wood, bark chips, and plant
remnants can hold moisture, cool the surface of the ground,
reduce weed growth, slow erosion, and improve the existing soil
as they decompose. Inorganic mulches consisting of rock or stone

help slow erosion, but do not lower the soil temperature.
Appropriate Maintenance
To be effective, a xeriscape landscape must be monitored with a
program of pruning, weed control, pest control, and irrigation

system adjustments.

4-60.020 Applicability

214

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

This Division applies to new developments and existing development as follows:

A. New Developments.

All new residential and non-residential developments must provide landscaping in compliance

with this Division.

On residential lots less than 0.5 acres, the required front and street-side yard areas shall be

landscaped with decorative rock, lawn, trees, shrubs, or other plant materials. Landscaping must be installed within six months from Certificate of Occupancy or Final Inspection for larger residential lots.

B. Existing Developments.

These provisions shall also apply to any redevelopment, addition, or remodeling of any multi family, commercial, or industrial property that requires a Building Permit and for which the improvement increases the footprint of the building or area of the developed portion of the property by 20 percent or more.

Further, these provisions shall apply to any building or site alterations that require a change of

use, change of occupancy, or Conditional Use Permit unless otherwise determined by the Director.

If over 95 percent of the gross site area of an existing development is hardscaped and no

changes are proposed that would change the amount of hardscaping, then the development would be exempt from these standards. The applicant must submit sufficient documentation to the Department to support this exemption.

For these properties, compliance with this Code to the greatest extent feasible is encouraged.

C. Overlapping Requirements.

In the event two or more landscaping standards overlap, the same landscape treatment may be

counted toward meeting the requirements of both standards.

In the event one or more landscaping standards in this Division conflict one with another, the

Director shall determine which standard shall prevail.

D. Landscaping in the Public Right-of-Way.

Landscaping in rights-of-way shall comply with the landscaping and plant location requirements found in the Streets and Sidewalks Development Rules and Regulations.

E. Exceptions.

The provisions of this Division do not apply to the following:

Additions or alterations to an existing single-family dwelling;

New accessory structures in all zones; and

Accessory dwelling units.

4-60.030 Landscape Plan Review

A. Submittal of Landscape Plan.

A preliminary landscape plan shall be submitted for review to the Department. Submittal

requirements for preliminary landscape plans are included on the checklist with the application form for Development Plan Review.

215

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

The preliminary landscape plan shall contain the location, description, and number of proposed

materials, including new and existing ground covers, shrubs, and trees, and a brief description of the planting and design actions.

The Department shall review each landscape plan to verify its compliance with the provisions of

this Division and the Kingman Engineering Department will review the landscape plan for compliance with Arizona Administrative Code Title 18, Chapter 9, Article 7 (Direct Reuse of Reclaimed Water).

A final landscape plan must be submitted with the building permit application. Revisions to the

overall development plans or plats may require revisions to landscape plans at this point.

With the final landscape plan, the owner shall provide evidence that all plant materials are

guaranteed for a minimum period of 120 days from the date of final approval by the Department.

B. Final Landscape Plan Preparation.

A Final Landscape Plan shall be submitted for review and approval prior to issuance of a building permit or certificate of occupancy. The Final Landscape Plan shall be prepared to scale and shall identify, at a minimum:

Plant species, size, and quantity;

Irrigation system details;

Existing vegetation to be preserved or removed;

Landscape area dimensions and calculations;

Screening, groundcover, and decorative elements.

The Final Landscape Plan shall demonstrate compliance with all applicable provisions of this Code.

C. Final Inspection or Self-Certification Required.

Prior to issuing a Certificate of Occupancy, the Department shall inspect the subject property to

ensure that the landscaping has been installed in compliance with the approved landscape plan.

The Department shall have the right to reject landscape materials as being substandard as to size

and/or condition during the final inspection.

If the inspection determines that there are deviations from the final landscape plan, the Director

may approve an as-built landscaping plan that reflects all changes if the Director determines that the intent of this Division is achieved.

The applicant, property owner, or qualified design professional may elect to self-certify landscape

installation in lieu of direct City inspection. Self-certification shall include a signed statement affirming that:

a. All landscaping and irrigation has been installed in substantial conformance with the

approved Final Landscape Plan;

b. All plant materials meet required species, size, and condition standards;

c. The irrigation system is fully operational and water efficient;

d. Minor field adjustments, if any, do not diminish compliance with this Code.

The self-certification shall be submitted prior to issuance of a certificate of occupancy.

4-60.040 Landscaping Standards

F. Minimum Landscaped Areas for All New Development.

216

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

a. For all new nonresidential and multi-family development, a minimum of 10 percent of the net site area

shall be provided with on-site landscaping. Sufficient documentation must be provided on the landscape plan submitted to the Department for review to verify this requirement is met.

Landscape areas shall include trees, shrubs, vines, succulents, and groundcovers with minimum

numbers and sizes specified in Table 4-60.040.A and 4-60.040.B.

Landscape areas shall consist of ground treatments including any combination of landscape rock,

decomposed granite (min. 5/8”), organic mulches, turf, or similar products as approved by the Department to minimize dust and erosion.

Vegetation shall be selected, planted, and maintained so that at maturity, it will not interfere with

utility lines, vehicular parking, pedestrian circulation, sight triangles, and will not cause damage of sidewalks, pavement, and underground, overhead, or at grade utility lines and equipment.

a. All trees shall be planted a minimum distance from any property line, building, or structure equal

to at least half of the typical mature canopy radius.

b. All plant materials, with the exception of ground cover, shall be a minimum of fifteen feet from

any side of a fire hydrant.

c. All plants with significant spikes or thorns shall be placed a minimum of ten feet from all

sidewalks, paths, or gathering spaces.

The portion of the public right-of-way between the property line and public sidewalk shall be similarly

landscaped and maintained in addition to the on-site requirements. Landscaping in rights-of-way shall comply with the landscaping and plant location requirements found in the Streets and Sidewalks Development Rules and Regulations.

Any part of a site not graded, developed, and used for buildings, parking, driveways, sidewalks,

utilities, stormwater detention areas, and approved storage may be retained in its present vegetative natural state or landscaped. A ten-foot buffer must be maintained between any portions of a site left in its vegetative natural state and any adjacent buildings, structures, or developed properties.

B. Minimum Plant Quantities.

No one plant species shall comprise more than 50 percent of the quantity of required landscape

materials. All landscaped areas shall be composed of a combination of ground cover, shrubs, succulents, and trees as required by Table 4-60.040.A and Table 4-60.040.B.

Plant quantities must be distributed throughout the on-site setback areas as appropriate for site

conditions. Consideration should be paid towards screening, visibility, and pedestrian shade.

The Director may allow greater areas of lawn or turf if it can be demonstrated that the grass

species are drought-resistant and/or irrigation methods will not demand high water usage.

For water conservation purposes, artificial turf may be a maximum of 50 percent of the required

minimum landscape area, provided it has a permeable base. Other artificial landscape materials such as artificial trees, shrubbery, or plants shall not count toward the required landscape area.

If land is used for recreational or institutional purposes, artificial turf may be a maximum of 80 percent of the landscaped area.

All plants and trees shall be native species.

218

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

C. Parking Lot Landscaping.

Landscaping is required to reduce visual impacts created by parking areas in multi-family residential,

mixed-use, industrial, institutional, and commercial development projects.

A landscape buffer a minimum of 10 feet in width shall be provided along the site perimeter between

the parking lot area and a street or a parking lot area and a single-family or duplex residence.

If a landscape buffer is not feasible, the Director may approve use of a solid wall or fence no less than three feet in height between the parking lot area and the street or no less than six feet between the parking lot area and a single-family or duplex dwelling for screening purposes.

The following additional landscaping standards shall apply to all off-street parking lots for all new

development containing 15 or more parking spaces. Parking lot landscaping shall be counted as part of the required landscape area.

4. Parking Lot Landscape Islands.

a. One landscape island shall be provided at each end of each row of parking spaces between

the spaces and the adjacent drive aisle. In addition, one landscaped island shall be provided every 15 feet contiguous parking spaces within the parking space row. The specific location of the landscape island may vary within the parking row provided that there are no more than 15 parking spaces in a row without an intervening landscape island.

i. Each landscape island shall be a minimum of nine feet in width and be the same length as the adjacent parking space. Where double-row parking areas exist, the landscape islands shall be the length of two parking spaces. All measurements are to the outside face of the curbs.

ii. Radius curbing for landscape islands shall be provided along drive aisles with a minimum four-foot radius.

b. All landscape areas and islands adjacent to vehicular parking and access drives shall be

protected from vehicular traffic by the provision of concrete curbs, except where curb breaks are necessary to accommodate stormwater drainage flows from the parking lot into retention areas.

c. Parking areas used exclusively for outdoor sales and display of automobiles, recreational

vehicles, and watercraft as shown on an approved site plan shall be exempt from the landscape island requirements.

5. Landscape Island Planting and Tree Requirements.

a. Landscape islands shall each contain a minimum of one tree and two shrubs.

b. A minimum of 75 percent of the required parking area trees shall be shade trees.

c. For any portion of a parking area over which photo-voltaic solar collectors and/or other

structures are installed where they also function as shade structures, the minimum requirement for trees may be waived by the Director. If this requirement is waived, a minimum of three shrubs and three groundcovers shall be planted for every landscape island.

D. Intersection Visibility and Sight Visibility Triangle.

A sight visibility triangle must be preserved at all street intersections and intersections of

dedicated alleys or driveways with streets. Within the sight visibility triangle, no visual obstructions caused by landscape materials, signs, utility cabinets and equipment, or other structures are permitted.

219

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

The sight triangle shall be defined by a line on a horizontal plane connecting two points

measured 25 feet back in each direction of the intersection of two streets, a street with a dedicated alley, or a street and driveway intersection, thus forming a triangle, as shown in Figure 4-60.040.1 (Sight Visibility Triangles).

Within the area of the sight visibility triangle, the height of mature landscaping, walls, and fences

may not exceed three feet measured from the top of the existing curb grade or crown of abutting road, whichever is lower.

Within the sight triangle, trees shall be permitted as long as only the tree trunk (no leaves, limbs, etc.) is visible between the ground and a minimum of eight feet above ground, except during early growth stages.

The intersection and sight visibility standards do not apply to permanent structures existing on

the Effective Date, nor to utility poles or their supporting structures, or to areas where the Historic Overlay District or Downtown District apply.

Figure 4-60.040.1. Sight Visibility Triangles

E. Safety Requirements.

Consideration for vehicular, pedestrian, bicyclist, and fire safety shall be incorporated into all landscape designs. At maturity, plant materials shall not:

Constitute a driving hazard by interfering with safe sight distances or sight visibility triangles for

vehicular, bicycle, or pedestrian traffic;

Block vehicular, pedestrian, or bicycle ways;

Conflict with overhead utility lines, utility easements, overhead lights, or walkway lights;

Cause a fire hazard due to proximity to buildings; or

220

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

Cause damage or upheaval of sidewalks and pavement.

The Kingman Fire Department shall be consulted, as necessary, to determine safe proximities of vegetation to buildings and roofs.

4-60.050 Water Use and Irrigation Standards

A. Use of Reclaimed Water.

Sites located adjacent to reclaimed water lines in the public right-of-way next to the site, with the

exception of single- and two-family residential properties, shall be required to connect to the lines and utilize reclaimed water for the primary water source for irrigation in areas appropriate for direct reuse.

Turf areas used for parks, playing areas, or sports fields shall be irrigated by harvested rainwater,

greywater, or reclaimed water, unless it is not available, in which case the use of potable water is allowed.

Reclaimed water shall not be used in areas designated for edible landscaping or community

gardens.

Precautionary signage consistent with the applicable Arizona Department of Environmental

Quality (ADEQ) and City standards shall be required where reclaimed water is applied.

B. Irrigation System Specifications.

The installation of permanent irrigation systems is required.

All irrigation systems and landscape areas shall be designed, constructed, and maintained to

promote water conservation, and to prevent water overflow or seepage into the street, sidewalk, or parking areas.

Permanent drip-irrigation or low-flow bubbler irrigation systems, or other low-tech watering

systems, shall be designed, installed, and maintained to minimize water use and soil evaporation and prevent water from being wasted through inappropriate application techniques.

If potable water is used for permanent irrigation systems, they must comply with the following unless waived by the Department:

These systems shall utilize rain/moisture sensors to minimize the use of applied water;

Drip irrigation shall be used for all single-planted landscaping, with emitters rated for no

more than four gallons per hour (GPH); and,

Micro-sprinklers rated for no more than 45 GPH may be used for large, carpeted plantings.

Irrigation systems shall include an automatic timing controller for landscaping areas. However,

once plants have become established after three summer growing seasons, automatic timers may be turned off to ensure that water is conserved, and landscape areas are not overwatered.

A property owner may be required to turn automatic timers back on if established landscaping cannot be properly maintained in an acceptable condition without supplemental irrigation.

Rigid irrigation pipes and flexible drop system irrigation lines place under paved areas shall be

contained in pipe sleeves.

Pipes or lines carrying water under constant and intermittent pressure shall be buried to the City’s

standard depth for all irrigation systems of 12 to 18 inches.

Non-pressurized PVC and polyethylene lines shall be buried to a minimum depth of 12-inches.

221

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

Lawn, shrub, and bubbler heads closer than 12-inches to any paved surface shall be pop-up

heads.

Spray irrigation systems shall only be used for turf areas.

Temporary irrigation systems may be used for a period of one year or less, unless otherwise

approved by the Department, to establish hydro-seeded vegetation and to provide water to existing native vegetation preserved on a site to ensure its survival after construction has been completed. Temporary irrigation systems are not required to be buried.

C. Detention/Extended Detention Basins.

All above ground detention/extended detention basins may be treated with a native ground

cover seed mix. Where detention basins are in high visibility areas, they may be shaped and landscaped to provide the natural appearance of the basin, including shrubs and groundcover.

Where detention basins are constructed partially or entirely within buffers or other high-visibility

areas on sites:

a. Detention basins must be landscaped to meet the requirements of the landscape area in

compliance with Section 4-60.040 (Landscape Standards);

b. All surfaces may also be treated with a native groundcover seed mix, and the design of

such basins may be incorporated into the site plan; and

c. No trees shall be planted on the berms or dams of the basin unless the basin is entirely

excavated.

4-60.060 Maintenance

A. Maintenance of all landscaping shall be the responsibility of the owner, lessee, heirs, assigns, agent,

or other liable entity of the property. The goal is to ensure that all landscape areas are kept alive with a minimum of water especially after the plants are established in which case (depending on the plant species) watering needs may be reduced except in exceptionally dry or windy conditions.

B. Landscaped areas shall be regularly maintained, including pruning, mowing, weeding, trimming,

watering, refuse removal, fertilizing, and maintenance of the irrigation systems, to create an attractive appearance for the development.

The property owner is responsible for pruning any trees overhanging any sidewalk, path, or right-ofway within the City to maintain visibility of intersections and prevent interference with pedestrians or other modes of travel. A minimum vertical clearance of 8 feet from a sidewalk or pedestrian walkway or 15 feet from a roadway shall be provided as required by the Kingman Municipal Code.

C. Any dead plant material shall be removed and replaced with plants of the same size, variety, and

quality by the property owner within 30 days of its demise. This requirement to replace dead plant materials with similar plants may be waived by the Director if the remaining landscaping on site satisfies the minimum landscaping requirements.

D. Public and private utilities will be responsible for the replacement and repair of landscaping materials

specifically damaged by their construction in the public right-of-way. However, unless otherwise specified, the continuing maintenance of landscaping in the public right-of-way is the responsibility of the adjacent property owner.

222

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

4-70 Outdoor Lighting Standards

Sections:

4-70.010 Purpose

4-70.020 Applicability

4-70.030 General Requirements

4-70.040 Special Uses

4-70.050 Prohibited Outdoor Lighting

4-70.060 Exemptions

4-70.010 Purpose

The purpose of this Division is to support the preservation of dark night skies which are enjoyed by residents of the City. The Division establishes regulations that allow outdoor lighting for uses and activities consistent with the need for utility, safety, and nighttime attractiveness while minimizing:

A. Light escaping directly from fixtures or indirectly after reflection from surfaces into the atmosphere

which causes increased artificial sky brightness;

B. Glare arising directly from fixtures or from over-illuminated outdoor areas which interferes with

effective vision; and,

C. Light trespass onto adjacent properties or roadways.

4-70.020 Applicability

A. Existing Buildings and Uses.

Any new outdoor lighting installed on a building or parcel must meet the requirements of this Division for shielding and lamp type. This includes new external building, parking lot, or landscaping lighting.

B. New Uses, Buildings, and Major Additions or Modifications.

If a major addition or modification to a land use, building, development, or structure is constructed

on a property, the entire property must be brought into compliance with the requirements of this Division.

For the purposes of this Division, additions with an increase of 20 percent or more in the number

of dwelling units, gross floor area, and/or seating capacity as either a single addition or cumulative additions, are considered to be major additions or modifications.

C. Conformance with Applicable Codes.

A site lighting plan with sufficient information to demonstrate compliance with this Code must be submitted during site plan review. This includes a photometric plan for all uses, unless this requirement is waived by the Director.

4-70.030 General Requirements

A. Lighting Types.

Type 1 Lighting is permitted where accurate color rendition is required to preserve the effectiveness

of an activity listed below. Uses not included in the list below require approval by the Director.

223

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

a. Recognized Type 1 lighting applications.

i. Outdoor sales areas, including service station canopies;

ii. Primary customer building entry/exit areas (does not include service or emergency

entry/exits);

iii. Outdoor seating areas at restaurants;

iv. Outdoor display areas;

v. Outdoor assembly or repair areas where assembly or repair work occurs at night on a

regularly scheduled basis;

vi. Outdoor recreational field/track/arena areas; and

vii. External and internal lighting for signs, excluding neon.

b. Lighting sources used for Type 1 lighting must have a Correlated Color Temperature (CCT) of

2700 Kelvin (K) or less.

Type 2 Lighting is used for general illumination for utility, safety, or security purposes.

a. Recognized Type 2 lighting applications.

i. Pedestrian walkways, driveways, and roadways;

ii. Parking lots;

iii. Equipment yards; and

iv. Outdoor security.

Type 3 Lighting is used for decorative purposes.

a. Recognized Type 3 lighting applications.

i. Architectural illumination;

ii. Flag and monument lighting;

iii. Neon incorporated on signs; and

iv. Landscape lighting.

b. Decorative lighting that changes colors is only be permitted to change color every two minutes.

B. Light Trespass and Effective Shielding.

All light fixtures must be located, aimed, or shielded so that the direct illumination from the fixture

shall be confined to the property boundaries of the source.

All light fixtures must be fully shielded, except for residential lighting fixtures below 1,000 lumens,

which may be partially shielded. Residential property includes all properties developed primarily for residential uses.

Internal shields are preferred on new installations. If an external shield is used, its surface must

be painted in a nonreflective material to minimize glare (Figure 4-70.030.A (Shielding Configurations).

224

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

Figure 4-70.030.A Shielding Configurations

Permanently exposed unshielded string lights are permitted only in areas designated for outdoor

dining, seating, or gathering and must be white light with a clear bulb. String lights must comply with the light trespass and spillage requirements.

C. Motion Sensing Light Fixtures.

All motion sensing light fixtures must conform to all applicable standards of this Division, including

the shielding standards.

Motion sensing light fixtures used for safety or security purposes are exempt from the time limits

for outdoor lighting described in this Section.

Motion sensing lighting used for safety or security purposes shall use the lowest possible

illumination level to effectively allow for surveillance.

D. Time Limits for Outdoor Lighting.

All lighting must be turned off by 11:00 pm and/or no later than 30 minutes after the business

closes, whichever is later, and remain off for the remainder of the night or until the business reopens.

Legal non-conforming light fixtures may continue to be used, provided such fixtures are turned off by 11:00

pm and/or no later than 30 minutes after the business closes, whichever is later, and remain off for the remainder of the night or until the business reopens.

Seasonal lighting decorations, outdoor recreation facilities, and lighting used for safety or security

purposes are exempt from the time limits listed in this Section.

Trailheads shall be illuminated overnight beginning one half-hour before sunset until one half-hour

after sunrise to discourage unsafe activities.

4-70.040 Special Uses

A. Service Station Canopy Lighting.

All light fixtures mounted on or recessed into the lower surface of service station canopies must be

fully shielded. Such shielding must be provided by the fixture itself; shielding by surrounding structures such as canopy edges is not permitted.

Overhead lighting shall have a minimum lighting intensity under the canopies no greater than 50

footcandles directly under the overhang or canopy.

B. Outdoor Recreation Facilities.

225

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

Illumination levels for the field, track, or arena should be consistent with the International Dark-Sky

Association criteria for community-friendly outdoor sports lighting.

No illuminated sports facility shall be illuminated after the time limits listed in Subsection 4-70.030,

above, except as needed to conclude a scheduled recreational or sporting event in progress prior to the time limitation.

If there is more than one field at a facility, an on-site control system shall be provided to allow the lights to be turned on only for active sports fields.

Maximum lighting pole heights are as follows:

a. Pedestrian or bicycle pathways in a park or open space: 16 feet;

b. Bollard lighting along pedestrian or bicycle pathways: 4 feet;

c. Sport courts such as basketball, tennis, volleyball, or pickleball: 25 feet;

d. Athletic fields for football, soccer, baseball, lacrosse, or golf ranges: 80 feet;

e. Athletic fields for little league: 60 feet.

Exceptions to the height requirements may be approved by the Director in limited circumstances.

C. Street Lighting.

Standards for street lighting installed on public rights-of-way are located in the City Engineering

Standards . This document is available online.

Street lighting installed on private rights-of-way shall be considered Type 2 lighting and is subject

to the standards of this Division.

D. Outdoor Display Lots.

All site lighting outside of the outdoor display and sales area shown on the site plan shall conform

to the lighting standards described in this Section.

Lighting poles within the outdoor display area shall be limited to 16 feet along the perimeter of the

display area and 25 feet within the interior of the outdoor display area.

E. Parking Lots and Parking Structures.

Parking lot lighting poles shall be limited to 25 feet; however, within 50 feet of any residential

property line, the maximum height shall be reduced to 15 feet.

Ornamental parking lot lighting must be consistent with the architectural character of the primary

building or structure on the site.

Parking lot lighting should be designed to achieve uniform luminance throughout the parking lot to

the maximum extent possible. Landscape design should also be taken into consideration to ensure vegetation will not significantly block intended illumination.

Minimum lighting intensity shall be 0.5 footcandles (fcs) and maximum lighting intensity shall be

3.0 fcs within the parking lot. Lighting intensity shall not exceed 1 footcandle measured at any property lines adjacent to right-of-way or residentially zoned property.

All light fixtures used within open parking garages, including those mounted to the ceilings over

the parking decks, must be fully shielded. Such shielding must be provided by the fixture itself; shielding by surrounding structures is not permitted.

4-70.050 Prohibited Outdoor Lighting

The following types of outdoor lighting are prohibited:

226

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

A. Outdoor floodlighting.

B. Search lights, flood lights, laser lights, or similar high intensity light, except as necessary for official

emergency services or for meteorological data gathering purposes.

C. Any lighting device located on the exterior of a building or on the inside of a window which is

visible beyond the property boundaries of the lot or parcel with intermittent fading, flashing, blinking, rotating, or strobe light illumination.

D. Lighting that results in glare to motor vehicles on public rights-of-way.

4-70.060 Exemptions

A. Airport Lighting.

The Federal Aviation Association (FAA) required navigational lighting systems at private or public airports or heliports are exempt from the provisions of this Division. All other outdoor lighting not under the purview of the FAA at airport facilities must comply with the provisions of this Division.

Infrared Security Lighting.

Lights emitting infrared radiation used for remote security surveillance systems are permitted in all zones with the following restrictions:

Fixed lights must be fully shielded; and

Movable lights, such as spotlights attached to infrared sensitive cameras, must be mounted to a

fixed structure and lights cannot be directed higher than 20 degrees below the horizontal plane, measured from the center of the light beam.

Seasonal Lighting Decorations.

Temporary seasonal lighting installed between October 1 and January 15 is exempt from the provisions of this Division.

D. State and Federal Facilities.

State and federal facilities are exempt from the provision of this Division. Voluntary compliance with the intent of this Code is encouraged.

E. Special Events.

No public or private outdoor recreational facility shall be illuminated after 11:00 PM, except to conclude a permitted recreational or sporting event or any other similar activity conducted at, or in the facility which was in progress and illuminated prior to 11:00 PM.

F. Underwater Lighting.

Underwater lighting used for the illumination of swimming pools and decorative water fountains is exempt from the provision of this Division.

227

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

4-80 Parking and Loading

Sections:

4-80.010 Purpose

4-80.020 Applicability

4-80.030 Required Parking Spaces

4-80.040 Required Off-Street Loading Spaces

4-80.050 Parking Adjustments

4-80.060 Parking Area Design Standards

4-80.070 Maintenance

4-80.010 Purpose

The purpose of this Division is to:

A. Establish parking and loading spaces for all land uses that are sufficient but not excessive in

number, size, and arrangement;

B. Ensure the provision of adequate bicycle parking facilities to support bicycle trips;

C. Establish standards and regulations for safe and well-designed parking, unloading, and vehicle

circulation that minimize conflicts within parking lots and, where appropriate, create buffers from surrounding land uses; and

D. Allow flexibility to reduce the amount of parking spaces in appropriate locations and situations.

4-80.020 Applicability

A . General Requirements.

Parking and loading facilities required by this Division shall be constructed or installed prior to final

inspection or the issuance of a Certificate of Occupancy for the uses that they serve.

Property owners in the Historic Overlay Zone and Downtown District must make every reasonable

effort to provide parking and loading as required in this Division to the maximum extent feasible. The Department recognizes that there may be additional parking challenges and limitations within the Historic Overlay Zone and Downtown District due to the nature of this area.

B. New Buildings and Uses.

Parking and loading in compliance with this Division must be provided at the time any new building or structure is constructed or any new land use is established on a site.

C. Existing Buildings and Uses.

Parking and loading for change of use(s) in existing buildings and land uses are as follows.

For changes or expansions of a use or occupancy that result in an increase of floor area or

number of dwelling units of at least 20 percent or 1,000 square feet, whichever is smaller, additional on-site parking and loading must be provided for the addition.

For changes or expansions under this threshold, additional parking is not automatically required

but may be required if the Department determines that the change or expansion of use has the potential to create a need for additional parking.

228

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

4-80.030 Required Parking Spaces

Each land use type is subject to the following minimum parking and loading requirements, unless otherwise provided elsewhere in this Division.

A. Residential Zones.

Tandem parking may count towards the required parking for an individual residential unit.

On-street parking spaces located along the boundaries of a parcel may count towards the required

residential guest parking for multi-family residential parking requirements. For single-family and duplex uses, parking must be provided on-site.

B. General to all Zones.

Maximum Number of Parking Spaces.

Developments that provide parking in surface parking lots shall not exceed the minimum number of

parking spaces by more than five percent.

Parking Spaces Required.

The minimum number of parking spaces required shall be determined from Table 4-80.030.A

(Number of Motor Vehicle Parking Spaces Required). Uses not specifically listed in Table 480.030.A shall use the parking requirement for the most similar use, or as determined by the Department.

C. Calculation of Required Spaces.

The number of required parking spaces shall be calculated according to the following rules:

Calculations.

Any fraction greater than or equal to 0.50 shall be rounded up to the nearest whole number. No additional space shall be required for a fractional unit of less than 0.50.

Square Feet.

In any case where Table 4-80.030.A (Motor Vehicle Parking Spaces Required) expresses a parking requirement based on floor area in square feet (sf), the measurement shall mean square feet of gross floor area, unless stated otherwise.

Number of Employees.

In any case where Table 4-80.030.A (Motor Vehicle Parking Spaces Required) expresses a

parking requirement based on number of employees; the number of employees shall be calculated based on the largest work shift.

Sites with Multiple Uses.

If more than one use is located on a site, the number of required parking and loading spaces must be equal to the sum of the requirements calculated separately for each use.

Bicycle Parking Required.

One bicycle parking space must be provided for every 20 car spaces provided for all uses other than single-family or two-family residential uses.

Director Determinations.

a. Additional off-street parking spaces may be required if the Director determines that the use

will generate a need for more parking than is set forth in the schedule because of increased density, intensity of use, or other factors. The determination of spaces to be

229

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

provided shall be based on appropriate parking generation studies conducted by the City or by a consultant on behalf of the applicant.

b. The Director shall determine the parking and loading requirements for uses that do not

directly correspond to the categories in Table 4-80.030.A. The Director may require additional information from the applicant to assist in this determination, such as but not limited to: type of use, building design, or hours of operations.

4-80.040 Required Off-Street Loading Spaces

A. Off-street loading spaces are required for all nonresidential uses and must be screened from right-of way and adjacent residential uses.

235

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

B. The number of off-street loading spaces shall be based on the gross floor area of all structures or

buildings on a site, as shown in the below table. Small spaces are intended to accommodate deliveries small box trucks or passenger vans. Large spaces shall be designed to accommodate deliveries from a semi-truck. C. Loading spaces may not be part of the area used to satisfy the parking requirement and must be

located on the same lot as the use they are intended to serve.

D. When off-street loading spaces abut a residential zone, truck usage is limited to the hours between 6:

00 A.M. and 9:00 P.M.

E. Cross-dock or distribution facilities must provide trailer parking in addition to the loading spaces,

equivalent to the number of loading spaces.
