4-80.050 Parking Adjustments

The number of parking spaces required by Table 4-80.030.A may be reduced as follows if the Department determines any of the following criteria have been met. Parking reductions are cumulative for parking facilities meeting more than one of the following criteria:

Special conditions exist that will reduce parking demand at the site. Conditions may include the

nature of the proposed operation; transportation characteristics of persons residing, working, or visiting the site;

The use will adequately be served by the proposed on-site parking; and

Parking demand generated by the project will not exceed the capacity of or have a significant

impact on the supply of on-street parking in the surrounding area.

A parking demand study shall be provided by the applicant in accordance with established professional practices and included with any request for a reduced parking requirement.

A. Shared On-Site Parking.

Where two or more uses on the same site have distinct and differing peak parking usage periods (e.g., a theater and a bank), a reduction in the required number of parking spaces may be allowed.

Approval shall require a recorded covenant running with the land, recorded by the owner of the

parking lot, guaranteeing that the required parking will be maintained exclusively for the uses served and remain for the duration of the use.

B. Off-Site Parking.

For uses other than single-family dwellings and duplexes, off-site parking may be counted towards the required number of parking spaces, in compliance with the standards below.

236

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

On-Street Parking Spaces.

On-street parking spaces along a site’s corresponding frontages on a public or private street may be counted towards the required number of parking spaces where appropriate as determined by the City Engineer.

a. Where an on-street parking space is adjacent to multiple lots, the parking space shall be counted

towards the use on the lot whose frontage contains more than 50 percent of the parking space length.

b. Where on-street parking is not marked or dedicated for another purpose, 18 linear feet of curb

space will be considered an on-street parking space.

Off-Site Parking Facilities.

Parking spaces provided in off-site parking lots or structures may count towards the required number of parking spaces, as provided below.

a. Off-site parking facilities must be located within 200 feet of an entrance to the intended use,

measured by the shortest route of pedestrian access.

b. A parking agreement or easement between the property owners shall be executed and recorded

prior to establishing use of parking facilities and a copy of the agreement must be provided to the City. The agreement must include:

i. A guarantee among the property owners for access to and use of the parking facility; and

ii. A guarantee that the spaces will be maintained and reserved for the uses served for as long as such uses are in operation.

C. Mixed-Use Development.

For mixed-use development, the total requirement for off-street parking facilities shall be the sum of the requirements for the various uses computed separately, subject to the modifications set forth below:

To reflect the reduced automobile use associated with mixed-use developments, all commercial

uses that are part of a mixed-use development shall be eligible for a 15 percent parking reduction from the minimum off-street parking requirements in Table 4-80.030.A (Motor Vehicle Parking Spaces Required) without the need to prepare a parking demand study.

The total number of parking spaces required of a use or uses may be further reduced by the

Director if the applicant prepares a parking demand study that demonstrates a further reduction is appropriate based on the expected parking needs of the development.

D. Passenger Loading Areas.

If a designated passenger loading area is provided, the total number of required parking spaces may be reduced by five parking spaces for every passenger loading area, up to a total reduction of 10 parking spaces. The parking study must document how the passenger loading area will reduce parking need at the site. Additionally:

Passenger loading areas must be marked and designated.

Passenger loading areas must provide sufficient space for safe maneuvering.

Passenger loading areas must not block any mode of travel in the public right-of-way or in parking

areas.

E. Motorcycle Parking.

237

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

The Director may allow a reduction of one required motor vehicle parking space for each four motorcycle parking spaces provided to a maximum of five percent of the required motor vehicle parking spaces.

F. Heat Island Reduction Incentive.

To incentivize the reduction of ambient surface temperatures in parking areas, an applicant may reduce their required parking amount by 15 percent if at least 50 percent of areas not landscaped are shaded, utilizing light-colored materials with a solar reflectance index of at least 29, and/or a combination of shading and light-colored materials.

Shade may be provided by canopies, shade structures, trees, or other equivalent mechanisms. If

shade is provided by trees, the amount of required shading is to be reached within 15 years.

Trees must be selected from Appendix 3 (Recommended Landscape Plant Material List).

The applicant must provide sufficient technical documentation to the Department to support

granting this reduction.

4-80.060 Parking Area Design Standards

All parking areas must be designed and developed consistent with the following standards in addition to the applicable standards listed in this Title for lighting, landscaping, fencing and screening, and sight visibility.

A. Size and Dimensions of Parking Areas.

Parking Space Minimum Dimensions.

Parking spaces must meet the minimum dimensions established in Table 4-80.060.A (Parking Space Minimum Dimensions).

For parking facilities with less than 20 spaces, a maximum of 20 percent of required spaces may be compact spaces. For parking facilities with 20 spaces or more, a maximum of 10 percent of required spaces may be compact spaces.

B. Specific Types of Parking.

Accessible Parking Spaces.

a. All parking facilities that require parking spaces for disabled persons shall conform to the

Americans with Disabilities Act (ADA) and A.R.S. § 28-882. Where the standards found here conflict with other standards, the most restrictive shall apply.

b. Accessible parking spaces count toward the total parking requirement.

c. Accessible parking spaces serving a particular building shall be located on the shortest

accessible route of travel from adjacent parking to an accessible entrance, if multiple accessible entrances are provided then the accessible parking spaces shall be dispersed so that there are accessible spots at each accessible entrance; and,

d. In parking facilities that do not serve a particular building, accessible parking shall be located on

the shortest accessible route of travel to an accessible pedestrian entrance of the parking facility.

Tandem Parking.

Tandem parking is permitted for single-family residences, accessory dwelling units, and duplexes as follows:

a. Both tandem parking spaces satisfy the parking requirement of one residential unit; and,

b. Neither of the tandem parking spaces shall be required accessible parking spaces.

Bicycle Parking.

Bicycle spaces shall be provided in accordance with the following requirements:

a. Bicycle parking shall consist of either a lockable enclosure (locker) in which the bicycle is

stored or a rack to which the frame of the bicycle and one or both wheels can be locked;

b. Lockers and racks shall be securely anchored to the pavement or a structure;

c. Bicycle parking shall be placed in a convenient, highly visible, active, and well-lit location not

more than 100 feet walking distance from the main entrance and shall not interfere with pedestrian accessibility.

239

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

Motorcycle Parking.

Each motorcycle space must be at least four feet wide and nine feet deep to accommodate two wheeled motorized vehicles, including motorcycles, scooters, mopeds, golf carts, and similar vehicles.

Valet Parking.

Valet parking is allowed if an attendant is present or an automated system is in place to move

vehicles. If parking managed by an attendant is used for required parking spaces, an acceptable form of guarantee must be filed with the Director ensuring that an attendant will be present while the lot is in operation. The location of valet parking spaces and attendant area must be shown on the site plan.

C. Circulation and Safety.

Pedestrian Access.

a. On-site walkways must connect the main building entry to public sidewalks on each street

frontage. Walkways must be ADA compliant and follow the shortest practical distance between the main building entries and sidewalks.

b. Non-residential and multi-family projects must provide shared vehicle and pedestrian access

to adjacent non-residential properties for convenience, safety, and efficient circulation.

c. Walkways must be at least four feet in width, with shading and lighting as required by this

Code.

Public Service Vehicles.

a. Parking lots must be designed so that sanitation, emergency, and other public service

vehicles can provide service without backing up unreasonable distances or making dangerous turns or maneuvers. Both front-load and side-load trash trucks must be able to access dumpsters via a forward motion.

b. The Department shall consult with the City of Kingman Fire, Police, Parks and Recreation,

and Solid Waste Departments as appropriate.

Forward Entry.

a. Parking facilities of four or more spaces must be provided with suitable maneuvering room

so that all vehicles accessing the facility may enter an abutting street in a forward direction.

b. Parking facilities that require vehicles to back out into the street right-of-way, including

alleys, are prohibited except for single-family dwellings and duplexes. Parking facilities shall also provide sufficient turnaround space to allow vehicles to turn around without using a parking space.

Striping, Marking, and Painting.

a. Parking and loading areas, drive aisles, and approach lanes , must be striped, marked,

signed, and maintained in conformance with City standards. The Department may require the installation of additional striping and marking to facilitate safe and efficient traffic movement on- and off-site.

Curbing.

a. A continuous, permanent curb at least six inches high and six inches wide shall be provided

at least three feet from any wall, fence, property line, walkway, or structure.

D. Parking Area and Driveway Improvement Standards.

240

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

All parking areas, aisles, and driveways must be improved with a durable, dustless surface of

asphaltic concrete, cement concrete, or alternative such as pavers, brick, stamped concrete, or the equivalent of the above as may be approved by the Director.

Single Family Residential, Manufactured Homes, and Duplexes.

a. Primary Driveway:

i. Paved access shall be provided to the required parking spaces located outside the front yard setback.

ii. Overall width of the paved access shall not exceed the maximum allowed driveway

within the front yard.

b. Secondary Driveway:

i. Access to parking areas in excess of the minimum parking space requirements in Table 4-80.030.A and associated with a carport or garage must be improved from the abutting street improvements the full distance of the required minimum front yard setback of the zone or to the point where the side yard return fence terminates, whichever is the greater distance. Suitable materials are those that are listed in Subsection (D)(1) above.

All driveways must extend from the required parking spaces to the improved portion of the

adjoining street or to the property line if the adjoining street is City maintained, but not improved.

a. Should there be an intervening area of unimproved property due to right-of-way alignment,

aggregate base course (ABC) or equivalent shall be provided upon approval of the City Engineer.

b. Should future improvements be made to the adjoining street, the property owner shall, at

that time, improve any remaining unimproved portion of the driveway to the improved portion of the street.

Residential Driveway Widths.

Paving or other improvements for vehicular access to garage spaces or to side or rear yard parking areas may not exceed the maximum width for residential driveways as provided below. All widths are to be measured at the setback line.

a. Two-car driveways must have a maximum width of 30 feet.

b. Three-car driveways must have a maximum width of 40 feet.

c. For each additional garage parking space over three, paved access may be widened by ten

feet per space, not to exceed 50% of lot width.

E. Trailers, RV’s, and Boats.

The parking or placement of a camping or vacation trailer, recreational vehicle, utility trailer or boat in any zone for residential or storage purposes shall be prohibited except as described below.

Parking and Storage of Trailers, RVs, and Boats.

Unoccupied, non-commercial recreational vehicles, boats, trailers, truck campers, camping trailers, utility trailers, and other similar vehicles shall be stored, parked, or located only as follows on residentially zoned properties:

a. Such vehicles shall not be occupied for living, sleeping, or housekeeping purposes.

b. Such vehicles shall be parked or stored on driveways and parking areas improved with a

dustless surface unless the vehicle is located behind the front façade of the residential structur e, within a side or rear yard.

241

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

c. No portion of any such vehicle shall extend into or block any portion of the public sidewalk or

street.

d. No more than two such vehicles may be stored for each 20,000 square feet of lot area

provided that no more than four such vehicles are stored on any residential lot.

Overnight Parking of Travel Trailers, Motor Homes, and RVs.

a. The use of travel trailers, motor homes, and RVs for camping within the City is regulated

under the provisions of Kingman Municipal Code Section 7-80 (Camping Municipal and Commercial Property).

4-80.070 Maintenance

Parking and loading facilities, including landscaped areas, must be maintained free of refuse, debris, or other accumulated matter, and must always be kept in good repair.

242

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

4-90 Sign Standards

Sections:

4-90.010     Purpose and Intent

4-90.020 Applicability

4-90.030 Sign Permits

4-90.040 General Restrictions for All Signs

4-90.050 General Requirements for All Signs

4-90.060 Standards for Permanent Signs

4-90.070 Standards for Temporary Signs

4-90.080 Nonconforming Signs

4-90.090 Enforcement

4-90.100 Severability

4-90.010 Purpose and Intent

A. Purpose.

This Division has been adopted to ensure that all signs installed in the City are compatible with the unique character and environment of the community, and in compliance with the General Plan. This Division promotes the public health, safety, and welfare through a comprehensive system of reasonable, effective, consistent, content neutral, and nondiscriminatory sign standards and requirements.

B. More specifically, this Division is intended to:

Ensure that all signs are compatible with the unique character and environment of the City, and

that they support the desired ambience and development patterns of the various districts, overlay districts, and historic areas within the City;

Balance public and private objectives by allowing adequate avenues for both commercial and non commercial messages;

Ensure pedestrian and traffic safety by promoting the free flow of traffic and the protection of

pedestrians and motorists from injury and property damage caused by, or which may be fully or partially attributable to, cluttered, distracting, and/or illegible signage;

Prevent property damage, personal injury, and litter caused by signs that are improperly

constructed or maintained;

Protect property values, the local economy, and quality of life by preserving and enhancing the

appearance of the streetscape; and

Provide consistent sign design standards that enable the fair and consistent enforcement of these

sign regulations.

4-90.020 Applicability

A. This Division applies to all signs within the City regardless of their nature or location, unless otherwise

specifically exempted in Subsection 4-90.020.D (Exemptions).

Standards for permanent signs are found in Section 4-90.060 (Standards for Permanent Signs).

243

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

Standards for temporary signs are found in Section 4-90.070 (Standards for Temporary Signs).

The provisions of this Division shall be applied in a content-neutral manner. Noncommunicative

aspects of all signs, not related to the content of the sign, must comply with the provisions of this Division. “Non-communicative aspects” include the time, place, manner, location, size, height, illumination, spacing, and orientation of signs.

B. Nothing in this Division shall be construed to prohibit a person from holding a sign while picketing or

protesting on public property that has been determined to be a traditional or designated public forum, so long as the person holding the sign does not block ingress and egress from buildings, create a safety hazard by impeding travel on sidewalks, in bike or vehicle lanes, or on trails, or violate any other reasonable time, place, and manner restrictions adopted by the City.

C. Substitutions and Interpretations.

This Division is not intended to, and does not, restrict speech on the basis of its content,

viewpoint, or message. No part of this Division shall be construed to favor commercial speech over non-commercial speech. A non-commercial message may be substituted for any commercial or non-commercial message displayed on a sign, or the content of any non-commercial message displayed on a sign may be changed to a different noncommercial message, without the need for any approval or permit, provided that the size of the sign is not altered, and the sign otherwise complies with the provisions of this Division. To the extent any provision of this Division is ambiguous, the term will be interpreted not to regulate on the basis of the content of the message.

When there is any question regarding the interpretation of a provision of this Division, or its

application to any specific case or situation, the Director shall interpret the intent of this Division.

D. Exemptions.

The following signs are not regulated under this Division:

Building identification signs not exceeding two square feet in area for residential buildings and

four-square feet in area for nonresidential buildings;

Any sign, posting, notice or similar signs placed, installed, or required by law by a city, county, or a

federal or state governmental agency in carrying out its responsibility to protect the public health, safety, and welfare, including the following:

a. Numerals and letters identifying an address from the street to facilitate emergency response

and compliant with City requirements;

b. Emergency and warning signs necessary to warn of dangerous and hazardous conditions and

that serve to aid public safety or civil defense;

c. Traffic signs erected and maintained by an authorized public agency;

d. Signs required to be displayed by any applicable federal, state, or local law, regulation, or

ordinance;

e. Signs directing the public to points of interest; and

f. Signs showing the location of public facilities.

Historical plaques, commemorative signs, memorial tablets, and date-constructed stones not

exceeding four square feet in area;

Incidental signs not to exceed an aggregate of three-square feet in sign area in all Single-Family

Residential zones and 6 square feet in all other zones;

Landmark signs;

244

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

Real Estate signs:

a. Commercial:

i. Maximum height: Fourteen (14) feet;

ii. Maximum width: Eight (8) feet;

iii. Maximum area: 48 square feet;

iv. Shall have a minimum clearance of six feet from grade to the bottom of the sign;

v. Shall be constructed of a durable material and anchored to prevent sign from taking flight;

and,

vi. Shall not be located in any vehicular triangular line of sight.

b. Residential For Sale Yard Signs:

i. Maximum height: Six (6) feet;

ii. Maximum dimensions: 30 inches by 24 inches; and,

iii. Shall be removed within 48 hours upon close of escrow.

c. Residential Open House Signs:

i. Placement: Signs placed in a manner that obstructs traffic, blocks the vision of motorists, or impedes pedestrian flow are prohibited.

ii. Sign Size and Height: Open House Signs shall not exceed 18 by 24 inches, and the sign

rider shall not exceed 9 by 24 inches.

iii. Timing: Open House Signs shall be placed no earlier than 7:00 A.M. and removed no later

than 7:00 P.M.

iv. Separation: Open House Signs shall not be placed any closer than six feet from another

open house sign.

v. Signs shall not be designed to replicate traffic or safety signs.

Signs not readable from the public right-of-way, including:

a. Signs or displays located entirely inside of a building, within a courtyard, open-air pedestrian

space, or similar open area and not visible from the building’s exterior;

b. Signs intended to be readable from within a parking area or City park but not readable beyond

the boundaries of the lot or parcel upon which they are located or from any public right-of-way; and

c. Signs located within City Recreation Facilities.

Certain Historic and Architectural Features. Historical plaques erected and maintained by non profit organizations, memorials, building cornerstones, and date-constructed stones not exceeding four square feet in area.

4-90.030 Sign Permits

A. The procedures for submittal, review, and approval of Permanent Signs are provided in Section 2 40.100 (Sign Permit - Permanent Sign Structures) while the procedures for Temporary Sign Permits are provided in Section 2-40.110 (Sign Permits – Temporary Signs), including any required fees.

B. A Sign Permit is not required for a sign on property used exclusively for a single-family residence or

duplex.

245

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

C. Nonstructural Modifications and Maintenance.

A Sign Permit is not required for the following:

Changes to the face or copy of changeable signs;

Changes to the face or copy of an existing single-tenant or multi-tenant freestanding or building

mounted non-illuminated sign from one business to another with no structural or lighting modifications to the sign; and

The normal repair and maintenance of conforming or legal non-conforming signs, except as

identified in Subsection 4-90.040.E. (Sign Maintenance).

4-90.040 General Restrictions for All Signs

A. Location Restrictions.

Except where specifically authorized in this Division, signs must not be placed in the following locations:

Within, on, or projecting over public property including City rights-of-way, except as specifically

provided in this Division;

Any sign attached to any public utility pole, structure or streetlight, tree, fence, fire hydrant, bridge,

curb, sidewalk, park bench, statue, memorial, or other location on public property, except those signs approved as part of a special event permit on City property or banner signs permitted by the City on light poles within the City;

Any location that obstructs the view of any authorized traffic sign, signal, or other traffic control

device or which by reason of shape, color, or position interferes with or could be confused with any authorized traffic signal or device;

Any sign which is placed in a manner that would prevent or inhibit free ingress to or egress from

any door, window, vent, or any exit way required by the Building Code, or by Fire Department regulations (currently in effect);

Off the premises to which the sign refers, except as provided in Section 4-90.070 (Standards for

Temporary Signs);

Mounted, attached, or painted on a trailer, boat, or motor vehicle when parked, stored, or

displayed conspicuously on private premises in a manner intended to attract attention of the public for the purpose of advertising or identifying the business premises. This provision excludes signs indicating the name of the owner or business that are permanently painted or wrapped on the surface of the vehicle, adhesive vinyl film affixed to the interior or exterior surface of a vehicle window, or signs magnetically attached to motor vehicles or rolling stock that are actively used in the daily conduct of the business. Vehicles must be operable and parked in a lawful or authorized manner;

Painted, attached, or mounted on fuel tanks, storage containers and/or solid waste receptacles or

their enclosures, except for a manufacturer’s or installer’s identification, appropriate warning signs and placards, and information required by law;

Areas where a sign would cover the architectural features of a building, such as dormers,

insignias, pilasters, soffits, transoms, trims, or another architectural feature;

Tacked, painted, pasted, or otherwise affixed, to the walls of any building, barn or shed, accessory

structure, or on trees, poles, posts, fences, ladders, or other structures that are visible from a public way; and

246

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

Within the site visibility triangle (Refer to Section 4-60.040) that must be observed at all in street

intersections and the intersections of dedicated alleys or driveways with streets.

B. Display Restrictions.

This Subsection regulates the manner in which signs convey their messages by specifying prohibited display features that create distractions to the traveling public and visual clutter that mars the natural and architectural aesthetics of the City. Signs with the following display features are prohibited:

Any sign or lighting device, whether on the exterior of a building or on the inside of a window

which is visible beyond the boundaries of the lot or parcel, or from any public right-of-way, with intermittent, flashing, rotating, blinking or strobe light illumination;

Any sign with an exposed light source, except for neon LED strip lights incorporated into the

design of the sign;

Any sign which emits sound, odor, smoke, laser or hologram lights, or other visible matter,

including any sign that uses motion picture projection;

Any sign animated by any means, including fixed aerial displays, balloons, pennants, spinners,

including strings of flags, streamers, tubes, or other devices affected by the movement of the air or other atmospheric or mechanical means;

Any sign in which the sign body or any portion of the sign rotates, moves up and down, or any

other type of action involving a change in position of the sign body or any portion of the sign, whether by mechanical or any other means. Barber poles no larger than three feet high and 10 inches in diameter, and clocks, are excepted from this restriction; and

Strings of lights arranged in the shape of a product, arrow, or any commercial message.

C. Prohibited Sign Types.

Bandit signs;

Stuffed or inflated animals or characters used as signs;

Any sign which advertises a business no longer in existence or a product or service no longer

being sold, except landmark signs; and

Any signs not specifically allowed in this Division.

4-90.050 General Requirements for All Signs

A. Sign Message.

Any permitted sign may contain, in lieu of any other message or copy, any lawful non-commercial message, so long as the sign complies with the size, height, area, location, and other requirements of this Division.

B. Rules of Measurement.

Sign Area Measurement. Sign area is measured as follows:

a. Signs on Background Panel . Where the sign copy is mounted, affixed, or painted on a

background panel or distinctively painted, textured, or constructed surface, the sign area is measured as the sum of the smallest rectangle(s) that will enclose both the sign copy and the background, as shown in Figure 4-90.050.1.

247

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

Figure 4-90.050.1, Signs on Background Panel

b. Signs with Individual Letters . Where the sign has individual letters or graphics mounted

against a wall, fascia, mansard, parapet, or other building surface that has not been distinctively painted, textured or constructed as a background panel, the sign area is measured as a sum of the smallest rectangle(s) that will enclose each word and each graphic in the total sign except for the descending elements of lower-case letters, as shown in Figure 4-90.050.2. Sign area for individual letters or graphics will be counted as 75 percent of the area enclosing the sign copy.

Figure 4-90.050.2, Signs with Individual Letters

c. Signs on Illuminated Surfaces . Where the sign is mounted, affixed, or painted on an

illuminated surface or illuminated element of a building or structure, the sign area is measured as the entire illuminated surface or illuminated element, which contains sign copy, as shown in Figure 4-90.050.3.

248

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

Figure 4-90.050.3, Signs on an Illuminated Surface

d. Irregular Shaped Signs . Sign area for irregular shaped signs is determined by dividing the

sign into squares, rectangles, triangles, circles, or arcs as shown in Figure 4-90.050.4.

Figure 4-90.050.4, Irregular Shaped Signs

e. Multi-Face Signs . The sign area for multi-face signs, as shown in Figure 4-90.050.5, are

measured as follows:

(i). Two-Face Signs . Where the interior angle between the two sign faces is 45 degrees or

less and the sign faces are less than 42 inches apart, the sign area is measured as the area of one sign face only. Where the angle between the two sign faces is greater than 45 degrees, the sign area is the sum of the areas of the two sign faces.

(ii). Three- or Four-Face Signs . The allowable sign area is measured as 50 percent of the

sum of the areas of all sign faces.

249

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

Figure 4-90.050.5, Multi-Face Signs

f. Spherical, Free-Form, or Sculptural Signs . The sign area is measured by encasing the longest portion of the sign with four dimensionally equal lines, and the shortest portion of the sign with at least two dimensionally equal lines to form a cuboid. The sign area is 50 percent of the sum of the areas between the longest four dimensionally equal lines encompassing the cuboid, as shown in Figure 4-90.050.6.

Figure 4-90.050.6, Spherical, Free-Form, or Sculptural Signs

Sign Height Measurement .

a. Freestanding Signs . Sign height for freestanding signs is measured as the vertical distance

from the finished grade to the top of the sign, exclusive of any filling, berming, mounding or landscaping solely for the purpose of locating the sign as shown in Figure 4-90.050.7, excluding decorative embellishments as permitted by the provisions of this Section.

250

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

Figure 4-90.050.7, Freestanding Sign Height

(i). Higher Than Adjacent Grade . Where the natural grade at the base of a sign is higher

than the grade of the adjacent road, sign height is measured from the base of the sign, as shown in Figure 4-90.050.8.

Figure 4-90.050.8, Freestanding Sign Height, Higher Than Adjacent Grade

(ii). Lower Than Adjacent Grade . Where the natural grade at the base of a sign is lower

than the grade of an adjacent road, the height of the sign is measured from the top of curb elevation, as shown in Figure 4-90.050.9.

251

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

Figure 4-90.050.9, Freestanding Sign Height, Lower Than Adjacent Grade

b. Wall Signs. The height of building-mounted signs is the vertical distance measured from the

base of the wall on which the sign is located to the top of the sign or sign structure, as shown in Figure 4-90.050.10.

Figure 4-90.050.10, Wall Sign Height

C. Sign Illumination.

Internal Illumination . Internally illuminated signs include cabinet signs, single- or two-color LED

signs, signs constructed with pan channel letters, or indirect halo illuminated channel letters on an unlit or otherwise indistinguishable background on a freestanding sign or building wall.

a. To minimize glare, internally illuminated signs such as cabinet signs must either be constructed

with an opaque background and translucent text and symbols, or with a colored (not white, offwhite, light gray, or cream) background and generally lighter text and symbols as shown in Figure 4-90.050.11.

252

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

Figure 4-90.050.11, Internal Illumination

b. Cabinet signs may be used only in non-residential zones provided the illumination intensity

does not exceed 0.5-foot candles at the property line.

External Illumination.

a. Externally illuminated signs must be illuminated only with steady, stationary, fully shielded light

sources directed solely onto the sign without causing glare.

b. The light source for externally illuminated signs must be arranged and shielded to substantially

confine all direct light rays to the sign face and away from streets and adjacent properties as illustrated in Figure 4-90.050.12.

Figure 4-90.050.12, External Illumination

Neon.

a. Exposed neon sign lighting on permanent signs and single-color or two-color LED signs are only

allowed in non-residential zones.

b. Neon signs placed in a window are counted toward the total aggregate area for all window

signs.

Single-Color or Two-Color LED Signs.

a. Single-color or two-color LED signs are exempt from the sign area limitations for wall signs

and window signs established in Table 4-90.060.M (Standards for Wall Signs) and Table 490.060.N (Standards for Window Signs).

b. Any individual single-color or two-color LED sign must not exceed four square feet in area.

253

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

c. Single-color or two-color LED signs must be turned off daily at the close of business or 10:00

pm, whichever occurs last.

Electronic Message Signs.

a. One electronic message sign may be allowed as an integral component of a freestanding or

wall sign (Refer to Table 4-90.060.M (Standards for Wall Signs) and Table 4-90.060.Q (Standards for Monument Signs)) per lot or parcel in non-residential zones and for nonresidential uses in all zones.

b. Electronic message signs must not flash, blink, flutter, include intermittent or chasing lights, or

display video messages (i.e., any illumination or message that is in motion or that appears to be in motion). Electronic message signs may display changing messages provided that each message is displayed for no less than 15 seconds.

c. Night-time Brightness.

(i). Automatic Control . Electronic message signs must be equipped with photocell

technology to control and vary the intensity of light output depending on the amount of ambient light that is present to prevent overly bright luminance at night. Automatic controls must limit night luminance to a maximum of 100 nits when the display is set to show maximum brightness in 100 percent full white mode.

(ii). Manufacturer Certification . The applicant shall include with the Building Permit

application a written certification from the sign manufacturer that the nighttime luminance has been factory pre-set not to exceed 100 nits as described in paragraph c.(i). above, and that this setting is protected from end-user modification by password-protected software or other method as deemed appropriate by the Building Official.

d. Electronic message signs must be turned off daily at the close of business or 10 p.m.,

whichever occurs first.

e. Within the Planned Development Zones, the following additional standards apply:

(i). Kingman Crossing Planned Development Zone (KC-PDZ-1806).

(a). Intensity of Lighting. Between sunrise and sunset electronic message signs shall

be limited in brightness to a maximum lighting intensity of 0.6 candela (footcandles) over ambient light levels as measured using a foot-candle meter placed 100 feet from the sign.

(b). Control of Brightness. Each sign shall have a redundant system for controlling

sign brightness, including an automatic light sensing device or photocell that will adjust the brightness as ambient light conditions change, and a backup system based on local sunrise and sunset times.

(ii). Reserved for Future Use.

Changeable Sign Copy.

a. The maximum area of changeable sign copy is limited to only wall signs and monument signs

as established in Table 4-90.060.M (Standards for Wall Signs), and Table 4-90.060.Q (Standards for Monument Signs). This does not apply to any signs required by law.

b. The changeable sign copy must be an integral part of a permanent building-mounted or

freestanding sign.

c. Changeable sign copy may only be non-illuminated or internally illuminated.

Billboards.

254

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

a. Lighting fixtures used to illuminate a billboard must be mounted on the top of the sign structure.

All lighting fixtures must comply with the shielding requirements of Section 4-70.030.B (Lamp Source and Shielding)) and the time limitations provided in Subsection 4-90.050.C.

b. Billboards which exceed 301 square feet of area per sign face and consist of panels which are

designed to be installed and removed from the top of the structure are exempt from the provisions of Subsection 4-90.050.C if illuminated by an approved bottom-mounted outdoor advertising fixture(s) equipped with an automatic time control device which shuts off the fixture(s) between the hours of 11:00 pm and sunrise.

c. Bottom-mounted outdoor advertising fixture consist of no more than more than four individual

fixtures per sign face, produces a maximum of 40,000 lumens per fixture, and spills or casts beyond the sign face no more than two-and-one-half percent of the lumen output per fixture.

d. Billboards that include electronic message technology must be separated by a minimum of 600

feet measured from the sign base to any other billboard with an electronic message on the same side of the street. These signs must be located no closer than 300 feet from any off-premises sign without electronic message technology.

e. Billboards using electronic message technology must also comply with the following standards:

(i). Minimum Display Time. Any portion of the sign message that uses intermittently

changing displays must have a minimum duration of eight seconds.

(ii). Operational Limitations. Electronic message displays must be limited to messages

that appear or disappear from the display through dissolve, fade, travel or scroll modes, or similar transitions and frame effects that have text, animated graphics or images that appear to move or change in size or be revealed sequentially rather than all at once.

(iii). Intensity of Lighting.

(a). Between sunset and 11:00 pm the dimmer for electronic message displays must

be set to a maximum of 200 nits in full white mode.

(b). From 11:00 pm until sunrise, all illumination on a billboard must be extinguished.

Electronic message displays must be equipped with an automatic device to ensure compliance with these time limitations, except for amber alerts and other governmental emergencies. The automatic device must have the ability to dim the display through a photoelectric sensor that detects ambient light levels and adjusts the display intensity automatically to ensure the maximum nit levels are ensured.

(iv). Control of Brightness. Each billboard must have a redundant system for controlling

sign brightness, including an automatic light sensing device or photocell that will adjust the brightness as ambient light conditions change, and a backup system based on local sunrise and sunset times.

(v). Sign Conversions. Any sign with a static display must not be converted to include

electronic message technology unless it conforms to all applicable standards in this Division.

D. Sign Structure and Installation.

The installation of signs shall be enforced and administered by the Building Official. All signs and

sign structures must be designed to comply with the provisions of this Division, the applicable provisions of the Building Code, and constructed to withstand wind loads, dead loads, and lateral forces.

255

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

a. Any angle iron, bracing, guy wires, or similar features used to support a sign must not be visible

to the extent technically feasible.

b. Electrical service provided to freestanding signs must be placed underground and concealed.

Electrical service to building mounted signs, including conduit, housings, and wire, must be concealed or, when necessary, painted to match the surface of the structure upon which they are mounted. A Building Permit must be issued prior to installation of any new signs requiring electrical service.

c. All permanent signs allowed by this Division must be constructed of durable materials capable

of withstanding continuous exposure to the elements and the conditions of the natural environment, and must be permanently attached to the ground, a building, or another structure by direct attachment to a rigid wall, frame, or structure.

d. Raceway cabinets, as illustrated in Figure 4-90.050.13, must only be used in building mounted

signs when access to the wall behind the sign is not feasible, or when the Director determines that a benefit exists to preserve a historic or architectural feature of a building. In such cases, the raceway cabinet must not extend in width and height beyond the area of the sign and must match the color of the building to which it is attached. Where a raceway cabinet provides a contrast background to sign copy, the colored area is counted in the total allowable sign area allowed for the site or business.

Figure 4-90.050.13, Raceway Cabinet

E. Sign Maintenance .

All signs shall be maintained by any property owner, lessor, lessee, manager, agent, or other

person having lawful possession or control over a sign, building, structure, or parcel of land, in a condition or state of equivalent quality to which was approved or required by the City.

All signs together with their supports and appurtenances must be maintained in good structural

condition, in compliance with applicable Building Codes, and in conformance with this Division. Maintenance of a sign includes periodic cleaning, replacement of flickering, burned out or broken light bulbs or fixtures, repair or replacement of any faded, peeled, cracked, or otherwise damaged or broken parts of a sign, and any other activity necessary to restore the sign so that it continues to comply with the requirements and contents of the sign permit issued for its installation and provisions of this Division.

Required landscaped areas contained by a fixed border, curbed area, wall, or other perimeter

structure must receive regular repair and maintenance. Plant materials that do not survive after installation in required landscape areas are required to be replaced within three months.

The Building Official is authorized to order the repair, maintenance, or removal of any sign or sign

structure that has not been maintained and is dangerous or in disrepair, or which is erected or maintained contrary to the requirements of this Section.

256

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

Failure to maintain a sign constitutes a violation of this Division and will be subject to enforcement

action, in which case the Building Inspector may order the removal of any sign that is determined to be in disrepair or dangerous.

4-90.060 Standards for Permanent Signs

A. Applicability.

This Section establishes the standards for permanent signs.

Table 4-90.060.A1 and Table 4-90.060.A2 establish which sign types are allowed in each zone. Any

combination of allowed sign types may be used within a given zone unless specifically prohibited.

A sign type not specifically included in this Section may be allowed by the Director provided the sign

meets the intent of the zone in which it is proposed.

A permanent freestanding sign is only allowed in a parcel on which a primary use or building has

been established.

B. Sign Design Guidelines.

General sign design standards applicable to all permanent signs are included in Appendix 4 (Sign Design Guidelines).

C. Standards for Permanent Building-Mounted and Freestanding Signs.

The maximum total area for all building-mounted and freestanding signs is established in Table 4 90.060.B (Dimensional Standards for Permanent Signs).

The area of all building-mounted signs is included in the maximum total sign area for wall signs,

except when specifically exempted.

All permanent building-mounted and freestanding signs must comply with the corresponding sign

type standards provided in Tables 4-90.060.B to Table 4-90.060.S.

D. Standards for Permanent Building-Mounted Signs.

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

Awning Signs. Awning signs must comply with the standards provided in Table 4-90.060.C.

Table 4-90.060.C: Standards for Awning Signs
Col2
Standards
Requirements
Max. Sign Area
1 sf per linear foot of awning width.
Max. Sign Height
Min. 8 ft from the bottom of the awning to the nearest grade or
sidewalk.

Max. 20 ft.
Sign Placement
Must only be placed above the doors and windows of the ground
floor.
Awnings must not project above, below, or beyond the edges of the
face of a building wall or architectural element.
Sign width must not be greater than 60% of the width of the awning
face or valance on which it is displayed.
If an awning is placed on multiple storefronts, each business is
permitted signage no greater than 60% of the width of the storefront.
May project into public right-of-way with approval of an

Encroachment Permit.
Max. Valance Height
9 in.
Min. Horizontal Distance from Back

of Curb
2 ft.
Illumination
Non-illuminated or illumination allowed under the awning.



265

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

Billboard Signs. Billboard Signs must comply with the standards provided in Table 4-90.060.D.

Table 4-90.060.D: Standards for Billboard Signs
Col2
Standards
Requirements
Location of Billboards
Allowed with a CUP in the C-3, I-1, and I-2 Zones within 200 ft of the
right-of way of:
• Interstate 40;
• E. Andy Devine Avenue; and
• U.S. Highway 93/W. Beale Street
Also allowed on municipally-owned property with a CUP in the ROS

Zone within 200 ft of the right-of-way of Interstate 40.
Max. Billboard Area
300 sf per sign face.
In the Interstate 40 Corridor (Refer to the map in Section 3-70.040)

672 sf; Max. dimensions 14 ft x 48 ft.
Max. Billboard Structure Height
Measured from natural grade at

the base of the billboard
C-3, I-1 and I-2 Zones: max. 40 ft.

Interstate 40 Corridor: max. 60 ft.
Sign Placement
Prohibited in the Interstate 40 Corridor between the point of
beginning of the taper of the exit ramp through the point ending of
the taper of the entrance ramp on each side of the traffic
interchange.
Static display: Min. separation of 300 ft from any other billboard on
the same side of the street.
Electronic message display: Min. separation of 600 ft from a
billboard using electronic message technology on the same side of

the street.
Illumination
Refer to Section 4-90.050.C.7.
Other Requirements
Sign faces must be perpendicular to the right-of-way of the

permitted street frontage.

4-90.070 Standards for Temporary Signs

A. Purpose. The Council finds that the proliferation of temporary signs is a distraction to the traveling

public and creates aesthetic blight and litter that threatens the public’s health, safety, and welfare. In addition to the purpose provisions of Section 4-90.010 (Purpose and Intent), these regulations ensure that temporary signs do not create a distraction to the traveling public by eliminating the aesthetic blight and litter caused by temporary signs.

B. General to All. Temporary signs are allowed only in compliance with the provisions of this Section.

Permits. A Temporary Sign Permit is required to display a temporary wall banner sign; all other

temporary sign types do not require a Sign Permit. Each tenant in a multi-tenant building is entitled to a temporary wall banner sign in accordance with this Section.

Not Included in Permanent Sign Allowances. The area of temporary signs is not counted toward

the maximum total sign area established in Section 4-90.060 (Standards for Permanent Signs).

General Time, Place, and Manner Restrictions.

a. Time Restrictions. There is no limitation on the length of time that a temporary sign may be

displayed, except that the maximum display time for a wall banner is 30 days per calendar year.

281

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

b. Location Restrictions . Unless specifically exempted by this Section or the requirements of

A.R.S. § 16-1019 ), temporary signs must be placed in compliance with Subsection 4-90.040.A (Location Restrictions).

c. Prohibited Elements. Temporary signs must comply with the provisions of Section 4 90.040.B (Display Restrictions).

d. Design and Construction. Temporary signs must be of sufficient weight and durability to

withstand wind gusts, storms, etc. and must be professionally crafted.

C. Additional Standards for Temporary Signs.

Temporary signs are allowed in compliance with provisions in Table 4-90.070.E (Standards for

Temporary Signs).

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

Temporary Model Home Complex/Active Residential Subdivision Signs.

In addition to other signs that are permitted in association with a single-family residence, a model home complex or active subdivision consisting of 50 lots or greater may employ the following:

a. One Yard Sign Type I meeting the requirements of Table 4-90.070.F herein for each lot

located within the boundaries of an approved model home complex and one per boundary street frontage of the model home complex/active residential subdivision. Such signs shall not be illuminated except by ambient lighting.

b. One Yard Sign Type II meeting the requirements of Table 4-90.070.F herein for each lot

located within the boundaries of an approved model home complex. Such signs shall not be illuminated except by ambient lighting.

c. One Yard Sign Type III meeting the requirements of Table 4-90.070.F herein may be located

within the boundaries of the approved model home complex; however, if the model home complex is located adjacent to a collector or arterial street, the overall height of the Yard Sign Type III allowed under this paragraph may be increased to 144 inches with a maximum sign area of 48 square feet per side and a maximum height of 12 feet, provided the sign is designed to withstand wind loads as required by the building codes in effect at the time the sign is erected. A Yard Sign Type III permitted under this paragraph may be externally illuminated as outlined in Section 4-90.050.C.2.

d. One flagpole not exceeding a height of 45 feet may be erected within the boundaries of the

model home complex provided said flagpole is located within the building envelope or within the required front yard of the lot in which it is placed, but not located within any required side or required rear yard. A maximum of two flags may be displayed from said flagpole. The flag and flagpole permitted under this paragraph may be externally illuminated in accordance with Section 4-70.020.K of this Code.

e. Two flagpoles not exceeding a height of 20 feet may be erected for each lot located within the

boundaries of the approved model home complex. A maximum of one flag may be displayed from each flagpole. Flagpoles permitted under this section may be erected along the perimeter of the model home complex. Flagpoles permitted under this paragraph shall not be illuminated except by ambient lighting.

285

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

f. Six (6) total feather banner signs meeting the requirements of Table 4-90.070.F. Feather

banner signs permitted under this section may be placed along the boundary of the subdivision and shall not be illuminated except by ambient lighting.

g. One banner sign not exceeding three feet in height and five feet in length may be attached to

the trap fence. Said banner sign shall be equipped with ventilation flaps. Banner signs permitted under this paragraph shall not be illuminated except by ambient lighting.

h. One awning sign or canopy sign provided the awning or canopy is limited to the customer

entrance to the sales office associated with the approved model home complex. Awning signs or canopy signs permitted under this paragraph shall not be illuminated except by ambient lighting.

i. One building wall sign not exceeding four square feet may be affixed to the front elevation of

the model home sales office facing the street from which the model home complex takes access. Said sign may be externally illuminated or back-lit in accordance with Section 490.050.C.

j. Information regarding model home signs shall be included with the Temporary Use Permit to

be reviewed and approved with the model home complex. In circumstances where model home complexes in existence as of the effective date of this chapter are to be retrofitted for purposes of implementing signage permitted under this section, an amendment to the approved model home complex is required.

k. All model home signs permitted under this section shall be removed upon the conversion of

the model homes to a residential use.

4. Flagpoles (active single-family residential subdivision signs).

a. Where an active single-family residential subdivision includes an open space tract or

landscape tract located adjacent to a collector street or arterial street, a series of flagpoles not exceeding 20 feet in height may be erected within said tract provided said flagpoles are spaced no closer than 100 feet apart; however, flagpoles permitted under this paragraph may not be located adjacent to any local street regardless of the location of the tract relative to the local street. Further, flagpoles permitted under this paragraph may not be illuminated except by ambient lighting.

286

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

b. All active single-family residential subdivision signs shall be removed upon the close of

escrow of the last lot in the subdivision or upon cessation of use, whichever comes first.

Yard Sign Type III (temporary active single-family residential subdivision signs)

In addition to other signs that may be permitted in association with an active single-family residential subdivision, the following signs are permitted subject to the following time, place, and manner restrictions:

a. On-site: Yard Sign Type III temporary active single-family residential subdivision signs may be

located within the boundaries of an active single-family residential subdivision subject to the following restrictions:

i. The Yard Sign Type III shall meet the requirements of Table 4-90.070.F.

ii. One Yard Sign Type III may be located at the primary entry(ies) to the active subdivision.

iii. One Yard Sign Type III may be located within the perimeter landscape area along each

arterial or collector street frontage of the active subdivision.

iv. No more than four Yard Sign Type IIIs may be permitted per subdivision.

v. The Type III sign shall not be located within the public rights-of-way.

vi. The Yard Sign Type III may be externally illuminated as outlined in Section 4 90.050.C.2.

vii. The overall height of the Yard Sign Type III allowed under this paragraph may be

increased to 144 inches, provided the sign is designed to withstand wind loads as required by the building codes in effect at the time the sign is erected.

b. Off-site: Yard Sign Type III temporary active single-family residential subdivision signs may be

located outside the boundary of the active subdivision subject to the approval of a Temporary Use Permit and the following restrictions:

i. The Yard Sign Type III shall meet the requirements of Table 4-90.070.F.

ii. Two off-site Yard Sign Type III shall be permitted in conjunction with a model home

complex or active subdivision.

iii. The Yard Sign Type III sign shall be located on a vacant parcel ½ acre or 20,000 square

feet or greater; however, shall be located no closer than 2,500 feet to any other offpremise subdivision sign.

iv. The Yard Sign Type III sign shall not be located within the public rights-of-way.

v. Owner authorization shall be provided at the time of application for a model home

complex or Temporary Use Permit.

vi. The Yard Sign Type III permitted under this paragraph may be externally illuminated as

outlined in Section 4-90.050.C.2.

vii. The overall height of the Yard Sign Type III allowed under this paragraph may be

increased to 120 inches, provided the sign is designed to withstand wind loads as required by the building codes in effect at the time the sign is erected.

viii. Off-Premise subdivision signs shall at all times be maintained free of debris, trash,

weeds, etc.

ix. All active single-family residential subdivision signs shall be removed upon the close of

escrow of the last lot in the subdivision or upon cessation of use, whichever comes first.

287

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

x. Conex containers or other temporary structures for the purposes of advertising active

subdivisions or off-premises signage other than those specified in this section shall be prohibited.

Sign Walkers.

To promote pedestrian and traffic safety, sign walkers are subject to the following time, place, and manner restrictions:

a. Sign walkers are only allowed in the commercial and industrial zones and commercial planned

development zones;

b. Sign walkers are permitted:

(i). To stand on a sidewalk provided a minimum width of at least four feet allows for

unimpeded pedestrian movement.

c. Sign walkers are prohibited from standing in the median of streets, in travel lanes landscaped

areas, and unimproved rights-of-way areas. Sign walkers may not interfere with traffic or block pedestrians or bicyclists;

d. A business is only allowed to use sign walkers for up to 10 consecutive days in any 30-day

period and there must be at least a 20-day period before the commencing of another 10-day period;

e. Sign walkers are permitted only from 8:00 a.m. to 8:00 p.m.;

f. Sign walker signs must not exceed 10 square feet in the area;

g. Sign walker signs that include any of the following are prohibited:

(i). Audio devices to attract attention;

(ii). Any form of illumination, including flashing, blinking, or rotating lights; and

(iii). Animation on the sign itself.

h. Spinning, waving, throwing the sign in the air or any other similar erratic movements intended

to attract attention is prohibited;

i. No shade structure, chair, or other sitting device is permitted for a sign walker; and

j. Sign walkers are not required to get a sign permit.

288

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

4-90.080 Nonconforming Signs

Section 2-60.070 (Nonconforming Signs) provides the standards and regulations for nonconforming signs.

4-90.090 Enforcement

It shall be unlawful for any person, firm, or corporation to erect, construct, enlarge, alter, repair, display, maintain or use a sign within the City contrary to, or in violation of, any provision of this Division. The requirements of this Division shall be enforced in compliance with the enforcement provisions of Division 2-100 (Enforcement) within Title 2.

4-90.100 Severability

If any Section, sentence, clause, phrase, word, portion or provision of the Division is held invalid or unconstitutional by any court of competent jurisdiction, such decision shall not affect, impair, or invalidate any other Section, sentence, clause, phrase, word, portion, or provision of this Division which can be given effect without the invalid provision. The invalidation of the application of any Section, sentence, clause, phrase, word, portion, or provision of this Division to a particular property or structure, or any particular properties or structures, by any court of competent jurisdiction shall not affect the application of such Section, sentence, clause, phrase, word, portion or provision to any other property or structure not specifically included in said invalidation.

289

Title 4: Supplemental Standards

4-110 Specific to Civic Spaces

Sections:

4-110.010 Purpose and Applicability 4-110.020 Civic Space Standards

4-100.030 Purpose and Applicability

A. This Division describes the civic space types and standards. Each civic space type and the

associated standards are based on the context and intensity established by the intended development pattern.

B. The civic space standards may be applied in all zones throughout the City as determined by the

Director. Table 3-60.030.C (Allowed Civic Space Types) establishes the Downtown District Zone in which each civic space is allowed.

Title 5: Definitions
Title 5: Definitions
5-10 Purpose

This Title provides definitions of terms and phrases, land use types, and signage terms used in this Code that are technical or specialized, or that may not reflect common usage. If any of the definitions in this Title conflict with definitions in other provisions of this Code, these definitions shall control. If a word is not defined in this Title, or in other provisions of this Code, it shall assume the common meaning of the word or phrase. In situations where other clarification is needed, the Director shall determine the correct definition.

Remainder of page intentionally blank.

307

Title 5: Definitions

5-20 Definitions of Terms

5-20.010 Definitions “A”

Accessory Buildings and Structures: Buildings or structures (including, but not limited to, sheds, barns, garages, carports, artist or craft studios, home offices, greenhouses, detached solar systems, or shade structures) which are subordinate in floor area, intensity of use, and purpose to the principal building or use on a lot and which are located on the same lot as the principal building or use. Does not include playhouses with an area less than 200 square feet or similar play equipment.

Accessory Dwelling Unit (ADU): A subordinate living unit detached from, added to, or created within a primary dwelling that provides basic requirements for independent living, sleeping, eating, cooking, and sanitation. If the ADU is attached to the primary dwelling it shares a common wall or roof structure with the primary dwelling.

Accessory Wind Energy System: A wind energy conversion system consisting of wind turbines that convert wind power to mechanical energy.

Accessory Wind Energy System, Small DC: A wind energy conversion system consisting of wind turbines, a tower, and associated control or conversion electronics, which has a rated capacity of 10 KW (kilowatts) or less to be used primarily for on-site consumption.

Access: The location, means, or way by which pedestrians and vehicles shall have safe, adequate and usable ingress and egress to a property or use as required by the Subdivision Ordinance and this Code.

Acre: Forty-three thousand five hundred and sixty (i.e. 43,560) square feet of land area.

Active Single-Family Residential Subdivision : A subdivision consisting of single-family lots that are offered for sale to the public by a homebuilder or the master developer of the subdivision.

Agricultural Operation (Syn. Farming, Ranching): Farming, including plowing, tillage, cropping, and installation of best management practices for harvesting of food or fiber products, wholesale landscape nursery stock, and/or the breeding, boarding, or pasturing of agricultural animals.

Airport: A licensed facility where commercial, military, and private aircraft may land or take off and where additional accessory space is provided for repairs, services, storage facilities, and offices, and buildings for administration and passenger convenience.

Alley: A public or private right-of-way primarily designed to serve as a secondary access to the side or rear of those properties whose principal frontage is on some other street.

Alter or Alteration: Any change in size, shape, character, occupancy, or use of a building or structure that requires a permit under this Code or the Building Code.

Amusement Park (Syn. Theme Park): A facility where there are various devices for entertainment, including, but not limited to, rides, booths for playing of games or selling of items, and buildings or structures for shows and entertainment.

Animal Boarding Facility: An enclosed building or area within a building in which five or more dogs, cats, or other domestic animals are kept, maintained, trained, boarded, or offered for sale with or without compensation. Operations must be conducted within an enclosed building, unless an outdoor play area is shown on an approved site plan as an accessory use.

Animal Clinic (Syn. Veterinary Clinic): An establishment operated by a veterinarian licensed to practice in the State of Arizona that provides preventative care and minor medical procedures for animals. Crematory facilities are not allowed in a veterinary clinic.

308

Title 5: Definitions

Animal Hospital (Syn. Veterinary Hospital): An establishment operated by a veterinarian licensed to practice in the State of Arizona that provides clinical facilities and houses animals or birds for dental, medical, or surgical treatment. May include pens, stalls, cages, or kennels for quarantine or observation. Does not include commercial boarding facilities.

Animal Shelter: A facility operated by a public agency or private organization for the purpose of impounding, caring for, quarantining, adopting out, or euthanizing domestic animals such as dogs and cats. This may include kennel facilities, veterinary care, administrative offices, and public adoption areas.

Antenna: Any exterior apparatus designed to collect or radiate electromagnetic waves, including but not limited to directional antennas, such as panels, microwave dishes, and satellite dishes, and omnidirectional antennas, such as whip antennas.

Archaeological Resource: A type of cultural resource that includes any material remains or physical evidence of past human life or activities which are of archaeological interest, including the record or evidence of the effects of human activities on the environment.

Architectural Details: Details added to a building to enhance its design, including columns, window and door trim, cornices, ornate brickwork, or turrets.

Applicant (Syn. Engineer, Developer): A property owner or their authorized agent who submits an application to the city and is authorized to receive notices for any action taken by the city regarding the application.

A.R.S.: Arizona Revised Statutes.

Athletic or Fitness Club: A facility where active or passive exercises and related activities are performed primarily within an enclosed building for the purpose of physical fitness, improved circulation, or flexibility, and/or weight control.

Authorized Agent: A person or firm duly authorized by the property owner or developer to submit applications or represent the applicant on his, her, their, or its behalf. This includes monitoring review results and making decisions in the property owner or developer’s name.

Automobile Fueling Service Station and Convenience Store (Syn. Gas Station, Fueling Station): An establishment providing sales of vehicle fuel and such minor services as lubrication, oil and tire changes, and minor repairs. This use does not include any of the services listed under “Automobile, Vehicle Repair Garage – Major”.

Automobile, Truck, and Trailer Rental: An establishment renting automobiles, trucks and vans, trailers, motorcycles, and other motorized vehicles, excluding bicycles. No repair, repainting or remodeling is allowed other than minor repairs which are incidental to the rental of the vehicle.

Automobile, Vehicle Repair Garage – Major: Automobile repair or vehicle repair garages. The general repair, rebuilding, or reconditioning of engines, motor vehicles, or trailers, including but not limited to, engine or transmission rebuilding, collision service such as body, frame, or fender straightening and repair, vehicle painting or paint stripping, tire shops, welding or fabrication; axle, differential, or drive shaft work.

Automobile, Vehicle Repair Garage – Minor: Automobile repair or vehicle repair garages that are limited to installation of minor parts only and none of the activities specified under “Automobile, Vehicle Repair Garage – Major”.

Automobile, Vehicle Sales and Service, New and Used and Leasing : A retail or wholesale establishment selling and/or leasing of automobiles, trucks and vans, trailers, motorcycles, and other motor vehicles new or used.

309

Title 5: Definitions

Automobile Washing Facility (Syn. Car Wash): A facility for the washing or steam cleaning of passenger vehicles; includes automatic and non-automatic, self-service, and full-service facilities.

Awning: A roof-like cover which projects from the wall of a building. Generally used to shield a window, doorway, sidewalk, or other spaces from the elements.

5-20.020 Definitions “B”

Bar (Syn. Tavern): A business, which is not part of a larger restaurant, where alcoholic beverages are sold for on-site consumption. Includes bars, taverns, pubs, and similar establishments where any food service is subordinate to the sale of alcoholic beverages. Does not include sexually oriented businesses, which are separately defined.

Basement: A portion of a building located partially or wholly underground, having less than 50 percent of its clear floor-to-ceiling height above grade.

Base Station: The foundation, generally consisting of equipment and non-tower supporting structures, on which a wireless communication facility and other support equipment are situated.

Bed and Breakfast Establishment: Short-term accommodations offered by the owners or occupants of a single-family home, consisting of a room for the night and breakfast the next morning for one inclusive price. No more than five guest rooms may be provided within the same dwelling or accessory dwelling unit, if more than five guest rooms are provided on a single property then the use shall be deemed a hotel.

Bedroom: A room in a residence marketed, designed, or likely to function primarily for sleeping.

Block: Property fronting on one (1) side of a street and so bounded by other streets, canals, railroad rights-of-way, unsubdivided acreage, or other barriers (except alleys) of sufficient magnitude as to interrupt the continuity of development on both sides thereof.

BMX Racetrack: A dirt racetrack for the exclusive purpose of bicycle racing, including necessary accessory structures for parking, staging, control, and seating.

Boat: A small vessel or watercraft propelled on water by oars, sails, or one or more engines. A boat is not considered a recreation vehicle (RV) even though it may have facilities for temporary living quarters.

Breezeway: A roofed passageway, open on at least one side, providing direct access between ingress/egress points of two otherwise separate structures or two parts of a single structure.

Buffer: An area of land with natural or planted landscaping or other features intended to visually, audibly, or physically separate buildings, uses, or lots from each other or from right-of-way.

Building: A roofed structure built, maintained, or intended to be used for the shelter or enclosure of persons, animals, or property of any kind.

Building, Attached: A building having one or more common wall with another building when the primary uses of each building is independent of each other and there are no interior entries or exits between buildings.

Building, Detached: A building having no common wall or structural connection with another building.

Buildable Area: The portion of a lot or parcel available for development of buildings or structures, excluding those portions of the lot or parcel which must be reserved for open spaces, yard spaces, building line setbacks, or other purposes required by this Code.

310

Title 5: Definitions

Building Code: The currently in effect codes of the City of Kingman that establish minimum acceptable levels of safety for the construction, renovation, demolition, and occupancy of buildings and structures.

Building Coverage: The portion of a lot, expressed as a percentage, that is covered by all buildings and structures including accessory buildings and decks. The following are not included when determining lot coverage: paved areas, uncovered parking areas, single-level unenclosed covered parking areas such as a carport (unless the roof space is used for any use or activity), structures supporting solar collectors, breezeways, driveways, walks, patios, terraces, swimming pools, and landscape areas.

Building Face: The exterior elevation (front, rear, or side) of a building.

Building Site: A parcel of land occupied or intended to be occupied by a building or buildings and any accessory uses or buildings, together with all open space and yard requirements by this Code. Does not include any street, alley access, easements, or other right-of-way necessary for access to this property or as a means of access through the property to other properties.

Building Official: The Chief of Building Inspectors or his/her official designee, who is charged with the administration and enforcement of the Building Code.

Building Permit: A permit issued by the City to grant permission to do work on a regulated building or structure including for vertical construction, increased square footage, changes to land use, or additions to a residential or non-residential building or structure pursuant to this Code and the Kingman Municipal Code.

Bus, Rail Passenger Terminal: A facility for passengers utilizing public and commuter transportation including railroad or bus terminals. May include outdoor storage for the bus or rail company as an accessory use, subject to the requirements of this Code.

Business: Any individual, corporation, or partnership engaged in a commercial activity designed to produce income or profit including the sale, barter, or exchange of goods, wares, merchandise, or service. Also, could include the maintenance or operation of offices.

By-Right: A proposal or component of a proposal that complies with this Code, and may thereby be processed administratively, without public hearing.

5-20.030 Definitions “C”

Caliper: A measurement of the size of a tree taken six inches from above ground level for trees measured by a fixed arm scale and moveable arm tool.

Catering Service: An establishment for the preparation of food to be delivered to another location for consumption. Does not include food manufacturing. May be a primary use or an accessory use.

Carport: A permanent roofed structure with not more than two (2) enclosed sides and intended to be used for automobile storage.

Cemetery: Land dedicated, used, or intended to be used for the burial of the dead, including a mausoleum, when operated in conjunction with and within the boundaries of the cemetery.

Certificate of Appropriateness: Documentation that the Heritage Preservation Commission has reviewed and approved plans for proposed construction or development, including, alterations and demolition, in which major impacts on cultural resources have been mitigated.

Certificate of Economic Hardship: Documentation that the Heritage Preservation Commission, in lieu of issuing a Certificate of Appropriateness, has found that necessary mitigation measures would have certain unavoidable negative economic impacts, and are thus not required.

311

Title 5: Definitions

Certificate of No Effect: Documentation that the Heritage Preservation Commission has reviewed and approved plans for proposed construction or development, including, alterations and demolition, when there is no major impact on a cultural resource.

Certificate of Occupancy : An official document issued by the Building Official certifying that a building has been constructed in accordance with all applicable codes and that it is suitable to occupy.

Change of Use: The cessation of activities or conditions associated with a principal use of a property and the commencement of activities or conditions associated with a different principal use. A use shall be deemed to be changed when a change in the distinguishing traits or primary features of the use of a building or land, as evidenced by a change in parking requirements, change in occupancy designation and/or occupancy load, change in outdoor storage or other features occur after the effective date of this Code.

City: The City of Kingman, Arizona.

Civic Use : Indoor or outdoor not-for-profit or governmental facilities dedicated to arts, culture, education, recreation, government, transit, and municipal parking for public or semipublic use. May include civic or governmental facilities such as fire stations, libraries, places of assembly, schools, or public parks. Outdoor civic spaces are typically arranged in a manner that is pedestrian friendly or focused around a central open space with amenities for the public to gather or attend events.

Co-location: The mounting or installation of transmission equipment on an eligible support structure or wireless communications facility for the purposes of transmitting and/or receiving radio frequency signals for communications purposes by two or more different wireless communication service providers.

Commercial: A term collectively defining workplace, office, retail, and lodging functions for the purpose of describing general land use.

Commercial Campground: An area of land designed and designated to temporarily accommodate two or more camping parties, including cabins, tents, recreational vehicles, travel trailers, or a mix of the aforementioned uses.

Commercial Recreation Facilities, Indoor: These uses include all indoor commercial recreation and amusement facilities (except "Adult Entertainment" uses) including but not limited to bowling alleys, indoor sports arenas, indoor sports facilities, and video arcades. Does not include movie theaters, defined separately under “Theater.”

Commercial Recreation Facilities, Outdoor: These uses include golf driving ranges, outdoor stadiums, rodeos, music arenas, amusements parks, miniature golf establishments, ice- and roller-skating rinks, water slides, batting cages, archery ranges, pistol ranges and shooting ranges.

Commercial Vehicle: Any motor vehicle used for business or institutional purposes including transporting passengers or property that is required by the Arizona Department of Transportation Motor Vehicle Division to be registered as a commercial vehicle.

Common Area: Land in a residential development held in common and/or single ownership and not reserved for the exclusive use or benefit of an individual tenant or owner.

Community Garden: An area where neighbors and residents have the opportunity to collectively manage the cultivation of plants, vegetables, and fruits.

Conditional Use Permit: A permit issued by the Development Services Department upon approval by the City Council authorizing development or establishment of a use that is permitted in a certain zone only when specific conditions are met.

312

Title 5: Definitions

Condominium: The improvement of property in accordance with City standards where an undivided interest in common, in all or a portion of the property is coupled with the right of exclusive occupancy of any unit of airspace thereon.
