A condominium may include an individual interest in common in a portion of the building or buildings; a separate interest in a portion of a building; or a separate interest in a portion of the property together with an undivided interest in common in a portion of the property.

Congregate Care Facility (Syn. Nursing Home): See Institutional Residential.

Construction Supply Yard (Syn. Contractor’s Yard): An outdoor storage facility operated by or on behalf of a contractor for the storage of large equipment, vehicles, and/or materials commonly used in the individual contractor’s type of business. May include an accessory office or buildings for the storage and minor repair of equipment and vehicles.

Context: Refers to the development site itself, surrounding properties, and the neighborhood in which a development site is located.

Convent or Monastery: See Institutional Residential.

Cottage Court: A residential development that consists of a series of small, detached structures on a single lot, providing multiple units that are arranged to define a shared courtyard.

Council: The City Council of the City of Kingman, Arizona.

Cultural Resource: Historic and archaeological resources.

Cultural Resource Study: A document that describes record research and field surveying that identifies, and inventories cultural resources associated with proposed construction or redevelopment and then for such cultural resources, evaluates their significance, identifies and evaluates the potential impacts from the proposed work, and then prescribes mitigation measures designed to minimize any major impacts.

Curb: The edge of the vehicular pavement that may be raised or flush to ground level and may incorporate elements of a drainage system.

Custodial Care Facility: See Institutional Residential.

5-20.040 Definitions “D”

Day Care, Center: A facility regulated by the State that provides supervision for pay for less than 24 hours per day for nine or more children, elderly, or disabled persons unrelated to the proprietor in a facility other than a residence. This includes adult day care or adult day health as defined in A.R.S. § 46-191.1. A day care center for children may provide pre-school curriculum.

Day Care, Home: A home occupation in any dwelling established consistent with the State of Arizona requirements, which receives no more than eight children, elderly, or disabled persons unrelated to the proprietor for less than 24 hours per day for pay. The maximum number of eight children shall be reduced in number by the number of children residing in the dwelling under the age of 14 for a day care for children. This definition includes adult day care or adult day health as defined in A.R.S. § 46-191.1. A home day care for children may provide pre-school curriculum.

Density, Gross Residential: The number of residential dwelling units per gross area of land, typically listed as dwelling units per acre.

Density, Net Residential: The average number of residential dwelling units per acre of land for residential purposes including parks, open space, or recreational areas but excluding any land used or planned to be used as right-of-way, dedicated for public purposes, or nonresidential uses.

313

Title 5: Definitions

Designation : For the purposes of Section 2-40.080 (Heritage Preservation Procedures), the act of adding a historic district zone to a property or properties.

Development: Any building activity, any material change in the use or appearance of any structure or land, or the division of land into lots or parcels by a property owner. This includes, but is not limited to, the following activities or uses:

The reconstruction, alteration of, or material change in the external appearance of a structure;

A change in the intensity of use of land, such as an increase in the number of dwelling units in a

structure or on land, or an increase in the number of businesses, manufacturing establishments, or offices;

Commencement of drilling (except to obtain soil samples), mining, or excavation on a parcel of

land;

Demolition of a building or structure;

Clearing of land as an adjunct to construction, including clearing or removal of vegetation, any

significant disturbance of vegetation, or any soil manipulation; and

The placement of deposition of refuse, solid or liquid waste, or fill on a parcel of land.

Development Services Department: The Development Services Department for the City of Kingman, Arizona. [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

Director : The City of Kingman, Arizona, Development Services Director, or designee. [Ord. 1971, 11/19/2024]

Donation Bins: A container, storage unit, or structure placed for the acceptance of donations of unwanted household items, clothing, or other items of personal property from the public for charitable purposes. Excludes small donation bins placed inside a building or at the external façade of a building intended to collect monetary donations.

Drive Aisle: The travel way by which cars enter and depart parking spaces.

Drive-through: A facility at an establishment that provides services or goods at a window or door that the customer drives past to receive service or goods without being required to leave their vehicle, including the required drive-through stacking area, area in front of order and pick-up windows, and the exit lane or lanes to a public street.

Dry-cleaning: A facility that operates a system to clean clothing or fabrics with chemical solvents that have little or no water.

Duplex (Syn. Two-Family Dwelling): A building or lot containing two dwelling units designed to be occupied by two families living independently of each other. These units may be attached front-to-back, side-to-side or stacked one atop the other.

Front-to-Back: An attached building type with two independent living units with one unit placed

behind the other and sharing a common or fire resistant rated wall.

Side-by-side: An attached building type with two side–by-side independent living units sharing a

common or fire-resistant rated wall.

Stacked: An attached building type with two independent living units stacked one on top of the

other.

Dwelling, Multi-family: A building or lot containing five or more dwelling units used by five or more families living independently of each other.

314

Title 5: Definitions

Dwelling, Single-Family: One dwelling unit on a single lot of record or parcel. Single-family residential uses include all single-family detached dwelling units, factory-built homes, and manufactured homes. Accessory dwelling units (ADUs) may be permitted on a single-family lot, subject to the requirements of this Code.

Dwelling Unit: A single unit, either consisting of a whole building or portion thereof, that provides complete independent living facilities for one or more persons, including provisions for living, sleeping, eating, cooking, and sanitation. The garage area is not considered a part of the dwelling unit’s area.

5-20.050 Definitions “E”

Easement: A grant by the owner for use of land by the public, a corporation or person for designated purposes.

Elevation: An exterior wall of a building often depicted on a scale drawing that shows the relationship of the site grade to floor level and shows design features such as construction materials, height, dimensions, windows, and doors.

Emergency Service: Services to the public for emergencies and related buildings or garages (e.g., ambulance, fire, police, and rescue).

Encroachment: Any structure or structural element that breaks the plane of a setback or above a height limit.

Engineer: A registered professional engineer in the state of Arizona.

Engineering Standards: The Maricopa Association of Governments (MAG) Uniform Standard Specifications for Public Works Construction, as amended by the City of Kingman.

Entitlement: The legal process of obtaining approvals for a development application.

Equestrian Recreational Facility: A commercial facility for horses, donkeys, and/or mules, examples of which include horse ranches, boarding stables, riding schools and academies, horse exhibition facilities (for shows or other competitive events), and barns, stables, corrals, and paddocks accessory and incidental to these uses.

Equipment Rental Yard, Heavy: A service establishment where building materials and construction equipment are stored and serviced for rental or sale.

5-20.060 Definitions “F”

Façade: The exterior wall of a building exposed to public view, extending from grade to roofline and across the full width or height of the structure.

Fairground: A facility where fairs, festivals, displays, exhibitions, and other similar events are held. These events could include the buying and selling of goods; the exhibition of farm products, manufactured goods, and other items; livestock shows; motor sport events (except events that include top fueled and jet fueled vehicles); non-motor sport events; horseracing; and entertainment and various competitions. May include recreational vehicle overnight parking or camping areas for use by vendors or exhibitors during events.

Family: One or more persons, related to the other by blood, marriage or legal adoption, or a group of not more than five not all so related, together with their domestic servants maintaining a common household in a dwelling unit. A family may not include more than two roomers, boarders, or permanent guest not a part of the common household, whether or not gratuitous.

315

Title 5: Definitions

Fence: Any artificially constructed barrier of any material or combination of materials erected to enclose, separate, protect, confine, or screen areas of land. This includes walls which are not part of a building.

Finished Grade: The final elevation or surface level of the ground of a site at the completion of construction.

Flag: A fabric sheet of square, rectangular or triangular shape that is mounted on a pole. This category includes the State, United States, and/or flags of foreign nations. This definition does not include a "banner”.

Flammable Liquid, Gas, and Bulk Fuel – Storage and Sale: Includes liquefied petroleum gas or fuel oil storage for retail sales and storage of flammable liquids, gasses, and bulk fuels.

Fleet-Based Services: Passenger transportation services, local delivery services, medical transport, and other businesses that rely on fleets of three or more vehicles. This would not include towing operations or taxi or delivery services with two or fewer fleet vehicles on-site.

Flood Light: A specific form of light designed to direct its output in a specific direction (a beam) with a reflector formed from the glass envelope of the lamp itself.

Flood Plain: Any land area susceptible to being inundated by water from any source.

Floor Area: The sum of the gross horizontal areas of all floors of a building, or portion thereof. This area shall include accessory storage areas located within selling or working space, such as counters, racks, or closets, and any basement floor area devoted to retailing activities, to the production or processing of goods, or to business or professional offices. May be used to determine the off-street parking or loading requirements.

Freight or Trucking Facility: A facility for the receipt, transfer, short-term storage, and dispatch of goods transported by truck. Includes express and other mail and package distribution facilities.

Frontage: The portion of a property that is contiguous with a street, highway, right-of-way, or access easement. For corner lots, the narrowest portion of a property shall generally be considered the lot frontage; unless natural topography prohibits such.

Funeral Homes, Chapels, or Mortuaries: A state licensed business for the care, storage, and preparation of the deceased prior to burial or cremation. Funeral services may be conducted on the premises.

5-20.070 Definitions “G”

Garage, Private: An accessory building or an accessory portion of a dwelling unit, which is intended for and used for storing of the private passenger vehicles of the family or families residing on the premises.

Garden Supply and Nurseries: A place of business where retail and wholesale products such as plants, nursery products and stock, fertilizers, potting soil, hardware, power equipment and machinery, hoes, rakes, shovels, and other garden and farm tools, and utensils are sold. These centers may include a nursery and/or greenhouses.

General Plan: The comprehensive long-range policy guide for orderly growth and development in the City of Kingman adopted in compliance with the authority provided in A.R.S. § 9-461.05.

Guideline: A non-mandatory statement of desired, recommended, or best practice that allows some discretion or leeway in its interpretation, implementation, or use.

Group Home: See Institutional Residential.

316

Title 5: Definitions

5-20.080 Definitions “H”

Heritage Preservation: The term "heritage preservation" acknowledges that in addition to buildings or districts of buildings and architectural significance, the City also seeks to recognize, preserve, enhance, and perpetuate objects, structures, natural features, sites, places, and areas having historic, archaeological, cultural or aesthetic significance.

Historic Property: A single property with a specific historic district designation that includes development standards and guidelines that are individualized for the specific cultural resource being recognized.

Historic Resource Study: A type of cultural resource study used to locate and evaluate surface and above-ground objects, structures, natural features, sites, or places from the historic period with historical architectural, landscape architectural or cultural importance.

Home Occupation: Any occupation, profession, activity, or use which is conducted in a residence that is clearly an accessory use to the primary use as a dwelling unit and which does not change the exterior of the property, affect the character of the residential use, or bring customer traffic into a residential neighborhood above a level which would be typical for a residential neighborhood.

Homeless Shelter: See Institutional Residential.

Hooded Entry: A roofed gable or shed structure located above the entry door of a building designed to provide some protection from the elements in front of the door, and supported by corbels, one on each side of the door frame.

Hospital: An institution providing health services and medical or surgical care by persons licensed to practice medicine by the state. Commonly includes persons being admitted for an overnight stay or longer in order to obtain medical treatment. The term "hospital" also includes:

Any facility which is devoted primarily to providing psychiatric and related services and programs

for the diagnosis and treatment or care of persons suffering from emotional or nervous illness; and

All places where pregnant women are received, cared for, or treated during delivery, regardless of

the number of patients received.

5-20.090 Definitions “I”

Illumination, Direct: Illumination resulting from light emitted directly from a lamp or luminaire, not light diffused through translucent signs, or reflected from other surfaces such as the ground or building faces.

Illumination, External: Illumination resulting from the face of the sign reflecting light from an external light source intentionally directed upon it.

Illumination, Internal: A form of sign illumination that includes cabinet signs, single-color LED signs, signs constructed with pan channel letters, or indirect halo illuminated channel letters on an unlit or otherwise indistinguishable background on a freestanding sign or building wall.

Improvements: Private and public infrastructure, including but not limited to, all types of roadway construction, streetlights, street and traffic signs, sidewalks, pedestrian ways, bicycle ways, water lines, sanitary sewers, storm drainage structures, grading, and other community facilities of like nature.

Individual: Any person or his agent, lessee, firm, partnership, association, joint venture, corporation, or agent of the aforementioned groups or the state of Arizona or any agency or political subdivision of the state.

317

Title 5: Definitions

Ingress/Egress: The location of entry (ingress) or exit (egress) to a structure or property.

Institutional Residential: Facilities where individuals live under the care, supervision, or control of an organization. Residents are not related and provided services ranging from meals to counseling or medical care. Specific examples are defined below.

Assisted Living Home: An assisted living facility conducted within a single-family dwelling unit

that provides resident rooms for 10 or fewer residents.

Assisted Living Center: An assisted living facility that provides resident rooms or resident units

to eleven or more residents.

Assisted Living Facility (Syn. Nursing Home, Congregate Care Facility): A residential care

institution, including an adult foster care home, that provides or contracts to provide supervisory care services, personal care services, or directed care services on a continuous basis.

A nursing home provides inpatient beds or resident beds and nursing services to persons who need continuous nursing services but who do not require a hospital or direct daily treatment from a physician.

Convents or Monasteries: The living quarters or dwelling units for a religious order or for the

congregation of persons under religious vows.

Custodial Care Facility : A facility providing care and treatment in a protective living environment

for persons residing voluntarily, by court placement, or under protective control of federal, state, county, or city governance including, without limitation, correctional and post-correctional facilities, juvenile detention facilities, temporary detention facilities, chronically ill tenants, domestic violence victims, outpatient clients, and the developmentally disabled.

Dormitory: A building used for sleeping accommodations for over sixteen unrelated individuals

where such building is related to an educational, public, or religious institution and managed collectively. Communal meals in a dining hall area are often provided.

Group Foster Care: A licensed regular or special foster home that is suitable for placement of

more than five but less than 10 minor children.

Group Home: A residential facility for eight or fewer unrelated persons providing living facilities,

sleeping rooms, and meals in a family-like environment. The number listed does not include the operator, members of the operator's family, or persons employed by the operator as staff, except that the total number of persons living in a group home shall not exceed 10.

This use shall be considered as a single-family dwelling in terms of applicable building form standards.

Residents are supervised by a sponsoring entity or its staff which furnishes rehabilitative services to the group home residents. A group home is owned or operated under the auspices of a nonprofit association, private care provider, government agency, or other legal entity, other than the residents themselves or their parents or other individuals who are their legal guardians.

A group home imposes no time limit on how long an individual can reside in the group home. A group home is a relatively permanent living arrangement where tenancy is measured in years.

This category does not include a home for the developmentally disabled or other institutional uses such as protective living or sheltered care facilities.

Homeless Shelter: See Institutional Residential, Sheltered Care Facility.

318

Title 5: Definitions

Residential Healthcare Facility: Any of the following facilities or any other residential health care

facility as defined by the Arizona Revised State Statutes.

a. Adult behavioral health therapeutic home: A residence for individuals who are at least

eighteen (18) years of age, have behavioral health issues and need behavioral health services that does all of the following for those individuals:

i. Provides room and board;

ii. Assists in acquiring daily living skills;

iii. Coordinates transportation to scheduled appointments;

iv. Monitors behaviors;

v. Assists in the self-administration of medication; and

vi. Provides feedback to case managers related to behavior.

b. Adult day health care facility: A facility that provides adult day health services during a

portion of a continuous twenty-four-hour period for compensation on a regular basis for five (5) or more adults who are not related to the proprietor.

c. Adult foster care home: A residential setting that provides room and board and adult foster

care services for at least one (1) and not more than four (4) adults who are participants in the Arizona long-term care system pursuant to Chapter 29, Article 2 of this title or contracts for services with the United States department of veterans affairs and in which the sponsor or the manager resides with the residents and integrates the residents who are receiving adult foster care into that person's family.

d. Behavioral health respite home: A residence where respite care services, which may

include assistance in the self-administration of medication, are provided to an individual based on the individual's behavioral health issue and need for behavioral health services.

e. Residential care institution: A health care institution other than a hospital that provides

resident beds or residential units, supervisory care services, personal care services, behavioral health services, directed care services, or health-related services for persons who do not need continuous nursing services.

f. Sober living home: As defined by A.R.S. § 36-2061.

Sheltered Care Facility (Includes Homeless Shelter): A residential care institution for nine to

16 unrelated persons providing short-term services such as living facilities, sleeping rooms and meals for the homeless, pregnant teenagers, victims of domestic violence, or neglected children. The number listed does not include the operator, members of the operator’s family, or persons employed by the operator as staff, except that the total number of persons living in a sheltered care home shall not exceed 18. This does not include group homes, homes for the developmentally disabled, or custodial care facilities.

Supervisory Care Services: General supervision, including daily awareness of resident

functioning and continuing needs, the ability to intervene in a crisis, and assistance in the selfadministration of prescribed medications.

Integrity, Historical Resource or Cultural Resource: The authenticity of a cultural resource’s identity, judged by how evident the general character of the period of significance is, the degree to which the characteristics that define its significance are present, and the degree to which incompatible events are reversible.

319

Title 5: Definitions

5-20.100 Definitions “J”

Junk Yard: An establishment or place of business where used materials are bought, sold, exchanged, stored, baled, packed, dissembled, or handled including, but not limited to, scrap iron, and other metals, paper, rags, tires, bottles, and used building materials. Includes an automobile wrecking yard and uses of a similar nature but does not include a landfill or other solid waste disposal site.

5-20.110 Definitions “K”

No terms beginning with the letter K are defined at this time.

5-20.120 Definitions “L”

Landmark: A Property with a specific historic district designation known as the Landmark Overlay Zone.

Landscaped Area: The area of a lot or parcel excluding the footprint of buildings, driveways, parking areas, paved walks and patios, and undeveloped open space/natural areas.

LED (Light Emitting Diode): A semiconductor diode that emits light when a voltage is applied to it.

Letter Report: An abbreviated cultural resource study, in the form of a letter.

Library: A public or quasi-public facility operated as a repository for a collection of books, reference materials, or other similar items and made accessible to the public for reference or borrowing. Often provides public meeting space, community events, and other similar services.

Light Fixture: A complete lighting assembly (including the lamp, housing, reflectors, lenses, and shields), less the support assembly (pole or mounting bracket).

Light Fixture, Fully Shielded: Light fixture or luminous tube constructed and mounted such that all light emitted by the fixture or tube, either directly from the lamp, tube, or a diffusing element, or indirectly by reflection or refraction from any part of the light fixture, is projected below the horizontal. If the lamp or tube, any reflective surface, or lens cover (clear or prismatic) is visible when viewed from above or directly from the side, from any angle around the fixture or tube, the fixture or tube is not fully shielded.

Light Fixture, Partially Shielded: A light fixture constructed and mounted such that most light emitted by the fixture, either directly from the lamp or a diffusing element, or indirectly by reflection or refraction from any part of the fixture, is projected below the horizontal. Light emitted at or above the horizontal direction (sideways or upwards) arises only from incidental decorative elements or strongly colored or diffusing materials such as "honey" or colored glass or plastic.

Light Fixture, Unshielded: A light fixture that is not fully shielded or partially shielded.

Light Trespass: Light falling across property boundaries onto property not containing the original light source.

Live/Work: An integrated housing unit and working space, occupied, and utilized by a single household in either a single-family dwelling or multiple-family dwelling, that has been designed or structurally modified to accommodate joint residential occupancy and work activity, and which includes complete kitchen space and sanitary facilities and working space reserved for and regularly used by one or more occupants of the unit.

Livestock Auction, Feed Yards and Tack Sales: Land uses that include livestock auction areas; livestock feed yards; livestock loading pens; and sales areas. Includes facilities for the feeding of beef

320

Title 5: Definitions

cattle in lots or pens that are not used normally for raising crops and in which no vegetation intended for livestock feed is cultivated. “Livestock” is defined in City Code, Chapter 3, Section 3-1.

Lodging (Syn. Hotel, Motel, Resort, Timeshare): A building or group of buildings containing individual rooms or suites of rooms, each having a private bathroom, for the purpose of providing temporary overnight lodging facilities to the general public for compensation with or without meals, and which has common facilities for reservations and cleaning services and on-site management.

Lot: A parcel of land within a single block which, by reason of ownership, recording, or use is separate and distinct from other such parcels and has its principal frontage on a dedicated street, street easement, or private access way.

Lot, Area, Gross: The area in square feet lying within the lines of the lot.

Lot, Area, Net: The total horizontal area of a lot or parcel of land excluding street right-of-way and other publicly dedicated improvements such as parks, open space, or stormwater facilities.

Lot, Corner: A lot abutting on two or more intersecting streets where the angle of intersection does not exceed one hundred thirty-five (135) degrees.

Lot Depth: The distance between the mid-point of the front and rear lot lines.

Lot, Interior: A lot having only one side abutting on a street.

Lot, Key: An interior lot, one side of which is contiguous to the rear line of a corner lot.

Lot Line: The recorded boundary that legally and geometrically demarcates the boundary of a lot, except where any portion of the lot extends into an abutting street or alley, the lot line is at the street or alley line. The types of lot lines are as follows:

Lot Line, Front: The lot line that abuts the street. On corner lots the front line shall be the shorter of the 2 street lines as originally platted, subdivided, or laid out. Where lines are equal, the front line shall be that line which is the front by reason of prevailing custom of other buildings in the block. Properties with natural topography constraints and other eligible restrictions based on the Director’s discretion may have an alternate Front Lot Line determined as determined by the Director.

Lot Line, Rear: The lot line opposite the front lot line. Where the side lines meet in a point, the rear line shall be considered parallel to the front line or a tangent of the mid-point of a curved front line and lying ten (10) feet within the lot.

Lot Line, Side: Lot lines connecting the front and rear lot lines.

321

Title 5: Definitions

Lot, Through: A lot abutting 2 parallel or approximately parallel streets, of which one shall have recorded a Non-Access Vehicle easement.

Lot Width: If side lot lines are parallel, the shortest distance between the side lot lines. If side lot lines are not parallel, the length of a line at right angles to the axis of the lot at a distance equal to the front setback required for the zoning district in which the lot is located. The axis of a lot shall be a line joining the midpoint of the front and rear property lines.

Lumen: The unit used to measure the actual amount of light which is produced by a light source.

5-20.130 Definitions “M”

Machine Shop: An establishment engaged in the production and/or assembly of metal parts, including the production of metal cabinets and enclosures, cans and shipping containers, doors and gates, duct work, forgings and stampings, hardware, and tools, plumbing fixtures and products, tanks, towers, and similar products. Examples of these uses include, but are not limited to blacksmith and welding shops; platting, stripping, and coating shops, sheet metal shops, machine shops, boiler rooms, and woodworking shops.

Machine, Tool, and Household or Business Goods Rental: A service establishment that may offer a wide variety of household and business equipment, furniture, and materials for rental. Does not include construction equipment rental, which is separately defined under “Equipment Rental Yard - Heavy.”

Manufactured Home: A residential dwelling unit on a transportable structure built on a permanent chassis in a factory or manufacturing plant in compliance with the Housing and Urban Development standards that is designed to be used as a dwelling with or without a permanent foundation and under the regulation of the Arizona State Office of Manufactured Housing

Manufactured Home Park: A parcel of land divided into two or more manufactured home spaces for rent or lease.

Manufactured Home Space: A space for rent within a manufactured home park which has been designed to accommodate a manufactured home and with sewer and other utility connections.

Manufactured Home Subdivision: A subdivision designed exclusively for manufactured homes on individual lots that are individually owned.

322

Title 5: Definitions

Manufacturing and Processing – Heavy: This use accommodates manufacturing processes that involve and/or produce basic metals, building materials, chemicals, fabricated metals, paper products, machinery, textiles, and/or transportation equipment, where the intensity and/or scale of operations may cause significant impacts on surrounding land uses or the community.

Manufacturing and Processing, Light: This use accommodates manufacturing processes involving and/or producing apparel; food and beverage products; electronic, optical, and instrumentation products; ice; jewelry; and musical instruments. Light manufacturing also includes other establishments engaged in the assembly, fabrication, and conversion of already processed raw materials into products, where the operational characteristics of the manufacturing processes and the materials used are unlikely to cause significant impacts on surrounding land uses or the community.

Marijuana Terms: The following terms are related to marijuana uses:

Dual Licensee: An entity that holds both a nonprofit medical marijuana dispensary registration

and a marijuana retail establishment license.

Marijuana:

a. All parts of the plants of the plant of the genus cannabis, whether growing or not, as well as

the seeds from the plant, the resin extracted from any part of the plant, and every compound, manufacture, salt, derivative, mixture or preparation of the plant or its seed or resin;

b. Includes cannabis as defined in A.R.S. §13-3401; and

c. Does not include industrial hemp, the fiber produced from the stalks of the plant of the

genus cannabis, oil or cake made from the seeds of the plant, sterilized seeds of the plant that are incapable of germination, or the weight of any other ingredient combined with marijuana to prepare topical or oral administrations, food, drink, or other products.

Marijuana Establishment: An entity licensed by the Department to operate all of the following:

a. A single retail location at which the licensee may sell marijuana and marijuana products to

consumers, cultivate marijuana and manufacture marijuana products.

b. A single off-site cultivation location at which the licensee may cultivate marijuana, process

marijuana and manufacture marijuana products, but from which marijuana and marijuana products may not be transferred or sold to consumers.

c. A single off-site location at which the licensee may manufacture marijuana products and

package and store marijuana and marijuana products, but from which marijuana and marijuana products may not be transferred or sold to consumers.

Marijuana Facility: Includes a medical marijuana dispensary or a marijuana retail establishment

operated by a dual licensee that is co-located on the same property with a medical marijuana dispensary which has an active, valid medical marijuana dispensary registration from the State of Arizona Department of Health Services (DHS) or its successor agency as well as a marijuana retail establishment license issued by DHS.

Marijuana Retail Establishment: An entity licensed by DHS to operate a single retail location at

which the licensee may sell marijuana and marijuana products to customers.

Marijuana Testing Facility/Laboratory: DHS or another entity that is licensed by DHS to

analyze the potency of marijuana and test marijuana for harmful contaminants.

323

Title 5: Definitions

Medical Marijuana Dispensary: A not-for-profit entity defined in A.R.S. § 36-2801(11), that

acquires, possesses, cultivates, manufacture s, delivers, transfers, transports, supplies, sells, dispenses, or otherwise provides medical marijuana or related supplies and educational materials to cardholders as defined in A.R.S. § 36-2801(2).

Medical Marijuana Dispensary Offsite Cultivation Location: An enclosed, locked facility where

marijuana is cultivated by a medical marijuana dispensary as referenced in ARS § 36-2804.

Marquee: A roof-like structure of permanent nature which projects from the wall of a building.

Mansard Roof: Sloped wall segment or façade that imitates a building roof.

Medical Office (Syn, Health Care Facility): A building or portion thereof that provides outpatient medical services, imaging, or testing, health screenings, behavioral health services, or other healthrelated services. Includes, but is not limited to, medical offices, walk-in clinics, outpatient care, or urgent cares. Excludes hospitals, laboratory and research facilities, and all marijuana-related facilities.

Micro-brewery or Micro-distillery: A facility engaged in the on-site production, bottling, packaging, and consumption of beer and other fermented alcoholic beverages or spirituous beverages. Must be licensed by the proper state agencies and City departments where the service of food, retail sales, or entertainment may be accessory.

Mitigation: Measures, or the application of measures, that lessen impacts to the environment (built or natural). Mitigation can apply to the reduction of environmental, cultural, historical, or natural hazard impacts.

Mixed Use: A building or lot that contains more than one type of land use by integrating residential with non-residential uses, where the different land use types are in close proximity, planned as a unified complementary whole, and shared pedestrian and vehicular access and parking areas are functionally integrated.

Horizontal Mixed Use: Where two or more different types of uses are placed next to each other

on the same parcel.

Vertical Mixed Use: Where two or more different uses occupy the same building usually on

different floors.

Mobile Home: A residential dwelling which was built before January 15, 1976 and not constructed in accordance with the Federal Manufactured Housing Construction and Safety Standards Act of 1974, on a permanent chassis, capable of being transported in one or more sections, and designed to be used with or without a permanent foundation as a dwelling when connected to on-site utilities.

Model Home: A dwelling temporarily used as a sales office and/or as an example of dwelling units to be constructed in the development.

Motocross Racetrack: A dirt racetrack for the exclusive purpose of racing motorcycles or all-terrain vehicles, which could include necessary accessory structures for parking, garages, staging, control, and seating.

Multi-Tenant Building: A building in which two or more separate and independently owned, rented, leased, or operated occupancies are contained.

Museum: Public or quasi-public facilities operated as a repository for a collection of nature, scientific or literary curiosities or objects of interest or works of art, not including the regular sale or distribution of the objects collected.

324

Title 5: Definitions

5-20.140 Definitions “N”

Neon (Syn. Luminous Tube): An illumination source created when a glass tube filled with neon or other similar gas emits light when energized. The tube can be bent to form letters, symbols, or other shapes.

Nonconforming Lot: An existing, legally established parcel or lot of record that does not meet the lot area, lot depth, or lot width requirements for the zone in which it is located.

Nonconforming Structure: An existing, legally established building or structure, which does not comply with the standards imposed by the individual sections of this Code for the zone in which it is located.

Nonconforming Sign: An existing, legally established sign which does not fully comply with the standards imposed by the individual sections of this Code for the zone in which it is located.

Nonconforming Use: An existing, legally established activity, use of land, building, sign, and/or structure, which would not be permitted to be established as a new use in the zone in which it is located by the regulations of this Code.

5-20.150 Definitions “O”

Office: A building, or portion thereof, used for conducting the affairs of a general business, profession, service, or government.

Open Space: Any undeveloped space or area, improved or unimproved, set aside, dedicated, designed, or reserved for public or private use as active or passive recreational areas or for environmental, scenic, or stormwater management purposes.

Open Space, Usable: The minimum amount of private open area required with each unit of a multifamily, mixed use, or similar development.

Outdoor Storage or Display: The storage or display of any personal or business materials, products, or equipment outside of a building.

Owner: The person(s), firm(s), corporation(s), or other legal entity having the right of legal title to, sufficient proprietary interest in, or a contractual right to a lot or parcel of land.

5-20.160 Definitions “P”

Parapet: The portion of a wall that extends above the roof line.

Parcel: Any legally described lot created by a parcel assemblage, subdivision, deed, or other instrument recorded with the county recorder.

Park, Recreation, or Open Spaces or Recreation Facilities: Any public or private land developed for active or passive recreational use or set aside for beautification. May be open to the public for recreational activity, or serve as an historical, cultural, or archeological attraction.

Parking Garage: A building containing two or more stories of parking above natural grade that includes facilities providing both day or longer-term public parking. May be a property’s primary use or as an accessory use.

Parking Lot, Off-street: An open parking area provided on property that is not within a public street, alley, or right-of-way. May be a property’s primary use or as an accessory use.

325

Title 5: Definitions

Patio Cover: An unenclosed shade structure that is attached or detached to a building with posts or columns to support the outer portion, and open on three sides. The roof of a patio cover may be partially open or solid.

Perforated vinyl: A vinyl graphic film that is perforated to allow a full graphic image to be seen on the outside of a window, such that it is not possible to see into a building interior, yet it is possible to see through the image from the interior to the exterior of the building.

Period of Significance: The span of time associated with the significance giving aspects, features or characteristics of a cultural resource.

Person: Any individual, group of individuals, firm, partnership, association, joint venture, corporation, limited liability company, or any other group acting as an entity.

Personal Service Establishment: Businesses offering non-medical services directly to the customer such as barber or beauty shops, nail salons, estheticians, massage establishments, day spas, or tailors. May include limited incidental retail sales.

Place of Assembly: A building or portion thereof for people to gather for regularly scheduled programming. May include commercial entertainment facilities, convention facilities, public meeting halls, or indoor auction houses. Does not include theaters.

Place of Worship: A building or portion thereof for public or private worship services such as a church, synagogue, mosque, temple, convent or monastery, or similar structure/building. Also may include accessory facilities such as assembly halls, sanctuaries, classrooms, conference rooms and meeting rooms, kitchens, multi-purpose rooms, and storage.

Planning Commission: The City of Kingman, Arizona Planning and Zoning Commission.

Plant Nursery: The use of land, buildings, or structures for outdoor display or sales of flowers, plants, shrubs, or trees and may include the sale of commonly used landscaping materials such as containers, fountains, rock, soil or mulch. May include growing facilities or wholesale.

Plat: A map of a subdivision certified by a licensed, registered land surveyor containing a description of the subdivided land with ties to permanent monuments that provide information on land use or ownership.

Plat, Final: A map of all or part of a subdivision, including supporting data, essentially conforming to an approved Preliminary Plat and prepared by a registered civil engineer, a registered land surveyor, a landscape architect, or an architect in accordance with this Ordinance and applicable Arizona Revised Statutes.

Plat, Preliminary: A preliminary map, including supporting data, indicating a proposed subdivision design prepared by a registered civil engineer, a registered land surveyor, a landscape architect, or an architect in accordance with this Ordinance and applicable Arizona Revised Statutes. A preliminary site plan for a condominium development shall be considered a Preliminary Plat.

Porch: A covered or partially covered shelter along a building frontage. May be engaged, with two sides partially or fully enclosed by walls, or projecting, with three open sides.

Portable Storage Container: A rectangular container made of metal consisting of four vertical walls and a ceiling, in which one vertical end is designed as a door and not permanently affixed to the ground. Initially designed for shipping of containerized cargo but often reused for storage on a property. May not be used as a habitable space unless specifically permitted by the City.

Preservation: The act or process of applying measures necessary to sustain the existing form, integrity, and materials of a historic property. Work, including preliminary measures to protect and stabilize the

326

Title 5: Definitions

property, generally focuses upon the ongoing maintenance and repair of historic materials and features, rather than extensive replacement and new construction. New exterior additions are not within the scope of this treatment; however, the limited and sensitive upgrading of mechanical, electrical, and plumbing systems and other code-required work to make properties function is appropriate within a preservation project (Secretary of the Interior’s Standards).

Principal Building (Syn. Primary Building): The main building or structure on a lot where the primary use is conducted.

Printing Plant: A commercial printing operating offering services such as printing, imprinting, reproducing, screen printing, lithography, or duplicating images.

Private Club: A building, or portion thereof, that is owned or leased by an association that restricts the use of such premises to persons who are bona fide members paying annual dues and their bona fide guests. Does not include membership-based retail businesses.

Public Facility: Facilities for the conducting of public business constructed for various public agencies and includes courthouses, city halls, fire stations, police stations, post offices, governmental office, buildings, libraries, and museums, but does not include schools, or places of worship.

Public Services: Government or public services, utilities, and their facilities necessary to provide services for urban development. Does not include services provided by private utility companies.

Emergency Services: Services to the public for emergencies and related buildings or garages

(e.g., ambulance, fire, police, and rescue).

Public Services, Major: Services for the public that include water treatment facility, wastewater

treatment facility and other services that provide major public infrastructure services for urban development.

Public Services, Minor: Services for the public that include utility substations, pumping stations

and other transmission and distribution facilities. Includes the maintenance and servicing activities of governmental owned land, property, and buildings.

5-20.170 Definitions “Q”

No terms beginning with the letter Q are defined at this time.

5-20.180 Definitions “R”

Ranch: A lot, or portion thereof, used for the breeding and raising of large agricultural animals, but is not an agricultural operation.

Recreational Vehicle (RV): A mobile structure designed as temporary living quarters for recreation, vacation, camping, or travel use, which is either self-propelled or is mounted on or drawn by another vehicle with an area of less than 320 square feet. Examples include, but are not limited to, a travel trailer, camping trailer, fifth-wheel trailer, truck camper, motor home, park home, or camper van.

Recreational Vehicle (RV) Park: Any parcel of land authorized to be used in whole or part, with or without charge, for parking of travel trailers, pick-up campers, motor homes or similar devices for a residential occupancy either on a temporary or permanent basis.

Recycling Collection Facility: A lot or structure, or portions thereof, where the public may donate, redeem, or sell recyclable materials with limited processing activities.

327

Title 5: Definitions

Recycling Processing Facility: A structure or enclosed space used for the collection and processing of recycling materials for shipment.

Recycling Reverse Vending Machine: An automated mechanical device which accepts one or more type of empty beverage containers (aluminum, glass, or plastic) and provides a cash refund or redeemable credit slip.

Rehabilitation: The act or process of making possible a compatible use for a property through repair, alterations and additions while preserving those portions or features that convey its historical, cultural or architectural values (Secretary of the Interior’s Standards).

Repair Service Establishment: An establishment that provides repairs for household appliances and other personal property such as shoes, jewelry, technology, or watches. Does not include repairs for automobiles or other heavy equipment.

Research, Development and Testing Laboratory: A building, or portion thereof, that is used for scientific research, and the design, development, or testing of electrical, electronic, magnetic, optical and computer and wireless communication components in advance of product manufacturing, or the assembly of related products from parts produced off-site, where the manufacturing activity is limited and clearly secondary to the research and development activities. Includes pharmaceutical, chemical, and biotechnology research and development. Laboratories must comply with all applicable federal, state, and local regulations related to odor, dust, smoke, gas, noise, radiation, or other similar pollution.

Residence for Owner, Caretaker, or Manager: An accessory dwelling for the owner or manager of a use requiring 24-hour management.

Residential: A land use type that is designated to accommodate single-family and multiple-family dwellings. Includes mobile and manufactured homes.

Restaurant: An establishment whose principal business is the sale of food and/or beverages to customers in a ready-to-consume state and where food or beverages are primarily consumed on the premises. May include a drive-through.

Restaurant, Convenience/Take-Out: An establishment whose principal business is the sale of food and/or beverages to customers in a ready-to-consume state, but where food and/or beverages are primarily taken off-premises for consumption. Commonly incorporates a service or window counter, or a drive-through.

Restoration: The act or process of accurately depicting the form, features and character of a property as it appeared at a particular period of time by means of the removal of features from other periods in its history and reconstruction of missing features from the restoration period. The limited and sensitive upgrading of mechanical electrical and plumbing systems and other code-required work to make properties function is appropriate within a restoration project (Secretary of the Interior’s Standards).

Retention Basin : A stormwater storage facility that stores surface runoff from a site. May include parking lot islands or planting strips or swales in compliance with the City’s Kingman Area Master Drainage Plan.

Review Authority : An individual or official City body responsible for reviewing and making decisions on applications required by this Zoning Code.

Right-of-Way (ROW): Any access. Public rights-of-way may include areas required for public use pursuant to the General Plan. Public rights-of-way may be designated by Plat dedication or by conveyance of fee title or easements by deed.

5-20.190 Definitions “S”.

328

Title 5: Definitions

School: Any public or private institution providing primary, secondary, or tertiary education at standards equal to or greater than the standards prescribed by the State Board of Education or the State Board of Regents.

School, College: Institutions of higher learning, including public and private universities and other institutions of higher learning.

School, Micro: An institution that provides a personalized learning environment to a small group of students. Similar to a home-school, but where instruction is not necessarily provided by a child’s own parents or guardians or within a home environment.

School, Private: A non-public institution, other than a child’s home, where a curriculum of general instruction similar to a public school is imparted.

School, Vocational (Syn. Trade School): An institution offering instruction in technical, commercial, or trade skills such as automotive technician schools or real estate schools. Often a commercial establishment.

Screen: A solid, or nearly solid, wall or fence designed and constructed so as to conceal areas such as those used for refuse, mechanical equipment, parking, and service and loading bays from street and public view, and to separate potential incompatible land uses.

Self-Storage Facility: Real property used for renting or leasing individual storage spaces in which the renters or lessees customarily store and remove their own personal property on a self-service basis.

Service Call Establishment: An establishment that dispatches professional services from the business’ office to the customer’s location to provide a service such as, but not limited to, pest control, catering, electrician, plumbing, or personal services.

Setback: The minimum required distance on a lot measured from the lot line to any permanent structures except for specifically permitted encroachments.

Sexually Oriented Business (Syn. Adult Oriented Business): The existence, operation, opening or commencement of, or the conversion of an existing business to, or the addition to any other existing business of, or the relocation of any of the following: adult arcades; adult bookstores or video stores or novelty stores; cabarets; adult live entertainment establishments; adult motion picture theaters; adult theaters; massage establishments that offer adult service; escort agencies that offer adult service; sexual encounter centers; erotic dance or performance studios; adult motels, nude model studios, or similar establishments.

Nothing in the following definitions shall be construed to apply to the presentation, showing, or performance of any play, drama, or ballet in any theater, concert hall, fine arts academy, school, institution of higher learning or other similar establishment as a form of expression of opinion or communication of ideas or information, as differentiated from the promotion or exploitation of nudity for the purposes of advancing the economic welfare of a commercial or business enterprise.

The definition of sexually oriented businesses also shall not include an establishment where a medical practitioner, psychologist, psychiatrist, or similar professional person licensed by the state engages in medically approved and recognized sexual therapy.

Additional information on the types of businesses classified as sexually oriented businesses is provided in the following subsections:

Adult Arcade: Any place to which the public is permitted or invited and in which coin-operated or

slug-operated or electronically, electrically, or mechanically controlled still or motion picture

329

Title 5: Definitions

machines, projectors or other image producing devices are maintained to show images involving “specific sexual activities” or “specific anatomical areas” to persons in booths or viewing rooms.

Adult Bookstore (Syn. Adult Novelty Store, or Adult Video Store) : A commercial

establishment that offers for sale, rent, or viewing any of the following as one of its principal business purposes:

a. Books, magazines, periodicals, or other printed matter, photographs, medium, including but

not limited to any hard disk, floppy disk, diskette, disk pack, DVD, CD ROM, hologram, magnetic tape or cards or other similar photographic reproductions, or reproductions or slides or other visual representations that depict or describe “specific sexual activities” or “specific anatomical areas”; or

b. Instruments, devices, or paraphernalia that are designed for use in connection with “specific

sexual activities” excluding condoms, diaphragms, contraceptive inserts, contraceptive medications and other birth control or disease prevention devices prescribed by a licensed medical doctor or osteopathic doctor.

A “principal business” purpose must meet any one or more of the following criteria:

a. That 30 percent or more of its inventory, stock, or merchandise on hand at any time that is

comprised of items listed under the definition for an adult bookstore, novelty store, or video store;

b. That 30 percent or more of its gross income for any one calendar month from the sale or

rental, for any form of consideration, of the items listed under the definition for an adult bookstore, novelty store, or video store.

c. A commercial establishment may have other business purposes that do not involve the

offering for sale or rental of material depicting or describing “specific sexual activities” or “specific anatomical areas” and still be categorized as an adult bookstore, adult video store, or adult novelty store.

d. Such other business purposes will not serve to exempt such commercial establishment from

being categorized as an adult bookstore, adult video store, or adult novelty store. Such businesses shall exclude all minors from the premises or a separate defined section thereof because of the sexually explicit nature of the items sold, rented, or displayed.

Adult Cabaret (Syn. Adult Live Entertainment Establishment): A nightclub, bar, restaurant, or

other similar commercial establishment that regularly features:

a. Persons who appear in a state of nudity or who are seminude;

b. Live performances that are characterized by the exposure of “specific anatomical areas” or

“specific sexual activities”; or

c. Films, motion pictures, videocassettes, slides or other photographic reproductions that are

characterized by the depiction or description of “specific sexual activities” or “specific anatomical areas”.

d. An adult cabaret may be licensed to provide alcoholic beverages in compliance with Arizona

Revised Statute, Title 4, Chapter 2, Article 1.

Adult Hotel or Motel: A hotel, motel, or similar commercial establishment which:

a. Offers accommodations to the public for any form of consideration and provides patrons with

closed-circuit television transmissions, films, motion pictures, video cassettes, video

330

Title 5: Definitions

reproductions, slides, or other reproductions offered in photographic, electronic, magnetic, digital or other imaging medium or other visual presentations which are distinguished or characterized by “specific sexual activities” or “specific anatomical areas” as one of its principal business purposes; or,

b. Offers a sleeping room for rent for a period of time that is less than 10 hours; or

c. Allows a tenant or occupant of a sleeping room to sub-rent the room for a period of time that

is less than 10 hours.

Adult Motion Picture Theater or Adult Cinema: An enclosed or open-air commercial

establishment in which for any form of consideration films, motion pictures, video cassettes, slides, plate negative film, plate positive film or tape designed to be projected on a screen for exhibition, glass slides or transparencies, either in negative or positive form, designed for exhibition by projection on a screen, or images from an on-site or off-site electronic or magnetic storage medium, including but not limited to any hard disk, floppy disk, diskette, disk pack, DVD, CD ROM, hologram, magnetic tape or cards or other similar photographic reproductions or any other device capable of creating a display on a screen or other viewing media that are characterized by the depiction or description of “specific sexual activities” or “specific anatomical areas” are predominantly shown, irrespective of the number of patrons who may be able to view the presentation at any one time.

Adult Service: Adult service includes dancing, serving food or beverages, modeling, posing,

wrestling, singing, reading, talking, listening, or other performances or activities conducted for any consideration in an adult oriented business by a person who is nude or seminude during all or part of the time that the person is providing the service.

Adult Service Provider or Erotic Entertainer: Any natural person who provides an adult

service.

Adult Theater: A theater, concert hall, auditorium, or similar commercial establishment that

predominantly features persons who appear in a state of nudity or who engage in live performances that are characterized by the exposure of “specific anatomical areas” or “specific sexual activities”.

Erotic Dance or Performance Studio: A business that emphasizes and seeks, through one or

more dancers or other performers, to arouse or excite the patrons’ sexual desires.

Escort: A person who for consideration agrees or offers to act as a companion, guide, or date for

another person or who agrees or offers to privately model lingerie or to privately perform a striptease for another person.

Escort Agency: A person or business association that furnishes, offers to furnish, or advertises

the furnishing of escorts as one of its primary business purposes for any fee, tip, or other consideration.

Massage Establishment: An establishment in which a person, firm, association, or corporation

engages in or permits massage activities, including any method of pressure on, friction against, stroking, kneading, rubbing, tapping, pounding, vibrating, or stimulating of external soft parts of the body with the hands or with the aid of any mechanical apparatus or electrical apparatus or appliance. A massage establishment shall also include any bathing establishment operated in conjunction with the business. This paragraph does not apply to:

a. Physicians licensed in compliance with Arizona Revised Statutes, Title 32, Chapter 7, 8, 13,

14 or 17.

331

Title 5: Definitions

b. Registered nurses, licensed practical nurses or technicians who are acting under the

supervision of a physician licensed in compliance with Arizona Revised Statutes, Title 32, Chapter 13 or 17.

c. Persons who are employed or acting as trainers for a bona fide amateur, semiprofessional,

or professional athlete or athletic team.

d. Persons who are licensed in compliance with Arizona Revised Statutes, Title 32, Chapter 3

or 5 if the activity is limited to the head, face, or neck.

Nude Model Studio: A place in which a person who appears in a state of nudity or who displays

“specific anatomical areas” is observed, sketched, drawn, painted, sculptured, photographed, or otherwise depicted by other persons who pay money or other consideration.

Nude model studio does not include a class at a school that is licensed by Arizona, a college, community college or university that is supported entirely or in part by taxation, a private college or university that maintains and operates educational programs in which credits are transferable to a college, community college or university that is supported entirely or in part by taxation or a structure to which the following apply:

a. A sign is not visible from the exterior of the structure and no other advertising appears

indicating that a nude person is available for viewing;

b. A student must enroll at least three days in advance of a class in order to participate; and

c. No more than one nude or seminude model is on the premises at any time.

Nude, Nudity, State of Nudity: Includes any of the following:

a. The appearance of the cleft of the buttocks, a human anus, genitals, male genitals in a

discernibly turgid state, or female breast below a point immediately above the top of the areola; or

b. A state of dress that fails to opaquely cover the cleft of the buttocks, a human anus, genitals,

or female breast below a point immediately above the top of the areola.

Seminude: A state of dress in which clothing covers no more than the genitals, pubic region, and

female breast below a point immediately above the top of the areola. This definition shall not include any portion of the cleavage of the human female breast, provided the areola is not exposed in whole or in part.

Sexual Encounter Center: Any business, agency, or person who, for any form of consideration

or gratuity, and as one of its principal business purposes, provides:

a. Activities between persons when one or more of the persons is in a state of nudity;

b. The matching and/or exchanging of persons for “specific sexual activities”;

c. Activities between persons when one or more of the persons is in a state of nudity for the

purpose of engaging or attempting to engage in “specific sexual activities”; or

d. Physical contact between persons, when one or more of the persons is in a state of nudity

in the forms of tumbling, wrestling, or other similar activities for the purpose of engaging or attempting to engage in “specific sexual activities”.

Specific Anatomical Area: Means any of the following:

332

Title 5: Definitions

a. A human anus, genitals, pubic region, cleft of the buttocks, or a female breast below a point

immediately above the top of the areola that is less than completely and opaquely covered; or

b. Human genitals in a discernibly turgid state even if completely and opaquely covered.

Specific Sexual Activities. Means any of the following:

a. Human genitals in a state of sexual stimulation or arousal;

b. Sex acts, normal or perverted, actual, or simulated, including acts of human masturbation,

sexual intercourse, oral copulation, or sodomy;

c. Fondling or other erotic touching of the human genitals, pubic region, buttocks, anus, or

female breast; or,

d. Urinary or excretory functions as part of or in connection with any of the activities described

in subsections a, b, and c above.

Sheltered Care Facility: See Institutional residential.

Shopping Center (Syn. Commercial Center) : A development designed as a group of commercial buildings and uses planned, developed, and managed as a unit with off-street parking provided on the property. Incorporates cohesive circulation, parking, utilities, landscaping, architectural style, and featuring pedestrian friendly connections regardless the number of parcels or pad sites.

Showroom/Sales Office: A building or portion thereof where merchandise is exhibited or where samples or displayed and the sale of services and related products are conducted. Examples include flooring, décor, remodeling services, or other contractors. May be a primary or accessory use.

Sidewalk: A concrete public way with curb, gutter and driveway cutouts constructed in accordance with adopted Improvement Standards and located within the Street Right-of-Way.

Sign: A structure, device, figure, display, message placard or other contrivance, or any part thereof, situated outdoors or indoors, which is designed, constructed, intended or used to advertise, provide information in the nature of advertising, provide historical, cultural, archeological, ideological, political, religious, or social information, or direct or attract attention to an object, person, institution, business, product, service, message, event or location by any means, including words, letters, figures, designs, symbols, fixtures, colors, or illumination. Additional sign-related terms are defined below.

Abandoned Sign: A sign that identifies a business, lessor, owner, product, service, or activity that is no longer on the premises where the sign is displayed.

A-Frame Sign: A pedestrian oriented sign that is not permanently affixed to a structure or the ground to advertise special goods, services, or products offered on the site (also known as a Sandwich Board Sign or an Upright Sign).

Approved Bottom-mounted Outdoor Advertising Fixture. A system of lighting which is installed at or on the lower portion of a billboard.

Awning Sign: Any sign that is part of or attached to an awning, canopy, or other fabric, plastic or structural protective cover located over a door, entrance, window, storefront, or outdoor service area.

Balloon: A brightly colored bag made of flexible material, inflated with air or other gas, and sealed, often to make it rise in the air.

Balloon Bobber: A reusable pre-formed balloon with regular air made of a durable PVC vinyl that does not need to be inflated, and typically attached to a short pole.

333

Title 5: Definitions

Bandit Sign: Any sign that is placed on public property or on private property without the consent of the property owner or as authorized in Division 4-90 (Signs).

Banner Sign: A temporary sign constructed of cloth, plastic, vinyl, or similar material and securely attached to a wall or support structure.

Billboard: A permanent structure for the display of a commercial or non-commercial message.

Blade Sign: A permanent sign that is perpendicular to the face of a building and projects outward from the building face.

Building Identification Sign: A sign consisting of letters or numbers applied to a building wall, engraved into the building material, or consisting of a sculptural relief which contains the name of the building or describes its function, but which does not advertise any individual tenant of the building or any products or services offered.

Building Mounted Sign: Sign attached to, connected to, erected against the wall, parapet, or fascia of a building or structure with the exposed face of the sign in a plane parallel to the vertical face of the building or structure.

Canopy Sign: A sign that is printed, painted, or affixed to a canopy, typically used to accent building entries.

Channel Letters: Three dimensional, individually manufactured letters or figures with an open back which may contain a light source to provide light onto the sign background against which the channel letters are silhouetted.

Commercial Message: A message conveyed by any sign that is solely intended to interest, entice, or solicit any person to participate in commercial transactions with a business, including offers of goods, cash, discounts on products or services, or other items, including the offering of free goods or services made in exchange for or with the intent to induce the recipient’s willingness to receive information relating to a possible commercial transaction.

Directional Sign : A sign erected to inform the viewer of the approximate route, direction, or location of a facility or tenant.

Directory Sign: A sign on a multi-tenant site providing information including a list of tenants and occupants, addresses, and suite numbers or a map of the building or complex. These signs are typically internal to a center, oriented to pedestrians, or motorists circulating in a parking lot rather than vehicles travelling on an arterial street.

Electronic Message Sign: A sign or portion of a sign that utilizes computer-generated messages or some other electronic means of changing its characters, letters, numbers, illustrations, display, color, and/or light intensity, including animated graphics and video, by electronic or automatic means. An Electronic Message Sign is not a Single- or Two-Color LED Sign .

Feather Banner Sign: A sign that is taller than it is wide and made of a flexible material (typically cloth, nylon, or vinyl) and mounted to a pole.

Fixed Aerial Display: An aerial advertising device that is tethered to the ground.

Freestanding Sign: A sign that is erected or mounted on its own self-supporting permanent structure or base detached from any supporting elements of a building.

Freestanding Projecting Sign: A freestanding sign type featuring a double or single sided sign face, projecting outward at a perpendicular angle from a sign pole or post.

334

Title 5: Definitions

Fuel Pump Topper Sign: A sign affixed to the top of an operable fuel dispensing pump used to advertise goods offered for sale on the same parcel on which the fuel pump is located.

Halo Illumination: A form of sign illumination in which neon tubing, LED, or similar lights are mounted within the letter to illuminate the mounting surface causing a halo of light around the letter.

Historical Plaque: A sign that memorializes a person, event, building, former use of a place, or something else of historical significance.

Incidental Sign: A sign which provides incidental information including security, credit card acceptance, business hours, open/closed, directions to services and facilities, or menus.

Individual Letter Sign: A cut-out or etched letter or logo which is individually mounted on a building wall, or freestanding sign

Landmark Sign: A sign determined by the City to have attained a high degree of community, cultural, aesthetic, or historic significance.

LED Sign: A sign consisting of light emitting diodes (electronic components that let electricity pass in only one direction) that emit visible light when electricity is applied.

Logo Sign: A stylized group of letters, words, numbers, or symbols used to represent and distinguish a business or product.

Marquee Sign: A permanent sign structure placed over the entrance to a building and typically used for a theater or other entertainment use.

Monument Sign: A freestanding sign, with a solid base that is equal to or larger than the width of the sign face.

Nits: The standard unit used to measure the luminance of a surface, such as the face of a sign. One nit is equivalent to one candela (i.e. the light output of a common wax candle) per square meter.

Non-Conforming Sign: Any sign legally established prior to the Effective Date which does not fully comply with the standards imposed by the individual sections of this Code.

Off-premises Sign: Any sign that directs attention to a business, commodity, service, entertainment, product, structure, use or property different from a structure or use existing on the property where the sign is located, and/or any sign on which space is rented, donated, or sold by the owner of said sign or property for the purpose of conveying a message.

On-Premise Sign: A sign directing attention to a business, commodity, service, or offered upon the same premises as those upon which the sign is maintained.

Painted Wall Sign: . A sign painted directly onto the exterior wall of a building and having no sign structure.

Pan Channel Letter: A specific type of sign letter consisting of a metal pan enclosure fabricated in the shape of a letter. The metal pan enclosure is used to house the lighting and electrical components of the letter and can be mounted directly to wall of a building. The sign face is usually made of colored plastic attached to the metal pan to seal it off from pests and harsh weather.

Pennant Sign: A temporary sign made of flexible materials longer than it is wide, often triangular in shape, and frequently displayed with other pennants on a string.

Perforated vinyl: A vinyl graphic film that is perforated to allow a full graphic image to be seen on the outside of a window, such that it is not possible to see into a building interior, yet it is possible to see through the image from the interior to the exterior of the building.

335

Title 5: Definitions

Permanent Sign: A sign constructed of durable materials and intended to exist for the duration of time that the use or occupant is located on the premises.

Pole Sign: A sign that is mounted on one or more freestanding poles or other similar supports so that the bottom edge of the sign face is not in contact with the ground.

Post Sign: A permanent sign mounted on either a single post or two or more posts.

Professionally Crafted or Painted Sign: A temporary or permanent sign that is made to a high standard by a competent individual(s) skilled in sign design, fabrication, and installation.

Prohibited Sign: Any sign not in accordance with or authorized in Division 4-90 (Signs).

Projecting Sign: A sign that is perpendicular to the face of a building and projects outward from the building face.

Pylon Sign: A tall, freestanding structure used for advertising, characterized by a signboard supported by one or more poles or pillars that are a minimum of eight feet off the ground. Pylon signs typically advertise multiple businesses and are highly visible from a distance.

Raceway: An enclosed conduit for electrical wiring.

Raised Letter Sign: A sign containing copy, logo, and/or decorative embellishments in relief on the face.

Roof-Mounted Sign: Any sign erected, painted, or attached on or over the roof of a building.

Service Island Canopy Sign: A sign mounted on or under a service island canopy, including on a fascia.

Sign Changeable Copy: A sign that is designed so that characters, letters, numbers, or illustrations can be manually or mechanically changed or rearranged without altering the face or surface of the sign.

Sign Copy: Any graphic, word, numeral, symbol, insignia, text, sample, model, device, or combination thereof that is primarily intended to advertise, identify, or notify.

Sign Face: The exterior surface of a sign, exclusive of structural supports, on which is placed the sign copy.

Sign Spinner: A lightweight, durable, and colorful device designed to be affected by the movement of air so that it spins or rotates in a manner to capture attention.

Sign Structure: The supports, uprights, bracing and/or framework of a sign.

Sign Walker: A person who wears, holds, or balances a sign.

Single-Color or Two-Color LED Sign: A permanent or temporary sign or portion of a sign composed of single-color or two-color LEDs that displays static or changeable sign messages using characters, letters, and numbers only. Examples of these signs include, but are not limited to, "open" or "closed" signs", ”time and temperature" signs or signs indicating the number of available spaces in a parking facility.

Static Display: A sign face that does not change within a twenty-four hour period.

Temporary Sign: A sign constructed of paper, cloth, vinyl, fabric, or similar material, which is intended for a definite and limited period of display and which is not permanently affixed to a structure, sign area, or window.

336

Title 5: Definitions

Valance: The vertical front face of an awning, parallel to the face of the building to which it is mounted.

Vehicle Sign: Any sign on or affixed to a truck, van, automobile, trailer, or other vehicle, with the exception of magnetic door signs.

Wall Sign: A sign affixed to or erected against the wall or fascia of a building or structure, with the exposed face of the sign parallel to the plane of wall or fascia to which it is affixed or erected.

Wall Banner Sign: A temporary sign and constructed of cloth, bunting, plastic, paper, or similar nonrigid material, and securely attached to the wall or support structure for which it is advertising. Flags are not considered temporary wall banners.

Window Sign: A sign posted, painted, placed, or affixed in, on, or within a window, or otherwise exposed to public view through a window.

Yard Sign, Type I: A small temporary sign typically constructed of corrugated plastic and supported on an H-shaped wire frame used for example, for advertising by local businesses or by election campaigns. (Syn: Lawn Sign).

Yard Sign, Type II: A sign mounted on a single post installed securely in the ground with a small sign hanging from a cross-bar mounted parallel to the ground.

Yard Sign, Type III: A large typically wooden sign mounted on two posts installed securely in the ground.

Single Tenant Building: A building with no more than one owned, rented, leased, or operated commercial occupancy is contained.

Site Plan: A graphic depiction showing the accurate location of features on a site such as existing and proposed structures, paved areas, ingress/egress points, and landscaped areas in relation to property lines and streets along with any additional information required by this Code or the Director.
